# Decision log

Newest entries at the bottom. Format: date — decision — reason.

- 2026-09-28 — The 2026 scope is a season-ready MVP with alerts issued by admins (human in the loop). — A calibrated forecast can't be validated before November, and wrong automated alerts would cost lives and trust.
- 2026-09-28 — Use open and global data only for now (ThaiWater public API, Sentinel-1, FABDEM, GSMaP/IMERG, Open-Meteo/GloFAS, OSM). — No agency data agreements are in place yet.
- 2026-09-28 — Approved additions: LINE OA plus offline and SMS fallback; Patani Malay plus voice reporting; vulnerable-household pre-registration; restricted location visibility plus authority verification. — These fit local language, connectivity and security realities.
- 2026-09-28 — Stack: Next.js PWA, Supabase (Postgres/PostGIS, Auth, RLS, Realtime), MapLibre with OpenFreeMap and PMTiles, Python pipeline with Google Earth Engine, hosted on Vercel. — Spatial queries and RLS are central to the app; no per-map-load fees; low cost.
- 2026-09-28 — Sign-in: LINE Login and phone OTP are equal options for users; phone OTP for authorities; email magic link for admins (invite-only). — LINE is free and near-universal in Thailand; OTP is the fallback and verifies authorities.
- 2026-09-28 — Budget: about $30/month off-season, up to $150/month from October to December. — Non-profit running costs.
- 2026-09-28 — Notifications: web push is primary (free); LINE multicast goes only to users in affected tambons; the LINE plan is upgraded only for October to December. — LINE bills per recipient.
- 2026-09-28 — Alert levels: normal, watch, warning, evacuate, return (ปกติ, เฝ้าระวัง, เตือนภัย, อพยพ, กลับบ้านได้), issued per tambon. — Simple, familiar colors, and they include the return signal.
- 2026-09-28 — Priority warning zones: Pattani River, Kolok, U-Taphao. — Best gauge coverage and the most people exposed.
- 2026-09-28 — Add donations to support the app (running costs, improvements, admin/volunteer support), with a public transparency page. The feature stays behind a flag until the receiving entity and legal check are settled. — Sustainability and donor trust; keeps fundraising separate from emergency flows.
- 2026-09-28 — Donations go to a dedicated Thai bank account in the founder's name for now (used only for the app), disclosed on the page, with no tax receipts. This moves to a foundation once one is registered. — Fastest path; the switch later is a settings change.
- 2026-09-28 — Giving method: Thai PromptPay QR only. — Zero fees and universal among Thai donors; simplest to reconcile.
- 2026-09-28 — Admin support means small stipends for active admins, paid only from the surplus after running costs, capped, based on logged shifts, and approved by the super admin. Only totals are public. — Rewards volunteer time while keeping the finances sustainable and transparent.
- 2026-09-28 — Donation numbers appear in a Transparency tab on the main dashboard (secondary tab, never the default, no badges), behind the donation flag. — Public accountability without pressuring people during emergencies.
- 2026-09-28 — Donations fund only the app's maintenance and operation. The app does not collect or distribute relief for affected people for now; every donation screen says so and links to official relief channels. — Clear scope, legal safety, donor trust.
- 2026-09-28 — The design is hazard-agnostic from day one (hazard_type on alerts, reports, SOS, safe places; a hazards config table). Only flood is active; flash flood, landslide, fire and earthquake show "coming soon" placeholders; SOS works for any emergency. — Cheap now, expensive to retrofit; many hazards can't be forecast, but SOS still helps.
- 2026-09-28 — App name: Jaga (Thai จากา), Malay for "watch over, take care of". Logo: an eye drawn with terrain contour lines. Taglines: ดูแลกันและกัน / Jaga diri, jaga jiran / Watch over each other. Brand: slate #1D3B53, teal #2F9C95, IBM Plex Sans Thai. Details in docs/brand.md. — Not flood-specific (fits multi-hazard), short, easy to say in Thai and Malay, and speaks to community care. The eye mark is to be tested locally for surveillance connotations.
- 2026-09-28 — Repo layout: CLAUDE.md at the root; spec, science plan, brand, prompts and this log in docs/; logo files in public/brand/ and icons in public/icons/ (from jaga-starter.zip, whose logo files are the clean versions). — One source of truth per file; the zip and duplicates were removed.
- 2026-09-28 — The repo lives outside cloud-synced folders; nothing in scripts or config depends on its path. pipeline/data/ is gitignored in the app repo. — Cloud sync breaks node_modules, local databases and large data folders.
- 2026-09-28 — (c) SOS: the phone number is optional for anonymous SOS; only location is required. The phone field is prominent, with the hint "Without a number, rescuers can't call you." — Safety rule 1: nothing may block an SOS.
- 2026-09-28 — (d) SOS rate limiting never rejects an SOS. While a case is open, repeat SOS from the same device or phone merge into that case as updates. Above a threshold, new cases are flagged "suspected spam" for admin review; admins can bulk-dismiss them; authorities see the flag. — Abuse control without ever dropping a real emergency.
- 2026-09-28 — (e) Personal phone numbers are always private. An organization's official phone is public only if the org opts in (organizations.public_contact_opt_in, default false). Authorities may read other units' POC phones within shared coverage; every reveal is logged. — Closes the gap between safety rule 5 and the access matrix.
- 2026-09-28 — (f) Vulnerable-household data is readable only by verified authorities with rescue or coordination capability covering the tambon, and by admins (logged). — The stricter of the two rules applies.
- 2026-09-28 — (g) "Not updated" (stale) is a marker, not a sixth alert level: the level badge stays and a grey "not updated since [time]" marker is added. Five levels + stale marker. — The level must never be hidden or downgraded when an update is late.
- 2026-09-28 — (h) No eye icons anywhere in the UI except the logo. Watch uses a backpack icon (get your go-bag ready). — Avoids surveillance connotations and keeps the eye unique to the brand.
- 2026-09-28 — (i) Seed all 77 provinces (codes, names, simplified geometry) in A1: active for Pattani, Yala, Narathiwat and Songkhla, coming_soon for the rest. A3 shows the others disabled with the same no-implied-safety rule as placeholder hazards: "Jaga doesn't cover this province yet. In an emergency call 1784 or 1669." — Requested in the original brief; an uncovered province must never look safe.
- 2026-09-28 — (j) Donation tables (donation_settings, donations, expenses, expense_categories, admin_shifts, monthly_finance) are created in A11 only, not in A1. — Keeps the season-critical schema small; donations ship after launch.
- 2026-09-28 — (k) Budget: about $45/month off-season (Supabase Pro + Vercel Pro), up to $150/month October–December. Vercel Hobby during development; move to Vercel Pro before launch. Revisit Cloudflare in 2027. The owner confirms free non-commercial use with Open-Meteo; the app shows Open-Meteo attribution. — Vercel Hobby is non-commercial only; the old $30 target did not cover both plans.
- 2026-09-28 — (l) The tide/sea-level source is decided in A8: evaluate ThaiWater's tide and storm-surge data first, Open-Meteo Marine as backup. — The science plan had not chosen one.
- 2026-09-28 — (m) Revised the same day: ThaiWater water-level history is limited to 365 days per request, not in total; older years are reachable (for example PAT002 back to 2012). S1 archives all available water-level years, and the 2024 hindcast uses gauges wherever they exist, plus rainfall, GloFAS and SAR. — Verified against the live API.
- 2026-09-28 — (n) pipeline/data/ holds all pipeline data. S1 selects stations by province code plus a bounding box (tambon boundaries arrive in A1). — One data root; no dependency on the app database.
- 2026-09-28 — (o) The owner chooses an SMS provider this week. Fallback if it is not ready by launch: authorities may also sign in with LINE Login, and the admin verifies their POC phone by calling it during verification (A2). — Authorities must be able to sign in on 25 October.
- 2026-09-28 — S1 archive storage: pipeline/data/ is a clone of the private repo nafiscode/jaga-data (tidy Parquet, stations.csv, coverage report, resume state). Raw API responses stay out of git; the first full archive, raw included, is attached to a jaga-data release. — Nothing exists only on one laptop; the code repo history stays small.
- 2026-09-28 — S1 scheduled jobs run in GitHub Actions in the app repo and push to jaga-data with a fine-grained token (JAGA_DATA_TOKEN, jaga-data only): weekly refresh, hourly rain every 6 h, and a daily time-boxed backfill of daily rain to Oct 2017 that stops once complete. Fallback if ThaiWater blocks GitHub's servers: Windows Task Scheduler. — The app repo is private, so the job lives next to the code.
- 2026-09-28 — Hourly rain has no history in the ThaiWater API (only the last ~42 h), so it is collected every 6 h from now on. Hourly rain before today comes from GSMaP/IMERG. Daily rain history is available in 31-day windows. — Verified against the live API.
- 2026-09-28 — Malaysia publicinfobanjir: robots.txt allows crawling, but the site states "All Rights Reserved" and grants no reuse licence. No downloader is built until the owner gets permission from JPS/DID. — S1 says to stop when the terms are unclear.
- 2026-09-28 — Development may continue on another machine from A0 onwards. Everything is pushed to GitHub; SETUP.md describes a fresh Windows setup. — Nothing should exist on only one machine.
- 2026-09-28 — The app repo (nafiscode/flood-warning-app) becomes public; the data archive (nafiscode/jaga-data) stays private. — Transparency for a public-interest project; the archive is third-party data kept for analysis.
- 2026-09-28 — License: Apache-2.0 for code and docs. The Jaga name, the จากา wordmark and the logo/icon files are not covered (README "Name and logo", NOTICE). — Permissive reuse of the code, while forks can't pose as the official alert service.
- 2026-09-28 — Project contact: jagaapp.th@gmail.com. Used in the ThaiWater User-Agent ("Jaga/0.1 (non-profit flood warning; <contact>)"), SECURITY.md and the README. Scripts read it from CONTACT_EMAIL (environment, else .env.local, else .env.example), never hardcoded. It is not a git commit email. — One public address for the project; config, not code.
- 2026-09-28 — Git commits use the owner's GitHub noreply address (135354913+nafiscode@users.noreply.github.com), never a personal email. — Keeps personal email out of a public history.
- 2026-09-28 — Actions safety: workflows default to a read-only token; jobs that use JAGA_DATA_TOKEN run only in nafiscode/flood-warning-app (never in forks); no pull_request_target; third-party actions pinned to commit SHAs; fork pull requests need the owner's approval before workflows run. — The token can write to the private data repo.
- 2026-09-28 — Keepalive: a workflow re-enables the scheduled workflows through the GitHub API on the 1st and 15th of each month. — Public repos disable schedules after 60 days without activity; the data jobs commit to jaga-data, so this repo can go quiet off-season.
- 2026-09-28 — Superseded the same day: nafiscode/flood-warning-app stays private for now. The LICENSE/NOTICE and the keepalive workflow were removed (the 60-day schedule disable applies only to public repos); README says "not yet licensed for reuse". Kept: noreply commit email, project contact via CONTACT_EMAIL, and the Actions hardening. — Owner's decision; the license choice (Apache-2.0 proposed) is revisited if the repo goes public.
- 2026-09-28 — The ThaiWater collectors and their workflows moved to a new public repo, nafiscode/jaga-collectors, which writes to the private jaga-data. The app repo keeps no collector code. — Estimated Actions use: ~1,050 min in October and ~700/month after for the collectors, plus ~1,000–1,500 min of app CI in October, would exceed the 2,000-minute private allowance. Public repos have free standard-runner minutes, so the app repo keeps its full allowance. Chosen over GitHub Pro.
- 2026-09-28 — Hourly rain is collected every 12 h (was 6 h), and the weekly job probes long-silent water-level stations cheaply instead of downloading a year of nulls. — Halves the hourly job's minutes; 12 h still leaves 30 h of margin in ThaiWater's ~42 h window.
- 2026-09-28 — jaga-collectors (public): read-only default token, JAGA_DATA_TOKEN jobs run only in that repo, approval required for outside contributors' workflows, private vulnerability reporting on, keepalive twice a month. No license yet (all rights reserved). — The token can write to the private data repo; public repos disable schedules after 60 days without activity.
- 2026-09-28 — History of flood-warning-app and jaga-data rewritten so every commit uses the GitHub noreply address; force-pushed before any other clone existed. — The owner's personal email is kept out of commit metadata.
- 2026-09-28 — Superseded the same day: back to one code repo. The collectors and their workflows return to flood-warning-app (pipeline/ingest_thaiwater, .github/workflows), which is to become public so the scheduled jobs use free public-repo minutes. The archive stays in the private jaga-data repo (a public repo can't have a private branch, and ThaiWater's reuse terms are unclear). jaga-collectors is retired. Kept: 12-hourly rain, cheap probe for silent stations, the Actions protections and the keepalive. — Owner wants fewer repos; two repos (public code, private data) is the minimum that keeps the data private without paying for minutes.
- 2026-09-28 — Before going public: personal details (the owner's city, the original brief, work-laptop mentions, personal-account wording) were reworded or removed from the files and from all of history; the old starter zip was removed from history too. License: Apache-2.0 for code and docs; the Jaga name, wordmark and logo files are excluded (README, NOTICE). — Public code without exposing the owner's personal information; forks can't pose as the official alert service.
- 2026-09-28 — Malaysian gauges (publicinfobanjir, Kolok/Kelantan) are parked: no downloader and no permission request to JPS/DID for now. — Owner's decision; the earlier entry (no downloader without JPS/DID permission) still applies if this is picked up again.
- 2026-09-28 — The repo lives on D: on the owner's personal laptop (e.g. D:\jaga); this machine keeps C:\dev\jaga. Tool caches (npm, uv, uv-managed Python, pip, Playwright browsers) move to D:\cache through user settings (SETUP.md). Nothing in the repo assumes a drive letter. — The personal laptop has only ~16 GB free on C:.
- 2026-09-28 — Development uses a free Supabase cloud project (jaga-dev, Singapore region, owned by the project account) instead of a local Docker/Supabase stack. Migrations go up with `supabase link` and `supabase db push`; the live app gets its own Supabase Pro project later. Free projects pause after ~7 days without activity and are restored from the dashboard. — Docker Desktop and WSL2 would take 20–60 GB on a full C:; the cloud project costs nothing.
- 2026-09-28 — Large pipeline outputs go to Cloudflare R2, in two buckets: jaga-tiles (public; hazard/, extents/, stage/, assets/, manifest.json) and jaga-rasters (private; dem/, sar/ and the source rasters). Science outputs only, never user data (SOS and report media stay in Supabase Storage behind RLS). Keys are write-once and versioned; manifest.json names the current file per layer. Plain R2 storage only: no Workers or other paid Cloudflare products without asking. A payment method is added; expected cost $0/month within the free tier. Replaces (k)'s "revisit Cloudflare in 2027" for R2. — R2 makes a whole bucket public or private, downloads are free, and large files stay out of git and off the laptop.
- 2026-09-28 — Buy a domain (about $10/year, Cloudflare Registrar) before launch; tiles are served from tiles.<domain>. The r2.dev URL is for development only. — r2.dev is rate-limited and not for production; a custom domain adds Cloudflare's edge cache, which keeps tile reads inside the free tier.
- 2026-09-29 — RLS policy tests run in two places: on the laptop against the jaga-dev cloud project, and in CI against a throwaway local Supabase on the GitHub runner (Docker is built into GitHub's runners; public-repo minutes are free). One Node harness runs each test in a transaction that is rolled back, against whatever database URL it is given. — Every push and pull request is checked on a clean database without the dev password, and the laptop still needs no Docker.
- 2026-09-29 — A0 choices: Jaga draws its own SVG icons (check, backpack, triangle, running person, house, clock, phone, lifebuoy) instead of an icon library; Thai URLs have no prefix (/ is Thai, /ms and /en for the others); Vercel previews are set up at the end of A0. — Full control and no stray eye icons; the printed address opens in Thai; a preview lets the owner review on a phone.
- 2026-09-29 — The URL alone decides the language: no redirect based on the phone's language setting. — Printed links behave the same for everyone, and each URL can be cached as-is during an alert burst.
- 2026-09-29 — The four hotlines (1784, 1669, 191, 199) sit in the app shell at the bottom of every page, as plain tel: links with the number visible, and stay available offline via the service worker's fallback page. — Safety rules 4 and 7 from the first build, not only once the home screen exists.
- 2026-09-29 — Times shown to users use the 24-hour clock in Asia/Bangkok in all three languages. — Thai and Malay readers use 24-hour times, and users and authorities should read identical times.
- 2026-09-29 — Database commands run against jaga-dev through SUPABASE_DB_URL (npm run db:push, db:reset, db:migrations via scripts/db.mjs), not supabase link. — No Supabase account token on the laptop; the connection string is never printed.
- 2026-09-29 — /dev/* review pages exist only in local development and Vercel previews; production returns 404. — Review tools shouldn't be public.
- 2026-09-29 — Until launch, JAGA_DEV_PAGES=1 in the Vercel production environment keeps /dev/* review pages visible on the pre-launch deployment; the A10 launch checklist removes it. — Pushes to main deploy as Vercel production, and the owner reviews on a phone before any domain points there.
- 2026-09-29 — Superseded (2026-09-28, repo on D:): development stays on the owner's current laptop in C:\dev\jaga, which has plenty of space and no longer needs Docker. The other laptop is a possible later move, not planned. SETUP.md now uses C:\dev\jaga, keeps moving the caches to another drive as an optional step, and has a "Moving to another machine" section listing the only local files to recreate (.env.local, the jaga-data clone, per-repo git identity (noreply), GitHub login; optionally the Claude notes). The Supabase and R2 data stay in the cloud; only keys move. — A machine move should cost an hour, not a migration.
- 2026-09-29 — A0 wireframes approved (home, SOS flow, admin alert console, Transparency tab), with the six recommendations: (1) at Evacuate the SOS button moves directly under the red hero, above the checklist; (2) checklist items can be ticked, remembered on the phone only; (3) the public issuer line says "Jaga admin" plus the source, and admin names appear only in the console; (4) SOS has one "Send SOS now?" confirmation screen, with no countdown or auto-send; (5) the "Send by SMS instead" button stays hidden until a project SOS SMS number exists, and the offline SOS screen shows the 1784 and 1669 call buttons; (6) an Evacuate alert needs a second confirmation screen with tambon and recipient counts, without typing a number. — At Evacuate, getting help comes first; volunteer admins stay private; SOS stays two taps; no button may point nowhere.
- 2026-09-29 — A1 boundaries: OCHA HDX COD-AB Thailand (Royal Thai Survey Department, CC BY-IGO, attribution in NOTICE). All 77 provinces at ~500 m; districts and tambons of the 4 active provinces at ~10 m (geom) and ~100 m (geom_web), simplified together as one coverage so borders stay seamless; seed committed (5.3 MB). — Reproducible from the repo alone, far finer than alerts or a home pin need.
- 2026-09-29 — Phones and household details live in owner-only tables (profile_contacts, sos_contacts, authority_unit_contacts, households). Anyone else reads them only through security-definer functions that check coverage and capability and write audit_log (reveal_sos_phone, reveal_poc_phone, households_in_tambon, reveal_profile_phone). — RLS can't hide single columns or log reads; this enforces rule 5 in the database, not the frontend.
- 2026-09-29 — The SOS suspected-spam flag lives in its own table (sos_review), not on sos_requests as spec section 8 lists it. — The access matrix says a requester must never see the flag, and the requester can read their own SOS row.
- 2026-09-29 — Visitors and users can't insert SOS rows directly; the A5 submit function creates them (merge while open, flag suspected spam, always deliver). — One place implements "never reject" and the merge rules.
- 2026-09-29 — First weights, to tune with responders: rank_safe_places = 0.5 × pipeline score + 0.5 × closeness (0 at 20 km); sos_priority = 2 per vulnerable flag (max 10) + depth (ankle 1 … roof 10) + 0.5 per person (max 10) + 1 per 10 min waiting (max 12). — Needed for A3/A7 screens now; both only order lists, never hide a case or a place.
- 2026-09-29 — Watching places for others (spec 4.9): up to 10 watched places per user (was 3 saved places), each with a notifications switch and, optionally, the name and phone of the person there, stored only with the user's consent confirmation and readable by nobody else (not authorities, not admins). A user can send an SOS for a watched place (marked as sent by a relative, with both phones; only covering authorities and admins can reveal them, logged) and register a household there. One notification per alert per user, listing every affected place. On home, a watched place with a more severe alert than home is shown first. — Many people at risk are elderly without smartphones; their relatives watch for them, call them, and ask for help on their behalf.
- 2026-09-29 — The tambon of every SOS, report, household, watched place, safe place and home is computed from its location by a database trigger, never taken from the client. — The tambon decides which authorities see a case; a client must not be able to route it elsewhere.
- 2026-09-29 — Vercel deploys only the Next.js app: vercel.json declares a single "app" service and .vercelignore leaves out pipeline/, docs/ and tests/. — Vercel auto-detected the Python pipeline as a second service; it runs in GitHub Actions and must not become a Vercel service or cost.
- 2026-09-29 — Vercel project "jaga" (Hobby) deploys main to https://jaga-neon.vercel.app, with only the four public environment values; no Vercel integrations (the Supabase integration would copy secret keys into Vercel). Branch and deployment URLs stay behind Vercel sign-in. The address is added to the jaga-tiles CORS list. — A live pre-launch address for review on a phone; secrets stay off Vercel.
- 2026-09-30 — All GitHub Actions jobs run on ubuntu-24.04 instead of ubuntu-latest, until after the 2026 flood season. — GitHub moves ubuntu-latest to Ubuntu 26 from 19 October, six days before go-live; a new runner image could break CI or the data jobs in launch week. Move to 26 deliberately in January 2027.
- 2026-09-30 — actions/checkout moves from v4.4.0 to v5.1.0 (pinned by SHA) in every workflow and the ThaiWater setup action; v6 and v7 wait until after the season. — v5 runs on Node 24 (Node 20 is deprecated on GitHub runners); its only breaking change concerns pull_request_target, which Jaga never uses.
- 2026-09-30 — astral-sh/setup-uv moves from v6.8.0 to v10.2.0 (pinned by SHA) in the pipeline tests and the ThaiWater setup action, supersedes "setup-uv waits until after the season" (same day). — Removes the last Node 20 warning; none of the v7–v10 breaking changes (server-url, manifest-file, cache pruning off by default, cache off for pull_request_target/workflow_run/release) affect Jaga, which sets enable-cache explicitly. Verified by a manual hourly-rain run.
- 2026-09-30 — Phone sign-in (SMS OTP) comes last: A2 ships LINE Login for residents and authorities (authority POC phone verified by an admin call, decision o), email magic link for admins, and phone OTP built behind the SmsProvider interface with only the console adapter, its button hidden in production. Phone sign-in goes live after launch (owner, 30 Sep): the owner picks the SMS provider after 25 Oct, then registers the sender name (can take days to weeks) and I add the adapter. Supersedes "the owner chooses an SMS provider this week" (decision o, spec open item 3). — Alerts go by Web Push and LINE and don't need SMS; SMS is only for sign-in codes, and the LINE fallback covers the launch season. Known gap until phone sign-in is live: a resident without LINE can send SOS and see alert status but can't subscribe to push or save watched places (push subscriptions need an account); an account-free push subscription per tambon may be proposed in A2.
- 2026-10-01 — Logo: the "Sheltered j" replaces the contour-eye mark (supersedes the logo part of the 2026-09-28 name-and-logo entry). It is the j of Jaga with its dot under a small teal canopy: a person sheltered by an umbrella and, gently, someone watching over them. The color palette (slate and teal), the typography and the taglines stay as they are. Same file names in public/brand/ and public/icons/; the PNGs are rendered from the SVGs with `npm run icons`. The icon rule (h) becomes: no eye icons anywhere in the UI, and no umbrella icons except the logo. The resident test stays open (spec open item 11). — The owner chose it on 1 October from the alternatives on the tracker: it says care and shelter first, where the eye could read as surveillance, and it works as the app icon and inside the written name.
- 2026-10-05 — LINE sign-in goes through Supabase's custom OAuth2 provider (`custom:line`: LINE's authorize, token and userinfo addresses, scopes `openid profile`, email optional, PKCE), not a server-side bridge of our own. If the test on jaga-dev fails, fall back to the bridge (admin-created user, then a server-verified one-time link). — Supabase has no built-in LINE provider; the custom provider keeps sessions, refresh tokens and the LINE identity inside Supabase Auth with the least code. LINE signs web ID tokens with HS256, so the OIDC provider type and signInWithIdToken cannot verify them.
- 2026-10-05 — S2 exports per-scene flood extents only for the Nov–Dec 2024 and Nov–Dec 2025 events; every other Oct–Jan season from 2017 gets its maximum extent and its share in the flood-frequency raster. Exports go to the project account's Google Drive, then to jaga-rasters on R2; Google Cloud Storage is not used. — Several hundred per-scene rasters could exceed the free Earth Engine quota and R2's 10 GB; GCS bills for storage.
- 2026-10-05 — Earth Engine runs under a Google Cloud project owned by the project account, registered for non-commercial use, with its ID in `EE_PROJECT`; the laptop signs in once with `earthengine authenticate` (credentials outside the repo). CI does not use Earth Engine. — Every Earth Engine call needs a registered project; no key file to store or leak.
- 2026-10-05 — Admins: the super admin invites an email address; an account is created for it (no email sent) and the admin role is granted by the database only when that person signs in through a link sent to that address. Invitations lapse after 14 days. The first super admin is set with `npm run admin:super -- <email>`. The sign-in form answers the same whether or not an address is invited. — Only invited, confirmed identities can become admins; the form can't be used to find out who is one.
- 2026-10-05 — "Verified" flags are set by the database only: a personal phone is verified when it is the OTP sign-in number; an authority's contact phone when it matches the registrant's OTP number or an admin confirms it by a call, and changing the number clears the confirmation. The LINE user ID is copied from the LINE sign-in identity and is not writable by the client. A unit cannot be verified with an unconfirmed contact phone. — Before this a registrant could have marked their own phone as confirmed, and alerts could have been pointed at someone else's LINE account.
- 2026-10-05 — An organization's official phone is public only when it opted in and an admin has verified one of its units (tightens decision e). — Otherwise anyone could publish a phone number by registering.
- 2026-10-05 — Personal, sign-in and admin pages (/account, /authority, /admin, /sign-in, /api) are never stored by the service worker; the proxy refreshes a session only when a session cookie is present and never blocks a request. — On a shared phone the next person must not see the last person's screens; visitors and alert bursts cost no extra work.
- 2026-10-05 — SMS sign-in codes: at most 5 an hour per phone, enforced in the Send SMS hook (only a hash of the phone is kept). The limit per network address is Supabase Auth's own rate limit, because the hook request carries no address. — Per-phone is the abuse that costs money; Thai mobile networks put many phones behind one address.
- 2026-10-05 — LINE sign-in through the Supabase custom provider is confirmed on jaga-dev and stays; the server-side bridge fallback is not built. Settings that made it work: manual configuration with issuer `https://access.line.me`, scopes `openid profile`, users without email allowed (SETUP.md, "One-time: LINE sign-in"). — The first real sign-in stored the LINE user ID on the profile, and the Official Account's Messaging API returns the same ID, so alerts can reach the person who signed in.
- 2026-10-05 — S2 method choices (owner): fixed backscatter drop −3 dB for VV and VH where the per-tile Otsu threshold is not accepted; VV alone counts as flooded in the flood count and the frequency (the extent rasters keep VV and VH as separate codes); flood patches under 8 connected pixels are removed per pass; dry reference = median of 1 Feb to 30 Apr of the season's start year, per orbit; 10 m pixels for the two priority events (Nov–Dec 2024 and 2025). The pixel size for the nine seasons (10 or 20 m) is decided after the compute used by the 2024 exports is measured. These are the values already in `pipeline/sar_floods/config.yaml`; none has been validated against observed floods yet. — Otsu was accepted on 3 of about 1,100 tile checks for Nov–Dec 2024, so the fixed drop decides the maps; the owner kept the suggested starting values so the first exports can run and be judged.
- 2026-10-06 — Earth Engine: the project `jaga-ee` moves from the Community tier (150 EECU-hours a month) to the non-commercial Contributor tier (1,000 a month), which needs a billing account attached but is not charged for non-commercial Earth Engine use; the owner makes the change in the Cloud Console. A Partner-tier application may follow. The processing is made cheaper before further exports. Amends the 2026-10-05 Earth Engine entry (no billing account). — The 21 exports for Nov–Dec 2024 used 232 EECU-hours, more than a month of the Community tier; the 2025 event and the nine seasons do not fit it, and the quota only resets on 1 November, after launch.
- 2026-10-06 — S2: each orbit's dry reference and layover mask are computed once per season and stored as Earth Engine assets (`sar_floods prepare`); `run` reads them. S3 (HAND and hazard baseline) starts only after the 2024 S2 files are downloaded and checked (owner, 6 Oct). — About 60 % of each pass's compute went into recomputing them (profiled on one area); the owner wants S2 settled before S3.
- 2026-10-06 — S3 starts with HAND while S2 continues (owner: start it if it does not depend on S2). HAND needs only the DEM; the hazard classes wait for the S2 extents and frequency. Working box 99–103° E, 5–9° N, catchments cut from the DEM's own flow directions; FABDEM and JRC occurrence are exported once from Earth Engine to Drive; WhiteboxTools (free, `whitebox` package) does the hydrology. — Keeps the science track moving during the Earth Engine runs.
- 2026-10-06 — Open for the owner: FABDEM is licensed CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 (checked 6 Oct). HAND and the hazard layer derived from it must be non-commercial, attributed and shared under the same licence; the code stays Apache-2.0. If that does not suit Jaga (for example because of donations), the alternative is Copernicus GLO-30, a surface model with a freer licence and worse results under plantations. Until decided, FABDEM is used and nothing derived from it is published.
- 2026-10-06 — One super admin: the project account. Everyone the super admin invites is an admin. The app can neither invite nor grant a super admin, and cannot change one; the role is set only with database access (`npm run admin:super`), which stops if another account already holds it. Supersedes the "admin or super admin" invitation of 2026-10-05. — Owner's decision; one account that can manage admins keeps the chain of responsibility simple.
- 2026-10-06 — Map pickers (home pin, authority coverage) use MapLibre with the OpenFreeMap basemap, loaded only when the person opens a map. The map starts with Jaga's own tambon outlines (`/api/geo/tambons`, the ~100 m web geometry) and puts the basemap underneath when it arrives. In the coverage form the checkbox list stays the source of truth; the map only ticks and unticks it. MapLibre's worker files are copied from node_modules to `public/vendor/maplibre/<version>/` on install, dev and build. — Forms must work on slow connections and without the basemap; MapLibre 6 looks for its worker next to its module, which no longer exists after bundling.
- 2026-10-06 — The LINE Login channel is published (owner, 6 Oct), so anyone with a LINE account can sign in to the pre-launch site as a plain user. — Friends are testing sign-in on Android phones; a Developing channel only admits people with a role on it.
- 2026-10-06 — S2: the nine seasons (2017–2025) and the flood frequency are made at 20 m (owner); the two priority events stay at 10 m. Every pass of a season is exported on its own and the season raster (extent, valid count, flooded count) is combined on the laptop (`sar_floods maximum`); the Earth Engine export of the event raster is no longer used. The seasons' per-pass files are intermediate and stay local; the season rasters and the frequency are uploaded. — Measured on Nov–Dec 2025: 10 m seasons would need an estimated 700–1,100 EECU-hours against about 640 left in October, and the frequency feeds 30 m hazard classes. The Earth Engine event raster cost 54 of 130 EECU-hours and the local one has identical counts; it differs only on 4.4 km² that every pass sees in layover or shadow, coded 250 (masked) locally and 255 (no data) before.
- 2026-10-06 — Admin sign-in links are sent through the project Gmail account: an app password on that account, entered as custom SMTP in the Supabase dashboard (steps in SETUP.md). The app password lives only there. Tested the same day: a second admin, invited from the laptop, received the link within seconds and reached the admin pages. To repeat on the live Supabase project at launch; fallback if Gmail ever refuses or delays the mail: a mail service on the project domain. — Supabase's built-in email reaches only members of the Supabase organisation; this costs nothing and needs no new account.
- 2026-10-07 — LINE sign-in from another app's built-in browser (Messenger, Facebook, Instagram, TikTok): the sign-in page detects it and offers "open in your browser" first (an intent link on Android, Safari's address scheme on iPhone, written steps as fallback); signing in there stays possible. The owner chose this over completing the sign-in in whichever browser LINE returns to. — Reported 6 Oct: the LINE app returns to the phone's own browser, which lacks the one-time key the sign-in started with, so the first attempt ended in "link expired". Keeping the key in the database would fix it in one step but would no longer tie a sign-in to the browser that started it.
- 2026-10-07 — A LINE sign-in may finish in another browser than the one that started it (owner, after testing: the "open in your browser" button does nothing on iPhones before iOS 17, so it stays only as a help). The one-time key of a started sign-in is kept in the database for ten minutes (`sign_in_flows`, closed to every client) under a random id that travels in the return address, and can be taken once. When a sign-in finishes in a browser that did not start it, the person first sees "You are signed in as [name]" with "Not me, sign out"; a browser that is already signed in keeps its session. Adds to the entry above. — From Messenger and similar apps the LINE app returns to the phone's own browser, so the first attempt ended in "link expired". The cost: a sign-in is no longer tied to one browser, so a prepared link could sign someone in to the sender's account; the notice is the answer to that.
- 2026-10-08 — The "open in your browser first" notice inside Messenger and similar apps is removed again (the entry of 2026-10-07 that introduced it is superseded; the one about finishing in another browser stands). — The owner confirmed on 8 Oct that LINE sign-in from a Messenger link works on the first attempt on an iPhone and on two Android phones, so the extra step only cost a tap, and its button could not work on iPhones before iOS 17.
- 2026-10-08 — Until launch, the public pages show no alert level for a tambon without an alert: a grey "Jaga is not in service yet" notice (start date, follow official warnings, call 1784) replaces green Normal, until the owner switches a launch setting on. After that, no alert = Normal as in spec section 7. — Nobody is monitoring before 25 October; green on a public address would read as "no risk" (safety rule 10).
- 2026-10-08 — Example alerts, safe places and reports for reviewing the A3 screens exist only on the /dev review pages, marked as examples; nothing made up goes into the database. — The pre-launch address reads the same database, so an invented alert for a real tambon could be seen or shared as real.
- 2026-10-08 — A3 build choices: the home screen is at `/` and never loads the map; the map dashboard is at `/map`. Both pages are the same static HTML for everyone; the phone adds the area and the alert status. A visitor's area (from GPS or a list) is kept on the phone only; the GPS position is sent once to find the tambon and is not stored. — Static pages can be cached through an alert burst and open fast on slow 3G; nobody has to sign in to see their alert.
- 2026-10-08 — Public data comes from a few addresses that are the same for everyone and kept at the edge for 60 s (`/api/public/status`, `/api/public/map`, `/api/public/places`); a failure is an error, never an empty list. Green "Normal" is shown only from a status checked in the last 30 minutes; an older copy (phone offline) shows alerts with the time received, but "couldn't load" instead of Normal. — 5,000 people opening the app after a push must not reach the database, and an old copy must not hide a new alert.
- 2026-10-08 — The launch switch is the server setting `JAGA_IN_SERVICE=1` (default off), to be set on launch day (A10 checklist). The project help line (`NEXT_PUBLIC_PROJECT_LINE`) is shown only when set. — One place to turn "not in service yet" into Normal; no button may point nowhere.
- 2026-10-08 — Flood reports reach the public only through `report_hex_bins()`: approved reports of the last 72 hours, counted per hexagon with 600 m sides (about 1 km2) on a fixed UTM 47N grid, with the deepest water reported and the newest report's time cut to the hour. — Safety rule 6: a single report must not point at a house or a moment. The owner may want another size or period.
- 2026-10-08 — When two alerts in force name the same tambon, the newest one counts. River gauges are never "normal" without reference levels or without a reading in the last 6 hours, and are drawn without status colors (the status is in words on a tap). — The admins' latest word wins; the alert palette stays reserved for alerts issued by people (safety rule 2).
- 2026-10-08 — Until A4, the SOS and Report buttons lead to pages that say plainly they are not working yet and offer 1784 and 1669 as call buttons. — A button must not look as if a request was sent.
- 2026-10-08 — S2, after the owner looked at seven exported views of the 29 Nov 2024 pass (ArcGIS Pro; tracker, "Flood map look"). Confirmed by the owner: the flood shown on the Tak Bai–Kolok plain and in the fields south of Pattani town is real; Pattani town itself was flooded although the radar shows almost nothing there; in the Sai Buri valley the low land between the meanders should be flagged too, and the ground masked by the slope rule along the banks is floodplain. Not judged (owner unsure): the land masked by HAND alone in south-west Songkhla and inland of Chana, and whether the VH-only fields south of Pattani are paddy. Decided: (1) the slope mask applies only where the land is more than 5 m above the nearest drainage (`masks.slope.min_hand_m: 5`); (2) the small-patch filter is an area, 800 m², at every pixel size (`min_connected_area_m2`, 8 pixels at 10 m, 2 at 20 m); (3) VV alone still counts as flooded; (4) both priority events and the nine seasons are exported again with these settings (parameter hash `c97e0bf`, about 240 EECU-hours estimated), the earlier rasters (`cd222f1`) are kept as they are. Follows for S3: no radar flood in built-up land must not be read as "does not flood". — The slope rule alone masked 873 km² within 5 m of drainage, banks and levees inside the floodplain; at 20 m the 8-pixel filter dropped patches four times larger than at 10 m and the season maps showed about 18 % less flood than the events.
- 2026-10-08 — The owner looked at the A3 screens: the Thai checklists and level summaries are approved as written; report hexagons stay at about 1 km2 for 72 hours. The Malay texts still wait for the Malay reviewer. — Safety text needs the owner's reading before launch.
- 2026-10-08 — "Places I watch" is built now, under Account (`/account/places`): add by GPS or a pin on the map, a name only the owner sees, notifications on or off, and optionally the name and phone of the person there, saved only with a tick that the person agreed. A pin outside the covered tambons is kept and shown as "outside the area Jaga covers". Households at a watched place come with spec 4.7. — The owner asked for it before A4, because SOS for a relative needs a watched place.
- 2026-10-08 — S3: the hazard classes use the radar flood frequency with a floor, not "ever flooded" (owner, following the suggestion). Starting values: a cell counts as flood-prone from radar when it was flooded in at least 2 of the nine seasons or in more than 2 % of its valid passes. The values are checked against the second-run rasters (`c97e0bf`) before the classes are built, and changed only with a new entry here. — In the first run 21 % of the observed land was flagged at least once, 30 % of that on a single pass in nine seasons; a single flag is as likely noise, wet soil or field work as flood.
- 2026-10-08 — S3, HAND (owner, following the suggestions): (1) streams start at 1,000 cells of contributing area (0.9 km² at 30 m); (2) mapped rivers (OpenStreetMap `waterway=river`) are burned into the elevation model before flow routing, so that modelled streams follow the real channels in the plain, and HAND is measured on the unburned surface; (3) land that drains to the sea or Songkhla Lake without meeting a stream gets its height above sea level as HAND. — The four candidate thresholds scored alike against mapped water, so a middle value is taken; below 20 m only 57 % of mapped river cells had a modelled stream within 60 m at this threshold; 294 km² of coastal land in the provinces had no HAND value.
- 2026-10-08 — FABDEM stays the elevation model for HAND and the hazard map (owner). Jaga is fully non-profit and the donation feature is off; the owner may never switch it on, and before doing so will ask FABDEM's licensors (and the other non-commercial sources) whether donations for hosting change anything. Layers derived from FABDEM are published under CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 with its credit line; the code stays Apache-2.0. Every organisation whose data is used is credited: the list is `docs/attributions.md`, the source for the app's credits page (A10). Closes the open question of 2026-10-06. — The licence fits non-commercial use, and FABDEM is the better model under rubber and oil palm.
- 2026-10-08 — A4, SOS before launch (owner, from four choices): the SOS button keeps leading to the call-first page ("nobody receives requests from the app yet" plus 1784 and 1669) until `JAGA_IN_SERVICE=1`. The whole flow is built and runs in development; the endpoints answer 503 while the switch is off, and `JAGA_SOS_SENDING_FOR_TESTS=1` is only for the end-to-end tests, which run a production build. Supersedes the "until A4" entry above. — A request nobody is watching must never look as though help is coming (safety rule 1).
- 2026-10-08 — A4, how a sender without an account follows their own case (owner): `submit_sos()` returns a random 24-byte token, kept in the phone's storage and sent in the `x-jaga-sos-token` header; only its SHA-256 hash is stored, and the token is never in an address or a log. The alternative, an anonymous Supabase session per visitor, was declined. — No account, no extra step before the request goes out, and a database dump hands out no open cases. The cost: clearing the browser's storage loses the way back to the case, which still reaches rescuers.
- 2026-10-08 — A4, who may report flooding (owner): reports need an account, as in spec section 3; a visitor is offered sign-in and told that asking for help never needs one. Reports may be refused (12 an hour per person, a setting); an SOS never is. — Fewer reports nobody can follow up, and a reporter the authorities can ask.
- 2026-10-08 — A4, the offline "SMS instead" button (owner): built behind `NEXT_PUBLIC_PROJECT_LINE`; while no number is set, the offline screen shows only the 1784 and 1669 call buttons. The owner's own number is not used. — No button may point nowhere, and no personal number belongs in the app's settings.
- 2026-10-08 — A4 build choices. Every SOS write goes through one database function (`submit_sos`, then `sos_add_details`, `sos_add_location`, `sos_close`, `sos_timeline`): the merge of a repeat into the open case, the suspected-spam flag above 6 requests in 24 hours (a setting in the new `system_settings` table), the priority score and the duplicate flag all happen there, so no client can get them wrong. An SOS outside the four provinces is stored without a tambon, so admins still see it. The offline queue is in IndexedDB and is emptied by the page (on opening, when the connection returns, every 15 s while something waits) and by the service worker through Background Sync where the browser has it. Photos and voice notes go straight from the phone to two private Supabase buckets (`sos-media`, `report-media`) under the case or report id, after the request itself has been stored. — The request for help must never depend on a photo, a session or the browser doing the right thing.
- 2026-10-08 — The app shell (owner's notes, evening): a **Home** link in words beside Map and Account, because nobody taps a logo to go back; the header (logo, the three links, the languages) **stays put while the page scrolls**, as the hotline bar already does; and on a laptop the app **fills the browser window** (up to 1,152 px) instead of a phone-wide strip, with the home screen in two columns there - what is happening on the left, what to do on the right. A phone is unchanged, including the order of everything on the home screen and the rule that at Evacuate the SOS button sits directly under the hero. — A long page (a checklist at Evacuate, the details of an open SOS) left the way back out of reach, and the app is also opened on laptops, by authorities and admins among others.
- 2026-10-08 — A person's **own places are drawn on the public map** (owner's request): their home and the places they watch, as teal rings with a switch to hide them and a tap for that place's alert level. They are read with the person's own session (`/api/me/places`, never cached) and drawn only in their browser; nothing personal is added to the public map data, which stays alert levels, report hexagons, safe places and gauges (safety rule 6). A place pinned outside the covered tambons is left out of the map, as the map covers only the four provinces. Teal marks whose they are, never a status: the level is in the card and in the tambon's colour (docs/brand.md). — Watched places were only on the home screen and under Account; on the map is where someone looks to see whether their mother's village is in the warned area.
- 2026-10-09 — GISTDA's open flood API is used, for two things (owner): (1) the repeated-flood layer (`/features/flood-freq`, 13 years, 2011–2023) as a second opinion on the radar flood frequency of S2/S3; (2) the near-real-time flood extent (`/features/flood/1day`, `3days`, `7days`, `30days`) as a layer on Jaga's public map during the season. Base address `https://api-gateway.gistda.or.th/api/2.0/resources`; every endpoint takes `bbox` and the DOPA codes `pv_idn`, `ap_idn`, `tb_idn`, so the four provinces are asked for directly (90, 94, 95, 96). The free API key is registered by the owner and never reaches the browser: the map asks GISTDA through Jaga's own cached route, like the other public data, and the key lives in the server settings only. The layer is always labelled with its source and its time window, and GISTDA's own words that the impact figures are not field-verified are not repeated as fact (safety rule 8). Not in the open API and still to ask the owner's GISTDA contact for: flood extents per event for 2017–2025, an elevation model, and any field observations. — The key is free and self-service, the data covers the far south, and it is the only second flood map of the area we can get; but it is satellite-derived like ours, so it is a consistency check and never ground truth — in particular it will share the blind spot the owner found in built-up Pattani town.
- 2026-10-09 — Each place a person watches has **its own colour** on the map, from a ten-colour ramp (`lib/mine-colors.ts`) ordered by measured difference so two places added one after another never look alike; none of the ten is an alert colour, and a test keeps it so (the alert palette is for alert levels alone, docs/brand.md). The **name of each place is written on the map** under its pin, and always drawn: MapLibre would otherwise give the basemap's own village names priority and hide the person's own places. **Hover (laptop) or tap (phone, tablet)** adds the person there and the tambon, district and province; it is built from text nodes, never HTML, because the person typed it, and the stored phone number never goes on the map. The legend under the map lists every place beside its colour, so the colours are readable for someone who cannot tell these hues apart. — The owner's notes of 8-9 Oct: with ten places allowed, identical pins tell you nothing, and the label should be readable without pointing at every pin.
- 2026-10-09 — S2: orbit D164's passes of the 2025 season are processed against the dry season of 2026 (February–April 2026), an exception to the rule that the reference comes from the months before the flood season (owner; `reference_exceptions` in `pipeline/sar_floods/config.yaml`, outside the parameter hash). — The orbit has no February–April 2025 images, and its pass of 24 November 2025 (06:02 Bangkok) is the only one within days of the Hat Yai flood of 19–21 November 2025. Land cover may have changed between the flood and the reference; the run log names the reference year for every orbit.
- 2026-10-09 — S3: plan for the hazard classes approved (owner). On a 30 m grid, four classes with the limits in a YAML file as starting values: High (flooded often by radar, for example in at least 4 of 9 seasons or in both priority events), Medium (meets the frequency floor of 2026-10-08, or flooded in one priority event), Low (not flagged by radar but low-lying, HAND under 2 m), Minimal. Where the radar is blind (built-up land, land under the terrain mask) the class comes from HAND alone and is marked as such: HAND under 2 m is at least Medium, under 5 m at least Low. Checked by building the classes without the 2025 event and scoring them against it; the resulting areas are shown to the owner before anything goes on the map. Buildings and built-up land from Google Open Buildings. Benchmark for built-up land: the centre of Hat Yai, heavily flooded on 19–21 November 2025 (owner), where the radar shows nothing and HAND is under 2 m on 69 % and under 5 m on 96 % of the ground. — A town the radar cannot see must never be shown as safe.
- 2026-10-09 — Releases from Bang Lang dam become part of Jaga (owner). The dam (EGAT, hydroelectric, on the Pattani River in Yala; dam at 6.1551° N, 101.2721° E, main spillway at 6.1501° N, 101.2764° E) fills in the peak season and has to release water, which floods land downstream with little or no warning. (1) A verified dam operator may send one kind of alert themselves, a dam release notice, to a fixed downstream zone and from a reviewed template; admins are told at the same moment and can extend or cancel it. This widens "alerts are issued by admins" for this one case; a person still publishes every alert (safety rule 2). (2) Before launch: collect the dam's hourly data, work out arrival times and the affected zone from the river gauges, show the dam's state to admins, and add a dam release template that admins send. The operator's own screen is built after someone at the dam has agreed to use it. (3) Arrival times are shown as a range with a safety margin, never as one time (safety rule 8). — By the owner's account these releases are the main cause of disaster on the Pattani River. The dam's data is free: ThaiWater serves storage, level, release and spill by the hour, with history, and EGAT's own gauges from the dam's tailwater to Pattani town are already in the archive.
- 2026-10-09 — A **weather widget in the header, and a weather page behind it** (owner's request). Source: Open-Meteo (free for non-commercial use, no key), which the science plan already names for rain forecasts; the credit line is shown wherever its data appears. The browser never calls Open-Meteo: `/api/public/weather` and `/api/public/geocode` do, with the point rounded to about 1 km, so no phone's IP address or exact position reaches a third party and the edge serves one cached answer for 15 minutes to everyone nearby. The place is the one already chosen on the home screen (or the home saved in the account), so nobody is asked for their position twice; the weather page offers "use my location" (named by Jaga's own tambon lookup, not an outside service) and a typed place name, searched **worldwide** (owner's choice: people here have family and work across the border). The page shows now, the next 48 hours of rain, 24 hours hour by hour and seven days, with the time the model data is for, the source and the sentence that this is a forecast from a global model and not a Jaga flood alert, plus the line that Jaga's flood alerts cover Pattani, Yala, Narathiwat and Songkhla only (safety rules 8 and 10). It carries no alert level, badge or alert colour. The chip keeps the last reading on the phone and shows its hour once it is over three hours old; on the SOS screens it fetches nothing at all (safety rule 1), and elsewhere it waits for an idle moment so the alert status is never delayed. — The owner asked for the weather in the header's empty space; the rules above are what keeps ordinary weather from ever being read as a warning.
- 2026-10-09 — **The app is dark after sunset and light at sunrise, decided by the clock, with nothing for anyone to switch** (owner). This supersedes "the MVP ships a light theme only" in docs/brand.md, which is updated. Sunrise and sunset are worked out from the date and the area the person chose on the home screen (the middle of the four provinces until they choose one), with the usual sunrise equation in `lib/sun.ts`; no position is requested and nothing leaves the phone. A copy of that calculation runs inline before the first paint (`lib/theme-script.ts`) so the page never flashes white at night, and a test runs both over a year of hourly moments to keep the copy honest. **The alert palette is identical in both themes**: those colours mean a level, each badge carries its own background, and both pairs pass contrast on either page colour. Two roles the brand slate used to play alone are now separate tokens, equal by day and different after dark: `--jaga-ink` for headings and strong words, `--jaga-edge` for the outline of a button or chip, while `--jaga-slate` stays the filled navy of the header and the primary button. The map takes OpenFreeMap's dark basemap at night and draws our own outlines and dots light; a watched place keeps its white ring in both themes. — Daylight keeps the light theme, which is what reads outdoors in bright sun (the original reason); after dark a lit white screen is the wrong thing to hold in a flood at night.
- 2026-10-09 — **An SOS is dispatched to teams, not left on a board** (owner's request, four choices answered). Spec section 5.2 is rewritten and 4.6, 4.8 and 5.3 amended. The case is offered to the verified units covering its tambon one after another — `rescue` capability first, then `coordination`, then the admins' SOS monitor — with the alarm and a countdown on the unit being asked, **while the case stays on every covering unit's board the whole time so any of them can accept at any second** (owner's choice of the three dispatch shapes: serial rounds, everyone can still grab it). The offer window is a setting admins can change (owner's choice), default 60 s for a rescue unit and 90 s for a coordination unit; running out is not a refusal, and declining asks for a one-tap reason. A coordinator who gets a case by fallback gets it as a task to find a team. Who currently holds an offer is computed from the time elapsed on every read, so no server timer can fail; a one-minute cron only fires the notifications. Accepting takes the atomic claim that spec 5.2 already required; immediately after accepting, and never before it, the unit gives an arrival estimate in bands (under 15 min, 15–30, 30–60, over 60, cannot say yet), which the requester sees as a band with the time it was given, never as a clock time (safety rule 8). The requester's screen says "still looking for a team" with the time waited, and repeats 1784 and 1669, rather than implying someone is on the way. The responder's card has the exact point, copy-coordinates and a "Navigate" button opening Google Maps (a plain URL, no API key, no cost), with the warning that road directions do not know which roads are flooded. Each unit gets an on-duty switch; off duty is offered last, never excluded. Where no unit covers the tambon the case goes straight to the admins. Which place the help is for is **never asked before sending** — the request goes out with the phone's position and the status screen afterwards offers to move it to a watched place or a pin (safety rule 1). Built in A5. — The owner's point: a request must hunt for a responder like a ride request, not wait to be noticed. Serial offers with the board kept open keep the hand-off accountable without ever leaving a case unwatched, and a window admins own can be tightened in the flood peak.
- 2026-10-09 — **The requester sees the POC phone of the team that accepted their case** (owner, over the two safer options). This is the one exception to "personal phone numbers are never public" (safety rule 5, amended in CLAUDE.md), and it is fenced in by my own call, which the owner can overrule: only the sender of that case, only while the case is open and claimed by that unit, every reveal written to `audit_log`, and masked with the other personal fields when the case closes. Each unit has a tick in its settings, **on by default**, worded "When you accept an SOS, the person you are rescuing can see this number"; a unit that turns it off shows the organisation's official phone instead. — The owner wants the person in the water to be able to call the person in the boat, not an office. The fence and the plainly worded tick are what keep it PDPA-clean rather than a surprise to a volunteer.
- 2026-10-09 — **Jaga makes five sounds** (owner: notifications should have sounds, urgent for SOS and positive for rescued or safe, and a distinctive one of its own for a dam release), all generated in the app with the Web Audio API — no audio files, so nothing to download, cache or licence, and they work offline. An urgent two-tone alarm that repeats until answered, for an authority being offered a case; a short rising pair for the requester when a team accepts; three soft notes resolving downward for "rescued" and "I'm safe now"; one low double-tone for a new alert at Warning or Evacuate; and, for a dam release notice (spec section 15), a rising figure **repeated**, used for nothing else — the only public alert sound that repeats, because the water is already on its way. **Watch and Normal are silent.** Every sound is paired with a vibration pattern and with its own icon and words on screen, so sound is never the only channel (accessibility rule). Two honest limits, written into docs/brand.md: a browser plays nothing until the person has tapped once, so the authority console asks once to arm the alarm and stays armed; and a web push arriving with the app closed plays the phone's own notification sound, which no web app can replace. — A dispatch offer with a 60-second window is worthless if the phone stays quiet, and the sound of good news should not be the sound of an alarm.
- 2026-10-09 — **A dam release notice reaches the tributaries too, and sounds like itself** (owner, adding to the Bang Lang decision of today). The zone is no longer only the tambons along the Pattani River below the dam: it includes **the lower reaches of the streams that join it**, where a rising main stem backs water up into the tributary instead of letting it drain, so people on a side stream are warned as well. S7 defines that zone and its arrival ranges. The notice carries its own sound (spec 4.8, docs/brand.md), distinct from the alert double-tone and used for nothing else, and it is the one public alert sound that repeats. Sent by a verified RID or EGAT operator opening the gates or raising the spillway flow, and by admins from the same template until an operator has joined. — The owner's point: a release floods the side streams as well as the main channel, and the sound has to tell someone who is asleep that this one is water on its way, not a forecast.
- 2026-10-09 — **Jaga keeps a record of the help it carried and publishes it** (owner's request, four choices answered as recommended). New spec section 16, built after launch as phase A13 on the first season's real data. A nightly job aggregates requests and outcomes, who helped, alerts, and usage **per province per day**, then drops the raw rows; the public page is `/impact`, open without an account and linked from About and the footer only — never the home screen, never a dashboard tab, no notification and no badge, and nothing links to it from an SOS, report or alert screen (the discipline of safety rule 9). **Every verified unit's volume of help is shown — cases accepted, confirmed rescues, people reached, seasons active — with no ranking, no league table and no per-unit response times in public** (the hero score and its leaderboard stay in the consoles, which settles the public-leaderboard half of open item 4). **No individual is named**: credit goes to the unit and its organisation. Usage is counted by Jaga's own code with a **device hash salted per day**, so a phone cannot be followed from one day to the next, and no third-party analytics service is added. Province and day are the finest grain published, and any figure that could point at one household is suppressed. The page publishes the failures beside the successes (cases no team accepted, cases that reached the admins, the longest waits, cases closed unconfirmed), says plainly that **Jaga carried requests and the teams did the rescues**, and says that most help in a flood happens outside the app; the aggregates download as CSV and JSON. — The owner wants the merit of the organisations and volunteers on the ground shown transparently, and the project to be able to say what it achieved. A ranked table would change behaviour on the ground (a team looks better by accepting cases it cannot reach, and a one-boat volunteer group ranks last against a funded district office), and a named volunteer beside rescue places and times is a safety matter in these provinces — hence credit by volume and by organisation.
- 2026-10-09 — **The admins get a war room** (owner's request: a dashboard of who has registered and where, switchable to a map, and a way to see any SOS nobody has answered). Built as the first part of A6 at `/admin/war-room`, reached only from the admin console. One screen with six counters, a red band at the top for every case no unit has taken past the threshold, and three tabs: the case board, the people who have registered, and the map. The threshold is the new `sos_unclaimed_minutes` setting (15 minutes by default, spec 6.4). From a card an admin can show the requester's phone (through the existing logged function), assign the case to a verified unit covering its tambon, hand it back to the queue with a reason, write what they did, and judge the spam flag either way — each one a database function that checks the caller is an admin, writes a line in the case's history and one in `audit_log`. Nothing here can refuse, hide or delete a case, and a flagged case keeps its place in the queue (safety rule 1). Assigning notifies nobody yet, so the screen says to call the unit (A7 brings the push). **Two privacy lines, my call, the owner can overrule:** (1) the places people watch for others are shown as **counts per tambon only** — never a point, a label, a stored name or a stored phone — because spec 9 gives an admin none of them; homes are plotted because spec 9 already lets an admin read `profiles.home_point`. (2) No phone number is ever in the board or the people list, only "has a number"; a number is read one at a time through `reveal_sos_phone` / `reveal_profile_phone`, which log it. The board is polled every 20 seconds while the page is in front, and the admin's watch is marked in `audit_log` once per ten minutes rather than once per poll, so the log stays readable (spec 9 asks for admin reads of SOS data to be logged). Colour follows docs/brand.md: the only hue that carries a case state is the SOS red for "nobody has answered", everything else is the neutral tokens with an icon and words, and the map's density is one teal ramp because a count is not a status. `/dev/war-room` shows the whole screen with made-up cases. — The owner asked for it as a disaster-response dashboard; a case waiting with nobody on it is the only thing in Jaga that costs lives by being unnoticed, so it is what the page is built around.
- 2026-10-09 — **A display name is required of every account** (owner's request). It was already asked for by the form and the save action; now the database enforces it as well (trigger `profiles_display_name_required`: the name is trimmed, and once given it may not be set back to empty — sign-in may still create the empty default), and the pages that keep a person's own details (`/account`, `/account/places`, `/account/places/[id]`, `/authority/register`) send a nameless account to the name field and back. Reason: rescuers and admins see that name beside a request, so a blank one helps nobody. Nothing was added in front of `/sos` or `/report`: asking for help waits for nobody (safety rule 1).
- 2026-10-09 — **Weather maps on the weather page** (owner's request; four choices, all as recommended): one map with buttons for rain, temperature, humidity and wind, a slider over the next 24 hours, a square of about 130 km around the place the page is showing (a coarser 400 km grid when the person zooms out), and the forecast model as the only source for now — live radar (RainViewer) and GISTDA's flood extent were offered and left for later. There is no tile service and no key behind it: `/api/public/weather/grid` asks Open-Meteo for every point of a 9×9 grid in one request and hands the browser flat arrays of rounded numbers (about 25 kB instead of 115 kB), cached for 15 minutes at the edge; the centre is snapped to a lattice first, so everyone in the same part of a province shares one answer and no exact position is in the address. Cells are drawn as squares, never as a smooth image, because the model's resolution is about 11 km and a smooth picture would promise detail the forecast has not got (safety rule 8); the cell size is written under every map. **No colour of any of the four ramps is an alert hue**, and all of them keep at least as much blue as red, so no cell can read as Watch, Warning or Evacuate (docs/brand.md; a test enforces it). The person's home and the places they watch are pinned in the colours the public map already gives them, and each one's value is also written out in numbers under the map, for anyone who cannot read a colour. Nothing loads until the section is scrolled to: the map library and the grid together are the heaviest thing in the app. — The owner asked for maps of rain, wind, humidity and temperature around their place and their watched places; the budget rules out paid tiles, and Open-Meteo's own grid turns out to be enough.
- 2026-10-10 — **The impact dashboard is built now, with made-up figures, and only admins can open it** (owner, asking for it to be appealing and to make teams want to contribute more). It is the screen of spec section 16, at `/admin/impact`, fed from `lib/impact-examples.ts`; `/dev/impact` shows the same screen without signing in. Nothing is in the database and no table is created: A13 still builds the real nightly aggregates, and when it does, the admin page reads those while the example stays on the /dev page like every other example (decision of 8 Oct). The page says in its first block, above the heading, that every figure is invented, and every made-up organisation carries the word ตัวอย่าง in its name so an invented rescue count can never read as a claim about a real foundation. **On the owner's wish that it motivate teams, against the no-ranking decision of 9 Oct: recognition, not ranking.** Every unit has its own card with the volume of its help, the milestones it has reached and the nearest one still to reach, and it is compared with **its own** last season; the only ordered list is the month's honours, which has a top and simply ends — there is no bottom, no table position and no per-unit response time. Milestones are never awarded for speed (a team far from the water cannot be quick) and a season is never offered as the next step, because a team cannot go and earn one. **Charts:** one brand hue only. Jaga has a single teal, and the palette validator puts any two of its steps at about ΔE 10 for normal colour vision, below the floor of 15, so nothing encodes identity by colour: every bar is `jaga-teal-ink`, length carries the magnitude, words carry the identity, and each chart also offers its numbers as a table. The alert palette appears nowhere, because a count is not a status, and a test keeps it that way. Both themes come free from the brand tokens, and the screens were checked at 360 px and on a laptop by day and by night. — The owner wants to see and judge the page before the first season, and wants it to pull teams in; a league table would pull them in the wrong direction, so the pull is each team's own progress and the thanks it gets by name.
- 2026-10-10 — **The "now" card has a sky that moves** (owner's request, like the weather app on a phone): the sun in a bright sky, cloud drifting, rain falling or running down the glass in a shower, fog in layers, snow, a flash in a thunderstorm, and at night a dark sky with stars and the moon **in the shape it has tonight**. Drawn with plain elements and CSS only (`components/weather/WeatherScene.module.css`): no images, no canvas, no library, nothing downloaded, and only transform and opacity are animated, so a cheap phone draws it on the GPU. Under "reduced motion" the sky, sun, moon and stars stay and nothing moves; the rain and the lightning are removed rather than frozen. **Readability comes first:** every sky carries a 45% veil under the writing, and a test composites each sky's two colours with it and holds the temperature to at least 4.5:1 on all eleven of them. **No sky is an alert colour** and none is warm (blue is never below red in any of them), because orange and red mean Warning and Evacuate; a test keeps it so. The sky is decoration only: it is hidden from screen readers and says nothing the words beside it do not. **Sunrise and sunset** are shown in the card, worked out on the phone from the date and the place with the sunrise equation already in `lib/sun.ts` (no request, right for any place in the world), and the **moon phase** from the mean synodic month in the new `lib/moon.ts` — right to within about half a day, which is enough to draw it and name it, and it is never anything a decision rests on. Every sky can be seen side by side at /dev/weather?s=skies. — The owner asked for the card to look like the weather outside; in this app that has to be done without ever borrowing the colours or the attention that belong to an alert.
- 2026-10-10 — **The weather maps are drawn smooth, the hours play by themselves, and the pins have night colours** (three notes from the owner). (1) The field is no longer a grid of squares: the values between the model's points are filled in (bilinear) into a 192×192 picture that the map lays on the ground and the browser smooths, which is how a phone's weather map looks. The honesty that the squares carried is kept in words and numbers instead: under every map it now says "model values at points about 17 km apart, shaded between them, not measurements", the legend keeps the same numbered scale, and each of the person's places still gets its value written out (safety rule 8). Supersedes the "squares, not a smooth picture" part of yesterday's entry; nothing else in it changes. Every painted hour is kept in memory, so running the day round again costs nothing. (2) The 24 hours **play round on their own** when the map comes into view, one hour a second or so, with a pause button; a hand on the slider stops them, the page stops them while it is in the background, and a phone asking for less movement never starts them. (3) The person's own pin was too dark to see on the night map: there is now a **lighter twin of each of the ten place colours and of the home colour** (`MINE_COLORS_DARK`, `homeColor(dark)`), used on both the weather map and the public map once the app has turned dark, and a pin is always a coloured dot ringed with the background colour, so it stands out either way. None of the night colours is an alert hue either. — The owner asked for a map that reads like a weather app's, for the forecast to play itself, and for their own place to be findable at night.
- 2026-10-10 — **Bang Lang dam, its reservoir and the path released water takes are on the public map, always** (owner's request). The dam, the main spillway and the outlet are marked and labelled; the reservoir is drawn faintly enough to read the tambon alert colours through it; the 134.3 km of the Pattani River below the dam is drawn wider than anything else of ours, and the lower 5 km of each of the ten mapped tributaries is drawn dashed beside it (spec section 15: a rising main stem backs water into the side streams). It is on in every view and whatever the dam is doing, because where the river runs does not depend on today's weather. **The water colour never changes with the dam's state**: brand colours never show a status and only the alert palette does (docs/brand.md), so a river that turned amber when the gates opened would be a sixth alert colour that nobody has been taught. A confirmed release thickens the river's casing and the words do the rest. Both themes were checked in a real browser at both ends of the day; the daytime water colour is invisible on the dark basemap, so it has a night twin, and `--jaga-dam` in globals.css is kept equal to the map's own value by a test (MapLibre cannot read a CSS variable). — The owner asked for the dam and the water's path to be findable by the people who live along it.
- 2026-10-10 — **The geometry is OpenStreetMap's for now, and will be compared against S7's before it is replaced** (owner's choice from three). The reservoir outline (OSM relation 3224558, 43.0 km², 13 islands), the channel leaving the dam, the river down to its mouth and the tributaries are derived in the new `pipeline/dam_release` module from the waterway cache the hazard module already filled, so no new source and no new request. Every reach and every tambon carries a `source` column and the database key includes it, so the zone from HAND and the radar extents (S7 steps 2–3) can sit beside the OSM version for the owner to compare rather than replacing it unseen — the owner expects to replace it, but wants to see both first. The limits travel with the layer wherever it is drawn (safety rule 8): the line is the course of the river and **not the edge of a flood**, only the tributaries somebody has mapped are shown, and the spillway's own channel is not in OSM at all, so it is a point and not an invented line. — The owner wanted it on the map now rather than waiting for the analysis, and wants the choice of which geometry to keep to be theirs.
- 2026-10-10 — **Two tiers for a dam release, and code publishes neither of them** (owner: "a minor alarm with disclaimer as well, the real alarm … should give Admin notice and review and push for alarm"). The lower tier is a quiet notice the app shows itself, only to someone whose own area or watched place is on the river below the dam: it names the rule that fired, gives the dam's own figures with the hour they are for and how old they are, and says in as many words that **this is not an alert from Jaga** and that **nobody can yet say when the water arrives**. It carries no alert level, no alert colour, no sound and no notification. The upper tier writes one row into `dam_notices` for the admins, who see it on the war room below the unanswered-SOS gate and record whether they sent the real notice or not; sending it is still a person's act, and the dam release notice itself is the rest of A12 (safety rule 2). **There is no confidence percentage, deliberately**, although one was asked for: three releases in fifteen years, no hydraulic model and hourly readings cannot calibrate one, and a number like "75%" would be invented precision (safety rule 8).
