The grades are named criteria measured against 108,597 hourly readings and owned by admins in `system_settings.dam_grades`: *watchful* is storage above normal high (1,454.4 Mm³), inflow above 500 m³/s or a rise above 4 Mm³/h; *releasing* is any spill or a total outflow above 160 m³/s — a figure chosen before measuring, which the 99th percentile of outflow without spill then came out at (160.2). **A rule must hold for two readings in a row before it grades**, because the archive holds impossible single hours (1,944 m³/s with no spill against a record spill peak of 648; an inflow of 6,241); the lone reading is still shown, labelled as waiting for the next, and a real release confirms itself within the hour. Over the whole record that guard holds back 473 readings and leaves about 19 admin notices a year. — The owner wants people warned and the admins woken, without the app ever issuing an alert by itself or dressing a rule up as a probability.
- 2026-10-10 — **The dam's figures are read hourly inside Postgres, from the hourly series rather than the daily snapshot.** `pg_cron` runs `dam_fetch()` at seven minutes past each hour and the `http` extension fetches ThaiWater directly, so there is no function to deploy, no secret and no scheduler outside the database; the feed is free and needs no key. It reads `/analyst/dam_hourly_graph` over a three-day window, **not** `/analyst/dam`, which turned out to carry one row a day for this dam — with that, the two-reading rule would have taken two days to confirm a release and the rate of rise would have been meaningless. The window also fills in hours that were not published when the last run looked. **The feed lags**: checked at 06:00 Bangkok on 10 Oct, the newest hour published was 00:00 and the hours between were missing, so `stale_after_h` is 12 rather than 6 and every screen shows the reading's own hour and age. This is a record that trails reality by hours and is not a real-time release warning — which is the argument for the dam operator's own screen in spec section 15. — Free, no key, nothing to deploy, and the one thing it must not do is make a six-hour-old figure look current.
- 2026-10-10 — **The Thai wording of the SOS and report screens is approved as written** (owner, after reading it). That covers the SOS screen and its optional phone hint, the offline and queued screens, the status timeline and its two buttons, the details asked after sending, and the flood-report form with its water-depth references. As with the A3 checklists, this is safety text the owner has now read; it changes only on their word, and the Malay versions still wait for the Malay reviewer. — Nobody but the owner can sign off words that a frightened person reads.

- 2026-10-10 — **OpenStreetMap's river stays on the map until S7's zone exists** (owner, confirming the arrangement of the entry above after seeing it drawn). Nothing is swapped and nothing waits: the OSM reaches, the reservoir outline and the 39 tambons on the path are what the map draws, the quiet notice uses and the admin notice names, for the whole of this season if S7 steps 2–3 are not finished before it. When the HAND and radar zone is ready it is loaded under `source = 's7'` beside this one and the owner is shown both, side by side on the map, before anything is replaced — they expect to replace it, but that is a decision to take on a comparison and not in advance. — Waiting for the better geometry would leave the people on the river with nothing; the one on the map now is honest about what it is (the course of the river, not the edge of a flood).
- 2026-10-10 — An **AI second reader** is used to review the Thai and Malay interface wording for register and naturalness, through **Antigravity CLI** (`agy`) on the owner's existing Google AI Pro subscription. Google stopped serving Gemini CLI's "Login with Google" on 18 June 2026 for the individual, AI Pro and AI Ultra tiers and names Antigravity CLI as the replacement, so Gemini CLI is not an option; Antigravity carries the subscription's quota, needs no API key and adds no cost or runtime dependency (nothing in `package.json`, nothing the app calls). Fences, mine and overrulable: it is **advisory only** — suggestions go to the owner as a list and only accepted ones are written into `messages/*.json`; it **does not replace the Patani Malay reviewer**, because it writes standard Bahasa Melayu and drifts away from the local variety; alert, SOS and hotline text still comes only from reviewed templates (CLAUDE.md, UI text), and nothing is machine-translated at the moment a person reads it; and it is run from a scratch folder holding only `messages/th.json` and `messages/ms.json`, never inside the repo, because the agent reads files and the repo holds `.env.local`. — The owner asked for a professional-register review of the wording and already pays for the subscription; a second reader catches translationese that the author of the drafts cannot.
- 2026-10-10 — **The dam and reservoir wording uses professional hydrological register** in Thai, Malay and English, matching the terms the operating agencies print (`dam.*` and `warRoom.dam.*`, commit be276b8). Scope is deliberately narrow, at the owner's instruction: hydrology and the dam only. Alert levels, checklists, SOS and flood reporting keep the wording already approved on 8 and 10 October, because that text is written for residents under stress and changing approved safety copy is not a wording exercise. Four of the changes corrected errors rather than register: **ใต้เขื่อน** ("under the dam") became **ท้ายเขื่อน** for downstream; **เครื่องผลิตไฟฟ้า** became **เครื่องกำเนิดไฟฟ้า** (generator); the "above normal" rule compared a volume against a *level* and now reads as a volume against the capacity at normal high water level; and the reservoir level carried no datum, which the archive settles — the record runs 87.18–115.94 m and peaks at the published normal high water level, so it is metres above mean sea level (**ม.รทก.**, m MSL, m aras laut purata), consistent with the gauge readout that already used ม.รทก. — The owner, a hydrology and GIS specialist, asked for the register of the field; imprecise terms also read as amateur to the agencies and officials who will use the admin screens.
- 2026-10-10 — **Open, for the owner:** reservoir storage is shown as a percentage of **maximum** capacity (`storage_max_mcm`, 1,589.8 Mm³ for Bang Lang), and the screens now name that denominator. Thai dam bulletins and the news commonly quote the percentage of **normal** capacity (`storage_normal_mcm`, 1,454.4 Mm³), which is why January 2021 was reported as 103.5% while Jaga shows the same 1,504.7 Mm³ as 94.6%. Naming the denominator removes the contradiction but not the mismatch with what people read elsewhere. Options: keep maximum, switch to normal, or show both. Not changed without the owner, because it is a code change and a judgement about which convention residents and officials will compare against. — Raised while revising the wording; the figure itself is unchanged.
- 2026-10-10 — **A bell in the header, for what someone missed** (owner's request; three choices answered). It holds **everything that concerned them**: alerts for their area and every watched place, including the ones lifted or replaced, a word from the admins, and the steps of their own SOS — the catch-up copy of what spec 4.8 sends by push and LINE. It works **without an account** (the alerts of a chosen area are public) and what has been read is kept **in the browser and, for someone signed in, with their account as well**, so a second phone opens with the same things read. The window is **fourteen days**: long enough for a whole flood episode, short enough to stay readable. Three lines held: an announcement carries no level and no alert colour, so nothing in the list can be mistaken for a warning (safety rules 2 and 10); a lifted alert keeps its level and says in words that it ended (rule 3); and nothing is fetched on the SOS screens (rule 1). A dam release is not a separate kind — under spec 15 it reaches the public as an alert an admin sends, so it arrives in the bell as one. Announcements have a table and an admin function but no screen yet; that belongs with the alert console in the rest of A6.
- 2026-10-12 — **The dam's panel shows the day behind the newest hour as well as the hour itself** (owner: add the average daily flow and volume where the dam's figures are). The mean outflow and the mean inflow over the last 24 hours in m³/s, the volume each amounts to in million m³, and the spillway's share of that volume when there was one. It is arithmetic on the readings already stored — no new feed, no new request — and the window ends at the **newest reading**, not at the clock, because the feed lags by hours. **Each mean says how many hourly readings it rests on**, because the feed has gaps and a "daily mean" standing on three hours has to say so (safety rule 8); fewer than two hours is not printed at all, since one hour would only repeat the reading above it under a word that is not true of it. **Why it earns its place:** the published hourly figure jumps about and can say the opposite of what the day did — at 17:00 on 11 Oct the newest hour had the dam releasing nothing, while the day behind it had passed 3.98 million m³ downstream, a mean of 46 m³/s — and the inflow figure is a residual of the storage balance, so single hours come out negative and only a day's sum means anything. Negative hours are summed as they are, never clipped, or a day's inflow would come out larger than the water that arrived. The dam's card carries both flows; the quiet notice carries only the day's release, because it is read on the home screen above the SOS button and what the reservoir takes in is the dam's own business. — A hydrologist reads the day, not the hour, and the hour alone was misleading on the very day this was asked for.
- 2026-10-12 — Hazard classes: in built-up land High needs radar flood in at least 6 of 9 seasons, and the two priority events alone do not make a built-up cell High; open land is unchanged (`radar_blind.built_high_min_seasons`). Asked on 10 Oct to choose between this and keeping the classes, the owner said to proceed; the suggested option was applied and can be reversed in the config. Buildings in High fall from 114,168 to 57,513; they stay Medium. — The smaller patch filter of the second radar run flags small patches between houses that the radar cannot tell from reflections. The hazard tiles are written by the pipeline itself (Python, `pmtiles` and `mapbox-vector-tile`, both free), because tippecanoe does not run on this Windows laptop without WSL or Docker.
- 2026-10-12 — **A5, second piece: the authority console and the sender's "a team is coming" screen** (owner: "if you need to start A5, please do"). `/authority` shows a verified team the cases in its coverage, the one being offered to it first with its countdown; accepting is one tap and asks nothing first, the arrival estimate is asked straight after and until it is given, and declining or giving a case back takes a reason from a fixed list of four, so nobody types in a flood and nothing a person typed travels with the tap. The sender's screen names the team, shows the estimate as a range with the time it was given, and gives the team's number after one tap, logged. Choices made while building, each reversible: (1) **the console asks again every 8 seconds instead of holding a Realtime subscription.** Who holds an offer is already worked out from the clock on every read (decision of 9 Oct), so polling needs no new table permissions, costs nothing, and cannot silently stop the way a dropped socket can; the countdown runs against the server's clock, not the phone's. (2) **A refresh that set out before a tap is thrown away**, found in a real browser: it could put the older board back and make an accepted case look unaccepted for several seconds. (3) **A case just accepted stays near the top until its estimate is given**, because the card is asking a question. (4) **The sender's screen checks every 20 seconds while a case is open** (it was 60), since a team accepting is the news that screen exists for; it is one open case, not the 5-minute alert poll. **Not built yet, and said so on the screens where it shows:** closing a case from the team's side with a photo and the 12-hour fallback (5.4), the board as a map, photos and voice notes opened from the card, hero points, the responsibility map, and the alarm with the console closed (web push, A7).
- 2026-10-12 — **The dam's card carries the last fortnight as charts, so a release that has been growing for days can be seen growing** (owner: "charts as well to show the trends of the water flow out from spillway, people can see if it's continuously increased for few days or a week, this also give people awareness"). One bar a day for fourteen days of daily mean outflow, and a second chart of the daily mean discharge over the spillway **only when there was any** — a chart of noughts says less than a sentence and costs more of a small screen. **The two charts are never two colours on one chart.** Jaga has a single teal; two of its steps sit about 10 apart for normal colour vision, under the floor of 15 that telling two series apart by colour needs, and the alert palette is reserved for levels a person issued (docs/brand.md). So they are two charts on **one shared scale**, in the river's own blue from the map, with the words above each carrying the identity — which is also what a reader with any colour vision gets. The palette validator passes both themes on contrast and fails only its categorical chroma floor, which is a check for telling categories apart and there is only one series here. **Three honesty rules are in the arithmetic, not the wording:** a day with no readings is a gap with no bar, a published day of nothing keeps a hairline ("released nothing" and "published nothing" are different facts), and a part-published day is drawn faint and left out of the run of rising days, or every morning would report a fall. The run is also said in a sentence, for someone who does not read pictures, and every figure is in a table under the charts. The charts are on the dam's card only, not in the quiet notice, which is read on the home screen above the SOS button. **The archive was filled back sixty days** with `dam_fetch_range()` (the fetch half of the hourly job, lifted out so there is one copy of the parsing), so the fortnight is real rather than half empty. — A rising release is a shape, and a shape is what a chart is for; the hour and the day could not show it.
- 2026-10-12 — **The dam's card also charts the reservoir behind the releases** (owner: build it, after I offered it as the next step). The storage at the end of each day, for the same fortnight, as a line with a wash under it, **on an axis from empty to the dam's maximum capacity (1,589.8 Mm³)** and with the capacity at normal high water (1,454.4) drawn across it and named. **The axis is deliberately not fitted to the days drawn.** Storage moves by a fraction of a per cent on an ordinary day — 743.9 to 748.3 Mm³ over the fortnight measured today — and an axis zoomed onto that range would turn rounding into a mountain range; against the dam's own capacity the same fortnight reads as what it is, half full and flat, while a filling reservoir climbs visibly towards the line the operator works to. The day-to-day figures are in the table, which gained a storage column. A line is used rather than bars because storage is a level, not a quantity that starts from nothing each day; **a gap in the feed breaks the line** rather than being drawn through, because a line across a gap is invented data. Same single hue as the release charts, no alert colour, both themes, and the chart is left out entirely when the dam's capacity is unknown. — The releases are the effect and the reservoir is the cause; seeing the cause fill is what gives a person warning before the gates open.
- 2026-10-12 — **A5, third piece: finishing a case** (owner: "build, follow your suggestions"). A team reports "rescued" with at least one photo; that does not close the case. The person's own answer does, and **they can answer no**: "No, I still need help" sets the report aside, puts the case back into the relay at once (the same team included, since the likeliest cause is a mistaken tap) and is logged. This answer is not in the original spec 5.4; it is added there, because without it a wrong or false "rescued" would end someone's request in every team's view. After 12 hours without an answer (`system_settings.rescue_confirmation.fallback_hours`) a second verified team or an admin may confirm with a photo and a note of their own, never the team that reported it nor the same person through another unit. Choices made while building, each reversible: (1) **no new status**: "rescued" with `closed_at` empty means reported and unconfirmed, and `closed_at` is set only by a confirmation, each one a row in the `rescue_confirmations` table that has existed since September (hero points will hang on that row). (2) **The note is optional for the team's report and required for the fallback**: a crew is not made to type, but a confirmation standing in for the person's word must say how it was checked. (3) **Evidence photos go in the same private bucket** under `<case>/proof/`, writable only by a covering team or an admin, and a report counts only photos that were really uploaded. (4) A card's photos and voice note are opened by a tap, through ten-minute links made with the responder's own session, so the storage policy decides who sees them. **Fixed on the way:** a sender whose team had arrived could not close their own case, because the closing function listed the open statuses before "on site" existed. **Not built:** the fallback has no screen for admins yet (the function accepts them), LINE confirmation (A7), hero points, the board as a map.
- 2026-10-12 — **The dam's card has a way in that needs no aim** (the owner could not find it: "Where can I see the dam's card?"). It opened only by tapping the dam, its river or its reservoir on the map, and a river is a thin thing to hit: measured on the live map, 4 taps in 665 over the middle of Yala found it, and at the four-province view on a phone it is a mark a few pixels across. Two changes. **The legend names the dam, so the legend opens it**: a button under the dam's rows in the map legend, "ดูข้อมูลเขื่อน… และกราฟย้อนหลัง", 58 px tall, one tap, at any zoom. And **a tap is a thumb, not a pixel**: the map now counts anything within 12 px of the tap, which took the same 665 taps from 4 hits to 29. The layer drawn on top still wins, so a safe place or a gauge beside the river keeps its own tap. — A card nobody can open is a card nobody has; the fault was mine and it was invisible to every test, because the tests mock the map.
- 2026-10-12 — **A5, fourth piece: points, the responsibility map, the board as a map, and the admins' confirmation** (owner: "looking good, build it"). **Points (5.5)** are written by the database the moment a confirmation is recorded, never by a screen: 10 for the case, 2 a person, 3 more with vulnerable people (`system_settings.hero_points`). Three guards of mine, each reversible in that setting or the trigger: the head count is **capped at 20** for points, because it is a number the sender types; **a team earns nothing from a request its own account sent**; and there is one live award per team and case. Nothing is given when nobody held the case. The board of thanks stays inside the consoles (decision of 9 Oct) and is grouped by the province of the case. **The responsibility map (5.6)** calls a tambon a gap when **no verified rescue team** covers it, coordinators or not, since a coordinator does not pull anyone out of the water; a gap is drawn in the map ink, not in an alert colour, because a missing team is not an alert level, and every gap is also written out as a list, which is the part that can be read aloud and acted on. It carries team names and capabilities only, never a phone number. **The board as a map (5.1)** opens on the team's own cases, or on its own tambons when there are none, and loads MapLibre only when that tab is opened so the case list never waits for it. **Admins** get a list at the top of the war room of rescues nobody has confirmed, with the same photo-and-note form the teams use once the waiting time has passed. **Not built:** revoking points from a screen (an admin can do it in the database; the ledger keeps the row), the reports feed and the vulnerable-household list of 5.7, LINE confirmation (A7).
- 2026-10-12 — Arrival times for a release from Bang Lang are stored per tambon as ranges (table `dam_arrivals`, 39 tambons): the first rise in hours after the gates open, the highest water in hours after the highest release, and a warning time that is the earliest arrival with 20% taken off. The owner asked that they should not rest on low water alone if there is another way, so they come from the floods the gauges have seen since 2020 as well as from the turbines' daily pulses; the short lags of the largest floods set only the earliest bound, because rain below the dam shortens them. Yala town 9 to 18 hours, Pattani town 16 to 44 hours for the first rise. — No flood release has a clean record (January 2021 arrived on a river already rising from rain), there is no hydraulic model, and one time would be invented precision (safety rule 8). The limits are in pipeline/dam_release/METHODS.md.
- 2026-10-12 — **The dam's state is shown in colour, in Jaga's own alert hues** (owner: "normal should be green, risky should be red, attention should yellow"; they chose the alert palette over a separate traffic light, and the cards plus the dam's map mark over colouring the river). **This supersedes the 10 Oct decision only for the state chip**: the river, the tributaries and the reservoir keep the water blue, because the river runs through 39 tambons whose own alert colours lie underneath it and a red line across a green tambon would contradict the level a person issued there. Green = within the normal operating range, yellow = worth watching (including a rule that has fired once and is awaiting the next reading), red = a confirmed release above the normal range, **grey = no figures at all — never green**, because a placeholder must not read as "no risk" (safety rule 10). **What keeps this apart from an alert a person issued** (safety rule 2): it is a **pill**, not the square-cornered badge a level wears; it never carries colour alone (icon + word, docs/brand.md); the dam's card now carries the "this is not an alert from Jaga" line the notice always had; and a **stale reading keeps its colour and gains the grey marker beside it** rather than being recoloured, which is the rule the alert levels already follow. It shows on the dam's card, the quiet notice, the admins' war-room row and the dam's three marks on the map. — The owner asked for a state anyone can read at a glance; inventing a second traffic light would have meant two different reds in a safety app.
- 2026-10-12 — **The Thai wording of the console's new tabs is approved as written** (owner, after reading it): the tab names, the cases-on-a-map legend, the responsibility map with its gap list, and the points board (`authority.views.*`, `authority.map.*`, `authority.coverage.*`, `authority.points.*`). Not yet said in so many words: the rest of the team's board and the sender's "a team is coming" and "is that right?" screens, which were put to the owner at the same time.
- 2026-10-12 — **Opening the dam's figures also moves the map to hold the dam and the places that person saved** (owner's request). The button in the legend now frames a box over the dam, its two outlets, **the whole reservoir**, and the person's home and watched places when those are being shown - so the distance between the dam and their own places is something they see rather than work out. The box never gets smaller than about 26 by 22 km, or a dam with nobody's places beside it would zoom to a rooftop, and it carries a 6% margin so a pin does not sit half under the edge of the screen. The move is animated (600 ms) because the reader asked for it; the first fit and a change of province stay instant. Their own pins are included only while they are shown, since framing a map around something invisible reads as a wrong zoom. — Two faults this found, both from looking at it in a browser: framing the dam's point alone cut the reservoir off the screen, and **`Dam.reservoir` was typed as a MultiPolygon when PostGIS returns Bang Lang's lake as a Polygon with 13 holes**, so the frame walked one level too deep and threw; the walk is depth-blind now and the type says both.
- 2026-10-12 — **A button puts Jaga on the phone's home screen, or installs it on a computer** (owner's request). Where the browser has an install dialog (Chrome, Edge, Samsung Internet; Android and desktop) the button opens it; on every iPhone and iPad, in Safari on a Mac and in Firefox no page may do that, so there the button shows the browser's own steps with a picture beside each, and inside Messenger, LINE, Facebook, Instagram or TikTok it says to open the page in a real browser first. The card is the last thing on the home screen and also sits on the Account page; it is never in the SOS or report flows, never on the home screen while the person's own tambon or a watched place is at Warning or Evacuate, gone once Jaga runs from its own icon, and "Not now" keeps it away for three weeks on that device. — An installed Jaga opens with no signal (safety rule 7), and on an iPhone web push only works for an installed app, so A7's alerts will depend on it. Not checked on a real iPhone: the wording of Apple's menu in Thai and Malay is from memory.
- 2026-10-12 — **The alarm a rescue team hears is the "fast two-tone"** (owner's final choice, by ear). Eighteen candidates were played on the tracker: twelve alternatives, then six blends of the two the owner first named (2 and 6, the wailing sweep); the choice is number 2 as it was. Six short bright notes, 988 and 740 Hz in turn, then a breath, repeating every 1.3 seconds, with a matching vibration of four short buzzes. It replaces the slower, rounder two-tone the app shipped with on 10 Oct. Still the only sound in Jaga that repeats for a team, still silent until the team has tapped once to arm it.
- 2026-10-12 — The hazard layer on the app's map (owner): it is **on by default** on the risk view; the classes are named สูง / ปานกลาง / ต่ำ (Tinggi / Sederhana / Rendah) and the layer พื้นที่เสี่ยงน้ำท่วม (Kawasan berisiko banjir); and it carries a short disclaimer under the map. The wording proposed for it: "แผนที่นี้ประมาณจากความสูงของพื้นที่และภาพดาวเทียม 9 ฤดูน้ำ ยังไม่ได้ตรวจสอบกับน้ำท่วมจริง และไม่ใช่การพยากรณ์ พื้นที่ที่ไม่มีสีไม่ได้แปลว่าไม่มีความเสี่ยง" (an estimate from the height of the land and nine seasons of satellite images, not checked against real floods, not a forecast; no colour does not mean no risk). Still open with the owner: the three purple shades proposed for the classes (#C9B1E8, #9A6BCB, #5A2A8C) and their look at seven views of the map. — The classes have only been checked against radar, and the tiles leave the lowest class undrawn, so without the last sentence an empty place would read as safe (safety rule 10).
- 2026-10-12 — Hazard map look closed (owner: "yes for all questions"): the three purple shades are approved (Low #C9B1E8, Medium #9A6BCB, High #5A2A8C: one hue, light to dark, none of them an alert colour), the disclaimer wording stands, and the seven views (Hat Yai, Pattani town, Yala town, Sai Buri, Tak Bai, Betong, Songkhla city) are accepted as drawn, including Songkhla city as Medium through the built-up rule. The layer can go on the app's map. The classes will be compared with GISTDA's flood layers once the owner has connected the API key; a disagreement found then can still change the rules. — The owner's eye was the only check available now; GISTDA's maps are the first outside one.
- 2026-10-12 — **The Thai wording of the rest of the team's board and of the sender's screens is approved as written** (owner: "Thai wording is good"). With the entry above this covers everything A5 put on screen: accepting, declining, the arrival estimate, reporting a rescue, the fallback confirmation, "a team is coming", and "the team says you have been rescued, is that right?".
- 2026-10-12 — **A5, fifth piece: the flood-reports feed and the vulnerable-household list for teams, and the resident's household form that feeds it** (owner: "proceed, go"). The list would have been empty without the form (the `households` table has existed since September and no screen wrote to it), so both halves of spec 4.7 were built: `/account/household/home` for a person's own home and `/account/household/<place>` for someone at a place they watch, with the PDPA consent tick required every time and, for someone else's home, a second tick that the person agreed. Choices made while building, each reversible: (1) **the household list carries no phone number**; a number is one tap, one household, and its own line in the access log, as for an SOS. (2) **Each opening of the list is one logged read**, so the console reads it once a sitting rather than every time the tab is tapped. (3) **Only teams with rescue or coordination capability get it**, checked per tambon in the database; a planning or support team is told plainly that it is not for them. (4) **A report names no reporter** to a team, although the privacy matrix would allow it: a team needs what the water is doing and where. (5) **A report an admin hid is not passed on**; one not yet checked is shown and marked as unchecked, because in a flood an unmoderated report is still better than none. (6) A household at a watched place copies that place's point into the household row by the person's own act; the place itself, its label and the stored person's name stay private. **Not built:** an admin view of households, taking points back from a screen, other units' POC phones for coordination (5.3), LINE confirmation (A7).
- 2026-10-12 — Safe-place candidates (S4), owner's choices: **nothing is excluded**, a doubtful site is flagged instead (the plan's hard exclusion for sites flooded above knee depth is dropped: the radar gives no depth and sees little in towns; I had recommended excluding radar-flooded and low sites and said a flagged site can rank in someone's top three until it is checked); travel is on foot to places for people and by car to high-ground parking; and the scored candidates are loaded into the development database as unverified (2,029 rows, 12 Oct). Build choices, mine: freeboard is taken in height above the nearest stream, not above sea level; a road piece counts as flooded only when two of its three sample points are on a radar flood patch of 1.8 ha or more (the plan's literal rule cut a hill town off from six sevenths of its houses); the road search is our own, without osmnx. — The acceptance line is not met: 50 of 377 tambons have fewer than three candidates, flat coastal ones where OpenStreetMap names few buildings and no ground is 8 m above a stream; they need the official shelter list (`pipeline/safe_places/official_shelters.csv`, empty). Details in pipeline/safe_places/METHODS.md.
- 2026-10-12 — Road embankments, noted for later (owner): the main highways here are built on fill about 2 m above the original ground. That makes them the natural high ground for parking cars, and it makes them barriers that hold flood water back. Two things follow, neither scheduled yet: high-ground parking should look at the highway embankments and not only at terrain 8 m above a stream (which finds none on the coastal plains), and Jaga should later show the authorities where water needs a path through a highway line (science plan, Module 8). — The owner knows these roads; the 30 m elevation model smooths a 2 m embankment away, so neither the parking rule nor the hazard classes see it today.
- 2026-10-12 — Highway embankments are parking candidates now (owner: "build from what we have now, don't wait for my input"): 564 points on motorway, trunk and primary roads where the radar saw no flood, up to three per tambon, chosen before the terrain candidates; their freeboard carries an assumed 2 m of fill and every one is flagged for the highway authority's word on whether cars may stand there. Tambons with fewer than three candidates fall from 50 to 29, those with none from 11 to 5. There is no official shelter list to wait for, so the candidates stand as they are until volunteers check them. — The owner's knowledge of the roads; a 30 m elevation model cannot see a 2 m embankment. The second half of Module 8 (where water needs a path through a highway line) is not built.
- 2026-10-12 — **A5, last piece: the admins' points ledger and household list, and other teams' contact numbers** (owner, after looking at /dev/authority: "all looks good, please build"). `/admin/points` lists every award the database made; an admin may take one back **with a reason, once**, and the row stays in the ledger struck out with why (spec 5.5 says a ledger, so nothing is edited or deleted). The team is not told automatically; that is a notification, and belongs to A7. `/admin/households` is the admins' read of spec section 9 ("read, logged"), on the same terms as a team's: its own page, because opening it is itself a logged read of health information, and no phone number in the list. In the responsibility map a team can ask for **another team's point of contact** with one tap (spec 5.3); the database has allowed that only within shared coverage, and logged it, since September. With this A5 is built except what waits on A7: LINE confirmation, and the alarm reaching a phone with the console closed.
- 2026-10-13 — The weather in the header is drawn as **a pill with the sky in it**, not as a line of underlined text (owner: nothing about it said that tapping it opens a whole page). The pill carries the same sky the weather page opens on (by day and by night, under the same veil, so the writing keeps 4.5:1 on every sky), the temperature in bold, and on a wide screen the place and an arrow. A pale glint crosses it every seven seconds and the icon drifts a pixel or two; a pointer lifts it, a press pushes it in. Nothing moves under reduced motion or on the SOS screens. At 360 px it is 61 px wide, with no arrow, because the three language names leave 65 px beside them and the frozen header must stay 107 px. — A pill reads as a button to anyone who has used a phone, and the sky in it shows what the page behind it is about.
- 2026-10-12 — The map (owner's notes): safe places are drawn only when a switch under the map is turned on, off by default, because there are 2,566 of them; they are squares with a small house or a P, so they cannot be taken for the person's own round pins; and the flood-hazard classes are on the map, on by default on the risk view, in the three approved purples with their names and the disclaimer, and a tap on a coloured place names its class. Tapping a safe place already opened a card with Navigate (Apple Maps on an iPhone, Google Maps elsewhere; built in A3), so nothing was added to the plan for that. — Thousands of dots buried the map and looked like the person's own places. Found on the way: the map's address asked for 5,000 safe places and was silently given the first 1,000 (the API's page size); it now reads page by page.
- 2026-10-13 — **The "Add to home screen" button and its moving guide are approved** (owner, after trying it on their iPhone: "love it"). The Thai menu names in the guide ("เพิ่มไปยังหน้าจอโฮม", "เพิ่ม") match the iPhone's own menu. — They had been written from memory. Still unchecked on a real device: Android, desktop, and the Malay names.
- 2026-10-12 — **One box finds a place for the pin: a name, pasted coordinates, a shared link or a plus code** (owner's request: people with little technical skill must be able to find their location; "be creative"). It sits in the location field of "add a place to watch", the home location and the household form. What is typed is read on the phone first (`lib/place-input.ts`): decimal or degree-minute-second coordinates in either order, Thai numerals, Google Maps, Apple Maps and OpenStreetMap links, `geo:` addresses and full plus codes need no server at all. A **name** is looked up in Jaga's own tambon list first, then in **OpenStreetMap's Nominatim** for landmarks (schools, mosques, markets, villages), with the weather gazetteer as a second source when Nominatim does not answer. A **short Google Maps link** (`maps.app.goo.gl/…`) is followed by our server, which may go to Google's map hosts over https and nowhere else, hop by hop (`lib/map-link.ts`). A **short plus code** ("V7HC+7Q เมืองปัตตานี") is completed from the town it names. Reasons and fences: Nominatim is free and needs no account or key, but asks for at most a request a second and no search-as-you-type, so the box searches on the button or Enter only, both routes need a signed-in session, and answers are kept in the browser for an hour; if use ever outgrows that, the search moves to a self-hosted or keyed service. The box only **proposes** a point: the map opens on it and the person moves the pin onto the house, and the pin is said in words ("หมุดอยู่ใน ต.บานา อ.เมืองปัตตานี จ.ปัตตานี"), because numbers mean nothing to most people. Nothing typed is stored or logged; a typed address reaches OpenStreetMap from our server, never from the phone.
- 2026-10-13 — **The installed app's icon carries the bell's unread number** (owner's request: a red circle with a count, as on Teams). The page hands the count to the system's App Badging API whenever it changes and clears it when the bell is read; the system draws the mark. — Limits, told to the owner: it shows on installed Jaga in Chrome and Edge on Windows and Mac now; an iPhone or iPad shows it only once notifications are allowed, which Jaga does not ask for until A7; Android shows a dot only for a real notification, never a number a page sets. And the number can only change while Jaga is open: counting an alert that arrives while the app is closed needs web push (A7), where the service worker will set it.
- 2026-10-12 — **Three animated figures of the dam's state are built for the owner to choose from; none is on the home screen yet** (their request: an animated figure of the dam's situation for the home page, "combine artistry and science", shown on a dev page first with alternatives). `/dev/dam-figure` draws all three in every state: **the cross-section** (the reservoir filling towards the capacity at normal high water, the wall, the jet leaving the outlet, water over the crest when a gate is open), **the river** (the water below the dam seen from above, streaks travelling as fast as the dam releases) and **the dial** (outflow from nothing to the largest release in the record, with the turbines' own limit marked and a needle that eases to the figure). **The science is the whole of what moves**: the water level is the storage against the dam's capacity, the speed is the outflow against the 800 m³/s of January 2021, the turbine mark is the 160 m³/s the archive measured, and a figure beyond the record is held at the end of the scale while the caption still prints what was published. **A dam that has published nothing stands still, in grey** — a lively drawing would say "all is well" with nothing behind it (safety rule 10). **Motion is never the only channel**: each figure carries the state's colour, an icon, a word, the numbers and an `aria-label`, and `prefers-reduced-motion` stops every animation while the drawing and its colour stay. Drawn as inline SVG, animated with CSS transform and opacity only: no canvas, no library, nothing downloaded, which is what a home screen on slow 3G can afford. — The owner asked to see alternatives before anything lands on the home screen, so the choosing is theirs and the wiring waits for it.
- 2026-10-12 — The flood-risk layer and the map's background (owner). (1) Colours: pale pink, rose and deep wine-red (Low #F2B8C6, Medium #DB6A9A, High #7A1238), drawn at 45% so the map shows through. This supersedes the purples approved earlier the same day. The owner asked for red, amber and yellow; those are the hues of Evacuate, Warning and Watch, so they chose this warm ramp, which reads as a risk map and was measured to sit clear of every alert colour. (2) The layer has its own switch and starts on. (3) Its outlines are smoothed: each class is blurred by about one 30 m cell and cut so that it keeps its area (the first try, cut at one half, lost 11% of High and 17% of Medium and was withdrawn the same hour; the published file has High +4%, Medium +2%, Low +8% against the raster, erring high). The smooth line is drawing, not knowledge. (4) Three map backgrounds, the person's choice: Standard (OpenFreeMap, as before), Terrain (the same with hill shading from Mapzen's open elevation tiles on AWS) and Satellite (Sentinel-2 cloudless 2020 by EOX, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, about 10 m, always shown as day). All free, no key, no account. — A satellite picture sharp enough to show houses would need a paid or account-bound service, which CLAUDE.md rules out; the screen says what this one is. At night the darkest class is the hardest to see on the dark map; left as it is for now and told to the owner.

The button carries the number of places, so an empty list is not hidden behind a tap, and the list is still fetched and kept on the phone whether or not it is opened, so it is there offline (safety rule 7). It is a native disclosure: it opens without any script. Not done, and offered to the owner: opening it by itself when the person's tambon is at Warning or Evacuate.

New sources `jaga-app-icon-shadow.svg` and `jaga-app-icon-touch.svg`; the maskable one is redrawn; the PNGs come from `npm run icons`. The shadow is on the square, never on the mark, and nowhere else (brand.md's "no effects" rule gains this one exception). — The owner asked for it, so the icon stands off the wallpaper. The laptop icon keeps its transparent edge and the shadow around the square. — The owner added the icon on their phone and did not want the white space around the logo; a phone cuts the tile itself, so a shadow cannot be drawn outside it and the only honest ways to make the icon stand off any wallpaper are inside the tile.
- 2026-10-13 — The map opens on the Terrain background (owner); Standard and Satellite stay one tap away. — Where the hills are says most about where water goes. Cost: the hill shading is a second set of tiles to fetch on a slow phone, on the map page only; the home screen never loads the map.
- 2026-10-13 — **The dam's drawing on the home screen: the cross-section, and who sees it** (owner: "Figure 1"). The animated figure of what Bang Lang is doing goes (1) inside the quiet dam notice, for someone whose own tambon or watched place is on the river below the dam, and (2) as a card of its own for everyone whose area is in Yala, Pattani or Narathiwat — and, on an ordinary day when the notice has nothing to say and draws nothing, for the people on the river as well, with a sentence that names their river instead of the one for everyone else. **Songkhla is left out**, on the evidence: the 39 tambons the released water runs through are in Yala and Pattani only and the river ends in Pattani Bay, so neither a release nor a failure of the dam sends water over the divide into the Songkhla Lake or U-Taphao basins; if that ever changes, `DAM_FIGURE_PROVINCES` is the one line to change. The card says in words that it is not an alert from Jaga but the dam's own published figures (safety rule 2), and a dam that has published nothing stands still in grey rather than looking calm (safety rule 10). — The owner asked for the figure where the notice goes and for the three provinces; the quiet-day case was found in a browser, where someone living on the river would otherwise have seen less of their own dam than a neighbour a province away.
- 2026-10-13 — Safe places are used as they are, without a field check (owner): the 2,566 candidates stay in the app, every one still marked unverified with its flags, and nobody is asked to take the field sheet out. The sheet stays in the pipeline's output in case volunteers appear. — The owner has nobody to send. A site's flags and the word "unverified" on its card are what tells a person how far to trust it.
- 2026-10-13 — On the home screen the card "Bang Lang Dam: how it stands" sits in the smaller column directly above "Open the map", on a laptop and on a phone or tablet alike, and loses its own link "See the map of the water's path" (owner). — The map button is the next thing under it, so the link said the same thing twice; and the left column is kept for the person's own alert level. The quiet dam notice, shown only while the dam has something to say to someone on the river, stays at the top with its link.
- 2026-10-13 — Two things the owner agreed to. (1) The tambon table is in the database (`hazard_by_tambon`, 377 rows, public to read, written only by the seed `45_hazard_by_tambon.sql` that the pipeline generates); no screen reads it yet, and the village-cluster table is left until one needs it. (2) The flood-risk layer has night-time shades: on the dark map the highest class is the brightest (#FFC4D6), Medium a rose (#E0679E) and Low a dim mauve (#7E5C74), drawn at 60%; by day nothing changes. — By day the highest class is the darkest, which on a dark map made it the hardest to see; the order of lightness is turned round at night so the class that matters most stands out, and the legend always shows the shades in use. The night shades were measured clear of the alert colours like the day ones.
- 2026-10-13 — Alert message templates live in a database table (`alert_templates`), seeded by the migration; admins pick one and cannot edit its words, and only the super admin can change a template (owner). The publish function takes a template id and builds the Thai, Malay and English text itself, so the console cannot send text nobody reviewed. — Wording can be corrected mid-season without a deploy, and the words stay out of reach of a hurried admin.
- 2026-10-13 — Until the launch switch (`JAGA_IN_SERVICE`) is on, every alert published from the console is saved as a drill (`alerts.is_drill`), which only admins can read: row-level security on `alerts` and `alert_tambons` hides it from the map, the home screen, the bell and any direct read (owner). The flag is set from the deployment's switch, never from the form, and a drill and a real alert cannot replace each other. — The live site and development share one database until launch, so a test alert would otherwise be shown to anyone who opened the site. Not covered: the dam release notice from the war room (A12) still publishes for real; it needs the same switch.
- 2026-10-13 — The console starts with one flood template per level (Watch, Warning, Evacuate, Safe to return), written by Claude in Thai, standard Malay and English and used without a review round (owner: "no review, I trust you"). Cancelling has no template: the alert simply ends, with the admin's reason kept in the history. — The owner chose speed; the Malay is standard Bahasa Melayu and still goes to the Patani Malay reviewer with the rest.
- 2026-10-13 — Alert console build choices (Claude, overrulable). The next update is chosen as a wait (1, 2, 3, 6, 12 or 24 hours), never typed as a clock time; expected-flooding and return windows are typed as Thailand time whatever zone the admin's laptop is in. The admin's free-text note follows the template's reason and is shown as typed in every language, never translated; the reason itself is stored per language (`messages.reason_ms`, `reason_en`). An alert in a tambon that already has one is allowed with a warning at the review step, because refusing could hold back a warning; "Replace" is the way to supersede. Cancelling needs a reason. The recipient preview counts homes and watched places now and says that push and LINE are not connected until A7. — Each is the smaller risk: a mistyped time zone, a machine-translated safety note, or a blocked alert would all be worse.
- 2026-10-13 — "Navigate" on a safe place asks which app first, instead of opening Apple Maps on an iPhone by itself (owner: most people prefer Google Maps). Offered in this order: Google Maps, Waze, Apple Maps (Apple devices only), and on Android "Another app on this phone", a `geo:` link that makes the phone show its own list of every installed app that can navigate. A line under the choices says map apps do not know which roads are flooded. — A web page cannot ask a phone which apps are installed, on iOS or Android, so the list cannot be a detected one: Google Maps and Waze are always shown, and their links open the app where it is installed and the app's own web page where it is not. iOS has no link that opens a system list of navigation apps; Android's `geo:` does. The choice is asked every time and not remembered. Free, no keys, no routing service (spec 4.3).
- 2026-10-13 — **The arrival ranges are shown** (S7 measured them at the river gauges on 12 Oct). Every tambon on the dam's path has a range for the first rise and one for the highest water, in hours after the gates open, with the safety margin already taken off the early end. They appear on the dam notice, the dam's card and the home-screen card, always with the sentence saying they come from gauge records rather than a forecast or a hydraulic model. A clock time is shown only where a person has said when the gates opened (the release notice), never from the hourly feed, which lags by hours. Someone watching several places is given the nearest to the dam, and a place on a side stream is told its time belongs to the junction. — The measurement exists now, and "nobody can say when the water arrives" had become untrue; what is still unknowable is kept visible instead of hidden.
- 2026-10-13 — **An admin can send the dam release notice** (`/admin/dam-release`, the rest of A12 part 1). The sender enters when the gates opened, the release in m3/s, optionally when it ends, the level and the next update; the words come from the reviewed template in Thai, Malay and English and the sender writes none of them; the zone is the dam's own tambons from S7 with no control to change it; the alert carries the measured onset window; every send and every withdrawal is in `audit_log`, and a notice sent in error is withdrawn in one tap. **The default level is `warning`**, settling the open item of 9 Oct: it is the middle of the three, so evacuate and watch are each one tap away, and the figures never choose it — a person does (safety rule 2).
- 2026-10-13 — **The home screen's dam card carries a button into the whole report** (`/map?dam=<code>`), and the report on the map opens with the animated cross-section above the state chip (owner). The drawing there has no caption of its own: the chip, the grade line and the figures below already say the state, and in a browser the three readings of "normal range" ran one under the other. — The owner asked for both; the map page keeps its Suspense boundary so it stays prerendered and offline-ready (safety rule 7).
- 2026-10-13 — On the map, the basemap's place names are drawn above Jaga's fills (flood-risk shading, alert colours, report hexagons, hill shading) instead of under them, and on the dark map they take the light ink and dark outline Jaga's own names use (owner: at night the names could not be read over the flood-risk layer). — Our layers were added after the basemap's, so every fill landed on top of its names, and the dark basemap writes them in a dim grey. Names stay under Jaga's outlines, the dam, safe places and the person's own pins. By day only the order changes.
- 2026-10-13 — The map carries a button "All 4 provinces" on its top edge, opposite the zoom buttons, that pulls the view back from the person's own area to the whole service area in one tap (owner). The province selector is left as it is. — The map opens close around their tambon and watched places, and getting out to the whole picture took several zoom taps.
- 2026-10-13 — Thailand's time is shown in the header (owner: minimal, placed where it fits best): beside the weather on a wide screen as "Thailand time 14:05", and on a phone as "TH 14:05" in the few pixels above the weather pill. — Every time Jaga shows is Asia/Bangkok, so someone abroad or with a wrong device clock can see what "now" is on that scale. The phone header had no free width in either row, and a line of its own would have cost page height: the frozen header stays 107 px. It asks nothing of the server.
- 2026-10-13 — On a phone the weather sits at the **top right of the header, level with the logo**, beside the bell (owner). The top row was already full, so the owner chose **three rows** over shortening the language names or moving the bell: logo with weather and bell; Home, Map, Account; the languages. The frozen header on a phone is **155 px instead of 107** (48 px less page), by their choice. From 640 px up everything fits on one row again, and a laptop is unchanged. The pill has its arrow on a phone now. The time (added the same day) sits at the end of the language row on a phone: beside the weather it pushed the top row onto a fourth line for anyone who has not chosen a place yet, whose pill says "weather" in words. — The owner's layout call; the fourth-line fault was found by a browser test, not by looking.
- 2026-10-13 — **A second kind of request: food, water and essentials** (owner: people stuck in a high house are safe but run out of food and water, "like on an island"; some teams and volunteers will register as food and water distributors, and it may be clothes and other must-have items too). A request is the same record as an SOS with `kind = supplies`, a fixed list of needs, a note and how long their supplies will last; teams gain a `supplies` capability. Spec 4.10. — Using the SOS record means it is never refused, works offline and without an account, and needs no second pipeline to keep safe. Four choices by the owner, all as recommended: (1) **its own button** on the home screen, so the SOS button keeps its two taps and no question in front of it; (2) offered to **distributors, then coordinators, then the admins**, while rescue teams see it and may accept but are never alarmed by it; (3) **a softer sixth sound, played once**, with a 10-minute window per team (an admin setting); (4) **fewer points** than a rescue: 5 per confirmed delivery plus 1 per person.
- 2026-10-13 — Supply requests, build choices (Claude, overrulable). (1) **"It got worse: I need rescue"** on the sender's page turns the same case into a rescue at once (the delivering team's claim is set aside, the rescue relay starts, logged), and a plain SOS from the same phone does the same; a supply request sent into an open rescue case stays a rescue and gains the needs. (2) Distributors see the exact point and may reveal the phone (logged) like any covering team, and need the same admin verification; the `supplies` capability does not open vulnerable-household data. (3) The spec's "no resource marketplace, no relief donations" stands: Jaga carries a request to a verified team and holds or collects nothing, and the form says so. (4) Medicine is on the list with "if urgent, call 1669 or send an SOS". (5) On every board a supply request is marked in words and an icon, never in the SOS red, sorts under every unheld rescue case, and is left out of the admins' count of unanswered cases; it is "overdue" after four times the rescue setting, at least an hour. (6) The older "Support" capability no longer says "food" in its description. (7) Repaired on the way: a repeat SOS now also merges into a case whose team has arrived (`on_site`), which the merge had missed. (8) The Thai and Malay wording is Claude's draft; the Malay is standard Bahasa Melayu.
- 2026-10-13 — GISTDA: the open API is the route, the download page is not (owner's look + Claude's test with the owner's key). The download page goes back to 2016 but holds processed maps (jpg, pdf, xlsx, kmz, zipped shapefiles) for whichever region GISTDA mapped on a day, often not ours, so nothing is built on it. The API accepts the key as an `API-Key` header (so it never goes in an address) and is read only from the server or the pipeline: **GISTDA's answers repeat the caller's key inside `links`, so nothing from them is passed to a browser or stored unstripped**. Repeated flood (`flood-freq`) covers 2011–2024 with one flag per year, about 479,000 polygons in the four provinces: used as a second opinion on our radar seasons 2017–2024 and on the hazard classes. The recent-flood layers (1, 3, 7, 30 days) are hexagons of about 0.12 km² with flooded area and counts of buildings, people, schools, hospitals and road length, and name the satellite passes behind them; they are empty for our provinces outside a flood.
- 2026-10-13 — GISTDA comparison done; the flood maps and hazard classes stand as they are (Claude's reading, overrulable; figures in `pipeline/gistda/METHODS.md`). Their repeated-flood layer (478,640 polygons, a yes-or-no per year 2011–2024) was set against the radar seasons 2017–2024 and the classes. Where GISTDA maps a flood, our radar has one at or within 60 m in 79–97% of its area; ours maps 1.2–4.3 times more, and 84% of the land we flag in one season only is never flagged by them, which supports the two-season floor. 96% of the land they flag three years or more is High or Medium. Their layer is as blind in the towns as ours (Hat Yai centre 1.6% in any year, Pattani town 1.3% in 2024), so it cannot check the built-up rule. Open: 104 km² they flag at least once is Minimal on our map, to be looked at. A "no" in their layer is not a dry year (2018 is empty for all four provinces), so it is never used as an all-clear.
- 2026-10-13 — The owner approved seven things that were waiting for their reading, in one message: (1) the **dam release notice** - its Thai, and the level starting at Warning (เตือนภัย) on the sending form; (2) the **food and water request** screens, the list of needs and the new delivery team type; (3) the **household form**, including its consent text for health information; (4) the **admins' war room**, with watched places kept as counts per tambon and not pins; (5) the **SOS and report wording**; (6) the **dam and reservoir wording** in hydrological register, with the storage percentage left as a share of maximum capacity and named as such on screen; (7) the **weather screens**. — Thai is the source of truth for copy and safety text is read by the owner before it counts as reviewed. The Malay of all seven is still standard Bahasa Melayu awaiting the Patani Malay reviewer. Still open with the owner: the look of the dam on the map ("dam OK") and the impact dashboard.
- 2026-10-13 — The owner approved the Bang Lang dam as it is shown ("Dam OK"): the dam, reservoir, river and side streams on the map, the quiet notice, the card with its state colour, the figures for the hour and the day, the fortnight's charts and the reservoir chart, and their Thai wording. This includes the choice not to print a confidence percentage for a release: the named rules stay (decision of 10 Oct). — It was the last dam item waiting for the owner's look. OpenStreetMap's river stays until the terrain-and-radar zone can be compared with it.
- 2026-10-13 — **Jaga never says whose time it is** (owner: the users are in Thailand, "time" is enough; in the header the number alone). The header clock is now only `14:05` (the word "time" stays for a screen reader and the tooltip, where a bare number says nothing). The "(Bangkok)" / "(เวลาไทย)" label is gone from every time in the admin console and from the forms that ask for one (the dam release notice, the alert's expected-time windows). Supersedes the wording in the entry above ("Thailand time 14:05", "TH 14:05"). Every time is still Asia/Bangkok, and the admin console still adds the viewer's own time when their laptop is in another zone; that one carries its zone name ("10:05 GMT+3"), which is what tells the two apart. — Fewer words in a header that is already tight; a unit test keeps the wording out of all three message files. Known cost (Claude's note): a form no longer reminds an admin abroad that the time they type is read as Thailand's.
- 2026-10-13 — Land GISTDA has flagged is at least Low on the hazard map (owner: "gistda low yes", Claude's suggestion). A cell that would be Minimal becomes Low when GISTDA's repeated-flood layer flags it in any year 2011–2024 and it is under 10 m above its nearest stream; never more than Low on that ground, nothing on higher ground (their flags there sit on masked slopes). Result: 99.4 km² and about 15,200 buildings move from no colour to Low (more than the 67 km² and 9,900 estimated before the decision, because a flag anywhere in a 30 m cell counts, as for the radar); High and Medium unchanged. Reason: a wrongly coloured Low costs little, an uncoloured place that floods costs more. The hazard layer now depends on GISTDA's data: its licence must be confirmed in writing before launch, and GISTDA is credited wherever the layer is shown.
- 2026-10-13 — **The impact dashboard moves, and is no longer all bars** (owner: make it look cooler, futuristic, animated, try other chart forms; a cup for the top performers, kept minimal). The page opens on a navy panel with a dial of the confirmed share and figures that count up; every request is one mark (four forms, one per outcome); waiting is a cumulative curve; the season is a wave (confirmed = filled area, all requests = dashed line); each province has a ring. **A cup goes only to the three "teams to thank this month"**, on their honours card and beside their name in the list of teams; it is brand teal, never gold (gold is the Watch yellow). — Kept from 9 Oct, unchanged: no ranking beyond that top three, no response time per team, the failures in the same size type, no alert colour. Claude's notes: identity is carried by form and words, not colour (the palette validator puts Jaga's teal and its neutral under the floor for telling two series apart, by day and by night); every movement is an entrance played once and is off under reduced motion, and the finished chart is the resting state; the rings show a share, so a province's size is in the figures beside it. The Thai and Malay of the new labels are drafts.
- 2026-10-13 — Supersedes "every alert is a drill until launch" (13 Oct): before the launch switch is on, the admin chooses at the review step between a drill and a **real alert**, each time; it starts on drill, so a real one is always deliberate. After launch every new alert is real. A replacement keeps the kind of the alert it replaces (owner: "I want to test with real alert, very few people know about this app anyway"). — A drill cannot show the owner what a resident sees; the cost is that anyone who opens the live site during a test sees a made-up alert, which the owner accepts.
- 2026-10-13 — An open app rings when a new alert arrives for the person's area, home or watched places (owner): the low double-tone for Warning and Evacuate, the dam's repeating figure for a release notice (30 seconds at most, or until a tap), once per alert per phone, never on the SOS screens. Watch and Safe to return stay silent, as docs/brand.md already says. — Limits no web page can remove: nothing plays before the person has tapped the page once, and nothing plays with Jaga closed; that needs web push (A7), which plays the phone's own notification sound.
- 2026-10-13 — The owner approved the impact dashboard as rebuilt today, with the Thai and Malay of its new labels, and decided that **it keeps its made-up figures for now**: the owner will say when to remove them, before launch. — Until the first season there is nothing real to show, and the look can only be judged with numbers on it. The page stays admin-only and says it is an example in its first block. To do before launch (owner's call): take the example data off `/admin/impact`; `/dev/impact` keeps it, like every other example page. The Malay is still standard Bahasa Melayu awaiting the Patani Malay reviewer.
- 2026-10-13 — GISTDA's recent flood on the map, build choices (Claude, overrulable). A switch under the map, **off by default**, shows the hexagons where GISTDA's satellites saw flood water in the last 3, 7 (default) or 30 days in the four provinces. The phone asks Jaga (`/api/public/satellite-flood`, kept at the edge for 30 minutes), never GISTDA, so the key stays on the server and only the shapes and the water's area are passed on. It is drawn in **violet**: blue is the "safe to return" level, and the layer must not look like an alert or a hazard class. Every state says what it is not: with flood, the day of the latest image and "this is not a warning"; with none, "this is not an all-clear" and why; when GISTDA cannot be reached (or the key is missing), that this says nothing about flooding, and an empty map is never drawn in that case. No taps on a hexagon and no per-hexagon counts of buildings or people yet. The Thai and Malay wording is Claude's draft.
- 2026-10-13 — **A LINE sign-in started in Jaga on the home screen finishes in Jaga on the home screen** (owner: after signing out and in again, the phone's browser was signed in and the installed app stayed signed out). The LINE app cannot return to an installed web app; it always opens the phone's browser, and on an iPhone that browser shares no cookies with the app. So: the sign-in link says when it is tapped inside the installed app; the browser LINE returns to then does not use the sign-in but leaves it waiting in `sign_in_flows` and shows "Go back to the Jaga app"; the app, which still holds the one-time key, collects it when the person opens it again (asked every 3 s while the app is on screen, for ten minutes at most) and signs itself in. The browser page keeps a second button, "Sign in here in this browser instead". Never on the SOS, report or supplies screens: the app does not ask there, so nothing pulls anyone off a request for help. A sign-in started in an ordinary browser, or inside Messenger, behaves as before (decision 2026-10-07). — No secret key is needed on the live site and no session is shared between two browsers; the waiting code is useless without the key only the app holds. Known limits (Claude's note): one sign-in signs in one place, the app or the browser, not both; and it is not yet tried on a real iPhone or Android phone.
- 2026-10-13 — **The impact dashboard has a map, at province grain and no finer** (owner: add a map view so people can see where in the four provinces the record was collected). One circle per province, placed inside it: its size is the requests Jaga carried (by area), its ring the share a team confirmed; tapping it shows that province's figures and moves the map in. The tambons no rescue team covers are hatched in the map ink, the same list the page prints in words. — Claude's fence, from spec 16: province and day are the finest grain published, so no request, household or team position is on the map and no tambon is shaded by its number of requests (a figure that small can point at one house). The example's three uncovered tambons had invented codes and now carry their real ones. Thai and Malay of the map's labels are drafts.
- 2026-10-13 — Merging duplicate SOS never closes anything (Claude, overrulable; spec 6.4 only says "merge duplicates"). A merged request leaves the teams' boards but stays open; its sender is shown the team that took the main request; an admin can undo the merge; and it undoes itself when the main request ends while the merged one is still open. A request a team already holds, a request others are merged into, and a rescue with a supply request cannot be merged. Pairs are offered on `/admin/duplicates` from what the database already notices (same phone, or within 300 m and two hours). — A wrong merge must never be the reason someone is not reached (safety rule 1); the cost is that a true duplicate can reappear after the rescue.
- 2026-10-13 — Exports (Claude, overrulable): SOS, reports and alerts as CSV or GeoJSON, 7 to 365 days. No export has a phone number, a name, a device id, free text, photos or voice. The SOS export is anonymized unless the admin ticks otherwise: no case id, position rounded to 0.01 degree (about 1 km), times to the hour. Each export is logged once with its row count. CSV cells that would run as a spreadsheet formula are neutralised. — Free text can carry a name or a number, so it is left out even of the full export; the anonymized form is what can be shared with a researcher.
- 2026-10-13 — The rest of the admin console (Claude, overrulable). Moderation: approving a report is what lets it count in the public hexagons; hiding takes it out of them and out of the teams' feed; nothing is deleted. Safe places: "rejected" removes a place from public lists without deleting it; a new place must fall inside a covered tambon. Announcements are written by the admin in each language, never machine-translated, and a language left empty shows the Thai. Handover notes cannot be edited or deleted. Reading the access log is not itself logged. — Each keeps a record instead of erasing one.
- 2026-10-13 — **Flood reports reach the neighbours at once** (owner). A report counts on the map and in a new home-screen banner, "Flood has been reported near your area", as soon as it is sent, for everyone whose home or watched place is in the same tambon or district; it says it is a report from users in the area that Jaga has not checked, and it is not an alert. Before this a report counted only after an admin approved it, and no approval screen existed, so no resident ever saw another's report. An admin can still hide one. — A report approved the next morning warns nobody. The banner wears no alert colour and no level, and sits under the alert status, so it cannot be taken for a level a person issued (safety rule 2); it shows counts per tambon only, never a point or a reporter (safety rule 6).
- 2026-10-13 — **Three different people make a tambon's reports "confirmed by residents"** (owner: more than three reports in a similar area means it is certainly flooded). I read "similar area" as the same tambon and count **people, not reports**, so one account cannot confirm itself; the number is a setting (`flood_reports.confirm_people`, 3). The screen says "several residents report the same", not "certain". — Jaga has not checked it either way, and a forecast or a report is never presented as certain (safety rule 8).
- 2026-10-13 — **A report stands until somebody says the water has gone** (owner): its own reporter ends it with one button, or other people in the same tambon report dry ground after it. Two choices of mine, for the owner to overrule: it takes **two** other people, so one person cannot take a neighbour's warning down, and a report lapses after **72 hours** whatever happens, so a forgotten one does not warn for ever. Both are settings.
- 2026-10-13 — **Water drawn on the map from reported depths: approved in the limited form, not built yet** (owner: "do as you recommend"). A soft patch labelled as estimated from residents' reports, only where at least two reports agree, only land lower than the reported water surface and connected to it within about 1 km, fading after 12 hours; and it goes public only after the method has been checked against the November 2024 and 2025 radar flood maps and the owner has seen how often it is right.
- 2026-10-13 — The owner tried the sign-in from Jaga on the home screen on their iPhone: the app is signed in when they come back to it. They asked that the browser send them back to Jaga, or at least say so. A web page cannot open an app on the home screen (a phone gives it no way to), so there is no button; the browser's page now shows the Jaga icon, the two steps (leave the browser, tap the icon) and says why. — Honest about the limit instead of a button that does nothing. Android is still untried.
- 2026-10-13 — **Check of the water patch computed from a reported depth, before anything is drawn** (the condition set the same day). About 2,850 stand-in reports were placed on radar-flooded cells of each of the November-December 2024 and 2025 floods; each was given the depth class a person standing there would report (from the flood's own edge on the 30 m terrain), and the patch was computed as planned: land at or below ground + depth, connected to the report, within a radius. Compared with the radar's flood on observed land: within 1 km, 49 % (2024) and 54 % (2025) of the patch was radar-flooded, against 31 % and 34 % for simply shading the whole circle, and the patch held 68 % of the radar's flood; within 500 m, 54 % and 58 % against 38 % and 41 %; within 300 m, 58 % and 62 % against 44 % and 47 %. About one patch in five was mostly wrong (under a quarter flooded). — So the terrain adds some skill but about half of what it would shade was not flooded on the radar, in a test that favours the method (the depth came from the radar's own flood edge; a real "knee-deep" is rougher). The radar also misses water under trees and in towns, so the true figure is somewhat better than this. Not built; the owner decides whether this is good enough to show, and in what form. The script and its results are kept locally (`.claude/local/patchcheck.py`).
- 2026-10-13 — **Where the weather starts by itself** (owner: after signing out and in again the weather had to be given a place again). When no place was picked for the weather on this phone, it is the person's own location (the home saved in their account, else the area chosen on this phone's home screen); for someone with neither, **the first place they watch**, shown under the name they gave it; with none of these, nothing, and the weather page asks. A place picked on the weather page still wins. — People outside the service area mostly use Jaga for a relative's house, and that is the weather they want. Supersedes the order of 9 Oct only by adding the watched place. Not done: a place picked on the weather page is kept on that phone only, not in the account, so it does not follow the person to another browser or phone.
- 2026-10-13 — Every alert in force for a person's places reaches them, with sound, whenever it was issued (owner). The places are the area they chose, their home and the places they watch, together. An alert still in force never drops out of the bell (before, one issued more than 14 days ago did); ended alerts keep the 14-day window. An open app rings for each such alert the phone has not yet shown, including for someone who has only just chosen an area or signed in. Supersedes "Watch is silent": every level an admin can issue rings, Warning and Evacuate at full volume, Watch and Safe to return at half (the half volume is Claude's reading of "all alerts"; overrulable). — A person who starts using Jaga during a flood must learn at once that their tambon is already under a warning. Unchanged limits: no sound before the first tap on the page, and none with Jaga closed until web push (A7).
- 2026-10-13 — **No water is drawn on the map from reported depths this season** (owner, after seeing the check of the same day: about half of what the method would shade was flooded on the radar). The report hexagons with their depth, now visible to everyone at once, stay the only mark of a resident's report, beside the satellite flood layer. To be looked at again after the season, with real reports to test against. — A line that is wrong half the time would be used to plan routes.
- 2026-10-13 — `GISTDA_API_KEY` is on Vercel as a sensitive environment variable (owner). It is the first secret there; the Supabase secret key and the LINE channel secret stay off it as before. Reason: the key is free, read-only and for open data, the route that uses it passes none of it on, and the alternative (fetching inside the database) was half a day of work for no gain in safety. Checked on the live site: the three windows answer and the key is in none of the answers.
- 2026-10-13 — Alert sounds (owner): each level has its own sound and each lasts at least five seconds (Watch a soft chime, Warning a low double-tone, Evacuate a high-low call, Safe to return three notes settling downward; shapes by Claude, not yet chosen by ear). This replaces the single short alert tone and the half volume for Watch. On iPhone the app marks its sound as media so the ring/silent switch does not mute it. The owner also asked that an alert sound regardless of mute and Do Not Disturb: **a web app cannot do that** — no page can raise a volume set to zero or break through Do Not Disturb, and nothing plays with the app closed until web push (A7), which uses the phone's own notification sound and obeys its settings. Breaking through silence needs a native app with Apple's Critical Alerts permission (and the Android equivalent), which is a separate project. — Said plainly so nobody plans on a sound the phone will not make; the screen, the bell and the vibration carry the alert as well. Also fixed: an alert did not ring when the first tap of a visit was on the bell itself, because opening the bell marked it read before the sound could start.
- 2026-10-13 — **A7, first part: a published alert is pushed to phones (web push)**. A phone that has turned notifications on is told when an alert names a tambon it follows (its area, home, or a watched place), with the app closed. Choices made while building: (1) **a visitor can subscribe**, not only an account, because an alert must reach someone who only chose their area; the subscription names nobody and holds the followed tambons. (2) **The addresses are read by the publishing admin's own session** through a logged database function, so Vercel still holds no key that can read the database; the send runs after the admin's page has answered. (3) **Only the browser makers' push services are accepted as addresses** (Chrome/Android, Firefox, Safari/iOS, Edge), so Jaga's server cannot be made to call anything else. (4) **A drill is never pushed.** (5) The card that asks appears on the home screen at every alert level, asks only from a tap, and says the phone decides the sound: a web notification cannot override mute or Do Not Disturb. (6) New free dependency `web-push`; a VAPID key pair per site (`NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`, `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`), which the owner adds to Vercel. — Still to come in A7: pushes for SOS status changes and for the dispatch offer to a team, the count on the icon from a push, LINE, and the quota record.
- 2026-10-13 — The bell's unread number is a white number on a filled red circle, the way other apps show it (owner). It uses the SOS red, the one red the app has; this is a deliberate exception to "red only shows a status", limited to this badge. The number on the installed app's own icon is drawn by the phone or computer and cannot be styled by Jaga. — People read a red dot with a number as "something new" without being taught.
- 2026-10-13 — "Share Jaga" (owner): a card on the home screen and the Account page with one button that opens the phone's own share sheet (copy, LINE, WhatsApp, Messenger...); where a browser has none, the link is copied, or shown to copy by hand.
