It always shares the home page in the reader's language, never the page they are on, and it is hidden, like the install card, while their tambon is at Warning or Evacuate. A link to any Jaga page now carries one preview picture (1200 x 630, slate and teal, logo, the three taglines, the four provinces; `npm run og`, file `public/og/jaga-share-1.png`) and Open Graph tags. The address comes from `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`, else Vercel's live address. — People pass an app on through the chats they already use; one picture for every page means a shared link can never show anybody's data. Nothing about sharing is counted or sent to Jaga.
- 2026-10-13 — **Phone notifications for alerts work on the owner's iPhone ("push works").** The two keys are on Vercel; the card `pushkeys` is closed.
- 2026-10-13 — **A7, second part: notifications about a request for help.** The offer goes to the phones of the team whose turn it is, "nobody has accepted" to the admins' phones, and each step of a case (accepted, on the way, arrived, reported done) to its sender's phone, which they turn on from the request's own page after sending, never before (safety rule 1). How it is sent: the database works out what is owed from the same rows and clock the boards read and hands each thing out once (`push_due`); only Jaga's server may ask, proving itself with a token **derived from the push signing key it already holds**, so no new secret goes to Vercel; the server asks whenever something happens to a case, and the database knocks on the server once a minute (pg_cron), which is what moves an offer to the next team's phone with nobody's app open. A notification names a tambon or a team, never a place or a person. — The cause of these notifications is a clock or a stranger's request, not an admin's action, so they cannot ride on the acting person's rights as an alert's does. A one-minute floor means a later team in the queue can hear up to a minute late; the case is on every covering team's board throughout. Set up per project with `npm run push:gate -- https://<site>`.
- 2026-10-13 — **The flood-risk colours stand, by day and at night ("risk colours OK day and night").** The owner looked at the layer on the live map in daylight and after sunset: pale pink, rose and deep wine-red by day; at night the order of lightness turned round, the highest class the brightest. Nothing changes. — The night shades had gone out without being seen in a browser; this is the check that was owed.
- 2026-10-13 — **Why the owner's test alert came late and silent on the phone, and the fix.** The live site had never sent a push: it held both signing keys but not `CONTACT_EMAIL`, which the sender also required, so every send was skipped without a trace and the alert only appeared when the bell next looked (every five minutes). The sender now falls back to the site's own address as the contact a push service may write to, so a missing variable can no longer stop a notification. — Found from the database: no delivery row and no address read for an alert published after two phones had subscribed.
- 2026-10-13 — **Every new item in the bell is heard once, and an announcement sounds positive** (owner). Two sounds added (eleven in all): `announcement`, a bright rising four-note figure, nothing like an alert or the alarm; and `notice`, two quiet notes for anything else (an alert that has ended, news of one's team). A step of one's own request uses the sounds its own page makes. When several things are new at once one sound plays, and an alert always wins. An announcement is now also pushed to the phones it is for, like an alert. **When a push arrives while Jaga is open, the bell looks again at once** instead of waiting for its five-minute turn. — Still true and said to the owner: a web app cannot sound through a phone's silent mode, Sleep focus or Do Not Disturb; only the phone's owner can let Jaga through, in the phone's own settings.
- 2026-10-13 — Every request carries a journey: a short line of steps showing what is done, what is being waited for now and what comes after (owner). It covers a request for help, a request for food and water, a team's registration and a flood report; it sits on each request's own page and on the Account page, and one page, `/requests` ("My requests"), lists them all, reached from the home screen, the Account page and the bell. A request for help is followed with the key the phone holds, so the page works without an account. The bell now has an item for every step somebody else takes: accepted, set out, arrived, reported done, confirmed, handed back, and a team's registration verified or suspended; a request for food and water is worded as a delivery. Build choices (Claude, overrulable): a step is marked done only when the record says so and a step not yet reached carries no time; a hand-back puts the journey back at "looking for a team"; progress uses the brand colours, never the alert palette; what the person did themselves (sending, cancelling) is not bell news. — People should not have to ask where their request stands, and a registration that waits on an admin's phone call should say so.
- 2026-10-14 — **A7, LINE: an alert also goes out through the LINE Official Account, to the people it concerns only.** Recipients are people who signed in with LINE, whose home or a watched place with notifications on is in the alert's area, and who have not blocked the account; one message per alert, in the person's language, as a Flex bubble with the level in words on the alert palette, the reviewed message, the next-update time and a button into Jaga. A drill is never sent. The LINE ids are read by the publishing admin's own session and logged, as for web push. LINE's webhook (`/api/line/webhook`, signature checked) records who added or blocked the account, and anything written to the account is answered with that person's own area status through the Reply API, which is free. After every send the month's figure is taken from LINE itself and the admins' log gets a warning once at 70 % and once at 90 %. — The account is on LINE's free plan: **500 messages a month**, 2 followers today, so nothing is spent on a broadcast and nothing is paid for. Choices of mine: (1) **built as routes of the web app, not Supabase Edge Functions** as the stack note says, because this laptop deploys the app by pushing to GitHub and has no way to deploy functions; the two LINE secrets therefore go to Vercel, beside the push keys. (2) Someone who signed in with LINE is taken to be a friend until LINE's webhook says they blocked the account, because LINE Login adds the friend at sign-in and LINE lists followers only for verified accounts. (3) When the month's quota is spent LINE refuses the rest; the alert still goes by web push, the app and the bell, and the shortfall is recorded beside the alert. Not built yet: the rich menu, the rescue-confirmation button, LINE for a team's offer, and the LINE figures in the alert review step.
- 2026-10-14 — Smaller A7 pieces: the war room's "assign" now tells the sender's phone at once instead of at the next minute's tick, and a push puts a plain mark on the app icon where the system allows it (the real count replaces it when Jaga opens).
- 2026-10-13 — **A guide for letting Jaga through Do Not Disturb and Sleep** (owner). A web app cannot override either, so the notifications card now carries a folded, animated guide (a drawn phone plus the same four steps in words) showing where the phone's own list of allowed apps is: iPhone and iPad via Settings, Focus; Android via Settings, Notifications, Do Not Disturb. It is on the home screen, the Account page, a request's page and the teams' board, on phones only; right after notifications are turned on the card stays and the guide opens by itself. It says plainly that only the person can set this and that a notification let through still follows the phone's volume and silent switch. The menu names (Thai, Malay, English) are written from memory and Android's differ by brand: to be checked on real phones.
- 2026-10-13 — **The dam's downstream zone is built from the elevation model, anchored on the highest levels the river gauges have recorded (S7 step 3).** Land no higher above the river than the river has been seen to rise at Bannang Sata, Yala, Ban Brido and Pattani town (2.5, 4.5, 1.3 and 1.2 m above the model's river surface), within 6 km of the channel and joined to it; an outer band one metre higher. 224 km², about 36,500 buildings, 51 tambons (the OpenStreetMap path lists 39). Nothing in the app changes until the owner has seen both and chosen (decision of 10 Oct). — One uniform rise fitted neither the incised river at Yala nor the flat coast; the gauges say how high the river really gets at each. A level-pool reading, not a hydraulic model: it says which land is low enough, not how much water a release brings.
- 2026-10-13 — **The outside check of Bang Lang's figures for the 2024 and 2025 floods does not close the question.** Satellite radar confirms the reservoir was filling through both floods, as the record says. The irrigation department's gauge 18.6 km below the dam (X.77) shows about three times more water per km² from the 291 km² below the dam than the dam reports arriving from the 1,992 km² above it, in both floods; that gauge's rating moves by a factor of two between years, so it cannot say whether the cause is rain, the gauge or the dam. Recorded as open; the rainfall S5 collects is the next evidence. — The owner doubted the operator's record (12 Oct); it is neither confirmed nor contradicted, and the app must keep saying that the dam's figures are the operator's own.
- 2026-10-14 — **S5 started without the owner's event table: the flood timeline is filled from Thai news and the irrigation department's flood summaries.** The owner was not in the area during either flood and old news is hard to find (13 Oct). `pipeline/data/event_timeline.csv` (private repo) has 39 rows, 13 of them resting on a dated, specific statement; nearly all the usable dates are the department's over-bank periods and peaks per station, not news. The hindcast therefore defines a flood from the gauges themselves (the key gauge at or above its bank for three hours) and prints the timeline beside it. — A table the owner cannot fill would have held S5 up for nothing, and the gauges give onset to the hour where the news gives a day at best.
- 2026-10-14 — **First provisional thresholds (S5), and how they are chosen.** Three zones (Pattani River at Yala X.40A, Kolok at Sungai Kolok X.119A, Khlong U-Taphao at Hat Yai X.44). Signals: basin rain over 24 and 72 hours (GSMaP on Earth Engine), the levels at the key and the upstream gauge, GloFAS discharge (reported, not used for a threshold). Per level the candidate with the best critical success index is kept (floods caught against false alarms), Watch from 72-hour rain, Warning from a river level or 24-hour rain, Evacuate the key gauge at its bank. Result: Pattani Watch 180 mm in 72 h (4 of 7 floods, 3 false alarms, median lead 15.5 h), Warning X.40A at 16.0 m (7 of 7, 4 false, 6 h); Kolok Watch 100 mm (12 of 14, 8 false, 42 h), Warning X.119A at 8.8 m (14 of 14, 3 false, 7 h); Hat Yai Watch 220 mm, Warning 150 mm in 24 h, on one flood only. All `provisional: true`; they suggest a level to an admin and never publish one. — "Catch every flood" was tried first and gave a Watch that would stand most of the season. Six seasons of gauges and no held-out test: these are the best case, to be revised after the season.
- 2026-10-14 — LINE alerts are switched on for the live site (owner): the Official Account's two keys are in Vercel, its webhook points at `/api/line/webhook` and is active, and the owner's test alert arrived on LINE beside the phone notification. A first test failed because the access token had been pasted cut short; a signed, empty delivery to the webhook now also says whether LINE accepts the server's token, so a wrong one can be found without sending anything. When the site's address changes, the webhook has to be pointed at the new one.
- 2026-10-14 — **The owner accepts the first provisional thresholds for now ("thresholds OK for now").** They go to the admins' signal dashboard (A8) as they stand, marked provisional, and are revised after the 2026 season. — The owner is the hydrologist; six seasons of gauges cannot carry more than a starting value.
- 2026-10-14 — **A LINE alert says "your area", not a list of tambons** (owner, after seeing the first real one). A LINE message goes to one person about their own places, so the reviewed template is filled with "your area" where the app lists the alert's tambons; nothing else of the reviewed text changes. A small line under it says how many tambons the alert covers and points into Jaga, so the size of the area is still stated. An alert not made from a template (the dam release notice) keeps its stored words. The app, the bell and the phone notification are unchanged.
- 2026-10-14 — Rescues a team finds by itself can be recorded and credited (owner). A verified team records one from its board, at the place and at the moment: the phone's own position (no pin by hand; it must be minutes old at most), a phone number for the rescued person or a relative, and a photo are all required. It is worth **half** the points of a rescue that came through an SOS, never as much (the setting cannot be turned to 100%). It counts at once only when the place gives reason to expect a flood - an alert in force in that tambon, a standing flood report within 2 km, a request for help within 1 km in the last three days, or a tambon the hazard map calls flood-prone - and nothing looks repeated. Otherwise it waits for an admin, who approves it or refuses it with a reason; an admin can also take back one that was counted. — The people a boat crew finds have no phone, no signal or have never heard of Jaga; that work was invisible. Nobody on the other side can confirm it, so the proof is taken on the spot and anything unusual goes to a person.
- 2026-10-14 — Field rescues, build choices (Claude, overrulable). Held for an admin when: no sign of flooding there; the team recorded another within 200 m in 24 hours; more than 10 in a day; the phone number was used on another record in 30 days; the position is vaguer than 200 m; the place is outside the team's coverage; or an SOS is still open within 300 m (that rescue belongs to that request). "Flood-prone" is judged per tambon (High + Medium + Low of the hazard map at 20% or more), because the hazard map itself is not in the database point by point. All thresholds are in `system_settings.field_rescues`. The rescued person has no name in the record; the position and photo are readable by the recording team and admins only; the number by no client at all - an admin asks for it and each look is logged. The team sees the outcome as a journey on its records and in the bell. Not built: recording offline and sending later (the rule "at the moment" needs the server's clock), and showing these separately on the impact page. — Each threshold is a guess to be tuned once real records exist.
- 2026-10-14 — **An alert names the reader's own place, not its whole list of tambons** (owner). An alert published from a template now keeps the template's words with their `{area}` beside the filled-in ones (`template_th|ms|en` in `messages`; alerts already published were given them). The home screen and the map card put that one tambon's name there, a watched place its own tambon; the bell, the phone notification and the LINE reply say "your area". Nothing else of the reviewed text changes, the filled-in words with the full area stay on the record and on the admin screens, and an alert with no template (the dam release notice) is shown as stored.
- 2026-10-14 — **The dam's release zone is the elevation-model zone with its outer band (owner: "use the zone with the outer band").** It replaces the OpenStreetMap river line plus side-stream reaches as the dam's geometry (source `s7`; the river's own line stays OpenStreetMap's). A release notice goes to 55 tambons instead of 39; the map shades the land the river has been high enough to reach and outlines the band one metre higher; a tambon off the channel takes the arrival times of the nearest point of the river. La-Ae leaves the list. Supersedes the decision of 10 Oct to keep the OpenStreetMap path until both could be compared. — Missing someone costs more than warning someone who stays dry, the elevation model is only good to about a metre on the plain, and Pattani town lies in the outer band. The wording for such tambons (Thai and Malay drafted by Claude) says the land is low and joined to the river, never that water will come.
- 2026-10-14 — A seasonal outlook is added to the plan, and its first step is built (owner asked whether Jaga can forecast a heavy-flood season; science plan Module 9, prompt S8, `pipeline/seasonal_outlook`). What it will be: odds for a drier, normal or wetter November–January season, with its past hit rate beside it, for the admins first; never a flood forecast, because the bursts that flood towns cannot be foreseen more than one to two weeks ahead, and a dry-leaning outlook never lowers readiness or reads as an all-clear. What the record of 45 seasons says so far (CHIRPS rain, NOAA ENSO index; one rainfall record only, to be checked against a second): the two wettest five-day bursts are 2025 and 2024, both after a neutral Pacific; El Niño seasons were in the dry third 5 times in 9 and in the wet third once; July–September 2026 is a strong El Niño (+2.16), so the season total leans drier than normal, on thin evidence; season totals rose about 14% a decade in this record while the worst burst shows no trend. Nothing of it is shown in the app until the forecast models have been scored against past seasons and the owner has decided the wording.
- 2026-10-14 — **A8, the rest: forecast rain, modelled flow and tide per zone, the admins' signal dashboard, and changing a threshold from it (owner: "build them now, use your judgement").** Choices made without the owner, all overrulable. (1) The three feeds are Open-Meteo's (weather forecast, flood API for GloFAS, marine API for sea level), fetched hourly by a database job; free, no key, no account. **Tide: ThaiWater's own tide data was not evaluated first, as the phase prompt asked; Open-Meteo Marine was taken because it answered at once.** That evaluation is still owed. (2) Live rain on the dashboard is a weather model's, while the thresholds were set on satellite rain (GSMaP), which the database cannot fetch; the screen says the two are not the same measurement. (3) The suggestion's rules: key gauge at its bank = Evacuate; at its Warning level, or the upstream gauge over its bank = Warning; rain fallen over a threshold = that threshold's level; rain only forecast = never more than Watch; a gauge silent for six hours gives no reason either way and is named as a blind spot. (4) The suggested level is written in words in an outlined box, never in an alert badge or colour, with a link to the alert console; nothing is published from the page. (5) A threshold an admin sets stops being provisional, survives a rerun of the hindcast and is written to the access log. — An alert badge means a person issued that level; a suggestion must not look like one (safety rule 2). A forecast is not an observation (rule 8). Found on the way: the dam-release form printed a two-day arrival as "0.5–2 hours" (days shown under an hours label) once the zone's furthest tambon passed 48 hours; fixed.
- 2026-10-14 — **A8, first slice: the river gauges are fetched every 15 minutes by a database job, and the zones and provisional thresholds are loaded.** `gauges_fetch()` (pg_cron, like the dam; no Edge Function, no secret, no cost) asks ThaiWater for the latest reading of every gauge in the four provinces and stores level and flow in `observations`; 48 gauges, three warning zones (the catchments) and the seven provisional thresholds come from the generated seed `46_zones_gauges_thresholds.sql`. On the public map a gauge's `warning_m` is its bank and `watch_m`, on a zone's key gauge only, is the hindcast's Warning level; the wording there is about the gauge, not an alert. The owner also asked that the dam's zone be clearer over the flood-risk colours: it is hatched with a cased outline. — The prompt named Edge Functions; the dam's job has run on pg_cron without a miss and needs nothing deployed. The feed is not real time: at night the irrigation department's gauges were six to nine hours behind, and the map says "no data in the last 6 hours" for them rather than showing an old level as current.
- 2026-10-14 — **The automated alarm: admins first, public if nobody acts (owner's choice; narrows safety rule 2).** An admin ticks a river gauge (or every gauge in a warning zone at once, from a button at the top of the signal dashboard). When the gauge's two latest readings, both under 3 hours old, reach the zone's Warning or Evacuate level, every admin's phone rings with a draft built from the reviewed template, the zone's tambons and the readings. If no admin sends an alert for that zone or dismisses the draft within 20 minutes, Jaga publishes the draft itself, marked as not reviewed and logged (issuer: the admin who ticked the gauge, else the super admin). If the river is back below the level by then, nothing is published. A higher level replaces a waiting lower one; one auto-publication per zone and level per 12 hours. Before launch it publishes drills only; an admin switches it to real alerts. Also: "Write an alert" on the dashboard opens the alert form with the suggested level's template, the zone's tambons and the readings already filled in, and the dashboard has a map of every gauge, coloured by its own reading against its own levels, with the tick in each gauge's panel. Claude's choices, overrulable: the 20 minutes, the two readings, the 3 hours, the 12 hours, and that rain and forecasts never trigger it. — The owner sleeps in another time zone and may be the only admin awake in a season; a gauge over its level with nobody at the screen should still reach the people downstream. The two readings and the drop-back check keep one wrong reading from publishing anything.
- 2026-10-14 — Field rescues, the three things left open are built (owner: "build them"). (1) **No signal:** the phone keeps the record - position, the time it was taken, number, photo, all taken at the place - and sends it when the signal returns, from whichever page of Jaga is open. A record arriving more than 15 minutes after its position was taken is accepted for 24 hours and **always** waits for an admin (hold `sent_later`), because the server cannot vouch for a time it did not see; older than a day it is refused. The rescued person's number stays on that phone until the record is sent, then it is removed. (2) **Admins are told:** a held record reaches the admins' phones once (the team and the tambon, never the place or the person), and their bell shows how many are waiting. The admins' push addresses are handed only to Jaga's own server, never to a team's session. (3) **Impact page:** rescues teams found themselves are a line of their own - counted, people, teams, waiting, refused - and are never added to the requests Jaga carried. The page still shows made-up figures. — The crews that find people are the ones without coverage; and a figure reported by the teams themselves must not be mixed into the figures the app can vouch for.
- 2026-10-14 — The admin console's pages are grouped (owner: the row of links had grown too long). Four groups, each a button that opens its pages: **Watch the situation** (war room, signals, impact), **Warn people** (alerts, dam release, announcements), **Needs your review** (verify units, field rescues, reports, duplicates, safe places) and **Records and team** (households, points, handover, exports, access log, admins for the super admin). The review group shows how many units, field rescues and reports are waiting; the admin home lists the same groups with a line on what each is for. Households sit under records, not review: an admin reads that list but approves nothing in it. The open panel stays under the hotline bar. — One list in `lib/admin-menu.ts` feeds the menu and the home page, and a test fails if a new admin page is in no group.
- 2026-10-14 — Seasonal outlook, steps 1 to 4 done (owner: "Do those 4 in order"; figures in `pipeline/seasonal_outlook/METHODS.md`). (1) ERA5-Land confirms what CHIRPS showed: 2025 and 2024 hold the two wettest five-day bursts of 45 seasons; the Pacific explains about a fifth of the differences in season totals; totals have risen 9–14% a decade; a rise in the worst bursts is not shown. (2) NOAA's free NMME hindcasts (1991–2020) score close to chance here: the best hit rates are 43–53% against about 33% for chance, and this year the multi-model mean and three models forecast more rain than in any of their past runs, during a strong El Niño, so their lean is not used. (3) The outlook for season 2026, for the admins only (table `seasonal_outlooks`): **no reliable lean** — the Pacific alone leans dry (odds 50/33/17), the one model that passed leans normal; "prepare as for any season". A method is used only if it beat both "always normal" and the Pacific alone on both rainfall records and this year's forecast lies inside its own past range; a lean is shown only when every usable method agrees. (4) A public version is proposed, not built (Claude's recommendation: admins only this season, score the outlook in February, decide again before 2027). Not used: IRI's data library and Copernicus SEAS5, which now need an account (owner's call).
- 2026-10-14 — The ThaiWater rain archive's `rain_daily` is not daily rain (found while building the outlook; Claude's reading from the numbers, not yet confirmed with ThaiWater). Values from `rain7d_graph` behave as running multi-day totals: day-to-day correlation 0.84, 6% dry days, monthly sums several times CHIRPS and ERA5-Land. Nothing in the pipeline uses it yet; it must not be used as daily rain (for the S5 thresholds or anything else) until what the endpoint returns is established.
- 2026-10-14 — **A7, the rest of LINE** (owner: "build all those"). (1) **The offer to a team reaches its LINE too**, beside the phone notification: a tambon, the kind of request and a button onto the board, never the place or the person. (2) **The sender confirms from LINE** (spec 5.4): once the team reports it done, the sender gets a question with two buttons, "yes, I'm safe" and "still need help" ("received" and "not received" for food and water). A press comes back through LINE's signed webhook; Jaga checks the LINE account that pressed it is the one that sent the request and then acts as the sender, through the very functions the app's own two buttons call, so the case closes, or goes back to looking for a team, exactly as in the app. (3) **A menu under the LINE chat**: "my area status" (answered in the chat, free), "safe places near me" (Jaga's home screen) and "ask for help" (Jaga's SOS screen - it opens the screen and sends nothing by itself), drawn and published by `scripts/line-richmenu.mjs`. (4) **The month's LINE figures on the admins' Alerts page**: quiet with room, a warning from 70 %, and a plain statement when the month is spent that alerts still reach people by phone notification, the app and the bell. The review step already showed the LINE reach and the quota after the send (A6); it now reads LINE's real count. — Each request message costs one of the month's 500, like an alert; with today's 2 followers that is nothing, and the quota line is where it will show first.
- 2026-10-14 — The season outlook is public (owner: "outlook public"; Claude had recommended admins only this season, because the skill is close to chance and a "less rain than usual" line could read as an all-clear). Built as proposed: a closed "Season outlook" fold under the map on the map page only — never on the home screen, in alerts, in notifications or beside an alert level; plain styling with no alert or status colour. Every showing says the lean (this season: no clear lean), what points that way (the Pacific), how often such outlooks were right here ("about 5 times in 10"), that the worst floods come from a few days of rain that cannot be foreseen this far ahead ("prepare as for any year"), and that alerts come from Jaga's admins and DDPM, not from the outlook. Visitors read it through `public_seasonal_outlook()`; the table stays admins only. Shown until the end of January of its season. Thai and Malay wording are Claude's drafts.
- 2026-10-14 — The public season outlook keeps quoting the skill of the best usable method ("about 5 times in 10", NCAR CCSM4), not the lower figure of the Pacific alone (about 4 in 10) (owner: "I won't quote the lower one").
- 2026-10-14 — **River gauges on the public map, live and animated (owner: "cool and clear on the flood-risk layer"; A8 item 4).** Each gauge is a small glass of water: how full says where the river stands against its bank, the water sways, a gauge near its bank sends out a slow ripple and one over it a quick double ripple, a chevron shows a rise or fall of 5 cm or more in about three hours, and a gauge silent for six hours is an empty dashed glass. Drawn in the map's water colour (the dam river's), never an alert colour, and the state is always written too ("near bank", "over bank" beside the gauge; the card adds level, bank, rise and "a measurement, not an alert"). Gauges now show on the risk view as well as the live view, and the map asks for them again every 5 minutes (a small route of their own, cached 60 s). No motion under reduced motion. The public words no longer say "watch/warning level" for a gauge (they read like alert levels): "below / close to / at or over its bank". Claude's choices, overrulable: the water colour rather than the alert hues, the 5 cm chevron, the 5-minute poll. — A reading is a measurement, not a level a person issued (safety rule 2), and the river must be readable at a glance on the hazard colours.
- 2026-10-14 — **Watch, Warning and Evacuate for every river gauge (owner asked Claude to suggest them; admins can change them).** Evacuate = the listed bank; Warning and Watch scored against every time the gauge went over its bank since 2017 (at least 6 h and 24 h median lead), where at least three floods exist; otherwise set from how fast that river rises (95th percentile of 6 h and 24 h rises). A level may be exceeded at most 3 % (Warning) / 6 % (Watch) of the record's hours, which stops tidal gauges from getting levels the sea reaches every day. Key gauges keep the zone levels the owner accepted and gain a level-based Watch; saving a key gauge's levels changes its zone's threshold too (one value). Stored as provisional in `gauge_levels` (46 gauges), shown and set in the admins' gauge-map panel, every change logged; an admin's level is never overwritten by a re-seed. The automated alarm uses a non-key gauge's own Evacuate level (else its bank) to raise Warning. Nine gauges are flagged because the river stands within 0.3 m of their listed bank more than a tenth of the time; two never reached it. — Most levels (32 of 46) rest on rise rates, not on scored floods: they are informed placeholders, and the flagged gauges need someone who knows them.
- 2026-10-14 — **The zone's threshold boxes hold every gauge's levels (owner: fill the boxes in the Zones view with the suggested values).** Each zone's box now has rain thresholds plus one Watch/Warning/Evacuate box per gauge in the zone, prefilled. The key and upstream gauges' boxes are the zone's `key_level_m` and `upstream_level_m` thresholds: one value, saved from either view. Two effects on the suggestion, my reading, overrulable: the key gauge at its Watch level now suggests Watch, and the upstream gauge suggests Warning at its own Warning level (X.77: 38.15 m) rather than only at its bank (39.7 m). — The owner wants to see and change the levels where the zone's thresholds are, not only on the map.
- 2026-10-14 — The public map's marks answer in place (owner: only their own places showed anything on hover or tap). A reservoir, a river gauge and a safe place now open a small pop-up where they are drawn - on hover with a mouse, on tap on a phone - saying how each stands: the reservoir's state, storage, outflow and the hour of its figures with "not an alert"; the gauge's state against its bank, level, three-hour change and reading time; the safe place's type, whether it is open, and the same Navigate chooser as the home screen. Each has "See all details", which opens the card under the map. A click or tap pins the pop-up until a tap elsewhere or Escape, and the map moves just enough to show a pinned pop-up whole. A tap opens the mark exactly under the finger before looking around it, so in a cluster it opens the mark it landed on. New button "My places" beside "All 4 provinces" frames the person's home and watched places (or the area chosen on the phone) and turns their pins on. — The card under the map was out of sight on a phone, so tapping a mark looked like nothing happened.
- 2026-10-14 — **Five more reservoirs get the Bang Lang treatment** (owner). ThaiWater serves five Royal Irrigation Department reservoirs in the four provinces and all five are now in Jaga: Khlong Sadao (57 Mm3, above Hat Yai), Khlong La (21), Khlong Chamrai (6), Klai Ban (3.7, Narathiwat) and Ban Baik (1.2, Yala). They are on the map with their live figures, their state chip, their own grade rules and their card; Khlong Sadao and Khlong La also have a release path, the tambons on it and a release notice an admin can send. — The owner asked for every reservoir that matters to the flood; Khlong Sadao's channel runs 123 km through Hat Yai, which is the benchmark flood of November 2025.
- 2026-10-14 — **A reading says how long it covers, and each dam is graded by its own rules.** The RID reservoirs publish once a day and have no history endpoint at all, so `dam_readings.window_h` carries the window (24 for them, 1 for Bang Lang) and every flow, mean and chart divides by it; `dams.grades` holds each dam's own thresholds, merged over the shared defaults. — A day's volume read as an hour's would make a release look twenty-four times smaller, and Bang Lang's 160 m3/s of turbines would never let a 6 Mm3 reservoir count as releasing.
- 2026-10-14 — **The RID reservoirs' grade thresholds are proportions of their own capacity, and provisional**: releasing at a tenth of the reservoir released in a day, watchful at a fifth arriving or a twentieth of rise; one reading grades, and the figures go stale after 48 hours. The reason code is `release_high` ("the release is high for the size of this reservoir"), never "above the turbines", because they have no power station. — They have no archive to measure a percentile against, so a stated proportion is the only honest scale; revisit after a season of readings (pipeline/dam_release/METHODS.md).
- 2026-10-14 — **A reservoir with no mapped channel below it shows its figures and nothing else** (Khlong Chamrai, Klai Ban, Ban Baik): no path, no zone, no release notice, and the screens say OpenStreetMap has no channel there. — Drawing a guessed line below a reservoir would be drawing a guessed warning zone (safety rule 8).
- 2026-10-14 — **The home screen carries only a reservoir that can really reach the person** (owner): their own area or one of their watched places is on that reservoir's own path. This supersedes the rule of 12 October, where everyone in Yala, Pattani and Narathiwat saw Bang Lang. Where two reservoirs reach the same place the larger one is shown, and the map's legend lists all six. — The owner's words: the ones which are not relevant need not be on the home page.
- 2026-10-14 — **Tide stays on Open-Meteo Marine; ThaiWater was evaluated and has none to give.** ThaiWater's public API has a sea-level service (`/analyst/sea_waterlevel_load`, `/analyst/sea_waterlevel_graph`) but it lists no agency and no station and answers empty, today and for Nov 2024; its station list for the four provinces has rain and river gauges only. Open-Meteo Marine at the Pattani mouth was checked against the tidal gauges in Pattani town (X.10A, BLGTD05), hourly, with the 48-hour mean taken out: correlation 0.89–0.93 in the dry season of 2026 and 0.88–0.89 in the floods of 2024 and 2025, at a lag of 0–1 hour, and the same daily range (about 0.5 m). Its level reads about 0.7 m above the gauges' m MSL, so the dashboard says to read its rise and fall, not its figure; nothing in the suggestion uses its level. — Closes the evaluation the A8 prompt asked for; a datum offset is a known property of a global model, not a reason to drop it.
- 2026-10-14 — **The signal dashboard reads itself again every 5 minutes while it is open**, except while an admin is typing into a form on it, and catches up when the tab comes back. — Gauges are fetched every 15 minutes; a console left open on a wall must not show the river as it was an hour ago, and a half-typed threshold must not be lost.

- 2026-10-14 — **Admins can view the app as another role** (owner: to see each role's experience). Chosen by the owner: a preview with made-up data, not a switch into real accounts - a pretend team in the real dispatch could hold a real SOS for 60 s. A fifth group in the admin menu, "View as another role", opens /admin/preview/visitor, resident (home in four states, and the map), rescue team (the console with an offer ringing, answering taps by itself), food and water team (supply requests only), team waiting for verification (the console's "not verified yet" and the registration's steps), and dam operator. The dam operator's own screen was not built; the owner chose to draft it now as a preview: the operator's one dam, when the gates open, how much, until when, the reviewed template, and a send button that sends nothing. It follows the decision of 9 Oct (the operator sends the notice to the fixed zone; admins are told at once and can extend or cancel), which my question that day misdescribed as admins reviewing first. Nobody can register as an operator until a contact at the dam agrees. Only admins can open the previews; they read no one's data. — Real test accounts would show live data with each role's real limits, but would need test teams kept out of dispatch first.
- 2026-10-14 — **The tide feed stays Open-Meteo Marine, now checked (the evaluation A8 owed).** ThaiWater's public API has no tide or surge series (its wave and storm items are pictures), so the model was checked against the river gauges the tide reaches, 2022 to 2026: at Pattani town (X.10A, BLGTD05) it matches in phase within an hour and in range within 7%; inside Songkhla lake the tide is a third to a half of the sea's and up to three hours late; the Kolok has no tidal gauge, so Tak Bai is unverified. — There was nothing in ThaiWater's public data to prefer, and the model holds where it can be tested. Method and table in pipeline/thresholds/METHODS.md.
- 2026-10-14 — **An admins-only usage dashboard, fed by Jaga's own counter** (owner's request: who uses the app now, on what device, from where, their watched places, current and accumulated figures, a map, animated). Four choices, all as I recommended: **Jaga's own counter** (no analytics service, no server logs; the open note goes through `/api/usage`, which adds the rough city Vercel reads from the internet address and passes it on with the push gate token, while page changes and a five-minute "still on screen" go from the phone straight to `usage_ping`, so a busy night costs no Vercel calls a minute); **location = the tambon chosen in the app plus that rough city, cut to 0.1 degree**, never GPS and never the address itself; **a random install code kept 90 days**, never joined to an account, name or number, after which only day totals stay (`usage_daily`, nightly `jaga-usage-rollup`); **watched places and homes as counts per tambon**, as in the war room. This replaces the 9 Oct "device hash salted per day" for the admin console only; `/impact` stays at province and day. My own calls, overrulable: a browser sending Global Privacy Control and a test browser are not counted; "on screen now" means a note in the last 6 minutes; the page's notes ("what the figures say") speak only from 10 phones up; the home screen's line "the area you choose is kept on this phone only" now adds that Jaga counts how many phones chose each tambon, never whose, because the old line would have been untrue. — The owner wants to see whether Jaga is used and helps; counts by tambon and city answer that without following any one person, and nothing paid or new is needed.
- 2026-10-14 — **Forecast rain was scored against the 2024 and 2025 floods; the live rule is unchanged until the owner chooses.** Open-Meteo's archive of past forecasts shows every model far too dry before the floods (2 to 40 times less than the satellite measured), so "the next 72 hours hold the Watch threshold" caught 1 of 6 floods. Open-Meteo's default model here behaves like ICON, the driest. The wettest of five models at 150 mm in 72 hours would have warned about two days ahead for Pattani and Hat Yai with one or two false alarms a season, but caught 1 of 3 on the Kolok. — Six floods are too few to pick a model and a figure without the owner; method and tables in pipeline/thresholds/METHODS.md.
- 2026-10-14 — **The usage dashboard is approved as built** (owner: "usage OK"), including the counting of the chosen tambon, the reworded line under the home screen's area picker, and the Thai and Malay on the page. — The owner looked at it and asked for no change.
- 2026-10-14 — **Forecast rain counts as the wettest of five models, Watch at 150 mm in the next 72 hours (owner's choice, from three options).** The hourly job asks Open-Meteo for ECMWF IFS, GFS, ICON, JMA and the default model in one request; the wettest each hour is the forecast rule's series, the default stays the chart's. A new threshold, forecast rain in 72 hours, Watch only, 150 mm in every zone, provisional. On the 2024 and 2025 floods this would have warned about two days ahead in Pattani and Hat Yai with one or two false alarms a season, and caught 1 of 3 Kolok floods. — Every model was far too dry before the floods; taking the wettest is what lets a forecast warn at all. Revisit after this season.
- 2026-10-14 — **A pop-up on the map is never taller than the map** (owner: a gauge's box was cut off by the map window). Three things together: the box is capped at the map's own height less its tip and a little air, and what does not fit scrolls inside it, with the scroll held in the box; the pop-up's own line about not being an alert is now the short one (`map.gauge.notAlertShort`, `dam.disclaimer.notAnAlertShort`), with the full sentence a tap away on the card; and the pop-up is drawn above the map's corner buttons, which were covering the gauge's own name. Measured on a 360 px phone: a gauge's box went from 313 px with the map at 318–384 px to a box that always fits, with "See all details" inside it. — A box bigger than its frame cannot be brought inside by moving the map, and the figures a person taps for must all be readable.
- 2026-10-14 — **Opening a card takes the page to it** (owner: clicking a reservoir in the legend opened the card but left the screen where it was). The card sits under the map and above the legend, so a button in the legend opened it off the top of the screen. Everything that opens a card — the legend's reservoir buttons and "See all details" in a pop-up — now scrolls the card under the frozen header (the header's height is measured, because it is taller than the page's scroll padding on a phone), gives it the keyboard, rings it once in brand teal, and corrects itself 700 ms later when the card has finished filling. A pinned pop-up is closed when the map is sent somewhere else, so it cannot end up pointing off the frame. — The owner clicked and nothing seemed to happen.
- 2026-10-14 — **The reservoir's report opens with an instrument panel** (owner: "for the dam information I want an awe experience to the user who knows the technical"). A slate panel at the head of the card: a dial of how full the reservoir is with normal high water marked on it, then storage, level, inflow, outflow and the change in storage, each with its unit and its datum. Three calls of mine inside it: the fullness is given against **both** denominators, the maximum and the capacity at normal high water that Thai bulletins quote (this settles the open question of 10 Oct by naming both rather than choosing); where the newest hour has no storage — which is how Bang Lang's feed arrived on 14 Oct — the panel falls back to the newest figure of the day and says which day, rather than leaving its best cells empty; and an hourly dam's flows carry the day's mean beside the hour, because the hour alone can say the opposite of what the day did. The panel is brand slate and teal, which never carry a status: the state stays in the chip, and the card still says these are the dam's own figures and not a level Jaga issued. The home screen is unchanged — it stays light enough for slow 3G. — Figures with their units, their denominators and their provenance are what a reader who knows water needs; decoration is not.
- 2026-10-14 — **The season outlook's Thai is approved, and S8 is done for 2026** (owner: "The Thai lines are fine"). The reruns after NOAA's November and December forecasts (about the 8th of each month) and the scoring in February 2027 stay on the tracker's Reminders tab. — They need data that does not exist yet.
- 2026-10-14 — **The Malay screens are written in Rumi (Latin letters), and Gemini reviews them in place of a Patani Malay reviewer for now** (owner: no reviewer available). Through Antigravity CLI on the owner's Google AI Pro, run by Claude from an empty scratch folder with message text only. Each batch is rewritten (standard Malay spelling, words Patani Malay speakers use, never softening a warning), then translated back into Thai by a fresh run that never sees the Thai, and compared with the Thai source; the owner decides on the lines whose meaning drifted and on every alert-level, evacuate and hotline line. First round: home, alert levels and checklists, SOS, safe places, hotlines, offline page and the four alert templates (about 340 lines); team and admin screens stay Thai and English. This supersedes the 10 Oct fence that Gemini does not replace the Patani Malay reviewer; a local reader for the five critical screens stays recommended, not required. Settles spec open item 1. — Launch is 11 days away and nobody is available; the blind back-translation catches wrong meaning, which is the risk that matters most in safety text, though not unnatural wording.
- 2026-10-14 — **The five RID reservoirs are approved as built** (owner: "reservoirs OK"). This accepts them as they stand: all six reservoirs on the map with their figures, state, card and report; the home screen carrying only a reservoir whose path reaches the reader's own area or a watched place; a reservoir with no mapped channel showing its figures and saying so rather than drawing a guessed line; the daily feed read as a day (`window_h`); and the Thai, Malay and English wording of the new lines, which were my drafts. Three things of mine the owner did not overrule and which stay open to a later change: the five reservoirs' grade thresholds are provisional proportions of each reservoir's own capacity (they have no published history at all, so Jaga's record of them starts now — revisit after this season); the operator reads "RID" rather than กรมชลประทาน; and none of the five has arrival times, because there are no gauges below them to measure travel against. — The owner looked at them and asked for no change.
- 2026-10-14 — **The map's satellite background is Esri World Imagery** (owner, when asked how: their own free key). Esri serves it only with a key from an ArcGIS Location Platform account; the free allowance is 2 million tiles a month and pay-as-you-go stays off, so nothing can bill. The key goes on Vercel as `NEXT_PUBLIC_ARCGIS_API_KEY`: a browser key by Esri's design, restricted in Esri's dashboard to Jaga's own addresses and to basemaps. Until the key is set, and for the rest of a visit once Esri refuses four tiles (allowance used up, key refused), the map shows EOX's Sentinel-2 picture as before, and the note under the map says which picture it is. The credit line is Esri's own ("Powered by Esri | Esri, Maxar, Earthstar Geographics, and the GIS User Community"). Not chosen: Esri's old keyless address, which still answers but is outside clear terms and could be cut off mid-flood. — Esri's picture is under a metre in towns, so single houses show, where EOX's 10 m picture cannot; satellite is not the default view, so the allowance goes only to people who choose it.
- 2026-10-14 — **A screen names the agency in full, in the reader's own language, with the short form in brackets** (owner): กรมชลประทาน (RID) in Thai, Royal Irrigation Department (RID) in English, Jabatan Pengairan Diraja (RID) in Malay — my Malay draft. The database still stores the code (`dams.operator`), and `operatorName()` in lib/dam.ts turns it into words at the moment of writing, so the three copies of a release notice each name the agency their own way. An operator Jaga has no name for keeps its code, which is why Bang Lang still reads EGAT — the owner asked only about the RID, and EGAT's full name can follow the same way in one line if they want it. This supersedes the note of 14 Oct that the operator is shown as "RID". — "RID" alone says nothing to a reader who has not met it.
- 2026-10-14 — **Jaga's address is jagaapp.org, and the app moves onto it now rather than at A10** (owner bought it at Cloudflare and asked for every step the new domain needs). Tiles are already served from `tiles.jagaapp.org` (R2 custom domain; checked: range reads 206, CORS, the second read a cache hit). The app follows: `jagaapp.org` on Vercel with `www` redirected to it, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_TILES_BASE_URL` set there, Supabase's Site URL moved to it, the push gate, LINE webhook and LINE menu pointed at it; `jaga-neon.vercel.app` keeps working until launch, and the r2.dev address is switched off once every app reads the tile domain. One of my findings: Cloudflare's default Browser Cache TTL (4 hours) overrode the manifest's 5 minutes, so the zone is set to respect the files' own headers. — Eleven days before launch, moving now leaves time to test sign-in, push and LINE on the address people will actually be given; a phone that installed from the old address has to add Jaga again, which costs little while almost nobody uses it.
- 2026-10-14 — **The critical Malay screens and the four alert templates were reviewed with Gemini and the result is in the app.** 337 message lines (home, alert levels and checklists, safe places, hotlines, offline page, SOS and food-and-water requests) and the 12 Malay fields of the flood templates went through Antigravity CLI (Gemini 3.8 Flash), run from an empty scratch folder with tool use refused: a rewrite pass, then a fresh pass translating the new Malay back into Thai without seeing the Thai, then Claude comparing that with the Thai source. Of 91 lines Gemini changed or Claude reopened, 78 rewrites were taken as proposed, 6 were corrected by Claude, 6 rejected (Thai script pasted into Malay, "Tiada banjir" where the data says "not detected", "Orang cacat", two button labels too long) and 1 fixed that Gemini had kept (an accuracy margin read as a distance). Two real errors were caught: the Return label "Selamat pulang" is a greeting, not "you may go home", and "terkepung" (besieged) carries a security meaning in the deep south. The templates change through migration `a9_malay_alert_templates`, which leaves a row alone if an admin has edited its Malay since. Before and after of every line is on the tracker's "Malay changes" tab. — Done without the owner at their request; the blind back-translation is what guards meaning, and nothing about naturalness in Pattani is proven until a local reader sees it.
- 2026-10-14 — **The stage-to-extent library (S6) stays off the map this season** (owner, on my recommendation). Built for Yala town (X.40A), Sungai Kolok (X.119A) and Hat Yai (X.44) from HAND tuned to the radar passes, it agrees poorly with the radar: CSI about 0.15 at all three, the same on held-out seasons, a third to two fifths of the radar's wet land caught and four fifths of the shaded land seen dry; Hat Yai puts 49 km² under water at bank level. The code is in `pipeline/stage_extent/` and the outputs stay on the laptop; revisit in February 2027 with the 2026 season's reports and radar. Reason: a shaded outline that is wrong four times in five would mislead people about where water is.
- 2026-10-15 — **A fourth map background, "Satellite + terrain"** (owner's request): the satellite picture (Esri with the key, else EOX) with the hills shaded over it from the same free Terrarium elevation tiles as the Terrain background. The shading is see-through light and shadow only (rgba colours, exaggeration 0.7), so the picture stays a picture; like the plain satellite it has no night version and shows the same note about what the picture is. Flat hill shading, not a tilted 3D view: the map keeps tilting and rotating off, and 3D terrain would cost low-end phones more. The Terrain background stays the default. — Ridges and valleys read on it where the photograph alone looks flat, at no extra cost (no key, no account).
- 2026-10-15 — **The map opens on "Satellite + terrain"** (owner), superseding Terrain as the default (13 Oct). The other three backgrounds stay one tap away, and the note on what the picture is now shows from the start. — The photograph is the ground people recognise, and the shading still shows where the hills are.
- 2026-10-15 — **The safe-return window (science plan Module 5) is built, with my own rules** (owner asked to build it). Per gauge, from the hourly record since 2020, October to March: hours from a flood's peak until the river stays 12 h below its bank and below Warning (12 h, not 6, because 6 h splits the tidal gauge X.10A at every low tide); median and the 25th–75th percentile over complete events, nothing under three; on the admins' signal dashboard beside the Return alert, never on a public screen. Yala town (X.40A): 34 h to below the bank (22 h to 3 days), Sungai Kolok (X.119A) about 2.5 days; Hat Yai has one over-bank flood, so no range. X.77's March–May 2026 record is left out: Bang Lang's daily turbine pulses now cross its provisional Warning (38.15 m), so that level needs a look. Reason: an admin writing a return window needs the record of past floods, given as a range (safety rule 8).
- 2026-10-15 — **After launch, Jaga collects ratings and feedback and keeps them as a record for continuous improvement** (owner). Planned as phase A15 and spec section 17: a 1–5 rating shown as faces with words, an optional topic and comment, anonymous allowed, sent from Account, About and the footer, and asked once at a few quiet moments (after a request is closed and confirmed, after a report ends, after a team finishes a case), never more than once in 30 days per phone. Never inside SOS, food-and-water, report or alert screens, in a notification, or on home during Warning or Evacuate, and never while a request is open. Each item is stored with its screen, language, app version, device kind, role and chosen district (never tambon or GPS), triaged by admins (new, read, planned, done, won't do) and linked to the decision or commit it led to; a contact only with consent, shown on a logged tap and cleared on close; comments anonymised after 12 months; a review each season. The details of the form and the asks are my proposal, to be confirmed when A15 is built. — Nothing in the app asks users how it works for them today, and a safety app has to learn from the people it serves without ever slowing a request for help.
- 2026-10-15 — **The Terrain background is Esri's terrain map by day** (owner's request): ArcGIS Basemap Styles `arcgis/terrain` (Esri's vector map with names in the page's language over its hill shading), through the same ArcGIS key and free allowance as the satellite picture. My own calls, overrulable: Esri has no night version, so after sunset Terrain stays the dark open map with the hills shaded; without a key, or when Esri refuses the style or four of its tiles, the open terrain for the rest of the visit; Esri's labels are drawn in Noto Sans from OpenFreeMap's font server, because Esri's font server has none of the font Jaga's own labels use. Found while testing: the style service checks the key's allowed web addresses (its tile server does not), so a page on any other address falls back to the open terrain, which is the behaviour wanted. — The owner asked for Esri's terrain; it costs nothing within the allowance it already shares with the satellite picture, which Terrain's vector and hillshade tiles now also draw on.
- 2026-10-15 — **Smaller-road embankment parking points may stand 500 m apart** (owner; highway and terrain points keep 1 km). Tambons with fewer than three safe-place candidates 8 to 5 (41 road points instead of 37); Tha Hin and Phang Yang still have none. Reason: in small tambons the 1 km spacing left no room for a third point.
- 2026-10-15 — **"Map" is now the Flood Risk Map, and the app shows people what it and an account can do (owner: more people should open the map and sign in; all eight of my proposals chosen).** The header, page title and home button say แผนที่เสี่ยงน้ำท่วม / Peta Risiko Banjir / Flood Risk Map. The map's five features (flood-risk areas, safe places with directions, the land dam water can reach, live river gauges, the live dam) are tiles on the home screen and chips above the map, each opening the map on itself (`?focus=`); the map has a closed "How this map was made" panel with the science and its limits. The home screen shows the person's own tambon from `hazard_by_tambon` (share of buildings on Medium or High land, a bar of the classes), labelled long-term risk and not today's alert, in the map's pinks, never alert colours; where little is marked it says that is not no risk, and classes the tambon has none of are left out. For visitors: "Sign in" (a pill) replaces "Account" in the header, a "What you get" card (alerts in LINE, watched places, household, reports, home on every device) with one tap into LINE, padlock hints on Report and on the map's My places, and a line under the bell's list with "Not now" for a week. Every sign-in link returns to the page it came from. Never on the SOS or supplies screens, and the card is hidden when the person's area or a place they watch is at Warning or Evacuate; SOS, alerts and hotlines still need no account (safety rule 1). — The science and the account's real benefits were invisible to most people; none of this stands between anyone and help.

A province picked from the list still wins; with no place at all the map shows all four. — The person sees everything they care about at once, then zooms in.

- 2026-10-15 — **Malay, second review with Gemini, English as the source (owner): stopped by Gemini's quota after 292 of 1,299 lines; the Thai still decides.** Scope: every screen a resident or visitor sees plus the four flood templates (team and admin screens stay out, as decided 14 Oct). Gemini (Antigravity, Gemini 3.8 Flash) rewrote 8 of 35 batches before the owner's AI Pro account answered "Individual quota reached", for every Gemini model, resetting in about 7 days (around 22 Oct); nothing was paid for or upgraded. The blind back-translation into English was done by a separate Claude agent that saw only the Malay. Of 58 lines changed or reopened: 16 rewrites taken, 3 corrected, **33 rejected**, 6 fixed by Claude. Nearly every rejection is the same thing: **the English says less than the Thai** (charge the phone *full*, lift things *to a high place*, *important* documents, *2–3* days, *which* team, *powdered* milk, a location that *may be off by* so much), and Gemini, told to follow the English, removed correct words from the Malay. So where the two sources differ the Malay follows the Thai, as CLAUDE.md says; 19 such English lines are listed for the owner. The blind check found two real errors that round 1 and Gemini both missed: **"Jika cemas" means "if you are worried"**, not "if it is an emergency" (*kecemasan*), in three SOS lines and the household form; and the line after sending an SOS said rescuers had "*menerima*" it, the app's own word for a team accepting a case, while the status says no team has (now "has reached the rescuers"). One reverse case: the Thai of `sos.status.waiting` says rescuers *have seen* the request, which the app cannot know; the Malay now says they *can see* it, and the Thai is flagged. Template change through migration `a9_malay_round2_templates` (the Watch reason only). — The owner asked for English as the source; the result shows the English is the thinner of the two, so a full run should give Gemini both, with the Thai deciding.
- 2026-10-15 — **On the map, the person's own places have their own shapes and a slow movement** (owner: home and watched places looked almost like the river gauges). Home = a map pin with a house in its head and a soft glow behind it that breathes in and out (3.6 s); a watched place = its own colour as a dot inside four focus brackets that close in on it and let go (3.2 s), "kept in view" without an eye (no eye icons, docs/brand.md). Neither is round like a gauge's glass, neither ripples outwards as a gauge near its bank does, colours stay the non-alert ones of lib/mine-colors.ts, drawn at 15 frames a second and still under reduced motion; the legend shows the same shapes. The shapes and timings are my choice. — A person must find their own places at a glance, and nothing of theirs may be mistaken for a reading.
- 2026-10-15 — **On the home screen the dam's card sits at the bottom, just before "Share Jaga"** (owner), after the install card; it was directly above the way into the map (13 Oct, superseded). The quiet dam notice at the top of the left column is unchanged: it appears only when the dam has something to say to someone on its river. — The card is background for people near the river, not news; the screen leads with what to do.
- 2026-10-15 — **A 2D / 3D switch on the map** (owner: a 3D scene like ArcGIS Pro, with the button on the map itself). 3D raises the ground from the same free Terrarium elevation tiles under whichever background is chosen (Satellite + terrain gives the ArcGIS-Pro-like scene), tilts the map to 60° with a sky, and lets it be tilted and turned (two fingers, or the right mouse button); 2D lays it flat and north-up again. My own calls, overrulable: **2D stays the default** (3D costs a cheap phone far more data, battery and drawing, and pulls more Esri tiles from the free allowance); **heights are drawn 1.5 times real**, because the coastal plain where the floods are looks flat at 1, and a note under the map says so whenever 3D is on; the switch sits at the bottom-left of the map, clear of the zoom buttons and credits; in 2D a two-finger drag no longer tilts the map. — The owner asked for it; a raised scene shows where the water would run, and the exaggeration is stated so nobody reads it as real height.
- 2026-10-15 — **Sea and coast, a sixth feature of the Flood Risk Map, with a graded note for fishermen that never says "safe"** (owner asked for tide and waves near the coast as an animation and a short note on whether fishermen should go out, as its own section beside flood risk, river gauges and the dam; chose the graded note over figures-only or an admin-written note). Seven offshore points from Open-Meteo's marine and weather models; grades over the next 24 h: stay in port at waves ≥ 2 m (TMD's "คลื่นสูง 2-3 เมตร เรือเล็กควรงดออกจากฝั่ง"), wind ≥ 40 km/h or gusts ≥ 60 km/h; rough at waves ≥ 1 m, wind ≥ 30, gusts ≥ 45 or thunder; otherwise "low waves in the model"; "no forecast" when data is thin. The cut-offs are Jaga's own, from TMD's wording and the Beaufort scale, not validated against local accidents. Tide shown as rising/falling and turn times only (model sea level is ~0.7 m off the gauges). Water colour, never alert colours; points to TMD 1182 and the Marine Department 1199, kept on the sea card rather than in the four-hotline bar. TMD's open warning feed answered only a 2022 storm, so Jaga cannot yet show TMD's own marine warning beside the model.
- 2026-10-15 — **"My places" on the map is a tour** (owner): it visits the home, then each watched place in the order added, about 5.5 × 4.4 km around each, opening its card; a bar on the map gives previous / next (going round), a list to jump to one ("1/3 · name"), and Reset, which returns to the map's opening view with no card. With no saved place it still frames the chosen area, as before. — The person can check each of their places in turn without hunting for pins.
- 2026-10-15 — **Rain clouds over the person's own places on the map** (owner: an animated rain cloud over their home and watched places when rain is falling there, with lightning in a thunderstorm, on the map only). Eight clouds, one per rain group the weather widget already uses (drizzle, light rain, rain, heavy rain, showers, heavy showers, thunderstorm, thunderstorm with hail): more and faster drops as the rain gets heavier, slanted streaks for heavy rain, rain in bursts for showers with the sun or moon peeking behind by day or night, a double flash of white lightning in a storm (never yellow: yellow is Watch), hail pellets with hail. Nothing is drawn when no rain is falling. One cached route asks Open-Meteo's current weather for all the person's points at once (rounded to about 1 km, every 15 minutes while the map is seen); the clouds are HTML markers that take no taps, still under reduced motion, hidden with the pins. — The map is where people look at their places; a cloud only when it rains keeps it meaningful. Claude's calls, overrulable: model weather, not radar or a gauge (said in "How this map was made"); no words on the map for screen readers (the weather page has them).
- 2026-10-15 — **Whoever waits on an admin hears the decision in LINE and in the bell** (owner). Three things wait on an admin: a team's registration (verified or suspended; the bell already had it), a team's field-rescue record (approved or refused; the bell already had it) and a flood report (checked or taken off the map; new in the bell). `line_decisions_due` reads the decisions from audit_log and hands each out once to the LINE of the account it concerns (not to anyone who blocked the account; earlier decisions were marked as already told), with the bell's own words and one "Open Jaga" button; it runs right after the admin's action and on the minute's tick. Every bell item already rings once. The LINE menu becomes two rows: the three everyday buttons unchanged on top, "Flood Risk Map" and "Register a response team" (through sign-in) underneath (`richmenu-2.png`, published). My calls: a checked report is told as well as a hidden one; the message names the team or tambon only; these LINE messages count against the monthly quota like the rest. — A person who asked for something should not have to keep opening the app to learn the answer.
- 2026-10-15 — **Sea points and rain clouds open a pop-up on the map** (owner): hover on a laptop, a tap on a phone or tablet, like the gauges and reservoirs. A sea point shows its grade for fishermen, the waves of the next day and whether the tide is rising; a cloud shows whose place and what is falling there now. Each has "See all details": the sea opens the Sea and coast section, a cloud opens a new section of its own under the map, "Rain at <place>", with the next six hours of the same Open-Meteo forecast the weather page uses, when the forecast has the rain easing, the model time, and that it is not a flood alert. The clouds are HTML markers, so they take the pointer themselves and keep the map from closing their pop-up.

- 2026-10-15 — **The Thai copy was reviewed and polished by Claude** (owner: "review the Thai, leave the dam, reservoir and hydrology parts as they are; make it more professional yet not too official"; Gemini is no longer used). Scope: 2,403 lines and the four flood templates; left exactly as they were: the dam, reservoir, gauge and hydrology lines (`dam`, the war room's dam row, `signals`, `mapMethod`, `map.gauge`, the dam-release form, any line naming เขื่อน, อ่างเก็บน้ำ or ม.รทก.) and two namespaces another session is still writing (`sea`, `mapRain`). Six Claude agents reviewed one slice each against a written style guide; Claude decided every proposal. **292 lines changed**, meaning unchanged, so the English and Malay still match: the register (no ท่าน, no กรุณา, passives with ถูก made active, ณ and other officialese out), fixed terms used consistently (ประกาศเตือนภัย for an alert and การแจ้งเตือน only for a phone notification; "Jaga" not "จากา"; สถานที่ที่คุณดูแล; ที่ปลอดภัย; เบอร์โทร; ผู้ดูแล Jaga on resident screens and ผู้ดูแลระบบ on admin screens; ทีม where the English says team, which the teams' board had mixed with หน่วย), and proper weather terms (ปริมาณฝน, ความชื้นสัมพัทธ์, โอกาสเกิดฝน). Ten proposals were rejected (one would have broken LINE's 20-character button limit; the verification screens keep หน่วยงาน as registration does). **Two meaning fixes:** after an SOS the Thai said rescuers *have seen* the request, which the app cannot know; it now says they *can see* it (as the English and Malay do); and the map's feature list called the safe places จุดอพยพ (official evacuation points) though most are unverified. The Evacuate template now says ฝ่าน้ำ like its checklist (migration `a9_thai_review_templates`). Nineteen lines whose meaning is out of date or overclaims were listed for the owner, not changed. — Thai is the source of truth for copy; the owner asked for a professional, not bureaucratic, voice, and consistent terms are what make a safety app read as one voice.
- 2026-10-15 — **Before launch, every page and feature gets a short guided tour for each role, shown on a person's first two visits** (owner); planned as phase A16 in docs/prompts.md. My fences, overrulable: never on SOS, food-and-water, report sending or alert screens, never during Warning or Evacuate, never over the hotline bar; skippable, "don't show again", and replayable from a "Show me around" link. — New users, teams and admins should find their way without help, but nothing may stand between a person and a request for help.
- 2026-10-15 — **Two more pages planned (owner): "What Jaga can do" (A17, before launch), a short visual page per role showing what each can do; and "How Jaga works" (A18, before launch or soon after), knowledge pages for academics on the science (concepts, logic, open data sources, validation) and on the system (conceptual, general terms, no vendor names).** The science page explains without giving parameter values, thresholds or step-by-step methods, so it cannot be used as a recipe. — The app should show what it offers, and researchers should be able to judge the science, without the pages becoming a manual or a method to copy. Note: the code and the METHODS files are in the public repository, so a determined reader can still find the detail there; the pages themselves stay conceptual.
- 2026-10-15 — **A9 offline: map tiles are kept only as the person views them** (owner's choice of three). OpenFreeMap's terms allow offline downloads only when the person starts them, up to 3,000 tiles or 50 MB, with a User-Agent naming the app, which a web page cannot set; everything else counts as automated collection. So the service worker keeps OpenFreeMap tiles and glyphs, EOX satellite tiles and the terrain shading the person has looked at (up to 600 tiles, 30 days), and downloads none in the background. Esri's tiles are not kept beyond its own headers (its terms). What matters most offline is Jaga's own data, and that is kept: the area list, tambon outlines, the map's alert colours and safe places, hazards and gauges, as public same-for-everyone answers (network first, 14 days). Once a day, when the phone is idle and online, the app also keeps home, SOS, food and water and the map ready in the person's language, with the area list (about 100 kB; never on the SOS, report or supplies pages, never with Data Saver, fetched without cookies so no copy holds an account). A page never kept falls back to the offline page in its own language. — Safety rule 7: SOS and "choose your area" must work in airplane mode even if the person never opened them online; tiles in bulk would break a free service's terms for a background map.
- 2026-10-15 — **In Thai, secondary text (`text-small`) is 18 px, the body size** (owner's choice). CLAUDE.md says Thai body text is at least 18 px, and the 16 px style was used for Thai body text in many places (hotline names, safe-place details, the "checked" line). Hierarchy is now carried by colour and weight; Malay and English keep 16 px. Measured at 360 px: Thai elements under 18 px fell from 72 to 2 on home and from 15 to 2 on SOS (left: the clock's numerals and the จากา under the logo, both 12 px); the hotline bar is 5 px taller, the header 1 px, nothing scrolls sideways. — The rule was being broken by one shared style; fixing the style fixes every screen at once.
- 2026-10-15 — **Admins see what the automatic alarm did, and each admin chooses how much of Jaga's alerting reaches their bell** (owner). The Alerts page lists every automatic alarm of the last 30 days and what became of it (waiting, sent, dismissed, published by Jaga itself, river fell back, replaced); admins only. The admin's bell gets an item each time an alarm's state changes; a draft still waiting rings with the sound of the alert it would become. A tick on the Alerts page, per admin and off by default: on, every real alert Jaga sends anywhere reaches their bell as a real alert with its sound, marked "Elsewhere: N subdistricts"; off, only their own home and watched places, like everyone else. My call: a waiting draft rings whatever the tick, because Jaga publishes it by itself within 20 minutes unless an admin acts; drills are not added by the tick (they stay on the Alerts page). — An admin must know what the one automatic path did, and choose between hearing everything and hearing only their own area.

Rules and weights are mine (pipeline/highway_paths/METHODS.md). It stays advice for the provincial highway and irrigation offices, to be checked on imagery and on the ground, and is not shown in the app. Reason: the owner wants the authorities shown where water may need a path.

On at the start, as the owner chose: everything the map drew before (flood risk, gauges, sea, dam, dam release zone); safe places off. - 2026-10-15 — **The Flood Risk Map at the four-province view shows the four province outlines and their capitals only** (owner): the 377 tambon outlines, the basemap's own names (villages, roads, other towns) and Jaga's own reservoir, sea-point and gauge words appear once the reader zooms in a little past that view (0.4 of a zoom step, worked out from each screen's own size, so a phone and a laptop switch at their own overview). And the row of six chips stays frozen under the header once scrolled past; on a phone it shrinks to the six drawings, the names kept for screen readers. — The whole area reads at a glance, and the detail is one pinch away.

My requests keeps its last answer about a person's teams, reports and field rescues on the phone, only while they are signed in, never a phone number, removed when nobody is signed in, and says when it was saved. The contrast check (axe-core, free, test-only) measures twelve pages by day and at night with the alert colours on screen; the frozen header and hotline bar are unfrozen while it measures, because axe cannot judge text they overlap. — A9 asks that every saved item say its age and every primary action carry an icon; the contrast check is the only test that looks at what is really drawn behind the text.

Each said something out of date or more than the app can know; every fact was checked in the code first. An alert the automated alarm publishes by itself now reads "Jaga's automatic alarm (from a river gauge reading, not issued by an admin)" as its issuer, never "Jaga admin": the public status answer passes on only `{kind: auto_alarm}` (migration `a10_auto_alarm_issuer`, applied on jaga-dev). After an SOS, while no team has accepted: "คำขอของคุณถึงหน่วยกู้ภัยแล้ว กรุณารอการตอบรับ" (owner's wording, also on My requests). The SOS-sent line now says Jaga is looking for a rescue team covering the area and the admins see it too, true even where no team covers the tambon. The alert console names the automatic-alarm exception and the phone and LINE channels, drops "within about 1 minute", and says every level rings; the war room says an assignment notifies nobody. The risk-map notes name GISTDA among the sources and say "not checked on the ground" (the map was compared with GISTDA and a held-out season). The outlook says alerts are issued by Jaga and points to DDPM's own warnings (Jaga relays none). Also corrected: the gauge tile (Jaga checks every 15 minutes), the safe-place height (an estimate from maps), moderation (every standing report counts unless hidden), the usage note (a random code kept 90 days), two role-preview lines, the bell's "ข่าวสารจาก Jaga" for an announcement. The unused `sos.phoneBad` is removed. — A safety app must not claim more than it knows; these were the places it did.
- 2026-10-16 — **A hazard table per village, from village points found on the internet** (owner: "build village table", after I reported what exists). The official DOPA set ("village locations, 76 provinces", Open Data Common) cannot be downloaded (its server does not answer), so: the 2012 national village layer (a copy on GitHub, no licence stated) gives 2,211 village points, OpenStreetMap (ODbL) fills the tambons it leaves empty (39); each tambon cell goes to its nearest village point and the village gets the buildings per class there. **The 2012 rows are read only by verified teams and admins, and their seed lives in the private data repo, never in this public one**, because their terms are unknown; a 2012 village OpenStreetMap also has (same name within 1.5 km, 307 of them) is public under OpenStreetMap's names. First use: a team's board names the village a case is near, with the distance. 22 tambons, nearly all town centres, have no village (municipal communities). My calls, overrulable: the nearest-point split (a guess at village boundaries, said in METHODS), the 1.5 km match, 5 km as the furthest "near". — It lets a crew ask the way by a name people use, without waiting for an official file; replace the 2012 points with DOPA's when that file can be had.

The quiet line at the end of each page and the Account link stay.
