- 2026-10-16 — **"How Jaga works" is built (A18): two public pages at `/how-it-works` (the science) and `/how-it-works/system` (the system)**, open without an account, for academics, partner agencies and students. The science page sets out ten parts of the app in the same four fields each — the idea, what it can tell, what it cannot, how it was checked — with source chips, six drawings made in the page (radar change detection, height above the nearest stream, a gauge against its levels, the dam's travel times, the five parts of the system, the path of a request for help), a table of eight measured checks that keeps the unflattering rows (the radar's own 0.41 ceiling, 1 to 14 floods per zone), a section on what was built and deliberately left out (the stage-to-extent library, the highway stretches, rainfall forecasts as a trigger), and a contact line for researchers (CONTACT_EMAIL) instead of the methods themselves. No parameter values, no thresholds, no step order: the pages are to judge the work, not to reproduce it. The system page describes the app in general terms only and **names no vendor, product or brand**, checked by a test in all three languages (`FORBIDDEN_VENDOR_WORDS`); it covers the five parts, the access rules inside the database, privacy by design, how a request for help travels, why only people publish alerts and the one automated exception, what still works when parts fail, how it is checked, and what it costs to run. **Credits and thanks** (the owner's request): every organisation whose data Jaga uses, in four groups, with what it is used for, its licence, the credit line word for word, and a thank-you saying none of this exists without open data and none of them endorses Jaga; a logo is drawn only where a file sits in `public/credits/`, otherwise the card draws the initials, so no third-party logo goes into this public repository before the owner clears the brand terms. My calls, overrulable: two pages rather than one (each is its own address a paper can cite, and the switch is a plain link that works with no JavaScript); English leads and the Thai and Malay are my drafts (A18 point 3 allows it); the "what it costs" section says the running cost but asks for nothing and names no donation; a second quiet line at the end of every page beside "What Jaga can do", because there is no About page yet (A20 will hold both links). — The owner asked for a page that shows the GIS, remote-sensing, hydrology and system work behind the app to the people they have worked with, and that credits everyone whose data made it possible.
- 2026-10-16 — **Two diagrams, the data providers' logos and a way in from the map, on "How Jaga works" (A18)** (owner, same day, three requests). At 360 px each chain becomes a card and the four stages run down it. (2) **An architecture diagram** opens the system page: five bands read downwards — where the figures come from, the scheduled readers, the one database with the access rules inside it, what delivers it, and the people at the end — with what travels between two bands written on the arrow, and the database band drawn apart because that is the whole design; still no supplier, product or brand named, and the vendor test reads those lines too. (3) **Logos for nine organisations** on the credits cards: GISTDA, HII, EGAT, RID, TMD, OpenStreetMap, NOAA, JAXA and UC Santa Barbara, all free-licensed files from Wikimedia Commons, listed with their source and licence in `public/credits/SOURCES.md`, rasters resized to 128 px and re-encoded as WebP (about 100 KB for the set), each on a white plate so it stays legible at night. The owner chose "the ones whose terms allow it": the European space agencies, Esri and Google keep their initials, and no file of theirs is in this public repository; a mark is removed on its owner's request. (4) **"How this map was made" on the map now ends with a line into the full method** ("The whole method, layer by layer: how Jaga works"), so a reader who wants the detail has one tap to it. — The owner asked for the relationships between sources, analysis and results to be drawn the way a GIS office draws them, for the system to have the same treatment, for the providers to be thanked by name and mark, and for the map's short method note to lead here.
- 2026-10-16 — The coastal gate is measured and shown to admins as a modifier, never a trigger (science plan Module 10, `pipeline/coastal_gate`, migration `..s10_coastal_gate`, seed 49). The owner asked whether the sea blocking the Bang Nara's drainage could go into the risk, the forecast or the alerts; of the three options offered, the advisory one was chosen. — The data supports the mechanism strongly: at all four river mouths, in both the 2024 and the 2025 flood, the daily *low* water stood at the 96th-99th percentile of flood-season days while the high water was unremarkable, and the one flood with no sea component (Hat Yai, 18 Dec 2025) sits at the 78th. But the sea floods nothing on its own and the flag would fire about three times a season, so it answers how long water stays, not whether to warn: it feeds the safe-return window and can never raise a level or publish (safety rule 2).
- 2026-10-16 — The gate is not put into `suggest()` and not shown to the public this season. — An alert's wording comes only from reviewed templates, and the admin reading the dashboard already has the same information; a bare sea-level percentile shown publicly would read as a forecast (safety rule 8).
- 2026-10-16 — Sea level comes from Open-Meteo's marine model, compared only against each mouth's own record, never as an absolute height. — The model sat about 0.7 m off the Pattani tide gauge (A8) and its cell is a few kilometres offshore; ThaiWater lists a gauge at the Bang Nara mouth (station 2143 `NR`) but answers no readings for 2024-2026, so there is nothing observed to calibrate against.

Deferred to the owner, not started. — The gate work turned up that no zone covers tambons 9601xx, so the provincial capital's river has no signals or thresholds at all, and its only reporting gauge in the catchment (X.73, Tanyong Mat) sits about 14 m above mean sea level, well above the tide's reach. A new zone nine days before go-live is the owner's call, not mine.
- 2026-10-16 — **The live map becomes an operations console on a laptop, and is untouched on a phone and a tablet** (owner: "can we make Risk & Live Map an advanced dashboard when viewing from laptop, like a war room monitoring dashboard, futuristic GIS style, because people who view this page from a laptop are usually GIS or people who work in an authority or an office who are familiar with a dashboard", then "keep the same for iPhone/tablet view, do not change for now"). At 1024 px and wider **with a mouse or trackpad** (`@custom-variant console (min-width: 1024px) and (pointer: fine)`), /map lays its existing blocks out as a dashboard: the page runs edge to edge (the header with it), a readout strip of four figures sits under the hazard row, and below that a left rail of controls, the map at the full height left over, and a right rail that holds the map's switches, whatever a tap selected, the legend and the alerts in force. Each rail scrolls by itself so the map never leaves the screen, and `revealPanel` scrolls the rail rather than the window. The four readouts are the owner's choice: alert levels in force (as badges, icon and word, never colour alone), river gauges at or near their banks, Bang Lang's reservoir and release, and the standing resident reports; each says how far it reaches and when it was read, and the "minutes ago" line appears only once a reading is an hour old. The theme still follows the clock (the 9 Oct decision stands): a light console by day, a dark one after sunset, no switch. **The pointer, not the width, decides**, so a tablet in landscape — 1180 px wide — keeps the phone layout the owner asked to leave alone. My calls, overrulable: the strip is a readout and nothing in it is clickable; the map's switches went to the top of the right rail rather than a shelf under the map, which cost the map 140 px of a 790 px-tall laptop screen; a hazard that is still to come shows its placeholder and no console at all (safety rule 10); the reports tile says the standing 72-hour window rather than the "24 h" asked for, because that is the window the feed actually counts. Nothing new is shown and no endpoint changed: the console is the same public, aggregated answers a phone gets (safety rule 6), rearranged. — Written as one page and one set of components, laid out by CSS alone: the phone's own order of blocks is also the console's reading order, column by column, so no wrapper reorders anything and a 360 px screen renders byte for byte what it did before.

Everyone still reads what the admins do; the buttons follow the role the header already keeps on the phone, and the admin pages refuse anyone else as before. — A button that leads a visitor to a sign-in wall they can never pass is noise.
- 2026-10-16 — **The console's right-hand rail grows, and the dam's panel lays itself out by its own width** (owner: "increase the width of the right panel more, when opening Dam information it's too squeezed. The left side it's good already", then "it's fine, decrease the map width a bit to make sure the information panel on the right display and the user can read easily"). The rail is now `clamp(26rem, 32vw, 38rem)` - 461 px on a 1440 px laptop against 352 px before, 614 px at 1920 - and the map gives up the width, which the owner approved. The squeeze had a second cause: `DamReadout` sized itself by the **window**, so on a laptop it applied `lg:grid-cols-3` inside a rail a third of the window wide and laid its figures out in 23 px columns with the words spilling out of them. It now uses a container query (`@container`, `@lg`, `@3xl`), so it answers to the width it actually has; the steps are placed where the window steps used to fall, so a phone still stacks and a tablet still shows the dial beside two columns. Also: a card opened from the address (?dam=, ?focus=) scrolls the rail to itself on arrival, because on the console it opens below the map's switches - and only when the card is in a rail that scrolls, so no phone ever scrolls itself on load. — The panel is the thing people read on this page; the map is already the largest thing on it.
- 2026-10-16 — **The console's background is the weather where the person is, and the grid of lines is gone** (owner: "I can see background have grid lines, get rid of grid lines, you can use the theme background from weather widget, to show what's the current situation of the weather. The whole area have the animated background same as in the weather widget. but the weather info (minimal) should be on the empty side on the right side"). The console draws the same `WeatherScene` as the weather page and the header's pill - one picture of the weather in the app, not three - and the weather in words (icon, temperature, condition, place) sits at the end of the console's first line. Both are drawn only on the console and only once a place is known, the same rule the header's pill follows; neither asks the server for anything, because the pill is on every page and already keeps the reading fresh. **The veil over the sky is worked out, not chosen**: `consoleVeil` (lib/map-console.ts) returns the lightest veil that still leaves the console's weakest text at 4.5:1 against both ends of that sky, in each theme, and a test holds every one of the 18 skies to it. So a clear day comes through as a blue sky with the sun in it (veil 0.39) and a thunderstorm is held back (0.83), and no word's contrast ever depends on the weather. The sky is decoration and says so: hidden from screen readers, nothing of it animated but transform and opacity, and it stops for anyone who has asked for less motion. It is never a status - a flood level is only ever the alert palette with its icon and its word (safety rules 3 and 8) - and it says nothing about flooding. — The owner wanted the console to show the weather at a glance; computing the veil is what lets it be visible without putting a single word at risk.
- 2026-10-16 — **The weather is a bar across the top of the console, not the whole of it, and the console sits off the screen's edge** (owner: "the animated background should only show on the top bar where Risk & Live Map header include the Hazard buttons and that's all. Not extending to the below"; "the left panel should have a little bit space from the left edge to the first letter"; "move the weather detail on the left more"). The sky now spans the console's first three rows only - the title, what the page is, the weather in words and the hazard buttons - and bleeds out to the screen's edges and up to meet the header, so it reads as a bar continuing from it; the readouts, the rails and the map below sit on the page's own ground. The console's gutter is `clamp(1.75rem, 2.5vw, 3.5rem)` (32 px at 1280, 48 px at 1920) and the header takes the same one, so the logo and the page's first line start at the same place. The weather readout is held 2.5rem off the end of its row. A test holds the bar to the full width of the screen, past the hazard buttons and above the readouts and the rails. — A moving sky behind a dashboard of figures competes with them; behind the page's own heading it is a header.
- 2026-10-16 — **The home screen, the account and the admin home use the whole laptop screen too** (owner: "re-arrange the Home Page as well the laptop view. Extend the screen full for laptop like The Risk & Live Map. Re arrange layout to make it symmetric, I let you be creative"; "also do the same for Account and Admin page for Laptop view only"). All three now carry `data-console-page`, which takes the page and the header out to the screen's edges on the same gutter as the map console. **Home** becomes three even columns - what is happening, what to do now, and the person's own places and the way into the map - cut from the page's own order in two places, so a phone still reads one run of blocks and a tablet keeps the two columns it has had since 8 Oct (the second and third stacked in the right-hand one). **The account and the admin home** flow their cards into balanced columns (`columns`, up to four and three) rather than a grid of rows: a grid gives every row the height of its tallest card and leaves holes under the short ones, which on a wide screen reads as scattered rather than symmetric; columns fill and balance themselves, keep the written order for the eye and the keyboard, and never split a card. Headings and notices span the full width. My calls, overrulable: three columns for home rather than two wide ones, where to cut the home columns, and four/three as the column caps. Phones and tablets are untouched throughout - every rule is behind the same laptop-with-a-mouse breakpoint, confirmed on an iPad in landscape, where all three keep their 1152 px column and their present layout. — The owner asked for the window to be used and for the result to look symmetric.
- 2026-10-16 — **An admin can turn a registration down, with a reason the team is told** (owner: "Add also reject choice for admin to reject if can see that people are registering to test from their side or just trying for fun, we should be able to reject and give reason, line message or notification should come to them if they have logged in. You may draft with polite message, leave the reason as Place holder, so the admin can add the reason of rejection"). A registration could only wait or be verified, so a test registration sat in the queue for ever. `authority_status` gains `rejected`, reachable only from `pending`: a verified team is suspended, never rejected. A reason is required, because it is not an internal note — the account that registered reads it, in the bell and in LINE, inside a polite message from Jaga whose frame comes from the reviewed words in `messages/*.json` and whose `{reason}` is the admin's own text. Where it lives: `audit_log`, like every other decision, read back only through `my_unit_reviews()`; not a column on `authority_units`, because every verified team may read those rows. Nothing is deleted — the registration, its contact and its coverage stay, and "Approve this unit after all" is on the same page, so a no by mistake is undone by calling the contact and verifying. My calls, overrulable: the words of the polite message; the reason is cut to fit LINE's 160 characters with the whole of it one button away in Jaga; and an admin can turn a registration down before revealing the contact's phone, since a test registration is clear from the name and the area alone. — The owner asked for a reject choice with a reason that reaches the registrant.
- 2026-10-16 — **The admins' phones ring for a request no team has taken, including when there was no team to ask** (owner, after asking what happens if someone presses SOS after launch and no rescue team has registered yet). Two faults in the same line of `push_due()`. First, a case the relay could never offer — no verified unit covers that tambon (the state Jaga is in on launch day), the point fell outside every tambon, or building the queue failed — left `sos_offer_now()` with no row at all, so `o.exhausted` was null and no admin was ever notified; meanwhile `sos_responder()` has always coalesced that same null to true and tells the sender their case “is now with Jaga's admins”. The sender was promised a watcher nobody had woken. Nobody to ask now counts as everybody asked. Second, the owner chose to **hold the notification until the admins' own overdue mark** (`sos_unclaimed_minutes`, 15 minutes by default, theirs to tighten at the flood peak), which is the mark the war room already shouts at, so one number governs both; this also delays the notification for a queue that ran out quickly, which before fired at about two minutes. Nothing about delivery changes: the case is on every covering unit's board from the second it arrives, any covering unit may accept at any time, and the sender has had the hotlines in front of them since they pressed send (safety rule 1). Migration `..a10_unclaimed_push`, three RLS tests. Still true and not covered by this: the notification reaches only admins who have allowed notifications on a device, and there is no second channel — LINE has no admin path for an unclaimed case. — An uncovered tambon is the one case where a request for help can reach nobody at all, so it is exactly the case that must wake a person.
- 2026-10-16 — The weather page's map no longer plays its hours by itself; the play button starts them (owner). It opens framing every place of the person's own (the place shown, their home, every watched place), and asks for the wider forecast grid when they spread beyond the near one; with a single place it covers the grid around it as before. — Movement only when asked for; and the people the map is for want to see all their places at once. Supersedes the 10 Oct autoplay.
- 2026-10-16 — **Ratings, feedback and comments get a public page of their own, after launch (A15)** (owner: "Add Comment and feedbacks and rating page into the plans to do after launch", then two choices: comments public once an admin approves each one, and Jaga's own figures published "with the bad alongside the good"). A15 has been in the plan since 15 Oct, but it hung the form off the Account page, About and the footer and kept every word admins-only. Now `/feedback` is a page: the rating, topic and comment at the top, Jaga's figures and the approved comments below. **Sending is not publishing** — a tick separate from sending, and a display name or anonymity (anonymous by default), and then an admin approves, the same discipline as a report photo (spec 6.5). An admin may approve a comment about the app and nothing else: never one that asserts anything about flooding, water levels or whether a place is safe, even when true, and never one carrying a phone number, address, plate, named person or a particular case — a page of Jaga's own cannot carry an unchecked claim about the water (safety rules 3, 6 and 8), and the list says in words that these are opinions of the app, with no alert colour, level or hazard icon anywhere near it. A signed-in person deletes their own comment at any time; an anonymous sender does the same through a key kept on the phone, as a request for help is read back, because otherwise a published anonymous comment could never be withdrawn. The published figures follow section 16's rule rather than a showcase: the average and the number of answers, the trend by month, and **the lowest-rated screens named** beside what was changed about them; the finest grain in public is the whole service area by month, screen and language, and district, role, device, the moment of the ask and any case id never leave the admin console. My calls, overrulable: no average is published until a period has 20 answers (a 2.8 from eleven people reads worse than it is); the page keeps its address at Warning and Evacuate while every invitation to it stops, so a bookmark does not break; and the Thai words wait for the reviewer. Still after launch, not before: spec section 17, prompts A15, tracker part 36. — The owner wants people's own words on the app to be visible and answerable, not filed; approval is what keeps a public page in a life-safety app from becoming a channel for unchecked claims about the flood, and publishing the low scores beside the high ones is the same honesty the impact page already owes.
- 2026-10-16 — **The feedback form becomes three questions: a face, what you love, what should be better** (owner, after seeing the first drawing: “we want them to choose their expression … then I want them to choose from few choices or other then state what they like about Jaga. And another section is what they think Jaga to improve, and they can select multiple choices … let them add the custom feedback on both”). The single `topic` field of the 15 Oct draft is replaced by **two multi-select lists with a free-text box each**: `liked[]` + `liked_note`, `improve[]` + `improve_note`. Chips make the answers countable, which is what turns “what should be better” into a ranked work list instead of free text nobody reads; the box beside each keeps the lists from capping what a person can say, and is where the surprising answers come from. The improvement list is written from Jaga's **real** weak points rather than a flattering set — the Malay being unfinished, no warning zone on the Bang Nara, tambons no verified unit covers, slow opening on 3G, notifications not arriving, hard for older people — and offers **warned too late and warned too often as equals**, because a warning system fails in both directions and only the people living there can say which way (safety rule 8). Because the chips are countable, the published figures change too: the lowest-rated *screens* give way to **what people most want fixed, named and counted beside what was done about each**, which is a sharper form of the “bad alongside the good” chosen earlier the same day. My calls, overrulable: “what you love” is multi-select like “improve” (the owner only specified multi for improve) because two taps say more than a forced ranking; two boxes rather than one, so “good but slow” does not have to be split by an admin later; and each chip carries a stable code so rewording it does not break a season's counts. Drawn on the tracker's Feedback page look tab; still after launch, nothing built. — The owner wanted people to say what they love and what they want fixed in the same breath, in their own words as well as ours; the lists are what make a season's answers add up to something the team can act on.
- 2026-10-16 — **Two super admins, a Deputy Super Admin, and one page that keeps the admin team** (owner: "add one more as to collect the admins members, keep [the project account and the owner's own account] as the Superadmins... This page is only appear to superadmin unless super admin add another row of Admin as 'Deputy Super Admin'"), superseding the single super admin of 2026-10-06. `/admin/members` holds every admin: name, the address they sign in with, a **mandatory** phone number, and a **duty** the super admin gives them when adding. Four calls inside it, each with its reason. (1) The duty is a **label, not a lock**: every admin keeps every admin power, because hiding pages while the database still allows the write is a false sense of safety; a real restriction is its own phase with RLS and policy tests per duty. (2) The phone is personal data under safety rule 5, so it is **not a column on the roster**: it lives in `admin_member_contacts`, which has no select policy at all, the list shows the last four digits, and the number itself comes from `reveal_admin_phone()`, which writes the look to `audit_log`. (3) "Deputy Super Admin" is a **flag on the roster row, not a value in `user_role`**: a new role in the enum would mean revisiting `is_admin()` and every policy that names a role nine days before go-live, and the flag is read in exactly one place, `can_manage_admins()`. Only the super admin appoints one; a deputy cannot appoint another. (4) The way in Jaga sends an approved admin over LINE is the **console's plain address with no secret in it**: what lets them in is their role, which lasts until the super admin revokes it, so a forwarded message or a borrowed phone hands nobody anything — the owner was offered a one-time token link and a permanent token link and chose this. Two ways onto the list, both the owner's choice: the super admin adds somebody cold (bound to their account at first sign-in, by the address), or a signed-in person asks from /account and waits for an approval. The two addresses allowed to hold the role are read from `SUPER_ADMIN_EMAILS` in `.env.local` by `scripts/admin.mjs`, never written into this public repo (one of them is personal); nothing in the app can grant that role. — Known limit, told to the owner: LINE Login is requested with `openid profile` and carries no email, so a cold-added admin signs in by the email link and the LINE message reaches only those whose Jaga account is their LINE account; the page says which of the two each person is.
- 2026-10-16 — **Supabase Pro and Vercel Pro at their base plans only for the first season: about $45/month, no pre-bought capacity** (owner: "for now I will set for the minimum even the flood season can come soon but the app may not be known and used by many people yet"). Vercel Pro's included Flat Rate CDN tier (1M CDN requests, 1 TB transfer) plus its $20 usage credit, and Supabase Pro's 8 GB database and 250 GB egress, are left as they come; the $20 tier (10M requests) is bought only if a season needs it, and exceeding the included tier never interrupts service (Vercel moves the tier at the next cycle, not mid-month). Two settings go with it, both defaults that would otherwise reintroduce the hard stop Pro is being paid to avoid: Supabase's spend cap, **on** by default, is turned **off**, so passing a quota bills a few dollars instead of refusing writes including an SOS; and Vercel's spend management is set to notify only, never to an automatic action, because a budget action can pause the deployment. The public endpoints' 60 s edge cache (`lib/supabase/public.ts`) is what keeps Supabase's cost flat in the number of users, and the map tiles come from R2, not Vercel. The $150/month October–December ceiling of 2026-09-28 (k) stands as a ceiling, not a plan. — At a few hundred to a few thousand users neither base plan is near its limits, so capacity bought before there are users is money the first season does not need; the two hard-stop defaults are the part that must not be left as they are.
- 2026-10-16 — **The feedback page is approved as drawn, and held for A15** (owner: “nice love this, save for A15”, on the second drawing). Settled: the four-step form (a face; what you love; what should be better; send), both lists multi-select with “other” and a free-text box each, the admin approval queue with the publish rules of section 17, and the published figures showing the most-chosen improvement chips — named, counted, beside what was done about each. This also confirms the three calls flagged as overrulable: “what you love” is multi-select like “improve”, there are two boxes rather than one, and each chip carries a stable code so rewording it never breaks a season's counts. Nothing is built and nothing is brought forward: the build stays phase A15, after launch, and the plan rather than the drawing is the record (spec 17, prompts A15). Open when it is built: the Thai and Malay wording (the Thai to the owner's reviewer), and whether approved comments also appear on About. — Agreeing the shape before the build means A15 starts from a decided page, and the drawing cost an evening rather than the day the page itself will take.
- 2026-10-17 — **An admin who cannot sign in is said so on the page, and admin phone numbers are stored like every other one** (two defects found while testing the admin team page of 16 Oct, neither reported by a user). First: `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` is deliberately absent from the live site and the Auth admin API is the only thing that can open an account for an address, while the email-link form refuses to (`shouldCreateUser: false`, so an email link can never create an account). Adding somebody by email from jagaapp.org therefore wrote their roster row and their invitation and opened no account, and the page looked finished while that person could not get in at all. `admin_team()` now returns `has_account`, answered from whether an account exists for the address rather than guessed; the row carries a warning in place of its way-in line, a notice at the top counts everyone affected, and the add itself reports `addedNoAccount`. All three name `npm run admin:invite <address>`, which is the only thing that fixes it. Second: the team page passed the typed phone straight to the database, so "0891234567" was stored as typed while every other number in Jaga is `+66...` through `normalizePhone`. That is not cosmetic — the super admin reads these numbers from UTC+3, and a number beginning 0 cannot be dialled from outside Thailand. Both add and change now normalise, as the account page already did. — The first was found by reading what the live site has rather than what the laptop has; the second by an end-to-end test typing a number the way a Thai person types it, which the policy test had hidden by passing an already-international string.
- 2026-10-17 — **Test accounts are swept by address pattern, not by the ids of the run that made them** (owner: "I want all from your debris gone"). An end-to-end run that stops between `beforeAll` and `afterAll` leaves its fixtures on the project the live site reads: one such run on 15 Oct left a test account holding **super_admin**, plus two ordinary accounts, three organisations and a unit, all found on 16 Oct, nine days before go-live. Both specs' `afterAll` now delete every account matching their own address prefix, so the next complete run clears up after an interrupted one, and `accounts.spec.ts` also deletes the orphan organisations — organisations belong to no user, so deleting accounts never took them with it and they had been accumulating. The prefixes are disjoint between spec files (`e2e-team-%` against `e2e-super-/admin-/unit-/noname-%`) so files running side by side cannot delete each other's fixtures. Worth knowing when deleting any account: `auth.admin.deleteUser` fails with "Database error deleting user" while an `admin_invitations.invited_by` row points at it, that column being `on delete no action`, as are `alerts.issued_by`, `announcements.created_by`, `field_rescues.recorded_by` and `handover_notes.author_id`. — A cleanup that only runs on the happy path is not a cleanup; the stray super admin was the proof.
- 2026-10-17 — **Risk & Live Map stays open without an account through launch.** The owner asked what a sign-in wall on `/map` would cost and agreed to leave it as it is. Three reasons decided it: phone OTP is hidden until an SMS provider is chosen after launch, so "signed in" would mean "has LINE" for every resident; `/map` is one of the four pages the service worker warms for airplane mode and is prerendered for that reason, so a wall would cache itself as the map (safety rule 7); and the hazard switcher's "coming soon" placeholders and the small-boat note for fishermen are map-only, so a visitor facing a landslide or a rough sea would meet a sign-in form instead (safety rules 8 and 10). A wall would also protect nothing — rule 6 already keeps the map aggregated, and the public endpoints behind it stay open. Revisit after launch, and then only as a partial gate on the heavier layers. — Nothing a person may need in an emergency should ask who they are first.
- 2026-10-17 — **The console's four readouts ask for an admin's eye when something is serious, and only the alerts card wears an alert colour** (owner: "make the 4 cards blinking with color code of warning or serious alert. These only appear for Admin on laptop view"; then, offered the conflict, they chose the reading that keeps the rules). A card pulses with a ring that breathes about once every two seconds - far below the three flashes a second that can harm a photosensitive reader - and stops at a static ring for anyone who has asked for less motion. **Alerts** pulses in the alert palette (warning or evacuate in force; a Watch does not blink, it would blink for most of a wet week) and its badge still carries the icon and the word. **River gauges** (one at or over its bank), **the dam** (spilling, or the reservoir past the level it is normally held at) and **resident reports** (a tambon several residents confirm) pulse in the page's own ink, never in an alert colour: a reading is a measurement and a resident's report is not an alert, and neither may be dressed as a level Jaga issued (safety rules 2 and 3, and the 16 Oct decision that gauges are drawn in the water colour). Nothing rests on the movement or the colour: each card says in words what it says either way. Only admins see it, on a laptop; the person's own role now travels with their own places (`/api/me/places`, their own session, never cached), which decides what is **shown** and nothing else - every door is still held by the database's own rules (safety rule 5). My call, overrulable: a Watch does not blink. — The owner wanted a war room that catches the eye; the rules exist so that nothing which is not a published level ever looks like one.
- 2026-10-17 — **Both screens get an overview summary: the published level, and beneath it the live measurements in plain words.** (The owner: "all that we have the live monitoring we should have the overview summary whether everything is fine (normal situation) or label with something to be watch or warning or evacuate with summary description of the status", on the Home page and on Risk & Live Map. Asked where the label may come from, they chose the reading that keeps safety rule 2 whole.) **The label stays the official published level and nothing else** — `areaStatus`/`heroStatusFor` exactly as before, with the grey stale marker, the grey "not in service yet" and the grey "couldn't load" — and **no computed level is ever labelled or badged anywhere**. Under it comes a new section, **"what the monitoring shows now"** (`lib/monitoring.ts`, `components/monitoring/MonitoringNow.tsx`): discrete dated observations from the feeds the screens already read — river gauges against their own levels, the figures the dam publishes itself, residents' standing reports, and the forecast model's rain — each with its own age, its own scope and its own named speaker (the station operators, the dam's operator, residents, a weather model), in the page's own ink with no alert palette, no pill and no level icon. The composer returns **no** overall field at all: its `weight` is a sort key and is never shown, because a field summarising the whole picture would be a computed level in all but name. **Three states per observation, never two**, so "could not be read" can never be drawn as "nothing to report"; every quiet line states what was measured, where and when rather than that all is well; a gauge feed that answers while **no** station is reporting is "could not be read", not "all below their bank"; and a figure nobody could read is never a nought (safety rule 10). On the home screen the section sits under the hero, and **after the action checklist at Warning and Evacuate**, so nothing sits between a person and what to do. On the map it is a card above the map at **every** screen size — until now a phone saw no overall status on that page until it tapped a subdistrict — leading with the person's own subdistrict (drawn by the home screen's own `AlertHero`, so safety rule 3 comes with it and there is no second piece of level markup) and the province beside it, falling back to the province alone for a visitor with no saved area. On the laptop console the card is the first thing in the left rail rather than a row of its own: the page's order of blocks **is** the console's reading order, so that is the only place that puts it above the map on a phone without drawing it twice, and a full-width row would have taken its height off a map row that has only 18rem to give. **Rivers are scoped by distance** (`GAUGES_NEAR_KM`, 50 km, my figure and overrulable) from the person's own area, not by province: the public gauge feed carries a point and no province, and the four provinces' bounding boxes overlap, so counting by box would put one station in two provinces. Things with tambon codes (alerts, reports) scope by province; things with points (gauges) scope by distance. Home pays one extra request for it, a counts-and-points-only `/api/public/gauges/summary` asked for after idle and edge-cached 60 s, because the home screen must stay usable on slow 3G; the rain costs nothing at all, being the reading the header's weather chip already keeps. **This supersedes the 14 Oct 2026 season-outlook decision for this feature only**: that kept a computed composite off the home screen because it was a seasonal forecast that could read as an all-clear, and these are measurements with timestamps and named sources — the very thing that decision was protecting a level from being confused with. — The owner wanted one place that answers "how are things", and the rules exist so that nothing which is not a published level ever looks like one.
- 2026-10-17 — **The overview summary becomes one card: a status line with a moving mark, and a detail that opens where there is room for it.** (The owner, after seeing the first build: on a laptop "it should only fit in one card, so instead of 4 cards, just add one more card for this Live monitoring status ... if it's clicked it should open up the lower section under the Dashboard to show the details ... should not take the space on the left panel"; on a phone or tablet "just move the same to be under the Map legend. Move the button to see each Dam under it"; and the home screen uses the same detail.) **This supersedes the placement in the entry above, and nothing else about it**: the label is still only the published level, the composer still returns no overall field, and the three-states-never-two rule stands. What changed: the summary is no longer a block in the console's left rail but the **fifth readout** across the top, whose detail opens as a **band under the dashboard** spanning all three columns — so it cannot take height from a map row that has only 18rem to give, and the closed console is exactly the one approved on 16 Oct. On a phone and a tablet the same card sits **under the map legend**, and the buttons into each reservoir's figures moved beneath it. The home screen keeps its placement (under the hero, after the checklist at Warning and Evacuate) and gains the same card and detail, minus the person's own level, which its hero already carries. **The status line and its mark** (asked for as "a small animated symbol saying everything is normal"): the mark is the slow teal pulse the map's chips already use and it moves identically in all four states, so it can never read as a green light; the words speak about the **readings** and not the situation — all measurements within their normal range, with how many are reporting and when — because a plain "the situation is normal" is an all-clear worked out by code from feeds that lag by hours (the owner was offered both and chose this one). **Charts** ("if you can add them"): the rivers as a count of their latest states, the model's rain hour by hour, and the reservoir's fortnight, all in the water colour with length carrying the magnitude, as the dam's charts already are, and each with a sentence for a screen reader. **No river level over time**, and the chart says why: the public gauge feed carries each station's newest reading, its bank and a three-hour change, and no history, so the line would have to be invented. The owner was offered a new public endpoint for it and chose to stay with what the feeds already give. — One card, one status, and the detail where the screen has room for it; the rules exist so that nothing which is not a published level ever looks like one.
- 2026-10-17 — **The monitoring detail ends with its own way out, which returns the reader to the dashboard.** (The owner: "when details is expanded, in the details card, should have the button 'Hide the details' so when clicking this, it should hide the detail, and bring the view back to the main Dashboard view up at the top.") The detail is a screenful or two once the charts have drawn, so the control that opened it is off the screen by the time a reader has finished: a panel that can only be shut from above the fold is a panel a phone cannot shut. The button sits last inside the detail on all three screens, reuses the existing `monitoring.hideDetail` wording so no new copy needs translating, and on closing scrolls to the top of the dashboard through the same `lib/reveal.ts` the opening uses. The keyboard is handed back to the readout or card that opened it (`handBack`, new in lib/reveal.ts), because shutting a panel from inside it takes the focused button out of the page — and the same control is drawn twice, one copy hidden by CSS at any width, so the first one actually on the screen is the one that gets it. — A way in that does not come with a way out is only half a control.
- 2026-10-18 — **A theme switch beside the bell; a hand-picked theme lasts the rest of that day** (owner: "Add a switch of the theme, near the bell notification ... by default every visit should follow the timing like current. And then the user is free to change"). The sun still decides what every visit arrives as (`DaylightTheme`, `lib/theme-script.ts`): the switch only overrules it, and the choice is stored with the phone's own calendar day (`jaga.theme = {mode, day}`), so the next day starts from the sun again. The owner chose that span over "only this tab" and "until they switch back". `lib/theme` also holds the choice for as long as the visit lasts, so the hourly re-check cannot turn the app dark by itself at midnight while somebody is reading it. The pre-paint script reads the choice first, so the app never flashes the sun's theme and then corrects itself. — The time of day is right for almost everyone, and the person it is wrong for should not have to live with it.
- 2026-10-18 — **At phone width the weather pill shows its cloud without the word "สภาพอากาศ" until a place is chosen** (owner, choosing it over a taller header). In Thai that word made the pill 150 px wide, and with a third icon beside the bell the top row no longer fitted next to the logo: the header grew from 156 px to 204 px for exactly the visitor who has not chosen a tambon yet. The word is still read out (`sr-only`) and comes back from a tablet up. — The header on a 360 px phone is full, and a row of its height is worth more than a word on a pill that is already an arrow to somewhere.
- 2026-10-18 — **An admin signs in to the app on the home screen with the code from the email, not the link** (owner, after signing in from an iPhone: the email link "navigated to chrome and opened, but it doesn't go back to app that added to homescreen... I opened the app added to homescreen it's not signed in"; offered the three ways out, they chose the code). On iOS a web app added to the home screen keeps its own cookie jar, so a link can only ever sign in the browser that opens it. The machinery that solves this for LINE — the one-time key parked in `sign_in_flows`, the "go back to Jaga" page and `SignInFinisher` collecting the code inside the app (2026-10-13) — was never wired to the email link, which is exactly why residents were unaffected and only admins were locked out of their own app. Rather than extend it, the sign-in form now asks for the **numeric code the same email carries** and calls `verifyOtp` where it is typed, so the session is made in whichever Jaga the person is holding and no browser is involved at all. The link stays in the email and still works, which is the better path on a computer. Three details decided with it. (1) The tries at a code are counted in a **log of their own** (`otp_try_log`, 10 an hour per address, 60 per internet address) rather than in `otp_send_log` beside the sends: shared, ten mistyped codes would have eaten a third of the per-address budget that `email_link_allowed` counts, and Thai mobile networks put many admins behind one address, so during a flood one admin fumbling a code could have refused another a new link. (2) A send refused for being too frequent — by Jaga's own limit or by Supabase's, which is **two emails an hour on a free project** — now **still shows the box for the code**, because an email sent minutes ago carries one that is good for an hour and an admin in a flood must be able to use what they already have. (3) The wording names no number of digits: this project's codes are eight, the Supabase default is six, and a sentence that contradicts the email is worse than no sentence. The reply to the form is unchanged whether or not the address was ever invited, so it still cannot be used to find out who is an admin. — Requires the Magic Link email template to carry `{{ .Token }}`; without it the email has only the link and the box has nothing to type.
- 2026-10-18 — **Supabase's revocation of rotated refresh tokens is turned off, for both projects** (owner's choice, after the same iPhone report: the admin app "logged out by itself after few hours"). The database says plainly that nothing server-side cuts a session short — sessions on `jaga-dev` live for days and refresh dozens of times — but several of the owner's ended the same way: the newest refresh token revoked with no successor issued, which is what GoTrue does to a whole token family when a token that has already been rotated away is presented again. On a home-screen app that is easy to cause and impossible to prevent from our side: iOS drops requests in flight when it suspends the app, so a rotation can be recorded by the server while the `Set-Cookie` carrying its successor never reaches the phone, and the phone then presents the old token hours later, when the access token expires. With the detection off the old token stays usable and a dropped response costs nothing. The trade-off, stated: a stolen refresh token stays usable until it expires rather than being caught on first reuse — accepted because an admin silently signed out is a certainty every few hours while a stolen token is a possibility, and an admin who cannot get in cannot publish an alert. — The setting is per project: it must be set again on `jaga-live`, where it is on by default.
- 2026-10-18 — **The theme switch is approved as built** (owner: "Theme ok"). The switch beside the bell, a hand-picked theme lasting the rest of that day, and the weather pill giving up its word at phone width all stand as described in the two entries above. — Approved after trying it.
- 2026-10-18 — **The refresh-token revocation cannot be turned off before the Pro project, and anything that decides a way in is asked for more than once** (two corrections to the entries above, the same afternoon). First: the owner found that Supabase keeps “detect and revoke potentially compromised refresh tokens” behind the paid plan, so it cannot be changed on the free `jaga-dev` at all. The decision itself stands and nothing about the reasoning changes; only the day it can be carried out does. It becomes part of walkthrough 7, to be set the moment `jaga-live` exists, which is before launch in any case; until then an admin's home-screen app will keep signing itself out every few hours, and the way back in is the code, which now takes seconds. No code was written to work around it: every mitigation available on a free project is partial, and a partial one would have to be unpicked a week later. Second, found while answering why the Admin link had vanished after a code sign-in: the header asks `/api/me/role` **once** as a page opens, with an empty catch — and a phone drops whatever is in flight the moment an app on the home screen is switched away, which is exactly what reading the code out of the mail app does. One lost request therefore left a super admin with no way into the console until they closed Jaga and opened it again. The role and the person's own places are now tried up to three times and asked for afresh when the app returns to the screen or the connection comes back, until an answer arrives, and never asked again once one has. The rule this sets: **a one-shot fetch with a silent catch is not good enough for anything that decides whether a person can reach something** — the alert status already worked this way, and these two did not. — The same dropped-response behaviour of iOS is behind both the vanishing link and the sessions dying; only one of the two can be fixed in our own code.
- 2026-10-18 — **The map opens on its own, filling the laptop screen, with only the layer pickers beside it** (owner: "make the map part of the dashboard can be zoomed as full screen via a button within the map itself... only have left panel to select what layers to show... and of course should have buttons to back to the main console or go to home page"; in English the panel is now called **Basemap**, not Map background). A button among the map's own buttons opens it; the console is laid out again rather than torn down, so MapLibre is never reloaded and nothing drawn on the map is lost. What is left is the map, a left panel holding the six layer chips with the satellite's flood (GISTDA) and the safe places brought over to join them, and the way back - **Back to the console** and **Home**. The basemap moves onto the map itself as a button at its foot, the way a map application does it, and the 2D/3D switch and the zoom keep their corners. Escape comes back out. **The hotline bar is deliberately left above all of it and the map is held clear of it by its measured height** (safety rule 4: the numbers are one tap away, always, and a map that hid them would break that for the sake of a few pixels). Laptop only for now - the owner asked first for the laptop and then said a phone may have it too if it suits; it does not suit as drawn, because a 21 rem panel cannot stand beside a map on a 360 px screen, so a phone version is its own piece of work and is offered rather than guessed. My call, overrulable: the way back is in the panel and not also on the map, because two buttons saying the same thing crowd a map. — Somebody reading the map for a province wants the map, not the dashboard around it.
- 2026-10-18 — **The live monitoring leaves the reservoir out where it is nothing to do with the reader, and tells a quiet station apart from a feed that did not answer.** (The owner: "If the user's locations or watch places do not concern or getting affected by any Dam release or rising of the river level. Then hide the information on these, no need to say, can't read the dam info. And if sometimes from API there is no data of the live water gauge in the moment of Dam, just say 'No live data from the report on this station or dam at this moment'.") Two things are still never hidden, and both are deliberate limits on the first half of that: **a reservoir that is actually doing something** - a release or water over the spillway is an event, and this app does not go quiet about an event whatever the modelled reach of it says - and **a reader who has saved no place at all**, who is shown all four provinces everywhere else and so keeps the reservoir too. Hiding only ever means "this one is not about you", which can be said only of somebody whose own places are known. Where the note goes, the reservoir's fortnight chart goes with it, decided from the composer's own notes so the two can never disagree. The second half is a new `noFigures` on the dam note: `reading` when the feed answered and the station has published nothing for this moment (`monitoring.dam.noReading`), `feed` when the request itself did not answer (the old wording). Blaming Jaga for a station's silence is both untrue and the kind of noise that teaches a reader to skip the section. — An absence that is reported is still never an all-clear: what is dropped here is a fact about a place the reader is not in, and the section still says what it could not read about the places they are in.
- 2026-10-18 — **The map names the districts and then the tambons as the reader zooms in** (owner: "make the label from the basemap for District and sub districts (Tambon) to appear when zoom in... only full 4 province extent that only the province label should show... especially at the level of my location extent"). The basemap cannot do this: OpenFreeMap names towns and villages, not Thai amphoe and tambon, and on the satellite basemap it names nothing at all. So the names are Jaga's own, built from the area list the map page already fetches for the province picker (`/api/geo/areas`, `public.tambon_directory`), which means a named map costs **no extra request and no new endpoint** — each tambon is named at the point PostGIS already works out inside its outline, and a district at the middle of its tambons' points. The ladder follows the same figure the four-province view already uses, so a phone and a laptop each switch just past their own overview: provinces alone at the overview (unchanged), districts from the step where the rest of the detail comes back, tambons about one province-width further in, which is well before someone's own place fills the screen. The names queue **before** the basemap's own names, so where a village name and a tambon name collide it is the tambon name that survives. They are written in the reader's language, with the Thai ranks (อ., ต.) the rest of the app uses, and they are drawn above our fills so the alert colours never cover one. **Not laptop-only**: this is the map's own data and not a layout, so a phone gets it too — the zoom it starts at is worked out from the phone's own overview. — A dashboard that cannot tell you which tambon you are looking at is asking you to guess.
- 2026-10-18 — **The console's readouts are small until they are asked for, and the right panel folds away** (owner: "make the 5 cards on top of the map can be pinned or unpinned... by default these cards should only show minimal info... the idea is to have more visible on map part", and the same for the right panel). Small, a card keeps its heading and **its figure** — what is held back is only the lines beneath: the scope, the ages and the counts. A figure is never hidden, because a card that cannot be read at a glance is not a readout. Resting the pointer on one (or tabbing into it) opens the rest **over the map** rather than pushing the map down — the owner's choice when offered both — so the map never resizes under the pointer; a pinned card is different and does take its room, because it is meant to stay. The strip went from 224 px to 144 px, all of it to the map. Pins last as long as the page is open and no longer (the owner chose that over remembering them), so every visit starts with the most map. The right panel folds to the width of its own button, the map takes the room (672 px wide to 1136 px at 1600 px), the pointer on the folded strip brings it back over the map for as long as it is wanted, and **a tap on the map opens it for good**, because a tap is somebody asking for the detail. Two deliberate details: the panel's blocks are hidden by `visibility`, not by `display`, because several of them are `display: contents` wrappers that hold the phone's own arrangement together; and the way back is never hidden, so the panel can always be reopened from a keyboard as well as a pointer. Everything here is inside the console block — a wide screen with a mouse — so no phone and no tablet is touched. My call, overrulable: in a small card the figure is drawn a size down and the heading is one line. — Every pixel the dashboard gives up goes to the thing it was opened for.
- 2026-10-18 — **Approved: the live monitoring card, as built.** (The owner, after looking at it on the home screen and on Risk & Live Map: "now summary OK".) Everything in the three entries above it stands, and the two calls that were explicitly open are settled by the same word: **rivers scoped by distance** (`GAUGES_NEAR_KM = 50`) rather than by province, and **the detail opening as a band under the dashboard** rather than beside the map. Still not built, and still only on offer: a chart of a river's level over time, which needs a new cached public endpoint because the live gauge feed carries no history. — The approval is of a card that says what the instruments read; the badge above it is still the only thing that says what level is in force.
- 2026-10-18 — **The dam's figure carries its own figures, and is drawn as a dam** (owner: add storage, live capacity now, flow and reservoir level to the animation, "make the animation look more real dam... cooler yet easy to understand"). The cross-section is now an embankment with its crest road, core, intake tower, gated spillway chute, powerhouse and tailwater, and four readings are written on it: the share of capacity, the stored volume, the level in m MSL, and both flows, with the spillway's share named only while a gate is open. Everything that moves is still a published figure - the water stands at the storage, the gate lifts only when the dam reports a spill, the jet runs at the release - and a dam that has published nothing is drawn dry and says so (safety rule 10). Three calls inside it: the readings are **HTML over the drawing**, not text in the SVG, because SVG text scales with the box and would be 8 px on a phone and 30 px in the report, and each sits on a plate of the box's own background so it can be read over water, fill or sky; the figure takes its storage from `storageNow()`, the helper the report's instrument panel already uses, so the drawing and the panel can never disagree - and the fallback to the newest figure of the day fixes a real fault, because Bang Lang's hourly feed carries its flows hours before its storage and the reservoir was being drawn empty; and the water keeps the state's colour from 12 Oct, so it is green, amber or red rather than water-blue, with the state also in the chip, the icon and the words. — A reader who knows water wants the figures on the picture, not only underneath it.
- 2026-10-18 — **A reservoir's button takes the map to the lake, centred and lit three times** (owner: "in additional to move the map to the clicked dam button, also zoom in a little bit more than the default extent to the reservoir shape, also make it in the middle of the map and blink for 3 times. So the user can see clearly where the reservoir is in the map"). Every button that opens a reservoir — the legend's rows and the link inside the live monitoring — now frames **the reservoir itself** (`reservoirBox`, a floor of 0.09° so a small pond still gets a little country around it) rather than the dam, its two outlets and the reader's own pins together. **This supersedes the framing of 12 Oct for these buttons only**: that view existed so the distance between the dam and someone's own places could be read, and it is one step out from this one; what the owner asked for here is to be shown where the reservoir is. Overrulable in a line. The lake then lights three times, about one pulse every 1.2 seconds — far below the three flashes a second that can harm a photosensitive reader — and for anyone who has asked for less motion it is held lit once for exactly as long as the three would have taken. Nothing rests on the movement: the card with the figures is what the button opened, and this only says where to look. **Found by measuring rather than assuming:** the obvious way to light it — raising the reservoir layer's own `fill-opacity` with an expression matching the dam's code — is accepted by MapLibre without error and then draws nothing, because `fill-opacity` takes no per-feature value; the frame's mean brightness was identical with the paint set and without it (52.77 both times). It is drawn instead as a layer of its own holding that one reservoir, which measures 47.73 lit against 52.77 unlit. — Somebody told which water is being talked about should not have to hunt for it.
- 2026-10-18 — **While the map is on its own, what a tap opens is a dialog over the map's left edge** (owner: in the full-screen map "the button of 'see the sea and coast, for fishermen' does not open anything when clicked... it should open a dialog box with the details but it has close button as well (the position should be within the map but at the left edge, near to the left panel itself). Users should be able to click next to see different station"). The full-screen map draws no right panel, so every card that panel would have shown — the sea's stations, a gauge, a reservoir, a tambon — had nowhere to go, and the button looked broken. The **same** card now floats over the map beside the pickers, which is why there is nothing here that can drift out of step with the dashboard's version of it. Its own "close" is held at the foot of the dialog, so it can be reached without scrolling to the end of a sea report, and **Escape shuts the dialog first and leaves the full screen only on a second press** — the order anything layered is expected to come apart in. The sea's card also gained **Previous station / Next station**, which name the station they would go to and wrap at both ends: the full picker is still above for a reader who knows which station they want, and these are for reading through them, which is what somebody in the details is doing. They are in the card itself, so the phone gets them too — a small control, and the same one. — A button that opens nothing teaches a reader to stop pressing buttons.
- 2026-10-18 — **The rivers' and the reservoir's readouts open what they are about, and the map lights whatever a card is talking about** (owner: "by clicking these 2 cards also should bring to detail the same like Dam button. Apply for river gauges as well"; then "which station first? the nearest of the user's place, if users have multiple watch places, show the riskiest one first"; and, for the sea's card, clicking a station "should highlight the points of the tide measurement that was clicked"). **This reverses, for two of the five cards, the rule the strip was built on** (16 Oct: "it is a readout, not a control: nothing in it moves the map or switches a layer, so a glance costs nothing and a mis-click cannot lose someone their view"). The owner asked for it, so: the whole card is the way in — there is nothing small to aim at — and the pin inside it still pins rather than opening, which has a test of its own. Alerts and resident reports are untouched and remain readouts. **Which river station the card opens** is the person's own rivers first: those within `GAUGES_NEAR_KM` (50 km, the figure and the reason the live monitoring already uses — the gauge feed carries a point and no province, and the provinces' boxes overlap) of their area or any place they watch; among those the riskiest, and distance only breaks a tie between two reading the same. Somebody who has saved nowhere, or whose places have no station in reach, gets the riskiest of all four provinces rather than nothing. The order is total — risk, distance, newest reading, id — so pressing twice can never land on a different station. **The lighting** is now the same gesture for three things: a reservoir fills with the water colour, a gauge and a point off the coast get a ring of it. The ring is the water colour and never the page's ink, because on the satellite basemap the ink is slate on dark ground and could barely be seen; and never an alert colour, because a reading is a measurement (safety rule 2). A mark that is not in the frame is brought into it at the reader's own zoom; one already on the screen does not move the map. **Two faults found by the tests, both fixed in the app**: the lighting fired before the drawing effect had put the data on the map and then never fired at all, so it now waits for the draw that brings it; and `lib/reveal.ts` scrolled a console rail that could not scroll. — A card that says something is happening should be able to show you where.
- 2026-10-18 — **A hand-picked theme lasts until the sun next changes, not the rest of the day** (owner: "Regardless of which theme they use at day time, when night starts, the app should switch to dark theme automatically, also switch back to light when sun rises"). Supersedes the span agreed earlier the same day. The stored choice is `{mode, at}` and stands only while `nextChange(at)` is still in the future, so the app turns dark at sunset and light at sunrise on its own whatever was picked, and they can pick again afterwards. This also fixes a real gap in the first build, not only the span: `DaylightTheme` stopped consulting the sun at all while a choice was held and woke only hourly to re-apply it, so **a tab left open never changed theme again** - pick dark at ten in the morning and it was still dark the next morning. It now wakes at every sunrise and sunset whether or not a theme was picked, because that moment is both when the sun changes the theme and when a choice runs out. The day stamp, and the module variable that held a choice for the length of a visit, are both gone: midnight stopped mattering, which is what they were there for. — The sun is the rule; a choice is an exception to it, not a setting that outlives the daylight it was made in.
- 2026-10-18 — **The home screen says a rescue unit can register, because nothing anywhere did** (owner: "make the first page they see when open the app... see clearly Log in via LINE button... but mostly I would like to make Register a Unit button be the main focus now when they see, right now we don't get any registration, we need the response units to see and register without going to Account page"). The pre-launch post asked teams, อปพร. and volunteers to register; two days later ten real LINE accounts had signed in and **`authority_units` held one row, the owner's own, from 7 October**. The flow was not at fault — the form saves, the end-to-end test registers a unit against the same database, and the LINE round trip keeps `next` — so nothing was fixed there. What was at fault is that **every visible link on the live phone home page was listed and not one of them mentioned a team, a unit, volunteers or registering**: the only ways in were the account page, behind a sign-in, and the role picker on What Jaga can do, two taps in. Under the hero, and only there, there is now a pair of cards — the unit's way in and the resident's, as equals, the unit's written first. What the tambon's level is stays the first thing on the screen; this is the second. Four decisions inside it. (1) **It is in the static HTML**, gated on nothing but Warning and Evacuate, because the people it exists for arrive from a link on a slow phone, and a gate on the screen being awake or on an area being chosen would have hidden it from exactly them; a test holds that open. (2) **It goes at Warning and Evacuate**, like the install and share cards: a screen somebody opens while their tambon is told to leave is not a recruiting screen. (3) **It shrinks to one quiet line after three visits on that device** (the owner chose this over a close button and over leaving it forever) and is that line from the start for anybody who has a unit already — which the role endpoint now answers with a plain yes or no about the caller, counted through their own session, carried on the request the header already makes, revealing no unit's name, status or number. (4) **The five-reason sign-in card lower down the page is gone**, so the page holds one sign-in offer, one LINE button and one path worth measuring; its two strongest reasons are in the compact card and all five remain on What Jaga can do. Four of the seven new lines are existing lines of Jaga's said again, pinned to their sources by a test so a unit can never be told two different things about what registering does. The same way in is on the sign-in page, where it signs a unit straight into the form rather than linking to it (a link there is a redirect loop), and as one line on the map. **Two things were changed after measuring rather than before**: cards of matching height put the register button 70% down the page, so they sit at the top of the row instead; and side by side at 26rem left each card too narrow to hold its button on one line, so they only part from 34rem — which means they stack on every laptop and go two-up on a tablet. — Ten sign-ins and no registrations is a small sample, and if the real obstacle is handing an official phone number to an app that has not launched, a more visible button will raise the views and still not the units; what it buys is the ability to tell those two apart, by counting taps on the button against registrations completed.
- 2026-10-18 — **Approved: the way in for rescue units on the home screen, as built** (the owner: "units OK"). Everything in the entry above it stands: the pair of cards under the alert status with the unit's written first, the block being in the static HTML and gated only on Warning and Evacuate, the shrink to one line after three visits, the removal of the five-reason sign-in card from the home screen, and the same way in on the sign-in page and the map. The one measurement that was put to them with it is settled by the same word: on a 360 x 640 phone the card's heading sits at the foot of the first screen and its button is one short scroll below, because the area chooser above it is tall, and **the pair stays under the alert status rather than above it** — what the tambon's level is remains the first thing on the screen. — Approved after looking at it; what it is now for is to be counted, taps against registrations.
- 2026-10-19 — **The console's head carries a downlink strip, and it says nothing** (owner, of the animation added the night before: "could you make it cooler? somehow worth Airbus's styled level... should feel like they are seeing something live projected from satellite data"). What moves is a pass: the curve of the ground with the air lit on it, a graticule drifting under the orbit, a craft whose sensor sweeps a swath with a footprint, the track it has covered growing behind it, and a ground station fed exactly as the craft goes over it. **No figures, no times, no station names, nothing that could be read as a measurement** - the obvious way to make a strip like this look technical is scrolling telemetry, and it would be the one thing on this page that looks like data and is not (safety rule 8); Jaga's own satellite work has a layer, a card, a date and a source. The end-to-end test asserts the strip contains not one character. Three things decided by measuring rather than by looking: `translateX(100%)` is a share of **the element**, so the craft crossed about 60% of the strip and the covered track ran ahead of it - the craft now rides a full-width carriage centred on its leading edge, and the horizon path spans the same -5%..105%, which makes the track exact at every width rather than nearly right; the downlink is timed to where the craft actually is (79-83% of the pass), not to where it looked about right; and the strip is **shown only from 1360 px**, because below that the head row wrapped and the map lost 48 px of height - the map is what the page is for. Reduced motion holds the whole scene at the moment it builds towards, which two frames three seconds apart prove is still. — A dashboard may look like an instrument; it may not pretend to be reading one.
- 2026-10-19 — **"Put Jaga on your home screen" moves to the top of the home screen's third column, says what installing is good for, and is pointed at by one line high on the page.** (The owner: "Make the guideline which already have to let people see how to install this app to their homescreen on phone or laptop clearly. Currently maybe it's where people don't notice. Make it more noticeable and say what is the benefit of doing this.") The card was the last thing on the page, below the map, the safe places and the dam, and it stated only that Jaga would open from an icon. It is now the first card of the places column — 7% down a laptop's page, 57% down a 360 px phone's, measured — carries three reasons (it opens straight from the icon; with no signal it still shows the hotlines, the area's last known status and the saved safe places; notifications can reach this device, and on an iPhone or iPad only once Jaga is on the home screen), and wears the teal `offerCard` with a primary button instead of a plain card with a secondary one. Because a phone stacks the home screen's three columns, a quiet line under the hero and the two ways in brings the card into view through `lib/reveal.ts`; it is `lg:hidden`, since on a laptop the card is already near the top. Both are still kept off the screen while a place of theirs is at Warning or Evacuate, and "Not now" still silences them for three weeks. The second reason's picture is a phone, not the lifebuoy: that mark is SOS's alone. — Guidance nobody scrolls to is guidance nobody has.
- 2026-10-20 — **"Who built Jaga": one public page about the founder and developer, built in dev first for review** (the owner: "I want to add one page about the founder and the developer of the app which is my self... people who see the app and has doubt who developed this and how it can be trusted. And for people who know me personally but never knew me professionally should understand from this page that this is not surprise that why I could do this all. because my professional career led me to this"). Those are two readers with opposite questions, and the page is shaped to answer both in one pass: who, then **why**, then the career as a line where **every step ends on the part of Jaga that came out of it** — the civil engineering degree on the hydraulics under the dam-release zone, five hydropower years on the flood-hazard classes and the dam-break analysis, the national Earth-observation programme on the science pipeline and the admin console, the current enterprise work on why this is a finished service inside a phone bill rather than a demo. That last line is the whole argument for the second reader, so it is the only thing on the page drawn in a colour. Four decisions taken with the owner before building. (1) **It gets its own address, `/about`, and a third tab on "How Jaga works"**, so a sceptic judging the method and a friend arriving from Facebook both reach it. (2) **The employers are named in full** — a European space and defence company working for Thailand's space agency under THEOS-2, a hydropower consultancy, an engineering firm serving a national airports operator, the university that taught them, their professional body — which **lifts, for this page only, the rule of 28 Sep 2026 that no employer or city appears in any file of this repo**. That rule exists to keep the owner's employment out of a public repo they did not choose to publish; here the owner is choosing to publish it, and an unverifiable claim is worth nothing on a page whose only job is to be checked. The owner's personal phone number and city of residence are still not on it. (3) **Thai and English now, Malay with round 2 of the translation**, Thai written first as the source. (4) **A portrait slot that falls back to the initials**, so the page is complete and costs nothing extra to download before a photo exists. Three things the page does that a boastful version would not: it says the code was written with an AI assistant under review, it says the data is other people's and sends the reader to the credits, and it ends by saying this is the least important page in the app and pointing at three others. **Still the owner's to write: the three paragraphs under "why I built this"** are my draft of their reasons, including that they are from this part of the country. — A one-person flood-warning app has to answer "who are you" before it can ask anyone to act on a warning, and the honest answer is more convincing than the flattering one.

- 2026-10-20 — **Switching on "the flood a satellite saw" takes the flood-risk classes off, brings the map to the hexagons and blinks them three times; a close button under its own detail puts all of it back** (owner: "disable flood risk layer, zoom in to the GISTDA flood seen polygons, flash the layer 3 times to let the user see where they are... once close button clicked, it should reset back to the previous extent before the button was clicked, flood risk enabled back as well"). The layer is drawn over the hazard classes, and two sets of coloured areas on top of each other cannot both be read; and GISTDA's passes can land anywhere in the four provinces, so a layer switched on while the reader is looking somewhere else looks like a switch that did nothing. The frame that is remembered is the one the **map is actually showing** when the switch is pressed (MapView reports it on every move), not the last box something framed, because the reader may have panned since. Two deliberate limits: an answer with no hexagons in it moves nothing and hides nothing - there is nothing to look at, and the panel's words already say that an empty answer is no all-clear (safety rules 8 and 10) - and changing the window from 7 days to 30 reframes without overwriting where the reader started. — A layer that needs the reader to find it themselves is a layer most people will never see; and anything the app does to the map on their behalf has to be undoable in one tap.
- 2026-10-20 — **A reset button on the map: the first view and the layers the map opens with** (owner: "add a reset extent button in the map to let people click when they wanna come back to default extent and default layers set"). It is not the existing "All 4 provinces" button, which only moves the camera: this also puts the risk classes, the stations, the dam and its land and the sea back on, the safe places and the satellite's flood back off, the map flat rather than tilted, and the background back to whatever the time of day says - the same map a reader would get by opening the page again. — Six layer switches, a tour, a satellite layer and a 3D view are a lot of state to undo by hand on a phone.
