Push stays the default road; the alerts column, the publish function and the review step all start on push only. — The free plan is 500 messages a month, one per person, so one alert to 500 people would spend it.

- 2026-10-22 — **The Bang Nara warning zone waits until after launch** (owner: "leave it after launch"). — No time before 25 Oct; the gap (no warning zone covers Mueang Narathiwat's river mouth) stays on the after-launch list.

- 2026-10-22 — **No dry run before launch** (owner: "No time to dry run"). — Taken off the tracker; the drill that can really be sent (21 Oct) is the remaining way to show the alert system works.

- 2026-10-22 — **The Sheltered j logo is confirmed** (owner: "mark OK", closing spec open item 11 and the resident logo test). — The fallback mark without the dot is no longer needed.

- 2026-10-22 — **The Malay review is closed for this season** (owner: remove everything about the Malay review from Next steps). — Round 1 (Gemini, 14 Oct) stays in the app; round 2 and a Patani Malay reader are dropped from the launch list.

- 2026-10-23 — **The left rail's order, set by the owner: the reader's own pins first, then the six chips, then what the satellite saw** (the owner: "bring the Show my places on the map to the left side as well, make it at the top in the left panel. Move the Show flood seen by satellite (GISTDA) to be under Safe places"). This finishes the move begun on 22 Oct: every switch for what is drawn on the map is now in one column, and the right-hand panel keeps only the basemap and the legend. **"My places" is first after the way out of full screen** - that block is `display: none` until the map fills the screen, so the pins switch is the first thing on the page either way, and in full screen the way back still comes before the switches. **GISTDA sits under the chips**, Safe places being the last of them, which is what "under Safe places" means. **That position costs its visibility on a laptop, and the figure is on the record**: the rail is a column showing 324 px of 871, so under the chips the switch is **0 px visible** on a 1440x900 screen and 0 px again on a 1440x1080 one once the pins switch is there too - it is reached by scrolling the rail. It was put above the chips on 22 Oct for exactly that reason; the owner has now chosen the grouping over the visibility, and the grouping is a real argument - a reader who has found one of these switches has found them all. On a tablet held sideways the rail is a full-width row of columns, so there it is beside the chips and in plain sight (55 px of 78 without scrolling); on a phone nothing moved, because the phone band gives every explainer below the map `order: 4` whatever its place in the rail. Three ways to buy the laptop's visibility back, if the owner wants it: put it above the chips again on a laptop only, shorten the six chips (they are 477 px with their notes), or let the page scroll instead of the rail. **The full-screen allow-list caught the move again**: `[data-map-full] [data-console-area="rail"] > *:not(...)` is a list of what is kept, so `[data-mine-toggle]` had to be named there or the pins switch would have vanished while the map fills the screen - the same trap as the satellite panel on 22 Oct, and again it is an end-to-end test that would have found it. — The owner asked for one column of switches and chose where each goes; the laptop's scroll is the known price, recorded here so nobody re-opens it as a bug.

- 2026-10-23 — **The flood place pages are made to be worth staying on: a live strip and three figures at the top, each river gauge a card (glass, rise or fall, margin to the bank), nearest-to-bank first, and a list of what the map adds with one button to it** (owner: "most people find Jaga by SEO ... make them want to see more"). The slow teal pulse sits on every gauge whose reading is current and on none that is not. — A dot beside a stopped feed says "live" about nothing (the console rule of 20 Oct); counts of zero are never printed, so the strip cannot read as calm (safety rules 2 and 10).

- 2026-10-23 — **The layer rail scrolls while the map fills the screen** (the owner, on an iPad: opening the satellite's panel in full screen "shows the description but I can't scroll down to see the rest"). The rail carried `overflow: visible` from 18 Oct, the day full screen was built, when it held only the way out and the six chips and fitted on every screen it was built for. The satellite's panel moved into it on 22 Oct and opens to a window chooser, a reading, two notes and a close button - about 300 px more - and because the full-screen console is `position: fixed` there was no page behind it to scroll either, so the end of what the reader had just opened could not be reached at all. Two lines fix it: the rail takes the grid's free height instead of being a content-sized row (`grid-template-rows: minmax(0, 1fr) auto`, the other way round from before - the rail is the long one here and what sits under it is a two-line legend), and it scrolls inside its own column with `overscroll-behavior: contain` so that scroll never reaches the map behind it. Measured after: 1,126 px of content in a column of 638-794 px at three iPad and laptop sizes, and the panel's close button reachable at every one. **It is now held by a test** (`map-console.spec.ts`, "in full screen the layer rail scrolls"), which asks the reader's own question - can I get to the bottom of what I opened - of the rail rather than of a pixel height, so it holds whatever the panel grows into. That test is the answer to this being the third thing in three days to go wrong in that rail: the two before it were blocks moved in without being named in full screen's keep-list, and all three would have been caught by exercising full screen rather than reading it. — The owner found it; the rail had outgrown a rule written when it was short, and the fix is the rule the rest of the console already uses.
- 2026-10-23 — **The phone is laid out to Apple's Human Interface Guidelines for iPhone: one header row, a tab bar under the thumb, SOS one tap from every page** (the owner: read all of Apple's HIG, translate its principles and philosophy into the phone view; they chose "one compact row" for the header and "tab bar + slim hotlines" for the foot). All 172 HIG pages were read in three groups (foundations; components and navigation; patterns and system features) and the live site was measured at 360x640 and 390x844 with a finger, before anything was drawn. What the measurement found: the frozen header was 156 px and the hotline bar 93, **249 of 640 px (39%) before any content**, so the first screen was 391 px and the home status card did not finish in it - against "deference" (content first) and "important items at the top"; SOS was reachable only from the home screen, the hazard placeholders and the flood pages, not from the map, the weather or the account - against "the primary action is always reachable"; Home, Map and Sign in were three small underlined links at the top, the far end of a thumb's reach, where HIG puts top-level places in a labelled tab bar at the foot; and a few controls were under 44 pt (MapLibre's zoom buttons 29 px, the attribution toggle 24, the two map pills 37) with dam figures at 11 px. **What changed, below 640 px only** (a tablet and a laptop keep their rows): the header is one row of 59 px - the logo, the weather, the bell and a menu button; the languages, the colour switch, Sign in, About and Admin are behind that button, which drops from the header and never covers the hotline bar or the tabs (safety rule 4). The foot is two bars: the four hotlines, one short word under each number ("ปภ.", "เจ็บป่วย", "ตำรวจ", "ดับเพลิง"; the full name stays in the link's label for a screen reader), and under them a **tab bar of Home, Map, SOS and Account**, each an icon with a word, the current one marked by a bar and bold type and `aria-current` as well as colour. SOS is the one filled cell: Apple's advice for a row of equals is a single standout by style, not size. The tab bar is not drawn on /sos and /supplies, as About and Sign in were not: where someone is asking for help nothing leads away. A visitor's Account tab goes to sign-in and brings them back, as the old pill did. Chrome on a 360x640 phone is now about 173 px (27%), not 249 (39%), and the home status card fits the first screen. Smaller: the two map pills 48 px and, on a phone, one row at 390; MapLibre's zoom and attribution buttons 44 px as on a tablet; the "how and why" and "sign in" inline links get a taller touch area than their line; the dam figures' smallest text 13 px, from 11 and 12. **Where Apple's text and Jaga's rules disagree, Jaga's win:** targets stay at 48 px (Apple's 44 pt is looser), Thai text at 18 px with IBM Plex Sans Thai, the day/night switch stays although Apple advises against an app-specific one (it is in the menu now), an alert with a next-update time past stays visible with its grey marker (Apple's Wallet hides expired items), SOS gets no confirmation step (Apple's "confirm twice" is for recoverable actions), and the evacuation push keeps telling people what to do (Apple's notification guidance says not to). **Open for the owner:** the four short hotline words are new Thai/Malay/English copy and need the Thai review; the map detail card is still inline under the map, and HIG's answer is a resizable bottom sheet with a grabber (not built; it is in files another working session was editing); the in-service screens (SOS form, alert states, report) were checked on the build for the tab bar's behaviour only, not yet audited line by line against the HIG list. — The owner asked for Apple's philosophy applied to the phone, and its first rule is that content comes before chrome: a 391 px first screen under 249 px of header and bars was the opposite.
- 2026-10-23 — Correction to the entry above, measured on the live site after it deployed: the frozen chrome on a 360x640 phone is 178 px (header 59 + hotlines 57 + tab bar 62), 28% of the screen, not "about 173 (27%)"; it was 249 px (39%) before. — The first figure was from a build before the last tightening; the live one is what people have.

- 2026-10-23 — **The reservoirs' readout counts every reservoir, and asks for a look when any of them is doing something** (the owner: "why Dam Card, on the map, doesn't have pulse like the river gauges? ... it should check and say the overall situation of the all reservoir cards"). The rivers' readout has always counted all 48 stations; the reservoirs' counted **one** - whichever had a reading - so a dam going over its spillway two provinces away asked nobody to look, and that is exactly the difference the owner saw. `damsTally` now answers for all six: how many are over the spillway, how many are past the level they are normally held at, and which one the card is titled by. The blink follows that count, in the **plain attention colour the rivers use and never an alert colour** - a measurement is not a level, and only an admin's alert ever wears one (safety rule 2) - and the folded card says the same thing in words either way, so a reader who cannot see the blink loses nothing. **The small teal dot is a different thing and stays tied to the figure beside it**: it means the reading shown is current. Bang Lang's feed is a day old on the site as this is written, so the dot is off while the gauges' is on; a dot fed by some other reservoir's clock would say "live" about a number nobody has updated (safety rule 8). **The order a press walks through is now the owner's**: anything doing something first, worst first - most water over the spillway, then furthest past the normal level - and when every reservoir is normal, **the largest capacity first**, then the next. The dam the chips and the home screen mean is no longer put in front by name; it is the largest of the six anyway, and on a quiet day the reservoir holding the most water is the one worth opening. — The owner asked for one rule for both readouts; this is the rivers' rule, applied to the reservoirs.

- 2026-10-23 — **A "how the reservoirs stand" button in the map's left rail, above the fishermen's one, and the five readouts stand in a column beside the full-screen map on a laptop** (the owner: "add on the left button to see Reservoir Situation just on top of 'see the sea and coast, for fishermen'"; and "when open the Full screen map, also bring the 5 cards that show live monitoring to be on Right side of the map, align vertically"). Full screen put the readout strip away, so the reservoirs' figures could not be reached there at all; the button opens exactly what the readout opens - the worst reservoir, or the largest when all of them are normal - and the card's own Next and Back walk through the rest. The readouts' column is a **laptop only** and deliberately: the pickers already take 21rem from the left, and a tablet held sideways is 1,024-1,180 px wide, so a third column would leave the map about 350 px - less than it had before full screen was pressed, which is the same arithmetic that made the tablet two columns rather than three on 21 Oct. Beside the map the five cards are simply **open**: the hover trick exists on the dashboard to keep the strip short and give the map its height, and here it is the map that has the room. The live monitoring's "see the details" **leaves full screen on the way**, because the band it opens is not drawn there - a button that does nothing is how the owner found that same card on 21 Oct. — Full screen is the map's screen, but a reader watching a flood needs the figures beside it; the laptop has the width for both and a tablet does not.

- 2026-10-23 — **The three things the map asks of a reader are one card they can see: sign in and pin your places, put Jaga on this device, register your team** (the owner: "make that part a bit easy to see, since we want to promote people to log in and Install Jaga on their device to get alerts. And Register their team for getting the SOS help request"). They were two dashed grey footnotes in the right-hand panel, the weight of a legend's small print. Each row goes when it is done - signed in, installed, or already holding a unit - and the whole card goes when all three are. The install row **points at the install card, which is now on this page too**, rather than repeating the steps: the card is the one place where each kind of device's own steps are written, and both ask the same question (`useInstallWanted`), so a reader who said "Not now" is not asked again here. None of it is drawn while the map is on its own: full screen is for the map. **Nothing here is a door** - SOS, the alert levels and the hotlines need none of it (safety rules 1 and 4). The Thai for the first row is the owner's own wording. — The ask was visibility, and the cheapest honest way to get it was to stop writing the three asks as footnotes.
- 2026-10-23 — The owner looked at the phone layout on a real phone and approved it ("looks good"), and approved the phone map as it is ("phone map OK"). The map's detail card therefore stays under the map; the resizable bottom sheet HIG suggests is parked, to be built only if asked. — Their word on the real device settles what the HIG reading could only suggest.

- 2026-10-23 — **The laptop home screen for a first visit gets one question, then one setup card (options A + C): no area chosen shows only "Is your area at risk right now?" with the area chooser, SOS pinned top right and the hotlines bar, plus three quiet links (What is Jaga, risk map, rescue teams); once an area is chosen the page is that tambon's status, with one setup card (area, LINE sign-in, install) that shows only its first open step and goes for good when done.** Install is offered only after sign-in (the owner's answer, not on a second visit); "Report flood" stays hidden until sign-in and "food and water" moves under "Other ways to ask for help"; a "For rescue teams" link goes in the header. Laptop console only: phone and tablet layouts are unchanged, SOS and hotlines are never behind a step, and with no area chosen the page says there is no status to show and never an all-clear colour. **Drawn first on the tracker (tab "Home first visit"), approved by the owner on 23 Oct, then built the same day** (`lib/home-stage.ts` decides the stage: first, area, member or tense; `HomeFirst`, `SetupSteps`, `OtherHelp` and `TeamsLink` draw it; chosen by `useConsoleView`, so the first paint and every phone are unchanged). Setup is finished when the person is signed in and install is done, snoozed with "Not now" or not possible; then the card is not drawn again. The Malay lines are a first draft for the Malay reviewer. — The owner's screenshot showed about seven things asking for a tap at once with no first move; one decision per screen, ordered by priority, keeps the way into sign-in, the risk map and team registration without overwhelming a first-time visitor.

- 2026-10-23 — **The reservoirs' card names a reservoir only when it is the person's own and doing something; otherwise it says the situation and names none** (the owner, from Hat Yai: "why still says Bang Lang dam when I select my area in Hat Yai, it seems it's for every place, remove Bang lang dam on the card, just say the situation now"). The card was titled by whichever reservoir led the list, so every visitor in four provinces saw one dam's name and one dam's percentage whatever their river. **"Theirs" means on their river** - the release path reaches their own area or a place they watch, the same test the quiet dam notice has used since 13 Oct (`onPath`) - and nothing else: a reservoir that is merely near is not a warning, because water that cannot reach you is not your warning. **What the card says now**, in three cases. *Theirs and doing something* (over the spillway, or past the level it is held at): its name, its figure, what it is doing in a few words, and how many others are also worth a look - and it blinks. *Something wrong elsewhere*: a sentence, "1 over the spillway", no name, and it still blinks, because the owner's earlier ask was that the card speak for all six. *Everything normal*: "ทุกเขื่อนอยู่ในเกณฑ์ปกติ" and no dam named at all. **It will not say "normal" about reservoirs nobody has heard from**: with no reading from any of them it says there is no data, and with readings that are old it adds the grey "not updated" the single-dam card had - the sentence is a claim about now. The count under it is "readings from n of m", not "of m". **What a press opens follows the same split.** Something wrong on their river: that one first, then the rest worst first, and Next walks on. Something wrong elsewhere: the worst first. Everything normal: the full list, **nearest first to the area or places they have chosen, and the largest capacity first for someone who has chosen none** - and the dam legend under the map is drawn in that same order, so the list a press opens and the list a reader sees are one list. Nearest is a straight line from the dam's point to their nearest place and is used for the order only, never for naming. **Open for the owner**: "open up the all dam list" could also mean a list drawn inside the card; today a press opens the first reservoir's own card with Next and Back, and the full list is the legend, in order. — The owner asked for one rule: say the situation, and name a reservoir only when it is the reader's.

- 2026-10-23 — **The five readouts float on the full-screen map with a translucent surface, at 88% of the card colour** (the owner: "should float on the map itself and the card background is having transparency but not losing the text reading ability"). They took a 20rem column from the map; floating, the map keeps the whole width it had and the cards sit over its right edge, with the map's own zoom and attribution moved out from under them. **The 88% is worked out, not chosen by eye**: it is the share at which the weakest text on a card, the secondary grey, still reads at about 5:1 over both the blackest and the whitest a basemap can be, in both themes, against the 4.5:1 floor; a unit test holds that arithmetic against the palette so it moves when the palette does. The blur sits on top as a nicety, and where a browser cannot do it the card goes back to the solid surface rather than staying thin. Laptop only, for the tablet's reason: 20rem of card over a 1,024 px screen would cover a third of the map. — Floating and see-through are the owner's words; readability is the safety rule's.

- 2026-10-23 — **Visitors who have not signed in can say where by pasting exact latitude and longitude or searching a tambon name: a third way in on the home area chooser (beside "use my location" and the list), and a box above the pin map on the SOS, supplies and report screens.** Coordinates, long map links and Plus Codes are read in the browser (`lib/location-lookup.ts`), so pasting works with no signal and a pasted position stays exact; names are looked up in Jaga's own list of tambons and districts, with no third party. Landmark search through OpenStreetMap stays signed-in only (the owner chose "Jaga's own places only" on 23 Oct), and a short map link says it cannot be opened here rather than guess. On the SOS screens the box is an extra beside the existing GPS, map tap, area list and typed text; it is never a step and never blocks sending (safety rule 1), and a tambon found by name puts the pin in the middle of it and shows all of it so the spot can be tapped. The map's "My places" pin was dropped from the request: the map has no pin-drop, it only links to the account page, which already has the full finder. — Visitors could only say where by GPS, a list or a tap; people who know their coordinates, or only a name, had no way, and the SOS fallback most of all should not depend on a signal or an account.

- 2026-10-23 — **In the laptop's full screen, "See how the reservoirs stand" asks how to look: as meters, or in full; the meters are a column of real-looking dials floating where a reservoir's card opens, and can be pinned** (the owner: "show 2 choices, either to show as meters only? or full details? ... if the user select gauge meter then it should show all gauge chart but make it looks like real meter, to show on the map the real live water level percentage ... the user can pin this meters view to the map as well. I intend to make full screen as war room for users who use laptop"; then "float with the same background filled like the 5 cards on the right ... show the same location where the detail info of dams opened up (most left of the map). Align vertically"). **The choice is only asked in full screen on a laptop**; on a tablet, in the console, or on a phone the button opens the card at once, as before - the meters live over the full-screen map and nowhere else, and a tablet's map has no 20rem to lend. **Each meter is a 270-degree dial** (empty at the lower left, full at the lower right, the reading at the foot) on a dark instrument face in every theme: a glowing arc for the water, a tick every 5% and a long one every 10%, a bright notch where the reservoir is normally held, and a needle that sweeps up from empty and settles with the small overshoot a real needle has (no motion at all for someone who asked for less). **Nothing on a dial is an alert colour** - the arc is the brand's water teal whatever the level, because a level is a measurement and only an alert an admin published wears red or amber (safety rule 2). A reservoir over its spillway or past its normal level gets a slow ring of the same teal (about one beat every 2.4 s) **and the words** beside the dial; a reading nobody has refreshed greys the needle and says "not updated"; no reading rests the needle at empty and writes a dash, never mid-scale - a reservoir nobody has heard from is not half full (safety rule 8). A reading that rounds up to 100 is written 99.9 while the arc is not quite full. The geometry is pure (`lib/dam-meter.ts`) and tested; the component draws it and decides nothing. **The meters float on the same glass as the five readouts** (88% of the card colour, the share worked out for the 4.5:1 floor on the blackest and whitest basemap) in a column at the map's left edge **where a reservoir's card opens**; as that is one spot, one has to give way: unpinned, a card takes the meters' place; **pinned, the meters stay and the card steps beside them**. Each meter is a button that opens that reservoir's own card; they are in the readout's order (theirs and in trouble first, then nearest, then largest). **The pin is the reader's own and is kept on the laptop** (`jaga.map.meters.pinned`, localStorage only, never sent anywhere), so a war room that was pinned is up the next time full screen opens; closing the meters lets go of the pin, because a panel that comes back by itself is not closed. — The owner is making full screen a war room; the pin is what lets one stay on the map while the reader works, and the order of the reservoirs is the card's own.

- 2026-10-23 — **The residents' reports card and the river gauges card swap places in the readouts, in the strip and in the full-screen column** (the owner: "Swap resident reports card with river gauges position. Same in the full screen"). The order is now alerts, residents' reports, reservoir, river gauges, live monitoring. One list drives both, so nothing is written twice. — The owner's choice of what a reader should see first.

- 2026-10-23 — **Landmark search (the box for a watched place, a home location and the household form) now asks OpenStreetMap's own search and Photon together, and says the search failed when neither could be asked.** The owner could not find Songkla University in Hat Yai, a place they had found easily before. Jaga's own handling was sound; the likely cause is that OpenStreetMap's shared search turns away or throttles servers on shared cloud addresses, and when it failed the box fell back to the weather service's list of towns and said nothing, so it read as "not found" (it could not be confirmed from here: the live logs are not readable). Photon (photon.komoot.io) is a free search over the same OpenStreetMap data, with no key, no account and no per-request charge, so it needed no purchase; like OpenStreetMap's it is only ever sent the typed name and the map's rough centre, from our server, and only for someone signed in. Same-name searches are cached for a day to stay inside both services' fair use. An incomplete search that finds nothing now says "could not search, try again" rather than "not found". — A safety-adjacent box must not turn an outage into "no such place".

- 2026-10-23 — **In full screen the reservoir meters and a card open below the map's own tools and above its controls at the foot, so "All 4 provinces", reset, my places, the 2D/3D switch and the basemap button are never covered; while the basemap's list is open the panels step aside.** The owner found the pinned meters sitting on "All 4 provinces" and reset, and asked for the same to hold for the details panel. Both panels were fixed to the map's top-left corner (1.25 rem from the top), the same corner as the tools row (3 rem tall, 20-68 px at 1440 x 900) and above the 2D/3D switch and basemap button; they now start at 4.75 rem and keep 7 rem clear at the foot, and scroll inside themselves as before. Measured at 1440 x 900, 1366 x 768, 1280 x 720 and a tablet held sideways: every control reachable with the meters, a pinned card, and an unpinned card. The basemap list could not simply be given a higher layer (it lives in the map's own layer, below the panels), so the panels are hidden while it is open and come back as they were. — A panel that sits on the controls that move the map takes the way out of a view away from the reader.

- 2026-10-24 — **The impact dashboard's invented season is cleared from admins' view, and /impact opens to every visitor as a page with no figures.** Owner asked to clear the dashboard and make it public; of three options (public with no numbers, public with the example marked, admin-only cleared) they took the first. `/impact` (and `/admin/impact`) now show what the page will hold, its honesty rules (Jaga rescued no one, failures beside successes, nothing outside the app is counted, every figure dated), the privacy limits, and SOS and the four hotlines; no number and no zero, and it says an empty page does not mean nobody needed help. The made-up season stays in `lib/impact-examples.ts` and `/dev/impact` (development and preview only). The footer line to it is on every page except home, SOS, supplies and report, and nothing in an SOS, report or alert flow links to it (spec section 16). It is not in the sitemap until A13 puts real figures on it. Malay lines are a first draft for the reviewer. — A public record of help must never carry invented counts, and a zero would read as 'nothing happened'.

- 2026-10-24 — **The search box for visitors with no account now also finds landmarks, so a visitor can search "มหาวิทยาลัยสงขลานครินทร์" like a signed-in person; this supersedes "landmark search stays signed-in only" (12 and 23 Oct).** The owner found the box on the home chooser and the SOS pin map answered nothing for a landmark. `/api/geo/search` no longer needs an account; someone without one gets 12 searches a minute per address (`lib/search-allowance.ts`, kept in one server instance's memory, best effort) and then a 429 that the box words as "try again in a moment". The box (`LocationFinder`) sends a name to that route, which looks through Jaga's tambons then OpenStreetMap and Photon together, and says "could not search" rather than "not found" when the landmark search could not be made; if the route cannot be reached it falls back to the tambons held in the browser. Pasted coordinates still work with no signal and no server, and a pasted or landmark point stays exact. The box is still an extra way to say where, never a step in front of an SOS. The upstream answers are kept a day, the button searches (never while typing), and the services see our server's address, not the visitor's. — The tambon list alone could not answer a name the visitor actually types; the risk of an open door to a free service is held by the limit and the cache.

- 2026-10-24 — **A team can have several people: owner, dispatcher and field roles, joined by a code the owner approves (step 1 of the team war room).** The owner asked for a war room for rescue and food-and-water teams on a laptop or tablet, with field members on site who can help and photograph a finished rescue; until now one account was one team. A team is still one `authority_units` row (verification, coverage, claims, points, POC phone stay on it); people are rows in `authority_unit_members` with a role (`owner`, `dispatcher`, `field`) and a status (`pending`, `active`, `removed`). The owner makes a 24-hour, single-use code (only its hash is stored); whoever redeems it waits as `pending` until the owner says yes, so a leaked code opens nothing. Every access check that compared `authority_units.user_id` now asks `unit_access(unit, account, roles)`; `authority_units.user_id` stays and still counts as owner without a member row, every existing registrant is backfilled as an active owner (three teams on jaga-dev, none with a claim) and a trigger gives each new registration its owner row, so no team is disrupted and the migration and code can go out in either order. An owner has every dispatcher power, so existing teams are simply owners. A dispatcher reads and acts on the team's board within its coverage and is rung with the owner; the POC phone tick and the people are the owner's; every phone a dispatcher reveals is logged under their own account. A field member has no board until step 2 hands them a case. Ten wrong codes an hour lock an account out; failures are counted in `audit_log`. My defaults, overrulable (the owner has not yet answered): admins do not verify individual members, the owner vouches; points stay with the team; crew positions come later, opt-in and private to the team. Still open for the owner: whether a field member may see the exact point and phone of their own assigned case (it would stretch safety rule 5's "verified authorities" to a member vouched for by the owner, so it waits for the owner and step 2). Also decided (owner, 24 Oct): the team war room map (step 3) uses the main map's basemaps (`BASEMAPS` and `setBasemap` in `lib/map.ts`: streets, terrain, satellite, satellite with terrain), not a separate style. — Teams are more than one phone, and the people at a laptop and the people on site must not share one login.

- 2026-10-24 — **Vercel stays on the free Hobby plan for the 25 Oct launch; Supabase Pro goes ahead; Vercel Pro is bought when traffic or a trigger calls for it.** The owner: few teams have registered or set locations yet, so wait for traffic before paying. Supersedes only the Vercel half of 2026-09-28 (k) and 2026-10-16; the live Supabase Pro project, the spend cap off and the rest of the launch plan are unchanged. Cost is about $25/month until Pro is bought. What Hobby means, from Vercel's own pages read on 24 Oct: asking for donations is not commercial use (so the donation feature, off by default, is allowed), but being paid to produce or host the site is; the limits are 100 GB transfer, 1M CDN requests, 1M function calls and 4 CPU-hours a month; there is no spend management; passing a limit pauses the project (visitors get 503 DEPLOYMENT_PAUSED; Vercel's docs say wait up to 30 days, or upgrade). Database cron jobs run in Supabase, not Vercel, so Hobby's once-a-day cron limit does not touch the push tick, gauges or the automated alarm. Upgrade triggers, written in `docs/runbook.md` ("Vercel's free plan limit is reached"): any Usage meter at 50%; before the season's first Warning or Evacuate for a wide area or a heavy-rain forecast; before the donation feature is switched on or any admin stipend is paid; at once on a 503 pause. The load test also reports CDN requests per visit. Dropped: the "Vercel: notify, never pause" reminder (Hobby has no spend management; it returns on upgrade). — Paying $20 a month for traffic that does not exist yet buys nothing; the real cost of Hobby is a hard stop, which is why the triggers fire early and the upgrade takes two minutes. Open: Hobby gives no warning emails that Vercel documents, so the owner looks at Vercel → Usage daily in the first weeks.

- 2026-10-24 — **A field member sees the exact point and the requester's phone of a case assigned to them, and nothing else** (owner, via AskUserQuestion; step 2 of the team war room). This rewords safety rule 5: a verified team's active members count as the team, the owner vouches for them and admins do not verify them one by one. A field member never reads vulnerable-household data, other cases, the board or any other number; the access ends when the case closes or the assignment is released, and every reveal is written to `audit_log` under their own account. — A crew on site must be able to navigate to the person and call them, and the owner is the one who knows who is on the boat.

- 2026-10-24 — **The team war room: on a laptop or a landscape tablet the team's console becomes one screen** (owner: "build step 3"; and "utilize basemaps from our main map"). The queue and the ringing offer are on the left, the live map in the middle, the picked case with its crew on the right (under the map on a tablet), the team's record along the foot. It is the same console, data and taps as the phone's board - `CaseCard`, the alarm and the team switches are the same components - and the phone and an upright tablet keep their tabbed board; a team of one sees no crew list and a record without a per-person table, so a solo owner who goes out with the crew is not asked to use any of it. The map uses the main map's four basemaps (`BASEMAPS` and `setBasemap` in `lib/map.ts`: standard, terrain, satellite, satellite with terrain) with the main map's own labels; the choice is remembered on that device only. A case picked in the list is ringed and brought into view on the map, and a tap on a mark picks it; an offer that is ringing is put in front on its own so accepting is one tap. **Live updates:** the console listens for changes to `sos_requests`, `sos_claims` and `case_assignments` (added to the `supabase_realtime` publication) and reads the board again at once; it is only a nudge, Realtime hands a row only to someone whose own read policy passes, and the 8-second poll stays because an offer moves from team to team by the clock and no row changes when it does. The record is read from `unit_member_record()` (cases each person took part in, rescues each reported, owner and dispatchers only) beside the points ledger; points stay with the team. Not built: crew positions, notes on a case, a duty roster, recording a headcount without accounts. — A dispatcher at a laptop needs to see everything at once; the person in the boat does not need any of it.

- 2026-10-24 — **Every page but the map, read against Apple's Human Interface Guidelines on a tablet** (the owner: "see all other pages on tablet view against Apple HIG, anything to improve please do"). Swept 24 pages at 1,180x820 and 820x1,180 with a probe (`.claude/local/q-higpages.mjs`) that measures what the guidelines name - 44 pt targets, readable line length, sideways scroll, text size - and then looked at the pages. The signed-in screens (admin, authority) were checked through their `/dev/*` previews, which render the real components with example data; the real routes redirect to sign-in without a session. **Three things were wrong, and one thing was my probe.** (1) **The single-purpose pages stretched to the whole 1,152 px** that `#main` opens to from 1,024 px up: Sign-in's button was 1,060 px wide, SOS's phone-number field as wide as the screen. Apple's layout guide keeps text to about 672 pt on an iPad however wide the window is, so a shared `formColumn` (`lg:mx-auto lg:max-w-2xl`, 42rem = 672 px, in `lib/ui.ts`) now holds 13 roots - sign-in, report, requests, offline, SOS and its status, supplies, the two authority registration pages, account setup and places. Centred, not at the start: the header and hotline bar run the full width and a column hanging off the left under them reads as unfinished. Nothing changes below 1,024 px, where `#main` is already 640. SOS is on the list on purpose and gained no step, field or delay (safety rule 1): the red button is still 672x80. (2) **Paragraphs ran 118-123 characters a line** on the reading pages. A base-layer rule caps a paragraph at 42rem from 1,024 px, and a bullet-row list item (`flex items-start`, an icon and a sentence in the item itself) likewise; it is in `@layer base` so any page that says its own `max-w-*` still wins, and it leaves flex and grid paragraphs, bordered or filled cards and justified rows alone, so cards, figures and dashboards keep the width. **It must stand aside for the console, and that was learnt from a failing test, not foreseen**: the console's head note is one line by design - the head must stay under 60 px so the map keeps its room - and capped at 42rem it wrapped to two and the head measured 61.6 px (`map-console`, "the turning globe at the head ... takes no room from the map"). Every capping rule now sits behind `:not(:has([data-console-page]))`, and a unit test holds that. (3) **Targets under 44 px**: the 52 source chips on How it works (39 px, now `min-h-tap`), the credits' organisation links (25 px) and the Open-Meteo attribution (30 px) - both extended with an `::after` hit area, the app's own pattern, so nothing reflows - the usage chart's "view as numbers" summary (29 px), and **MapLibre's zoom (29 px) and attribution (24 px) buttons on every map**: the app builds nine of its own besides the main one - the pin on the SOS page, the dam, the weather, the authority board and coverage, four on the admin side - and only the main map had been fixed. That rule is for touch only (`pointer: coarse`), so a laptop with a mouse is unchanged, and it names MapLibre's own control classes rather than `.maplibregl-map`, because React owns a map container's class attribute and the library's class on it cannot be relied on (my first version did, and reached the main map and missed the weather and dam maps). **Which of my own findings were not findings**: my probe counted the header's theme and bell buttons (36x48) and the home screen's inline sign-in links as too small, but each extends its hit area with an `::after` to 48-54 px on purpose; the probe now measures the pseudo-element too, and the count of real problems fell accordingly. The SVG labels at 9-10 px on How it works and Features render at 15-29 px on a tablet (they are in SVG user units and scale with the figure); only a 360 px phone renders them at 8 px, which is a phone question and is left. **Considered and not done**: widening `#main` to 672 px in portrait (it is 608 now, against Apple's ~672), which would give back ~64 px but touches the header container the phone redesign shares; the portrait header is three rows (155 px) against two in landscape (107 px), the same 13% of the screen in both; and the 11-13 px labels inside the Features page's miniature phone screens, which are pictures of an interface and not controls. **Verification, and what I could not separate**: with the change, 40 of 40 end-to-end specs for the touched pages pass; the full 107-test run had 7 failures, of which three also fail at HEAD with my changes stashed (`dam:264`, `home:403`, `map-console:957`), one was mine and is fixed (the head note above), and three are a family that waits four seconds for a WebGL marker attribute and alternates run to run - 793 failed at HEAD and 853 failed with my change, each passing in the other run - and 9 of 9 passed when repeated three times each on my code. That is evidence of timing, not proof; it is recorded as such. — The owner asked for the guidelines applied to the rest of the app; the measure and the column are what they ask of an iPad, and the test that caught my own mistake is why the console is exempt.

- 2026-10-24 — **The war room fits a laptop's window, and an approved team always has one obvious way back to its board** (owner: "the map is too small for laptop view", "make the layout on the laptop easy to use and utilize the space", "make 'see your unit's status' a highlighted section for a registered team who already been approved... let this be also on the Map page, minimal but easy to spot, only for the registered team", "the join page should have a back button to My account as well"). **Laptop:** measured in a real browser as a team owner at 1440 x 900, the first version showed a sliver of map, because three tool buttons, a large notification card, a status row, a red banner and the view tabs were stacked above it. Now the console starts under the site header and is as tall as the window; the queue (290 px), the map (all the room left) and the case with its crew and the team's record (390 px) scroll on their own; the alarm and the on-duty and phone switches are one slim row (`TeamStatusBar`, the same switches and taps as the phone's cards); the offer in words is a red pill beside the title; the three tools are one "Team tools" menu; the notification card is at the foot of the right-hand column; the basemaps and the map key are laid over the map. The page is given the whole width of the window (`data-console-page="team"`). On a tablet held sideways it keeps two columns with the case under the map. **The way back:** `my_team_entry()` answers 'board' (the owner or a dispatcher of a verified team), 'mine' (crew only) or nothing, through the role answer the header already asks for and keeps on the device (`jaga.team`); the home screen shows an approved team a highlighted card (teal-edged, "Your team", button "See your unit's status") instead of the invitation to register - on the phone, in the laptop's right column under SOS and on the laptop's first-visit screen - and keeps it while a warning is in force, when the board is needed most; a registration still waiting keeps the quiet line to its status. The map page shows one pill, "Your team's board", beside its title. Nobody else sees either, and neither opens anything: the console is closed to everyone else in the database. **Back buttons:** the join page and the crew page now offer "Back to my account" beside "Back to home". The team page puts the people and the invitation side by side on a laptop. — A dispatcher's screen is only useful if the map and the next action are on it without scrolling, and a team that finds the way back in one tap does not need to be told twice.

- 2026-10-24 — **The team war room and the team entry are approved by the owner, and the Thai and Malay review of their new lines is skipped.** The owner tested on 24 Oct: the war room's map is big enough on their laptop, and a real push notification arrived after a member was approved. They chose to skip the Thai and Malay review for the new team, crew and war room lines, so those stay as written; the Malay is still a first draft for a reviewer, whenever one is found. — Recorded so the tracker stops asking for a look that has been given.

- 2026-10-24 — **A team that registers is told to the admins' phones once, as one quiet ordinary notification** (owner: "the push notification for admin to approve a team registration didn't happen on the bell, please check"). Checked first in a real browser as an admin: the bell did show it ("Open notifications, 1 new", "1 team has registered and is waiting to be verified"), so nothing was broken there; but the bell is only read when the app is opened, and the 22 Oct choice that the bell's seven queues never push or ring left an admin with the app closed hearing nothing, so a registration could wait a day while the team waited for a call. A registration is rare and its sender is waiting, unlike the 2 a.m. flood report that choice was about, so this one queue now sends one normal notification (no ring, no sound of its own, 24-hour life) to the admins' subscribed phones the moment the team registers, naming the team and nothing else, opening the admins' units page. Built like a held field rescue: `push_unit_waiting()` hands the admins' addresses to Jaga's own server only (the gate token), once per unit (`authority_units.admins_notified_at`), only while the unit is pending; it is sent after the answer has gone back to the team, and the bell has it either way. The other six queues stay silent as decided. — A team that has done the work of registering should not wait for an admin to happen to open the app.

- 2026-10-24 — **The automated alarm on jaga-dev is back on drill until launch day** (owner: "alarm to drill"). `system_settings.auto_alarm.mode` set to `drill` on jaga-dev; the migration seed already defaults to drill, so a rebuilt or new live project starts there too. — Before launch it publishes drills only (safety rule 2); the owner switches it to real on the signals page when ready.

- 2026-10-24 — **The registration push goes at high priority and to every admin phone at once** (owner: "the notification came but a little bit late"). The first version used normal priority one phone after another; an idle Android phone holds a normal-priority push back until it next wakes, while alerts, offers and case steps were already high. Priority changes speed only, not the sound: it is still the phone's own ordinary notification, no ring. The held field-rescue push to admins still uses normal priority; say if it should match. — Nobody should be told late that a team is waiting on them.

- 2026-10-24 — **The automated alarm stays real on jaga-dev; "alarm to drill" is withdrawn** (owner: "keep it real", minutes after "alarm to drill"). `system_settings.auto_alarm.mode` set back to `real`; 3 gauges are ticked on jaga-dev, so a real alert can now publish itself after the 20-minute grace (safety rule 2). — The owner's call; the seed default stays drill for a rebuilt project.

- 2026-10-24 — **The admin team page is approved, and the LINE way in is dropped from it** (owner: "team page OK, remove LINE out from that"). No application for LINE's e-mail permission; an admin added by e-mail signs in with the e-mail link, and only an admin who asks from their own LINE-signed-in account gets the LINE message. — Applying means LINE's review and holding admins' e-mail from LINE as well, and the owner does not want to spend LINE (no paid plan).

- 2026-10-24 — **Correction, same day: both Vercel AND Supabase stay on their free plans for the 25 Oct launch, and Jaga launches on `jaga-dev`; there is no separate paid `jaga-live` project before launch.** The owner meant "Pro and Vercel" in the earlier message and I took it as Vercel alone, so the entry above wrongly kept Supabase Pro; this supersedes its Supabase half and the live-project plan of 2026-10-15 for now. Cost is $0/month. Checked on Supabase's own pages: free projects "may" be paused after a week of low activity; daily backups are Pro only (free projects are told to export their own data); 500 MB database, 5 GB egress, two free projects; the refresh-token revocation setting stays unavailable, so the iPhone home-screen admin keeps the code sign-in as its way in (2026-10-18). `jaga-dev` was 52 MB on 24 Oct. Triggers for Supabase Pro are in `docs/runbook.md` (database over 300 MB, egress over half of 5 GB, before the season's first wide Warning or Evacuate, the day a sponsor's credit arrives, any pause). Because `jaga-dev` is now the live database, `db:reset`, the full e2e run and the RLS suite are not to be run against it without asking, backups are a dump from the laptop kept off the repo, and tracker walkthrough 7 is parked. The owner has written to Supabase and Vercel asking for sponsorship; a credit would make the upgrade free. Dropped with this: the environment switch on launch day, the live-project sign-in settings, the spend-cap step (it exists only on Pro). — Paying before there is traffic buys nothing; the cost of the free plans is a possible pause and no backups, which is why the triggers fire early and a dump is taken before every migration.

- 2026-10-24 — **The free plans run for one more week: launch on 25 Oct as is, and the owner decides on Pro for Vercel and Supabase at the weekend of 31 Oct–1 Nov, with a week of real numbers and any sponsor reply in hand.** The owner wrote to Supabase and Vercel asking for sponsorship on 22 Oct and has had no reply; expects little activity in the first week. Exceptions that bring the upgrade forward, from the runbook: a Vercel meter at 50%, the Supabase database over 300 MB or egress over half of 5 GB, the first wide Warning or Evacuate or a heavy-rain forecast, any pause, a sponsor's credit. Built the same night because `jaga-dev` is the live database: `npm run db:dump` (a Postgres 17 `pg_dump` in its own conda environment `pg17`, written outside the repo; first dump 4.4 MB) and a guard that refuses `db:reset` unless `JAGA_ALLOW_RESET=1`. Not done: restore test, test-data sweep, a free second project for tests. — A week of real traffic costs nothing to wait for and tells more than a guess; the dump and the reset guard are the two protections that cannot wait for the weekend.

- 2026-10-24 — **The basemap's village and hamlet names appear only at the zoom where the tambon names do** (the owner: "the village labels should only appear when zoom in very near to a tambon boundary, right now the village labels from basemap shows even when it's in Province extent, it's too crowded"). They came back with the tambon outlines, one step past the four-province view, and so were drawn over a whole province at once. They now start at the tambon-name step (about one province filling the map). Towns, cities and roads keep the earlier step: a reader who has zoomed in a little still wants to find Hat Yai. - 2026-10-24 — **The held field-rescue push to admins matches the registration push: high priority, all phones at once** (owner: "yes match priority for field rescues"). — The same reason: a record waiting for an admin should not arrive late on an idle phone.

- 2026-10-24 — **"What Jaga can do" and the pages that describe rescue teams now include the day's team features** (owner: update the animated rescuer section and anywhere else that still describes the old team). The rescue-team section grows from six to nine animated cards - adding the team's people (a code and the owner's yes), handing a case to whoever is going, and the war room on a laptop or tablet - and the food-and-water section gains the first two. The finish card says anyone on the crew can send the photos from their own phone; the team and food-and-water intros, the home screen's register card (kept word for word the same as the features page, as a test requires), the registration form's introduction and the 'database decides who may see what' point on how Jaga works now say that a team can add its own people and what a crew member can and cannot see. - 2026-10-24 — **The team war room's header sits on the same moving weather as the map console, with the weather in words, and a siren that is grey and still until a request rings** (the owner: "on the laptop view, add on the header the weather animation like the main map page"; "add also siren animated, grey and not moving when nothing happen, but when there are people request came should show red and blue with rotating, realistic"). The sky and its veil are the map console's own, unchanged, so a word on it has the same contrast arithmetic; only its placement differs. **Laptop only**: a tablet's header is short of room and the sky is decoration. The siren is a rescuer's beacon - red and blue lamps in a dome, turning with a glow thrown around it. It is on exactly when requests are ringing for the team (the count the alarm line already says) and otherwise grey and still. **Red and blue here are the lamp of the people answering, not a status** - it says "someone is asking", never how bad - and the words beside it say the same, so nothing depends on seeing it. **Photosensitivity**: two lamps opposite each other, one turn every 2.2 s, so a viewer sees about one flash a second, well under three; a test holds a turn to 1.5 s or more, and for someone who asked for less motion it does not turn and shows the red lamp lit and still. — The war room is where a team waits for a request; the beacon is the glance that says one has come.

- 2026-10-24 — **Vercel's active CPU, not its CDN requests, is the meter that will stop the free plan first, so the Vercel trigger becomes: active CPU past 3 hours, or any other meter at 50%.** The owner's Usage page on the night of 24 Oct, before launch: fast data transfer 3.6 GB (3.6%), CDN requests 192k (19%), function invocations 94k (9%), active CPU 1h 52m (47%). That is about 70 ms of CPU a call, so the 2h 08m left is roughly 36,000 page visits at three calls a visit; one 5,000-person alert surge would take 15 to 20 minutes of it. This corrects two things written earlier the same day: that CDN requests would run out first, and that the one-minute push tick used 43,000 calls a month (it only runs while a request for help is open, push_tick in migration a7_push_dispatch). Supabase's open incident that night was elevated Management API errors (dashboard and CLI), with Postgres, data APIs and the pooler at 100% in Singapore: Jaga was not affected. The decision to stay free until the weekend of 31 Oct–1 Nov stands; the recommendation for that weekend now leans yes on Vercel Pro unless the week is very quiet. — Numbers from the real account beat an estimate; the 50% line set earlier had already been passed by CPU.
- **24 Oct 2026, the guide tour (A16, without the admin tour).** Each page for residents and rescue teams has a guide that points at its buttons and sections and says in a line or two what each is for (`lib/guide-tour.ts`, `components/GuideTour.tsx`, words in `messages/*.json` `guide`). It opens by itself on the first visit only, never dims or blocks the page, and once closed (Close, Escape, Done, or leaving) it stays closed on this device; the "Show me around" button at the end of the page opens it again. It never opens by itself on /supplies, /report or the field-rescue record, or while the area shows an alert, and there is no tour at all on SOS. A step whose button is not on the screen (a signed-in or team-only button, a panel a phone folds away) is skipped, so one tour serves every role. Not synced to the account yet (A16 asked for that); Malay lines are a first draft. — The owner asked for an introduction, not a path they must follow; help must never be one step further away.

- 2026-10-24 — **A team that only brings food and water has the whole war room, in its own words** (owner: "fix that wording and check the war room with a food-and-water-only team, make sure they have all the features"). Everything is the same console and all of it works: queue, map with the four basemaps, the status bar, crew list, handing a case to a person, the record by person, Report: delivered. Three fixes came out of it. **Words:** the record strip said "confirmed rescues" and "Rescues reported" and gave the rescue points rule; it now says deliveries for a food-and-water team, cases for one that does both, and gives the rule that matches (5 points and 1 per person for a delivery). **The offer:** an offered request for food and water was not put in front of the team, so accepting took two steps where a rescuer's takes one; it is now opened the same way, with a calm teal pill in the header and no red banner and no alarm. **Accepting:** the card of an offered case was open only while the offer was pending, so accepting it closed the card on the war room, for rescue and food-and-water teams alike, just when the estimate, the crew and the report come next; accepting now keeps it in front. — A distributor should not be a second-class user of the console, and a card that vanishes on the tap that matters is a bug.

- 2026-10-24 — **A10 (launch readiness), A13 (impact numbers), A15 (ratings, feedback and comments), A16 (guided tours) and A20 (About page) are closed on the tracker** (owner: "close these on the tracker and A13 and A15"). - **25 Oct 2026, the guide tour remembers each step, not each page (supersedes the page-level memory of 24 Oct).** A button that was hidden on earlier visits (signed out, no team yet) used to be skipped and then never shown, because the page's tour had already been closed. Now a step counts as seen only once it has been shown on this device, so it appears the first time its button is on the page; closing the tour marks the steps it offered. "Show me around" still shows every step on the page. — The owner reported that buttons which appear after sign-in were never introduced.
