Until now the rescue and food-and-water previews only drew the example board, so on a laptop they showed the war room by accident, the food-and-water one still described a team that did both, and nothing showed what a crew member sees or the owner's page for inviting people. Now: the **rescue team** and **food and water team** previews each have two tabs, the case board (the war room on a laptop, with the record in that team's own words) and **Team people** (a person waiting for a yes, a dispatcher, crew, a sample invitation code); a new role, **Crew member**, shows the crew's own phone screen with one case handed to them (on the way, arrived, show phone, report) and one the team still holds ("Take this"). Every button answers from the made-up list and sends nothing: the crew and people screens take a `preview` setting that stops their polling, their requests and their photo upload, checked in a browser by watching for requests to the server (none). The role list, the admin menu and the three languages are updated, with tests. The home screen's team card and the map pill are not in the preview, because they depend on a real sign-in. — The preview exists so the owner can judge what each person experiences; it must not lag behind what each person now gets.

## 25 Oct 2026: The map opens with a fly-in from space
Decision: on a first visit in a tab the map starts as the Earth (NASA GIBS true-colour with clouds by day, night lights after dark) and glides to the four provinces; any touch stops it; skipped for reduced motion and Data Saver. Reason: owner request; decoration only, never in the way of an SOS or an alert.
- **25 Oct 2026, no guide tour on a phone.** Under 640 px wide neither the tour nor its "Show me around" link is drawn; tablets and laptops keep both. — The owner found it annoying on the phone.

## 25 Oct 2026: Team coverage layer on the main map
Decision: the authority responsibility map (spec 2, 9) now also appears on the main app map, on its own "Team coverage" switch (off by default). It reuses the console's exact data and styling — RPC `coverage_summary` via `/api/geo/coverage`, teal where a rescue team covers, the map ink for a gap, a teal outline on the viewer's own tambons (components/authority/BoardMap.tsx). Reason: the owner asked for registered team/authority coverage to be visible on the main map, not only in the console. Shown only to verified authorities and admins; the API returns nothing to anyone else (RLS + the security-definer RPC's own role check, safety rule 5). No personal data (per-tambon team counts only). Deliberately NOT added to the public "How this map was made" explainer, because the layer is private to teams and admins.

## 25 Oct 2026: A general announcement for areas with no rescue team, with a Share Jaga button
Decision: the admin announcements composer ("general alert") gains a recipient "Areas with no rescue team yet" — every service-area tambon no verified unit covers (RPC `admin_tambons_without_team`, admin only, computed server-side so the browser never sees where people live). A verified unit of any capability counts as a team (owner's choice, so planning/support-only areas are not nagged as team-less). "Use the suggested wording" pre-fills the TH/MS/EN boxes with an editable draft, picks that recipient, and ticks a new "Show a Share Jaga button" option. Announcements may carry a `cta` ('share_app'); the bell renders a Share Jaga button under the message (reuses the existing share sheet). The owner chose the exact wording, including that pressing SOS with no registered team means the app cannot facilitate help; I flagged that this risks discouraging SOS (safety rule 1) and offered softer wording — the owner kept the literal text, which is editable per announcement and does not block SOS or hide the hotlines. Reason: the owner asked for it, to push tambons with no responders to recruit teams. Still never an alert and never a level (safety rules 2, 10); delivery is the usual single, neutral announcement push, not an alarm. Migration 20261025100000; commit fca0b0e.

- 2026-10-25 — The sea beside the river on the public gauge card and map. The owner asked for the tide next to river level on the gauge card, animated on the map, with detail on tap, reusing `coastal_gate` and no chart. I proposed exposing only the state word and the owner agreed. New public function `gauge_sea_gate()` (migration 20261025120000) returns, per tidal gauge, `open` / `narrowing` / `shut` / `unknown`, the first shut day, and when the sea forecast was issued; no sea height, no percentile. The reference table stays admin-only. The rule is the one in `lib/coastal-gate.ts`; a forecast older than 12 h or missing gives `unknown`, never `open`. On the map a small swell mark sits beside the glass (slow when narrowing, flat under a bar when shut); only a shut sea is also written on the map, the card says all of it. Seed 48 (`gauge_recession`, which carries the tidal flag) was empty on `jaga-dev` and was loaded. Reason: the owner asked, and the word answers "how long will water take to leave" without a level or a height (safety rules 2, 8). Malay lines are a first draft for the reviewer.

- 2026-10-27 — **The war room's siren turns around an upright axis, and a food-and-water board lights it in yellow** (owner: "for the food and water distributor board, when there is a case the siren should show yellow spinning horizontally instead of no light at all; also make the siren in the rescuer spin horizontally, right now it's spinning vertically"). The first siren rotated its reflector in the plane of the screen, like a wheel facing the viewer. It now turns like a real beacon seen from the side: two lamps swing from side to side, large and bright as they pass in front, small and dim round the back, with a blurred glow of the same two lamps at the edges. Measured in a real browser, a lamp moves about 19 px sideways and 0 px up or down. The pace is unchanged (one turn in 2.2 s, a flash about once a second, well under three a second) and for someone who asked for less motion it shows the front lamp lit and still. Colours: red and blue for a rescue request, amber and yellow for a request for food and water waiting for the team, grey and still when nothing is. A rescue request wins when both wait. Yellow is also the Watch level of an alert, so the siren never says a level: it is a picture of the sentence beside it, "N requests for food and water are offered to your team", and a reader who cannot see it loses nothing. — A distributor's board should show it has something waiting, in a colour that is not the rescuers' alarm.
- 2026-10-26 — Every reservoir with no gauge below it gets an overflow zone: the flow path the elevation model itself gives, cut where the water joins a channel ten times its catchment, and a band deep enough to hold one day of spill from a full reservoir (pipeline/dam_release/spill.py; METHODS.md, "A full reservoir's own zone"). — Five of the six reservoirs had no zone, and for three of them admin_send_dam_release refused to send at all, so a reservoir filling up could warn nobody; the owner asked for the agricultural reservoirs to be treated like Bang Lang.
- 2026-10-26 — The depth of that band is set by a day's spill — the catchment above the wall times the wettest day on record over it times how much runs off (0.6 for the zone, 0.9 for the outer band) — not by the whole stored volume and not by a fixed rise. — The owner's choice of three options; it scales from a 1.2 Mm³ pond to a 57 Mm³ reservoir and is bounded by what the catchment can actually deliver in a day.
- 2026-10-26 — The zone stops where the receiving channel drains ten times the reservoir's own catchment, instead of running to the sea. — Past that junction the flood is the river's, and the river has gauges and thresholds of its own; a 6 Mm³ reservoir must not put its name on Hat Yai's flood. Known consequence: Khlong Sadao's notice list falls from the interim path's 17 tambons to 2.
- 2026-10-26 — The arrival times for these five are the reach length at an assumed channel speed of 0.5 to 2 m/s, labelled as assumed wherever they are shown, rather than left out. — No gauge stands below any of them so nothing can be measured, and a notice with no arrival window tells the reader less than an honest range.
- 2026-10-26 — The design rain comes from GSMaP over each reservoir's own catchment, nine flood seasons, and a seed is refused unless that fetch has run. The rain gauges are the cross-check. — ThaiWater's daily archive holds single "days" of over 1,000 mm that are running totals, not rain; a point gauge cannot carry a single day's extreme here. Where both can be read they agree within about 10%.
- 2026-10-26 — A reservoir being near full now grades as something: watchful at 80% of capacity, releasing at 95% and still filling. — For all five the capacity at normal high water is the full capacity, so the existing "above normal high water" rule could only fire above 100%, and a reservoir at 99% and climbing graded as quiet and lit nothing on the map.
- 2026-10-27 — The automated alarm of safety rule 2's exception is extended to the reservoirs: one an admin has ticked raises a draft at 95% and filling, on every admin's phone, from the reviewed template reservoir_full_warning that names the affected tambons; if nobody acts within the grace time Jaga publishes it itself, marked as not reviewed. All five are armed (supabase/seed/44_auto_alarm_dams.sql). — The owner asked for the same road the river gauges have. The draft is Warning, never Evacuate: these reservoirs hold 1.2 to 57 Mm³ and their zones are a screening band from a 30 m elevation model, so telling a tambon to leave stays a person's decision.
- 2026-10-27 — The same road now carries the all-clear, for the river gauges as well as the reservoirs: when the water is clearly back down (a reservoir under 85% of capacity, a gauge 0.3 m under the threshold that fired, two readings in a row) and the alert has stood six hours, a "safe to return" draft is raised and, if nobody acts, published, superseding the alert it answers. — The owner asked that an alert be updated when the water goes down instead of standing with its level until somebody noticed. The margins and the six hours are hysteresis: without them a reading wobbling across the line would flap between warning and all-clear, and an alert cleared minutes after it was raised is a false alarm for a person to withdraw, not for the machine to call safe.
- 2026-10-27 — Only an alert the automated path published itself is cleared automatically. — A person's alert is a person's to take down; the machine never overrules a human issuer.
- 2026-10-27 — Second review of the phone against Apple's HIG, on the live site after launch (360x640 and 390x844, 14 routes): the structure holds (header 59 px, hotlines 57, tab bar 62, nothing scrolls sideways, no target under 44 px except ones whose hit area is already stretched). Three things fixed: **the bell's badge hid the bell** once there were ten notices ("9+" is wider than the one digit it was drawn for and sat on the icon), so it now sits on the icon's corner at 13 px; the three step labels on "What Jaga can do" were 11 px, now 13; the workflow labels on "How Jaga works" were 12 px, now 14. Left alone on purpose: the 9-10 px numbers and words inside drawings (a marker's digit, labels in the home-help picture), which are illustration, not copy, and the how-it-works page's length. — A badge sized for one digit fails the first time the count reaches two, which only the live data could show.
- 2026-10-27 — The reservoirs' thresholds and the automatic-alarm switch live on /admin/signals, in a panel the shape of a river gauge's, and the admins own both. — A threshold only a developer can move is not a threshold the people who run the app own; the figures were set from the laptop and armed by a seed, which the owner rightly called out. Clearing a threshold switches that rule off rather than being refused, so a reservoir can be graded on release, inflow and rise alone.
- 2026-10-27 — The war room keeps its reservoir rows but shows only the ones asking for something: a notice to review, a grade that is not quiet, a reading waiting for confirmation, a stale feed, or no reading at all. The quiet ones are one line pointing at the signals panel. — The owner suggested removing the section once the reservoirs had their own place; the row there is the only place an admin can mark a confirmed release reviewed, and that decision belongs on the screen they keep open during an event.

- 2026-10-27 — **The guide tour's card never covers what it points at** (the owner: "review and improve" the tablet view, after the other sessions' work landed). Re-reading the tablet pages found one real defect among the new work: on the map the tour's "Map" step put its card on top of the map - on a landscape iPad across the top of the map and the readout strip, and in portrait **across the map's own toolbar**, hiding the reset and my-places buttons that the tour goes on to describe. The cause is the card's placement: it was centred horizontally and pinned to the top or bottom of the window, whichever half the target's centre was *not* in. That is right for a small target (a readout, a button) and cannot be right for the map, which fills most of an iPad window, so wherever it goes it lands on it. Apple's guidance for a popover that annotates something is that the annotated thing stays visible, controls included. The rule is now `placeCard` in `lib/guide-tour.ts`, pure so that it can be tested (the component's placement had no test at all): **the old choice is kept whenever it is clear of the target** (so small targets behave exactly as before), otherwise the other side of the window, otherwise **beside** the target if the whole card fits (a landscape iPad has ~400 px free to the right of the map, over the answer panel), and only if nothing fits - a portrait iPad, where the map is 608 px of 820 and nothing is either side - the bottom edge, which leaves the toolbar and zoom buttons along the top clear. **Measured on the page** at four iPad sizes: in landscape the card now covers 0 px of the map and 0 of its 4 toolbar buttons (it covered them all); in portrait it still overlaps the lower half of the map, unavoidably, but covers none of the toolbar. Five unit tests hold the geometries it was measured on. **Not changed, and said so**: the tour opens by itself on a first visit to every page, so on a portrait iPad a first-time visitor's first sight of the map is still partly covered, which only a different pattern would fix (a bottom sheet, or a tour that waits to be asked); that is a product decision for the owner, not a bug. — The tour is the other session's feature; the fix touches only its placement.

- 2026-10-27 — **A15 is built: ratings, feedback and the public comments page** (owner: "Build A15 now"). This supersedes the entry of 24 Oct that closed A15 on the tracker as not planned. Built as spec section 17 describes, which the owner approved on 16 Oct 2026 from a drawing of the phone and laptop screens, so nothing of the shape was re-proposed: `/feedback` collects and publishes, the four-step form (faces, what you love, what should be better, then the two consents) requires nothing, and the figures and approved comments sit under it. Migrations `20261027120000_a15_feedback` and `20261027140000_a15_line_feedback`. — The owner asked for it, and the season review it feeds is what decides what gets built next.
- 2026-10-27 — **Only `public_feedback()` reaches a visitor, and it returns four things**: the display name the sender chose, the two notes, and the chips, plus the month and the rating. Not the district, the role, the kind of phone, the language, the contact, the case, or the moment it was sent. — The row holds enough to identify somebody in a tambon of 4,000 people; the page needs none of it. Held by `tests/rls/feedback.test.ts`, which asserts the exact column list rather than the absence of one column, so a later `select *` cannot quietly widen it.
- 2026-10-27 — **A withdrawal key is stored only as its SHA-256, like an SOS token, and the key itself lives on the phone that sent the comment.** A cleared browser therefore loses the power to withdraw an anonymous comment; an admin can still take it down on request. — Spec 17 gives an anonymous sender the same power over their own published words as a signed-in one, and the only thing that can stand for "the person who wrote this" without an account is a secret the phone kept. The column is named `withdraw_key_hash` rather than the spec's `withdraw_key` to say so.
- 2026-10-27 — **A published figure needs 20 answers in the period, applied per period and per month; below it the page says how many more it needs** (`system_settings.feedback_public.min_answers`). — An average of 4.6 from seven answers is a mood, not a measurement, and a page of Jaga's own must not dress one up as one. "We are eight answers short" is itself an honest figure. The admins' own trends carry no such floor, with the count printed beside each one: a figure to act on is not a figure to publish.
- 2026-10-27 — **A comment with no words in it is never offered for the page, and an admin can never approve one whose sender did not offer it.** `admin_feedback_publish` refuses both, and refuses to hand a consent back on the sender's behalf. — The consent is the sender's, not the console's. A bare rating has nothing to show anyway.
- 2026-10-27 — **The invitation stops where help begins; the door never does.** No link and no ask on the SOS, food-and-water, report or alert screens, and none anywhere while a place of this person's is at Warning or Evacuate - while `/feedback` stays reachable at its address throughout. The tense check (`lib/use-tense.ts`) reads only the last alert status already on the phone and asks the server nothing, so it costs no request and works offline. — The donation discipline of safety rule 9, applied to the same risk: nothing may come between a person and help, and a quiet line at the foot of a page is still something.
- 2026-10-27 — **The quiet ask lives at the foot of "My requests" and on a team's own people page, not inside the SOS flow.** It appears once nothing of theirs is still open and something has finished (a request closed and confirmed, a report ended, a team's case confirmed), then not again for 30 days on that phone. — Spec 17 asks for the ask "after" those moments and forbids it on those screens; the page where somebody reads how their request went is the one place that is both. A team's case board was rejected for it: nothing invites a dispatcher to rate the app with a request waiting.
- 2026-10-27 — **The offline queue now sends requests for help before anything else** (`lib/queue.ts`, `BAND`), and its keys carry a counter so "oldest first" inside a band is a real guarantee rather than one that usually holds. — A15 put a third kind in the queue. Somebody with no signal can rate the app at ten past and press SOS at twenty past; strict time order would have spent the first round trip of a recovered connection on the rating (safety rule 1). Two items added in the same millisecond used to be separated by a random tail, so an SOS and its detail step could come out in either order.
- 2026-10-27 — **In LINE the chat asks the rating only, as five quick replies, and points at `/feedback` for the rest** (`line_feedback`, migration 20261027140000). A rating sent in a chat carries no consent to be shown and no display name, so it is admins-only like any other unoffered comment. **The rich menu is unchanged**: it has six buttons in two rows of three, the layout the owner approved on 16 Oct, and a seventh needs it redrawn and republished, which is the owner's call. Until then the way in from the chat is a plain word ("feedback", "ความเห็น", "pendapat"...), and the menu's entry would be a `feedback` postback the handler already answers, so adding the button later needs no code. — A chat can carry one question well and not four; and an approved design is not changed without asking.
- 2026-10-27 — **Contacts are cleared the moment an item is closed, and a nightly job anonymises everything around an answer after 12 months** (`feedback_retention`, 18:23 UTC). An approved comment keeps only what was consented to and already shown - the display name and the words - and keeps its withdrawal key; its account link goes, so from a year on the phone's key is the only way back to it. A comment nobody consented to show loses its words too; ratings, chips, month, screen and language stay as figures. — Spec 17's retention rule, with the one exception it implies: a published comment must stay withdrawable for as long as it is published.

- 2026-10-27 — **The guide tour outlines what lives in the header and the hotline bar, and puts its card beside them** (a second finding of the same tablet review, after the card-placement entry above). Walking every step of every tour on an iPad showed that the home screen's bell, weather and hotlines steps - three of seven - had **no outline at all**: the ring was clipped to the band between the frozen header and the hotline bar so that a tall target could never run over the hotlines, and a target that lives *in* the header or the bar is outside that band by definition, so the card said "this is the bell" and pointed at nothing. A target inside `header` or `footer` is now outlined where it is (the ring is `z-30`, above the header's `z-20` and the bar's `z-10`). Its card sits on the **near** side - just under the bell, just above the hotlines - rather than across the window from it, which is what the old far-side rule would have done now that the target has a ring. Two details that the first draft got wrong and a test caught: for a target in the bar the card is pinned 8 px from it by design, so asking for the usual 14 px of breathing room judged a correctly placed card to be colliding and sent it to the other end of the window; the margin is zero there, the bar itself being the separation. And my first test copied the hotlines rectangle from the probe, which reports the *drawn* ring with its 6 px padding, so the target looked 6 px higher than it is; the test now uses the element's real box. Measured on the page: bell and weather cards under the header, hotlines card above the bar, 0 px of overlap on every step in both orientations. **Known and left**: the map tour's "layers" step targets the whole layers column, which on a landscape iPad is a full-width row; its card must overlap some of it, and it hides 3 of 19 controls there. — Same feature, same owner request ("review and improve"), same rule: the thing being pointed at stays visible.

The tour bar replaces the map's tools row and spans the full width of the map (`inset-x-2`), so its reset button sits at the far right - under the readout column, which is fixed to the map's top-right corner. A new `data-map-tour="on"` flag on the console (set only while touring) drops the readouts to 4.75rem from the top, clearing the bar; they already scroll inside themselves, so they give up height, not content, and move only while the tour is open. Laptop full screen only - the normal console keeps the tour bar inside the map frame, below the readout strip, so there is no overlap there. — The reset button must be reachable; moving the readouts is the owner's own fix and the least that does it.

- 2026-10-28 — **On a tablet held sideways the layers panel is the first column and the map is as tall as the window's band** (the owner: the layers panel "is in the middle, not looking good, make it align left side", and "the map looks too small, make it extend the length vertical"). Two separate things, both measured on an iPad 11 (1,180x820) first. **The panel**: the rail under the map is a full-width row of three columns, and it flowed its blocks in the order they are written - my places, the layers, the satellite switch, the view row, the method card - so the one-line "my places" switch took the first cell and pushed the layers panel, 506 px tall, into the middle column, beside a blank 450 px under the switch. The blocks are now **placed by name**: my places at the top of the first column, the layers under it and spanning the rows beside it, the satellite switch and the view row stacked in the second column, the method card in the third. The last row is `1fr` so that a tall layers panel stretches nothing beside it, and the method card spans two rows so its height does not set the first row's (the first draft left a 40 px gap under the satellite switch where 12 belongs). With no saved places there is no "my places" switch, its row has no height, and the layers start there. DOM order, and so tab order, is unchanged - the placement is CSS only - and anything the rail gains later falls into the first free cell. **The map**: on 24 Oct, in the HIG pass, it was sized so that the whole map and the answer panel fit the first screen, `100dvh - 29rem`: 356 px on an iPad 11, 288 on a mini, a third of the window. I called that a trade then and it was the wrong one. It is now `100dvh - header - hotline bar - 1.5rem`, using the two heights that `DashboardView` already measures into `--jaga-header` and `--jaga-hotlines` on every load, so it follows the real window (two lines of Thai or one of English, an enlarged text size) rather than a guess: **619 px on an iPad 11 (was 356), 543 on a mini (288), 823 on a 13" (560)**, the answer panel the same. The page now opens on the top of the map and a short scroll takes the chrome above it out of the way and leaves the map filling the band exactly; the cost is that the first screen no longer holds the whole of it, which is what was asked. Nothing else moved: a laptop with a mouse, an iPad held upright and a phone were measured before and after and are unchanged (all of this sits in the tablet-sideways block and behind `:not([data-map-full])`), and full screen is untouched. Three tests hold the placement and the height. — Not verified by the e2e suite: jaga-dev is the live database and I do not run it there; the unit tests that read this stylesheet pass (45) and the page was measured at three iPad sizes.

- 2026-10-28 — **On a laptop a switch folds the readout strip away, and folded is the default** (the owner: "make this part collapsed so will have more height for the map ... increase the map, gaining space from the collapsed one, to fill the screen"). A "แสดง/ซ่อนการ์ดสรุป" switch now rides at the end of the control bar above the map (the hazard-and-province line), **not in a row of its own** — a dedicated fold line was built first and measured, and it ate half the gain (the map grew 38 px, not 90), so the switch was moved onto a line that was already there. Folded, the strip is `display:none`, its auto grid row collapses to nothing, and the map row — the `1fr` — takes back the full height (measured: +90 px on a 1,536×820 window). The choice is kept per browser (`jaga.map.tiles.open`); unset means folded, so a laptop opens on the most map. Laptop only, and off full screen: a phone and a tablet never render the switch and keep the strip as it was, and in full screen the readouts float on the map as before (the root carries `data-tiles` only when `laptop` and not full screen). — The owner asked for more map and for the strip collapsed; putting the switch on an existing bar hands the map the strip's whole height rather than trading it for a toggle bar. (2) `map.console.note` is shortened in all three languages ("Public data by subdistrict — no exact locations or personal details.") to leave the row more room. (3) Malay runs wider and its switch would not fit the head row (it wrapped to a second line), so in Malay the switch moves to the left of the hazard bar and the pickers open to its right on the same row (the owner, with a screenshot: "bring the button to the left and when clicked, the hidden buttons appear on the same row"); every other language keeps the switch on the head row with the whole bar folding away. One `renderFoldSwitch` placed by `locale`; the fold is split with `:lang(ms)` — `[data-map-console]:not(:lang(ms))[data-filters="shut"] [data-console-area="hazard"]{display:none}` for the rest, `[data-map-console]:lang(ms)[data-filters="shut"] [data-hazard-fields]{display:none}` for Malay. Measured on both th and ms. — Committed mine-only from a shared working tree (another session's rivers-layer work was in flight); the mechanism and storage are unchanged from the entries above.

- 2026-10-28 — **The map gains a river-body layer: where water normally sits, from OpenStreetMap, on its own "Rivers" chip** (the owner asked whether an accurate river body could be drawn from the DEM or anything we have). A DEM cannot give it: flow accumulation gives drainage centrelines and HAND gives height above the nearest stream, but neither gives the water-surface **width** of a channel, and a flat water surface in a 30 m DEM is noise, not a bank. So the layer is vector water, not terrain-derived. **Source**: OpenStreetMap via the public Overpass API (free, no account; the same tooling safe_places and dam_release use) — the water **areas** (`natural=water`, `water=*`, `waterway=riverbank`, `landuse=reservoir`, ways and relations) as digitised, plus the `waterway=river|canal|stream` **centrelines** buffered by a half-width (river 8 m, canal 4 m, stream 2.5 m) so the narrow channels OSM maps only as a line still show and the main rivers (Pattani, Sai Buri, Kolok, Bang Nara) always appear even where no bank polygon is drawn. (Rejected: JRC Global Surface Water is 30 m, blocky and needs Earth Engine, whose quota is spent; the SAR flood module masks permanent water out, so its frequency raster cannot be thresholded into rivers.) New `pipeline/rivers` module (`fetch`, `build`) unions the water in Web Mercator metres and tiles it to one PMTiles file with the pure-Python `pmtiles` + `mapbox-vector-tile` builder modelled on `hazard/tiles.py` — no tippecanoe; `build` prints the `r2sync push` + `manifest river-body` commands that publish it under `hazard/river-body/<date>.pmtiles`. In the app it is a context layer on its own chip ("Rivers"), **off by default** and the same in either view, wired like the hazard layer (`lib/river-layer.ts`, manifest key `river-body`); it is drawn beneath the hazard classes in the **map water colour** (`#1b6e8c` day / `#7ec8e3` night, `lib/dam-map.ts`), which by rule shows no status, and it carries a "How this map was made" point and a legend entry. The chip and its home tile stay hidden until the tiles are published. It is **never** a flood extent and never an all-clear (safety rule 10): the copy says it is where water usually is, not where a flood will reach, and never that anywhere is safe. — The owner wanted the real river body on the map; OSM gives the accurate shape with no new service or cost, and the buffered centrelines backstop the uneven coverage in the deep south. Not verified by the e2e suite (jaga-dev is the live database); typecheck, lint and the unit tests pass (flood-risk-map, river-layer), as do the new pipeline tests (`tests/test_rivers.py`).

Thai/English only (Malay's switch is on the hazard bar). — A placement tweak on the entries above; nothing else changed.

- 2026-10-28 — **On the laptop the basemap chooser moves onto the map itself, as full screen has had since 18 Oct** (the owner: "Like the Full map view, bring the buttons of the basemap be inside the map itself, once clicked then it shows the options. move the Map info up to fill the space"). The in-map basemap picker (a button at the map's bottom-left that opens the four choices) is no longer gated to full screen - it renders on the whole console (`onConsole`), and the 3D and satellite caveats now travel inside its panel so moving it drops no warning (safety rule 8). Its copy in the side rail is hidden on every console (`[data-console-area="side"] [data-basemap-panel]{display:none}`), and the "what's on the map" panel below rises into the gap. The phone is untouched: it has no in-map picker, so the rail's basemap stays there.

The "Show my places on the map" switch is gone from the left rail, and `showMine` is a constant `true` rather than state. Home and the watch places show from the first paint whenever there are any; their legend stays in the right-hand panel. — Their points are still the person's own, read from their own session and never part of the public map data (safety rule 6); nothing about who may see them changes.

- 2026-10-29 — **On the laptop the right panel's fold button moves into the panel's top-right corner, so the content fills the space it used to leave** (the owner: "move the info section on the right panel up to fill the space, the toggle button can be at the same level of the first text row ... right now it kept empty space row just for the toggle button"). Open, `[data-side-bar]` is lifted out of the flex flow and pinned to the panel's top-right (`position: absolute`); the content starts at the top and the first thing in it — the join or install card, the tap hint, or the safe-places switch, each marked so a sibling reset pads only the first — is held clear of the button, while the rows below keep the full width. Only while open: folded, the button is still the strip the panel collapses to. This also fixed a pre-existing bug it uncovered: folded, the button is wrapped in a `console:contents` span, which the `> *:not([data-side-bar])` hide rule did not spare, so it inherited `visibility: hidden` and only came back on a peek-hover; the rule now also spares a child that `:has([data-side-bar])`, so the re-open button is always visible in the folded strip. — Verified at 1,536×820: the first card's content ends at the button's left edge with no overlap, the content rose 60 px into the freed row, and the folded button is visible.

- 2026-10-29 — **The right panel's fold button now merges with the panel's edge and freezes while the panel scrolls** (the owner: "make toggle button merge boundary with the larger right panel" and "toggle button should freeze when scrolling"). It was `position: absolute` in the panel's top-right, which scrolled away with the content and sat a scrollbar's width in from the panel's edge. It is now a zero-height `position: sticky` strip pinned to the top (`inset-block-start: 0`), full width so its button rests at the inline end and spills out of the strip over the content, so it still takes no row of its own and stays in its corner as the content scrolls under it. To meet the panel's own boundary it is pulled out over the reserved scrollbar gutter by `margin-inline-end: calc(-1 * var(--side-gutter))`; `--side-gutter` is measured in DashboardView with a ResizeObserver (`offsetWidth - clientWidth`) rather than guessed, because a thin scrollbar is a different width on each browser and zero where scrollbars overlay — a fixed value would leave a gap on one machine and hang off the edge on another. It also sheds the border and rounding on its two outer edges and keeps them on the inner corner, so it reads as a notch of the panel, not a floating pill. Open state only; folded, it is still the strip the panel collapses to. — Verified at 1,536×820: the button's right edge meets the panel's (gap 0), it stays at the top after scrolling the panel 300 px, the first card still clears it, and the folded re-open button is still visible.

- 2026-10-29 — **The river body is drawn on top of the flood-risk shading and is on by default** (the owner: "Make the river body on top of the risk map, and turned on by default"). In `MapView` the river-fill layer is now added after the hazard fill (before the same `jaga-tambon-line` anchor), so it sits above the risk classes while staying beneath the satellite flood, the alert colours and the rest; its comment is updated to say so. In `DashboardView`, `showRiver` starts `true` instead of only when `?focus=river` opens the map. It is still the map's water colour by day and night, never an alert colour, still gated by `riverOnOffer` so the chip and layer appear only once the tiles are published — which they now are (published 29 Oct). — The river body is permanent water, a context layer, not a flood extent (safety rule 10): drawing it over the risk shading shows the real channel without ever reading as a flood or an all-clear.

- 2026-10-29 — **The sub-district (tambon) outlines become a switch in the map panel: off by default in the risk view, on by default in the live view** (the owner: "Add tambon layer in the panel for optional to turn on, but by default should turn off, in the risk view. But in the Now view, should turn on by default"). A new `tambon` focus/chip (`lib/map-focus.ts`, `lib/hazards.ts` `MAP_LAYERS`) with its own icon, legend swatch and copy in all three languages. The default follows the view: `showTambon = tambonChoice ?? mode === "live"`, where `tambonChoice` is `null` until the reader uses the switch, after which their choice holds across a change of view (the same pattern as a picked map mode). The outline layer (`jaga-tambon-line`) stays in the style either way — it is the anchor the fills are inserted before — so a new `tambonLines` prop on `MapView` toggles its **visibility**, not its presence; every other `MapView` caller leaves the prop undefined and keeps the always-on base behaviour. The outlines are not a home-screen tile (`TILE_FOCUSES` excludes `tambon`): they are a base reference layer, not a flood feature. A "How this map was made" point was added to match. — The outlines are the official administrative boundaries already on the map (Royal Thai Survey Department, via OCHA); drawing them is reference cartography, never a status (safety rule 10). The risk view stays uncluttered so the risk colours read; the live view shows which tambon an alert or report falls in.

- 2026-11-07 — **The Malay round-2 quality review is finished: ten corrections to the resident/visitor Malay** (the owner: "finish the Malay", and chose — AskUserQuestion — that I review it directly rather than re-run Gemini). Round 2 (15 Oct) had reviewed only 8 of 35 batches before Gemini's quota ran out; by now the app is launched and the 15-Oct batches were stale, so the whole resident/visitor Malay surface was re-reviewed fresh from the current `messages/*.json`: 2,477 lines across the resident/visitor namespaces, read in seven disjoint slices by seven Claude reviewers against the **Thai as source of truth** with English as cross-check, every proposed change then verified by me against the Thai before applying. Ten corrections to `messages/ms.json`, all meaning/fixed-term, no stylistic churn: three Watch-level labels read "Awas" instead of the fixed term **Berjaga-jaga** (`howItWorks.figures.gauge.legend.watch`, `.gauge.alt`, `.workflow.rows.gauges.product`); `push.sound.repeat` named the levels as "berpindah"/"amaran" rather than **Pindah**/**Amaran**; `journey.rescue.wait.accepted` told a waiting SOS sender their request "sudah sampai kepada pasukan" (already reached a team) when the Thai says only that rescuers **can see** it while a team is still being sought — corrected to "boleh melihat permintaan anda"; `dam.legend.open` invented a "carta 14 hari" not in the Thai (now "carta terdahulu"); `dam.path.creditSpill` used "berteduh" (take shelter) for the map's **shaded** area (now "berlorek", matching the sibling key); `province.levels.notInService` wrote an invented acronym "JPBM" for **DDPM**; `capabilities.items.supplies.text` used the banned "terkepung" for cut-off-by-water (now "tidak boleh keluar"); and `howItWorks.science.stats.seasons` had a garbled stray word "numbor". **Rejected one** proposal (`impactPublic.will.three`, tambon→mukim): app-wide the two renderings of ตำบล are genuinely mixed and "tambon" is the more common (83 vs 66), so a one-line nudge is churn, not a consistency win. **Not touched** (out of the resident/visitor scope the owner set): `authorityRegister.capability.supplies.hint` still carries the banned "terkepung", and the `/about` page copy lives outside this repo (private bucket at build time), so its Malay cannot be edited here. Templates were already covered by round-2 batch 01 and the later Thai-review migration, so no DB migration is needed and the live database is untouched. — The owner asked for Gemini originally as a stopgap, but rounds 1–2 showed flash leaks Thai script, inflates certainty and drops hedges; a direct review with the Thai deciding is both more capable and safer for live safety copy. Verified: typecheck, lint, and the 227 unit tests across the eight changed namespaces pass; full key and placeholder parity across th/ms/en held (4,563 keys each). A Patani Malay human reviewer is still the real next pass (owner, still open).

- 2026-11-07 — **The /about founder page is translated into Malay (with Gemini), kept out of the public repo** (the owner: "use Gemini to also build the About page in Malay? and still keep from public repo?"). The page's copy is not in this repository — it lives in the private data repo `pipeline/data/about/` and is pushed to the `site-content` Supabase bucket, which the build's prebuild (`scripts/fetch-content.mjs`) fetches into `messages/about/`; so the Malay was produced the same way and never enters the public code. Gemini (Antigravity `agy`, gemini-3.8-flash-high) translated the 121 translatable strings of the 141-key `about` namespace in five batches, given **both Thai and English with the Thai deciding** (the round-1/2 lesson), with a bio-appropriate brief: first person ("saya"), warm and professional not bureaucratic, Rumi only, and keep every personal/place/employer name, programme and product name (THEOS-2, CIMS, LinkedIn, ArcPy, IATA, ABET…), number, year and the hotline numbers 1784/1669/191/199 exactly. Structural fields (`road.order`, `check.at`, hrefs, `portrait`, the names) were excluded from translation and left as-is. Every line was then checked against the Thai: hotlines map correctly, the auto-alarm exception and the "no forecast is certain" limits are faithful with no added certainty, 0 Thai-script leaks, and the three job titles/training certs that are English in the Thai stayed English. `link` was fixed to the Malay title. Applied to `pipeline/data/about/ms.json` (committed in the private data repo, 73bdf5d) and pushed to the bucket with `npm run content:push`; it goes live at the next Vercel deploy. — The owner asked for Gemini here and the page is lower-risk than the safety screens (a bio, no alert levels); giving it the Thai as well as the English avoided the round-1/2 failure of deleting correct wording the thin English omitted. Verified: the About unit test (13) and `about` key parity (141 across th/ms/en) pass.

- 2026-11-07 — **The launch dry-run replay tool is built as `pipeline/replay`** (A10; owner picked it from the "do-now" list). It reads the observed November 2025 gauges and Bang Lang dam from the tidy archive (`pipeline/data/tidy/<variable>/<thaiwater_id>/<YYYY-MM>.parquet`) and feeds them into `SUPABASE_DB_URL` with the clock shifted to now at a configurable `--speed` (default 48×: a day in 30 min; a 4-day window ≈ 2½ h, matching the planned dry run), so the signal dashboard rises, a ticked gauge crosses its threshold, and the automated alarm rings as a drill. Writes gauges/rain → `public.observations` (matched by `thaiwater_id`) and dam → `public.dam_readings` (by `thaiwater_hourly_id`) — the same tables the live feeds write and the alarm reads. While a run is going it pauses the ingestion crons `jaga-gauge-readings`, `jaga-dam-readings`, `jaga-signal-forecasts` via `cron.alter_job(active := false)` and restores them on exit (or `replay restore`); it leaves `jaga-auto-alarm` and `jaga-push-tick` running, since those are what is rehearsed. Safety: the default `plan` command writes nothing; `run` refuses without `--yes` (or `JAGA_REPLAY_CONFIRM=1`), names the project ref/host first, and refuses a target equal to `LIVE_DB_URL` (the same guard as `scripts/db.mjs`); it only ever writes `observations` and `dam_readings`, never alerts/SOS/accounts, and can never publish an alert (safety rule 2). Adds one dependency to the pipeline, `psycopg[binary]` (free, no account). Built and verified by `plan` against the real archive (26,726 readings over a 4-day window) and 10 unit tests for the pure helpers; **not run against jaga-dev** (the owner runs it for the dry run, since jaga-dev is the live DB). — The dry run needs the Nov 2025 season played through the live tables with the crons held back, and it must be impossible to point at the live database by accident. Not yet: dam-release notices (raised inside the paused `dam_fetch()`), and a threshold-crossing preview in `plan`.

- 2026-11-08 — **A daily weather brief on the home screen, and severe weather as a notification** (the owner: "sending the notification to the users who selected their location everyday in the morning telling what is the overall weather in their area today... and if there is Severe weather, the alert also should be sent as notification"). Four choices the owner made when asked: (1) the **daily brief is an in-app card only, no push** — it is drawn on the home screen under the status for the place they already chose, built for the 06:30 Bangkok morning but worded for whatever hour they open it ("today" / "the rest of today" / "tonight"); (2) **severe weather is pushed automatically**, as advisory weather and never as an alert; (3) the brief's day is built for **06:30 Asia/Bangkok**; (4) the kinds pushed are **heavy rain, thunderstorm and strong wind**. Built as `lib/morning-brief.ts` + `components/home/MorningBrief.tsx` (the card, over the existing animated `WeatherScene`, entrance movement only and none of it under reduced motion), `lib/weather-advisory.ts` + `lib/weather-advisory-server.ts` + `/api/weather/advisories` (the hourly run) and migration `..a16_weather_advisory` (tables `weather_advisories`, `weather_push_log`; `weather_cells`, `weather_advisory_push`, `weather_tick`, hourly pg_cron at :05, all behind the A7 push-gate token). Thresholds, provisional and in one place: an hour over 15 mm or six hours over 40 mm, a thundery hour the model gives at least 60%, a gust over 60 km/h; at most one notification per tambon per kind per 12 h, and only the most serious kind when several are forecast at once. — The owner's reason is adoption: a reason to open Jaga on a quiet day means the app is familiar on the day it matters. Safety rule 2 is kept because none of this publishes an alert: no level is set, no tambon changes colour, the words say "a weather notification, not a flood alert" and name Open-Meteo with the time of its model data (rule 8), and the notification carries the ordinary notice vibration with no repeat knocks, which is what the owner asked for ("the sound of the alert should be the same as Normal alert not as alarm"). Two limits of the web, told to the owner rather than worked around: a closed app's notification sound cannot be chosen at all (the phone plays its own; only the vibration and the knocking are ours), and a notification cannot carry an animation, so the animation is the card. Two judgment calls of mine, open to being overruled: **nothing is sent between 23:00 and 05:00 Bangkok** (a forecast is not worth waking a street for, and real night risk is the alert's and the alarm's job), and **nothing is sent to a tambon at Warning or Evacuate** (there the alert is the message of the day; the card is not drawn either). Not applied to any database and the RLS suite not run: `jaga-dev` is the live database and the owner has not yet said I may.

- 2026-11-09 — **The automated alarm reads a gauge's own Warning level, not its bank** (the owner, after asking why no alarm rang while gauges showed amber on the map). `auto_alarm_reading()` graded a ticked gauge that is not its zone's key gauge on `coalesce(gauge_levels.evacuate_m, stations.warning_m)` — its bank, the level at which people leave — so it raised a Warning draft only once the river was already there. The zone's key gauge was never affected: it reads `thresholds.key_level_m`, which `admin_set_gauge_levels` keeps equal to that gauge's row in `gauge_levels`. The public map has graded on `gauge_levels` since `20261021120000_a10_station_status_gauge_levels`, so the two disagreed in the one direction that matters: on jaga-dev's five ticked gauges the alarm sat 0.29 m above the shown Warning on X.90, 0.75 m on X.274 and 1.55 m on X.77, and a gauge could stand amber on the public map for hours with no draft on any admin's phone. Migration `20261108230500_a10_auto_alarm_gauge_warning` makes a non-key gauge fire at `coalesce(gauge_levels.warning_m, gauge_levels.evacuate_m, stations.warning_m)`. Unchanged: a non-key gauge still never raises more than Warning on its own (the owner, 14 Oct 2026), the key gauge's two levels, the freshness, confirming-readings and quiet-window guards, and the re-read at the end of the grace time; the all-clear takes its bar from the event it is clearing (`e.threshold + clear.gauge_margin_m`), so the river now has to fall back to the Warning it was raised at. The number only ever moves down, so the alarm can ring earlier and never later, and a person still sends or dismisses the draft (safety rule 2). Verified against jaga-dev in a rolled-back transaction, nine cases before and after; RLS test `tests/rls/gauge-levels.test.ts` rewritten for the new rule; runbook updated. **Not applied to any database**: jaga-dev is the live one and this changes when a real alarm fires, so the owner decides when it goes on. — A warning that waits for the evacuation level is not a warning; the map and the alarm must read the same number.

## 2026-11-09 — Laptop map weather strip reads like the home weather card

On a laptop the map console's weather sky in the **light theme** came out as a washed, muddy grey: to carry the console's dark theme ink at 4.5:1 the veil bleached every sky toward near-white (lib/map-console.ts, `consoleVeil`). The owner, comparing it to the new home-screen weather card: the strip's sky "is not clear ... while the weather card for the daily brief ... appear good, and clear." Decision: the **map console** sky now uses the weather card's own recipe — veil it by the *scene*, not the theme (the card's scrim, `SCRIM_COLOR`/`SCRIM_ALPHA` 0.45), and write the loose text on it in the sky's own ink (white on dark/rainy skies, dark slate `#13293A` on bright ones), so a rainy sky stays deep and a clear one stays blue in either app theme. Only the loose text follows the sky (title, the public-data note, the hazard label, the weather in words); every pill and the province picker carry a solid background of their own and keep their usual colours. The **war-room** header bar (`data-sky-bar`) is unchanged — its header is the theme's ink, so it keeps the theme veil. ConsoleSky hands `--sky-scrim`/`--sky-scrim-alpha` and `data-console-sky-ink` down to app/globals.css, which picks the veil and ink; the scene's scrim already guarantees that ink at 4.5:1 on every sky (tests/unit/weather-scene.test.ts), so contrast holds. Verified live at 1,536×820 in the light theme (showers: veil `rgba(9,20,30,.45)`, white ink) and both ink directions by computed style; typecheck, lint and 144 map/console/weather-scene unit tests pass, with a new map-console assertion locking the rule in. — One picture of the weather in the app: the strip should read like the card, not wash out to carry theme ink.

- 2026-11-09 — **The automated alarm publishes at once and tells the admins afterwards** (the owner: “I want the alert goes live no need to wait for admin, only notify admin via notification bell when the alarm sent already, so Admin can understand the alarm went, notify to all Admins if there are more than one”). This supersedes the 14 Oct 2026 exception to safety rule 2, which drafted, rang every admin and published only if nobody acted within 20 minutes. Now a ticked river gauge, a ticked reservoir **and the automatic all-clear** (the owner picked all three when asked) publish in the tick that finds them, from the reviewed template, marked not reviewed and written to `audit_log`; every admin is then told by the bell and by a push that shakes with that level's own pattern (the owner's choice: bell + push + the alarm ring, not the quiet notice), though it never repeats its knock, because nothing is waiting on them. Migration `20261109010000_a10_auto_alarm_at_once`. Unchanged, and still the only things that decide whether to speak: two fresh readings agreeing, the gauge's own Warning level (8 Nov), the key gauge alone reaching Evacuate, the 12-hour quiet window, the 48-hour check for an alert in force, and the all-clear's harder test. **In place of the human check, a sanity guard the owner chose:** before publishing, the last three readings are checked for a jump no river makes (`system_settings.auto_alarm.sanity`, provisionally 2 m/h for a gauge and 5% of capacity an hour for a reservoir — my figures, the owner to correct them). A reading that fails it does not silence the alarm; it falls back to exactly the old behaviour — the draft is held, every admin's phone rings with the deadline, and it publishes anyway at the end of the grace time unless somebody acts — and the gauge is marked suspect on the signal dashboard until an admin clears it (`admin_clear_gauge_suspect`). So a faulty sensor can delay an alert by 20 minutes and can never stop one. **Told to the owner and deliberately not covered:** a sensor stuck at a high value reads exactly like a steady flood and no jump test can see it; catching that needs the gauges upstream to disagree, which is separate work. Also flagged: with the human check gone, the 12-hour quiet window is the only brake on repeated alerts, and only one of the three admin accounts currently has any push subscription. Verified against jaga-dev in rolled-back transactions (publish-at-once, hold-and-mark, the admin push owed once to three devices and the public alert once); 4 unit files and the rewritten `tests/rls/auto-alarm.test.ts` cover it; lint and typecheck clean. CLAUDE.md safety rule 2, docs/spec.md 11 and the runbook rewritten. — The owner's reason is time: in a fast rise, twenty minutes of waiting for an admin who may be asleep is twenty minutes the people downstream do not have. The cost is that a wrong reading now reaches the public before a person sees it, which is why the sanity guard, the suspect mark and the admins' ring went in with it.

- 2026-11-09 — **The dam operator's own screen is built, ahead of the contact at the dam** (the owner: "build it now, so when we get people to work on it, we can demonstrate it right away"). A12 part 2, which spec 15 had always held back until somebody at a dam agreed to use it; the owner reversed the order so there is something to show them. **The role:** a new `dam` capability a unit ticks at registration, which grants **nothing** — the power exists only once an admin ties the verified unit to a named dam (`dam_operator_units`, `admin_link_dam_operator`), after confirming with the dam that these are its people, and an admin unties it in one call. **The screen** is `/authority/dam`: the same form the admins use, their own dam filled in, the same reviewed three-language template, the same fixed S7 zone (not an argument at all), and — the owner's choice of three — the same watch/warning/evacuate starting at warning, so there is one rule to teach. Owner and active dispatchers send; a field member sends nothing. Part 1's `admin_send_dam_release` was lifted into a shared `publish_dam_release` so the admins' door and the dam's door cannot drift apart: a check added for one is added for both. The notice's last line now names the dam rather than a Jaga admin (`dam.release.issuerOperator`), because safety rule 3 puts the issuer on every alert. **Two powers the owner chose** (spec 15 had left them open): the operator may **withdraw a notice it sent itself** while it is in force — otherwise a wrong figure could only be fixed by telephoning an admin — and it may send **"the release has ended"**, because the dam is the only one who knows the gates are shut. The second is the one place this could have done harm, so it is narrow: closing the gates is **not an all-clear**. The water already released is still travelling and the furthest tambon in Bang Lang's zone is most of a day away, so the end message supersedes the notice **at the same level**, keeps its zone and its arrival window, and says in the reviewed words that the level stands; standing a level down remains an admin's act (safety rule 3). The operator may end a notice an admin sent for their dam; only an admin may withdraw one. **A drill switch** on this screen and, new, on the admins': `is_drill` with the audience `admins` (20261020220000) sends down the real function, the real zone and the real template with nobody outside Jaga's admins seeing or hearing it — which is how this gets demonstrated in a control room, and how the admins' own dam-release path can finally be rehearsed at all. A sent drill never carries the template's instructions. **The admins are told at the same moment** (spec 15): a new `operatorRelease` queue in the bell, and one push per notice at high priority from the server's own gate token (`push_dam_release_admins`), handed out once and only for a notice the dam sent itself. **One thing nobody asked for, and the reason it is in here:** every unit picks a coverage area when it registers, and bare `covers_tambon()` is what opens the exact location and telephone number of a request for help, residents' reports and their media, the household figures and the case board. That is right for a rescue team and wrong for a control room at a reservoir, which has no business with any of it. So a unit whose **only** capability is `dam` is now passed over by `covers_tambon()`, by `is_verified_authority()` (the one gate in front of about twenty of those reads), by the dispatch and by `reveal_poc_phone()`; a unit that runs boats as well as a dam keeps everything a team has. Known limit, written in the migration rather than left to be found: an account holding both a dam-only unit and a rescue unit is a verified authority through the second, and the case board takes the union of that account's units' coverage. Migrations `20261109120000_a12_dam_operator_type` (the enum value alone, as a new value cannot be used in the transaction that adds it) and `20261109120100_a12_dam_operator`. 44 RLS cases in the new `tests/rls/dam-operator.test.ts` and 9 unit tests; typecheck and lint clean. **Not applied to any database and the RLS suite not run**: `jaga-dev` is the live one, and testing the second migration needs the enum value committed first, so the owner decides when it goes on. The Malay is mine and still wants the Patani Malay reviewer. — The owner's reason is the demonstration: a working screen is a far better thing to put in front of EGAT or an irrigation office than a description of one, and the drill switch means they can press the real button themselves without a soul downstream hearing it.


- 2026-11-09 — **Search Console's four "not indexed" reasons, read and answered: one crawl rule opened, one written for the tile host** (the owner shared the Index coverage panel: 3 "page with redirect", 1 "not found (404)", 1 "duplicate without user-selected canonical", 1 "crawled – currently not indexed"). Fetching all 75 sitemap addresses first: every one answers 200 with a correct canonical and the full hreflang set, so nothing a reader would search for is affected and the panel is six pages that should not be indexed anyway. Two of the four had a real cause and are fixed. **(1) `/ms/sign-in?next=/ms/map` as "duplicate without user-selected canonical":** `/sign-in` and `/offline` were *both* closed in robots.txt and marked `noindex` in their own metadata, which is self-defeating — a crawler cannot read a tag on a page it has been told not to fetch, so the page stays in the index with no canonical in sight, and the `?next=` form reads as a duplicate of the bare address. Both paths are removed from `CLOSED` in `app/robots.ts`; the `noindex` they already carry is what removes them, and neither page holds anything or differs per reader. The pages that lead to somebody's data (`/admin`, `/authority`, `/account`, `/requests`, `/sos/`) stay closed — there the closed door and the crawl budget are worth more than a tag read once (safety rules 5 and 6). **(2) `https://tiles.jagaapp.org/` as a 404:** the bucket root has no index and correctly 404s, but the hostname had no crawl rules of its own — Cloudflare serves a *managed* robots.txt there whenever the bucket has none, and that file is content signals and comments with **no `Disallow` in it**, so the whole tile bucket stood open to crawlers. Nothing there is a page for a reader and every fetch is an R2 class-B read off the free tier, so a `robots.txt` with `Disallow: /` now goes in the bucket root, as `pipeline/r2sync/robots-jaga-tiles.txt` and a new `r2sync robots` command. That command deliberately **overwrites**, unlike `push`: a dated tile file is a fact and is write-once, while what Jaga asks of crawlers gets revised, and a rule that could only be set once would mean the Cloudflare dashboard to fix a typo in it. **The other two need nothing.** The three redirects are the three language copies of `/authority/register` (307 to `/sign-in`), which were taken out of the sitemap on 22 Oct for that exact reason — Google is remembering addresses it was offered before, and a redirect is the honest answer. "Crawled – currently not indexed" is one page Google chose not to keep; that is its call, not a fault to fix. Verified: all 75 sitemap URLs 200 and probed live; `robots.txt` rules locked in both directions by `tests/unit/seo.test.ts` (a new test asserts the noindex pages are *not* blocked, so this cannot be quietly undone); 16 `pytest` cases for r2sync including the overwrite; typecheck and lint clean. **Not pushed to R2** — writing to the public bucket is outward-facing and the owner runs it (`cd pipeline && uv run python -m r2sync robots`). — Two of the six were Jaga asking a crawler not to look at the very notice that would have taken the page out, and the tile host was paying for crawls of files no person is ever shown.

- 2026-11-10 — **Season state: how wet each catchment already is, and one soft notice an admin reads** (the owner: "do we have already the monthly trend of the River Gauges to see if the water is rising in the wet month ... with the rainfall continuously happening in the season so we can determin alert for preparation?", then "let's plan and build that, so we can also have an automatic soft alert when we see the river is consquently rising countinously for 1 week which supporting data of rainfall in the area suggest that the trend of the river level will be risen and can flood soon"). Jaga could not answer the first question at all: the signal dashboard reads 96 hours of gauge, three days of rain behind and five ahead. Science plan **Module 11**, built the same day as `pipeline/season_state` (+ METHODS.md, report.md, seed 61), migrations `20261110010000_s11_season_state`, `20261110010500_s11_daily_rain` and `20261110011000_s11_season_notice`, and a block on the admins' signal dashboard.

  **Four choices the owner made when asked.** (1) **Who it reaches: admins now, residents by one tap** — the bell and one quiet push carry the evidence, and the notice offers a Watch draft prefilled with the figures, which goes through the reviewed template and a person's send. Safety rule 2 is therefore untouched, and no narrowing of it was needed: nothing on this path writes to `alerts`, sets a level, colours a tambon or calls a publish function. (2) **Which gauges: inland only, chosen by how tidal each one is**, with the dropped ones listed. (3) **The rule needs both legs** — the floor up on five of seven days *and* the fortnight's rain in the top third. (4) **A silent row in the bell plus one push per episode** (not per day, and never the alarm's ring).

  **What the archive changed about the owner's own design.** The rule as first chosen had a third condition, that the floor also be above normal for the date. Measured before building: at X.40A the daily minimum sat at 11.8-12.2 m through 5-22 November 2025 against a November band of 12.82 / 13.01 / 13.48 m — the **bottom** third — and the river went over its 16.5 m bank on the 24th. The worst flood in this record fell on a catchment that was running low, and that gate would have been silent before it; at Hat Yai it cut the 2025 season from 23 firing days to 7. Put to the owner with the figures, who chose to **drop it as a gate and keep it as context** printed beside the grade. The same measurement answered the "how often would it speak" question: one to three episodes per zone per season over 2020-2025, which is what makes a push per episode bearable.

  **One method decision worth recording.** Deciding which gauges the catchment actually moves was done with a **harmonic fit**, not the variance ratio tried first: the sea's M2 (12.42 h), K1 (23.93 h) and O1 (25.82 h) against the exactly-24-hour line, which is human. A variance ratio kept every gauge Module 5 lists by hand as tidal (X.10A came out at 0.25, X.275 at 0.02) and threw out X.77, where the 1.6 m wobble is Bang Lang opening its turbines. The pulse is tested first, because a tall daily pulse leaks into the moon's neighbouring periods and would otherwise be reported as the sea. 32 of 46 gauges kept; the sea threshold is set at the figure that reproduces Module 5's hand-made list. **X.77 is dropped here and unchanged everywhere else** — Module 3 and the automated alarm still read it exactly as before; what is dropped is only its floor as a measure of catchment wetness.

  **Honest limits, written into the copy rather than left in a report.** It is **not early warning**: at X.40A the rule's November 2025 days are the 23rd to the 27th and the bank went on the 24th, so the dashboard says in so many words to read it beside the 72-hour rain, which is the signal with lead time. Terciles, never high percentiles, because the RID key gauges start on 13 February 2020 and six to seven seasons is the most any has; a month with fewer than three seasons holds nulls, and the season count is shown beside every cell. A gap yields no grade rather than a guess — eight whole consecutive days or nothing, fifteen whole days of rain or nothing — and the `blind` list says which, because an unjudgeable figure must never read as calm (safety rule 10). The terciles are GSMaP while the live figure is Open-Meteo, the same mismatch Module 3 already carries; `season_state bias` measures the ratio at identical points rather than leaving it assumed.

  Verified: 15 pipeline unit tests and the whole pipeline suite (310); 17 app unit tests plus signals, coastal-gate, notices and brand-tokens; lint and typecheck clean. **The three migrations are not applied and the RLS suite has not run here** — `jaga-dev` is the live database. In its place, 30 checks were run against jaga-dev in a transaction that was rolled back: the rule's four grades, a below-normal catchment still firing, a gap yielding no grade, short rain reading as unknown rather than dry, one notice per episode and `quiet` closing it, the bell beside every existing queue, the review writing its audit row, the push owed once per phone, and every role boundary for the two tables and the five functions. The alerts count never moved. The Malay is mine and still wants the Patani Malay reviewer. — The owner's question was a real gap: a preparation decision is made on a timescale the 24- and 72-hour rules cannot see, and antecedent wetness is the honest thing to put there. The cost of answering it is that a slow signal is easy to over-trust, which is why it grades, says what it cannot judge, and hands the decision to a person.


- 2026-11-10 — **A hub page at `/flood`, and a home-page title that says what Jaga is** (the owner: they want Jaga on page one for “แอปน้ำท่วม”, “แอพน้ำท่วม”, “น้ำท่วม 3 จังหวัด”, “แอปน้ำท่วม ภาคใต้” and the 3/4-province forms). **Told the owner first, because it changes what can be promised:** page one for the bare “แอปน้ำท่วม” is held by national media listicles (Thai PBS, The Standard, Bangkok Biznews, Post Today) and the government's own PR page for GISTDA's เช็คน้ำ; no title tag outranks those, and the way onto that page is to be *in* those listicles, which is outreach, not code. The geo-qualified queries are winnable and are where Jaga's actual readers are. **What was wrong:** the home page's title was “Jaga – เตือนภัยน้ำท่วมและขอความช่วยเหลือ” — it did not contain แอป at all, nor any region word; ภาคใต้ appeared nowhere on the site (0 times in `messages/th.json`), nor did 3 จังหวัด; and there was no page above `/flood/<place>` answering the general question, so the home screen had to carry it while also being the screen somebody opens mid-flood. **What was built:** a real hub at `/flood` (what Jaga does, where it covers, five FAQs, links to all fifteen place pages, the hotlines), `force-static` so it caches and opens on a failing connection, with schema.org FAQPage built from the same `FAQ_KEYS` the page prints so the structured data cannot drift from the words; `/flood` added to `INDEXED_PATHS`; and the home title split from the PWA name — new `app.seoTitle` for search, `app.pwaName` left alone so the installed icon's label stays short (verified: the manifest still reads “Jaga – เตือนภัยน้ำท่วมและขอความช่วยเหลือ”). **The owner's call on the wording** (AskUserQuestion, both recommended options taken): say ชายแดนภาคใต้ and name the provinces rather than claim ภาคใต้, which is fourteen provinces and would pull in readers Jaga cannot serve — the copy reads “3 จังหวัดชายแดนภาคใต้ ปัตตานี ยะลา นราธิวาส และสงขลา”, which is true and carries both the 3- and 4-province forms people type. Safety rule 10 is kept: the hub ends on the place pages' own coverage sentence, naming the four and saying to call 1784 or 1669 from anywhere else. Rule 8 is kept in the FAQ in as many words (a forecast is an estimate, never a certainty) and rule 2's answer matches the 9 Nov automated alarm; asking for help is the first thing on the page (rule 1) and the four hotlines are on it (rule 4). **One judgment call, easily reversed:** แอพ appears exactly once, as a parenthetical in the hub's opening line, rather than misspelling the copy throughout for a keyword. Verified: typecheck, lint, 13 new tests in `tests/unit/flood-hub.test.tsx` holding the rule-10, rule-4 and no-certainty claims so later copy edits cannot quietly drop them, plus the existing shell key-parity test (th/ms/en identical key sets) and the seo and flood suites; all three languages fetched from a dev server and rendering, all fifteen place links present. The Malay is mine and still wants the Patani Malay reviewer. — A page nobody can find helps nobody, and the one query Jaga should certainly win — somebody in Pattani looking for a flood app — had no page on this site aimed at it.

- 2026-11-10 — **The rivers' card on the public map says how many rivers have been rising for a week, and a press opens those first** (the owner, after Module 11 went live: *"now since we have the river gauges trend info, can we add this information in the map page console card for river gauges, instead of just saying 'All below their banks' it should also say how many of the rivers level has risen for the past weeks consistantly. And when clicked at the card, should open the rivergauge details that their level has risen first then later on sort with which river is related to the location first."*). Migration `20261110170000_s11_gauge_week_trend` adds `rose_days` (0-7) and `week_change_m` to `station_status()`; the console's rivers readout keeps the bank as its figure and gains a second line counting the rivers that rose on five of the last seven days; `gaugeRun`/`gaugesLit` gain a tier for them, between a bank reached and everybody else's rivers.

  **The threshold is not a new one.** Five of seven, read from the same `system_settings.season_state.season.rose_days_min` the admins' notice uses, so the public card and the admin bell can never say different things about the same river. The constant is mirrored in `lib/map-console.ts` (`ROSE_DAYS_MIN`) with a note that an admin changing the setting must change it there too.

  **Only the gauges Module 11 keeps are judged, and this is the load-bearing decision.** A tidal station's daily low follows the moon, so over a spring tide every river mouth would read as "rising for a week". A station is judged only if it has a `season_baseflow` row - exactly the 32 of 46 that passed Module 11's harmonic test. Measured on jaga-dev the same day: the tidal station ONE037 read 5 of 7 days rising, while the two real risers were X.67A (6 of 7, +0.43 m) and X.274 (5 of 7, +0.30 m). Without the exclusion the public map would have told residents about the tide. Nothing from `season_baseflow` leaves `station_status()` - only whether a row exists.

  **A gap is never a quiet week.** Eight whole consecutive Bangkok days and a reading inside 36 hours, or both columns are null; the card then counts nothing, says how many stations it could judge ("the week's trend read for 3 of 4 stations"), and the gauge's own card says why it has no figure. 31 of 48 stations were judgeable on 10 Nov. Cost measured before building: 105 ms inside a `station_status()` that already takes 370 ms and is cached 60 s at the edge, so the public map pays nothing noticeable.

  **The order a press opens.** Rank by what it reads, then distance, then the longer rise, then the newest reading, then the id - so a bank reached still comes first (the 20 Oct rule is untouched), a week of rising comes next wherever it is, and within that tier the reader's own river opens before a distant one, which is the owner's second sentence. Where no river is near a bank, the press frames and lights the rising ones (up to `LIT_MAX`) instead of a single station.

  Not touched: the home screen's live-monitoring card, whose smaller feed (`/api/public/gauges/summary`) deliberately does not carry this - a phone on slow 3G should not download it to be told how its own rivers stand. `weekRise()` counts such a feed as wholly unjudged rather than as calm, so it cannot be used by accident. — The measurement already existed and only admins could see it; a resident reading "all below their banks" for a fortnight of climbing was being told something true and useless.
- 2026-11-10 — **A19 built: where a road may be holding water back, in the authority console; and a planning office stops being a team in the field.** Module 8 part 2 had sat on the laptop since 15 Oct. It is now `/authority/highway-paths` for a verified unit with the `planning` capability, its own provinces only (spec 5.7): the flagged stretches best first, each with its reasons in plain words, its district and tambon, a map link and the figures behind it; a map layer of the sections over the flood-hazard layer; CSV and GeoJSON of that province alone; and one note per stretch the office owns. Migrations `20261110180000_a19_planning_not_a_field_team` and `20261110180100_a19_highway_paths`; loader `uv run python -m highway_paths load`. — Chosen by the owner on 16 Oct: the two offices that can act on this list get it as a page, not as a document pack, and never in the public app.

  **The first migration is the one that mattered, and it was not on A19's list as work - it was on it as a question.** A19 item 6 said to "check and state in the spec that `planning` alone opens no SOS, no household data and no phone reveal; if it does today, close that first". It did. `covers_tambon()` and `is_verified_authority()` asked only whether a unit was verified and covered the place — right for the rescue team they were written for. A19 invites a provincial highway office to register with province-wide coverage, so a verified office would have read every resident's exact address and telephone number across a whole province, to decide where a culvert goes. `unit_is_office_only()` (capabilities ⊆ {dam, planning}) now stands in the four gates `covers_tambon`, `my_unit_for_sos`, `reveal_poc_phone` and `is_verified_authority`, widening the dam operator's fix of 9 Nov by one capability. A unit that is `planning` **and** `rescue` — an irrigation project office that also runs boats — keeps everything a team has: the test is "an office and nothing else".

  **Two loose ends closed with it.** A drill addressed to "every unit" was delivered to an office by `drill_audience_users()` and then unreadable to it, because `drill_visible()` asked the field-team gate; it now asks whether the account holds a verified unit, which is what "every unit" means. And `/authority` would have opened an empty war room for a planning-only unit, as it would have for a dam-only one before 9 Nov; the dam-only branch is now an office branch serving both.

  **The wording carries the limits, and a test holds it there.** Never "this road blocks the flood": the radar sees extent and not depth, a wetter side can be a paddy or a natural low, and "no mapped opening" nearly always means "not mapped" (OpenStreetMap has 102 culverts for 2,099 km of main road here). All four limits are rendered with every list, and `tests/unit/highway-paths.test.ts` fails on a word list of confident phrasings in all three languages. The classes are violet, measured at least 60 points from every alert colour — the first grey I chose for "no sign" sat 59 points from `--alert-stale`, which in Jaga means "not updated since" on an alert that still holds, and those two must not be able to look like one another.

  **Province scope, and the 1,000-row trap.** An office works on a whole road, so the unit of access is the province, not the tambon. The map, the list and the download each come from a function returning one jsonb value rather than a table select: the API cuts a table at 1,000 rows without saying so and Songkhla has 2,000 sections, so a download would have silently lost three quarters of a province. The page view and each download go to `audit_log`; there is no personal data here to reveal, so that is a record of who looked, not a reveal.

  **The notes outlive the pipeline.** `highway_stretch_notes` is keyed on the stretch id as text with **no foreign key**, so a reload — which replaces every section and stretch row in one transaction — cannot take an office's work with it. The loader prints how many notes it left alone.

  **Verified, not applied.** Both migrations run clean against jaga-dev in a rolled-back transaction (38 checks) and the real 4,366 sections load and read back correctly through them (per-province counts matching `summary.json`, every section's line inside its own tambon); `tests/rls/highway-paths.test.ts` is **26/26 against jaga-dev**, `tests/unit/highway-paths.test.ts` 23/23 and `pipeline/tests/test_highway_paths_load.py` 12/12. Neither migration is applied: jaga-dev is the live database and the owner applies them. They sit behind A12's two, which are also still pending — `unit_is_office_only` tests the capability labels rather than writing an enum array literal precisely so that it can be run, and therefore tested, in one transaction with the migration that adds the `dam` label.

  Still the owner's: applying the two migrations, running the loader against jaga-dev, the Thai wording to their reviewer and the Malay to a Patani Malay reader, and whether to approach แขวงทางหลวง and โครงการชลประทาน in the four provinces now that there is something to show them.
