which reads as a sliver of "not stated" that nothing in the data says.

## 2026-11-23 - The warning zone is drawn on the public map, for every reader

The owner, 22 Nov 2026: "in the map should highlight the warning zone, for all users to see, not
just the affected users." Said while deciding what to do about SLA005, whose daily high tide
reaches its Evacuate level.

A warning zone is the river basin Jaga issues for: the unit an admin ticks a gauge into, and the
unit `auto_alarm_check()` publishes for. It was an internal idea. A reader saw only the result -
their own tambon's colour - and never the basin the warning was about. Somebody one tambon outside
an alert, upstream on the same river, had no way to see that the warning beside them follows the
water they live on.

**A line, never a filled area.** The map's colours are already spent: the alert palette on the
tambons, ink on report hexagons, safe places and gauges, the water colour on the river, the dam
and the sea, and ten cool shades on a person's own places. A filled zone would be a second amber
over the first, and it would hide the tambon colours underneath - which are the thing that
actually tells a reader about their own home.

**What the line says.** At rest: dashed, in the brand's edge colour, which by rule shows no status
(docs/brand.md), with the basin's name along it. In force: solid, thicker, in that level's own
colour from the alert palette, with the level in words on the label. The form changes as well as
the hue, so a reader who separates no colours at all still sees a thin broken line become a thick
unbroken one with a word beside it (accessibility: icon + text + colour, never colour alone). Two
MapLibre layers rather than one, because a dash cannot be driven from a feature's properties.

**One definition of "in force".** `zoneLevel()` asks `alertForTambon()` - the very function that
colours each tambon - for every tambon in the zone and keeps the most severe answer. Nothing in
the new code decides what is in force, so the outline and the tambons inside it cannot drift
apart. `normal` counts as no level: an all-clear must not thicken the line. A zone at rest is
drawn as context and says nothing about whether the basin is safe (safety rule 10).

**Nothing new is exposed.** `warning_zone_map()` returns the name, basin, tambon codes and
outline, and no level. It is **not** security definer: `warning_zones` and `warning_zone_tambons`
have been readable by `anon` since the first geography migration, as the tambon boundaries are, so
it runs as the caller and a later narrowing of those tables narrows this too. Verified against
jaga-dev in a rolled-back transaction, 24 checks, including that a visitor who has not signed in
really gets all three zones - which is the owner's request, not a side effect.

**Cost.** The three outlines are 1,027 points and 20 KB of GeoJSON at five decimals, and
`st_simplifypreservetopology` at 50 m and at 100 m removes not one point: the stored outlines are
made of whole tambons and are already coarser than that. So nothing is simplified - it would cost
accuracy and save nothing. It rides on the existing public map payload, whose 60 s edge cache
still holds because the geometry is static and the level is worked out in the browser.

Still open, and the owner's to decide: the 18 stations that stand in no existing zone span three
river systems, so the zone scheme itself needs their call before any zone is invented; and the
estuary gauges still need tide-aware levels before they can be armed.

## 2026-11-24 - Live CCTV hidden until a reliable source exists
The Live cameras window (built 22 Nov, decision 2026-11-22) is removed from the UI - unmounted from the gauge card and the dam report - and left in the code (`lib/cctv.ts`, `components/cctv/CameraWindow.tsx`, its test). The owner found DDPM's `cctv.disaster.go.th` under maintenance on 24 Nov; a re-check of DDPM, EGAT and ONWR confirmed none offers a reliable, embeddable, timestamped live feed for the four provinces (all JS apps that go down and cannot be framed). Reason: in a life-safety app a prominent button that often lands on a maintenance or offline agency page burns trust during the exact emergency it is for; nothing beats a flaky link here. Re-mount `<CameraWindow>` only once an official page is confirmed to stay up for our provinces. Supersedes the mounting part of 2026-11-22 (link-out design stands for when it returns).

## 2026-11-24 - A warning level must buy time, not just mark the bank

The owner asked why the automatic alarm was silent while rivers were up. It was not broken: the
cron had succeeded on every run, and the feed was honest - every stored reading matched ThaiWater
to the centimetre and the same timestamp. Nothing was above Jaga's own Warning line except SLA005,
the tidal gauge deliberately left unarmed. ThaiWater's own map was showing 15 of our 48 stations at
`situation_level` 4, but that grading is `(level - bed) / (bank - bed)`: how full the channel is,
not how close a flood is. PAT001 was 85.8 % "full" while sitting 2.63 m below its bank.

The silence was correct behaviour on rules that could not speak. Two measurements showed why:

- **Nineteen gauges had Watch less than 1 m below the bank, nine of them less than 0.30 m**, and on
  four Warning sat 3 to 5 cm under it. The cause is the 3 % / 6 % share floors, which lift a level
  the river stands above too often; on a gauge whose water sits high that percentile is centimetres
  under the bank, and the lift was overriding the rise-based levels that had real lead time.
- **No armed gauge had come within reach of its own Watch line in the entire six-week record**, and
  **42 of 48 gauges were not armed** - including 13 of the 15 ThaiWater was grading at level 4.

**Decided.** A level must also sit at least the rise that river makes, in its own record, over the
hours it is meant to buy: Warning the 6-hour rise below the bank, Watch the 24-hour rise, never
closer than 0.3 m / 0.6 m. The room comes from the record and never from a share of the channel,
because `gauge_channel`'s bed figure is wrong at several stations. Chosen over a flat channel-share
rule for exactly that reason, and over touching only the nine worst because the other ten are no
more defensible.

Four guards, each one found by running it: the move must buy at least 2 h of median lead (JNA002
bought 0.5 h and cost a false alarm - declined); it must be able to stay rare, or the gauge is
marked `needs_human_level` instead of fudged (SAY001 would stand above it 35.8 % of the record);
it needs 3 years of record (TBV029 has 36 days, and its 49 m "rise" had put Watch 48 m below
Warning); and it skips the sea's gauges, whose levels are their own piece of work (2026-11-22).

The tidal test reuses Module 11's harmonic fit but **not its threshold alone**: at 0.05 m the tidal
periods also fit a mountain river's noise, and it marked BLGTD01 (bank 48.68 m up), BLGTD02, PAT001
and X.77 as the sea's. A bank within 5 m of sea level is now required too, which reproduces Module
5's hand-written list plus three Songkhla-lake-shore gauges. A once-a-day swing is a dam opening
its turbines, not the moon (X.77, 0.52 m): recorded, never an exemption.

**`thresholds gauges` no longer writes the seed.** It writes the reports and stops; `--write-seed`
is needed, once the owner has read the before-and-after table in `pipeline/thresholds/lead_time.md`
where both sides are scored over the same floods. `jaga-dev` is the database the live site reads, so
a run that quietly rewrote the seed would change what the app warns on. A key gauge's accepted
levels (14 Oct 2026) are never moved; where the floor disagrees it says so (X.119A: it wanted 8.70,
the accepted 8.80 stands).

**No gauge is armed as part of this** (the owner, 24 Nov): arming one whose Warning is 4 cm below
its bank would publish only once the river was already overtopping. Levels first, then arming in one
reviewed batch. Nothing here publishes or raises anything - a level only ever starts a suggestion,
or the alarm on a gauge an admin has ticked (safety rule 2).

Also decided: **store ThaiWater's own grading** (`situation_level`, `storage_percent`,
`diff_wl_bank`), already in the payload `gauges_fetch()` collects every 15 minutes and until now
thrown away, and show it to admins beside Jaga's own grade. It is what the owner was looking at when
the two disagreed, and the app could not see it. Advisory only: it never publishes and never sets a
level.

## 2026-11-24 - Hat Yai flood cameras embedded in-app; far-south needs a municipality ask
Owner's bar for street-corner flood CCTV: embeddable AND timestamped. **Hat Yai (Songkhla)** clears it - Hatyai City Climate (SCCCRN, CC BY-SA 3.0) aggregates official Dept of Water Resources cameras and sponsor ones along the U-Tapao canal; the seven coded SCCCRN stations serve a live JPEG (`floodphoto/last/<name>.jpg`, CORS `*`, overwritten ~1/min) with the capture time burned into every frame and a water-level staff gauge in shot. The page blocks framing, but the image hotlinks via `<img>`, so a frozen/offline camera shows an old time on its own face (safety rules 8, 10). Built a closable in-app window (`components/cctv/HatYaiCameras.tsx`, `lib/hatyai-cams.ts`), shown on gauge cards in the Hat Yai basin only (bbox), images loaded only while open and refreshed on demand (remount re-fetches), credited and linking out. Jaga hosts nothing; no dependency added. **Pattani and Narathiwat**: no public municipality API/feed clears the bar. Narathiwat's เทศบาล Yakang camera is an internal IP camera (VI MonitorPlus) with no public endpoint - the only public images are a third party's fragile screen-capture-to-GitHub pipeline, unfit to depend on. Pattani: nothing municipal found. Path for both: a direct request to the เทศบาล (owner action), not a code change. Reason: give residents the real street/canal view where a trustworthy, self-dating source exists, and refuse a flaky or frozen one everywhere else.

## 2026-11-24 - A flood scene plays the moment a river reaches its bank, from the real channel
Owner's request: "when water level reaches any bank of the river... the user can see the flood simulation happen and how it's reaching the user's selected location", wanted "cinematic but using the real geographical information for that scene", alerting the user like the evacuate alarm and attached to the bell. Four design choices, taken with the owner against the options I laid out, all turning on keeping it honest in a live app in peak season:
1. **Drawn on the real channel cross-section, NOT a map flood extent.** A map footprint reaching a person's home is the S6 stage-to-extent library, which this log kept off the map on 14 Oct 2026 (CSI ~0.15, wrong four times in five). A cinematic version of a wrong footprint is the most persuasive possible way to mislead. The scene instead rises the live reading against the station's own measured banks (ThaiWater's record + the OSM-measured width, lib/gauge-section.ts) and spills it over the lower bank through the clip the card already uses - every pixel a measured figure (safety rules 2, 8, 10).
2. **Plays when the bank is topped, but the alarm only ever rides a real alert.** A gauge topping its bank is a measurement; only a human or the automated alarm on a ticked gauge publishes an alert (safety rule 2). So where an evacuate alert is in force the scene rides that alert's existing alarm + bell; where no alert is in force it will get a **measurement-grade** bell notice ("a measurement, not an official alert"), never an evacuate-styled push, never auto-published.
3. **Names the person's tambon and states measured facts only** - how far over the bank, the reading, its time and source (ThaiWater) - with no per-home depth. A per-home depth would need the DEM, i.e. the thing kept off the map on 14 Oct; held for a February 2027 revisit.
4. The scene keeps the map's water colour, never an alert colour as its own voice; it keeps the hotline bar one tap away (safety rule 4); and it refuses to open for a stale or silent gauge (safety rule 10; the 22 Nov 2026 frozen-sensor bug).

Built in stages. **Stage 1 (this commit):** the cinematic scene itself - lib/flood-scene.ts (over-bank test), components/map/FloodScene.tsx + .module.css, map.gauge.scene copy in th/ms/en, opened by hand from the gauge card when the river is over its bank. Self-contained, nothing auto-published, reversible. **Stage 2 (next):** the app-wide trigger (nearest gauge to the saved tambon, off the lightweight summary feed), the measurement bell notice, and attaching the scene to a live evacuate alert's notice. New Thai and Malay lines await the reviewers.

## 2026-11-24 - A quiet area still sees that alerts are in force elsewhere
The owner asked why a recent alert was not showing in the map's "Tambons with an alert now" card on a laptop, and asked that users on any device be able to tell that some area has an alert now, "so the users can see the app is working not just idle."

Diagnosis: nothing was broken. The home hero is scoped to the reader's own tambon (spec 4.1, safety rule 3) and the map's alerts list is scoped to the province the map is on - by design, the map opens on the person's own province and the list matches the selector. So an alarm two provinces away was correctly absent from both, and a reader whose own area was quiet saw a screen that looked idle.

Fix, in two places, neither of which carries a level or an alert colour of its own (safety rules 3, 10 - the hero and the map's own tambon colours stay the only things that say a level):
1. **Home screen, all devices:** one quiet line under the hero - "an alert is in force in N areas now - see the map" - shown when an alert at Watch, Warning or Evacuate is in force in an area other than the reader's own, and dropped while a place of theirs is at Warning or Evacuate (then the screen belongs to their own alert). The all-clear (`return`) does not raise it. Links to the map.
2. **Map alerts list:** when the map is scoped to one province while another is in alarm, the card says so and offers a one-tap "Show all four provinces" that pulls the whole view back. The province scoping itself is unchanged.

New helper `alertsElsewhereCount()` in lib/public-status.ts; counts tambons under Watch/Warning/Evacuate, excluding a given set. New Thai/Malay/English lines (`home.alertsElsewhere.*`, `map.alertsList.elsewhere/showAll`) await the reviewers. Reason: the public screens are deliberately scoped to the reader, which on a quiet day makes a working app look idle; a neutral, scope-aware pointer to the map fixes that without ever implying risk where there is none.

## 2026-11-24 - All CCTV hidden, including Hat Yai
Owner, same day the Hat Yai cameras were built: "hide all cctv, the Hat Yai one also not looking good." So every camera path is now unmounted from the UI, not just the agency link-out: the Hat Yai window (`components/cctv/HatYaiCameras.tsx`, `lib/hatyai-cams.ts`) is no longer rendered on any gauge card (import and call site removed from `components/map/DashboardView.tsx`), joining the already-hidden link-out `CameraWindow` (`lib/cctv.ts`). Nothing CCTV-related shows anywhere in the app now. All of it is kept whole - components, data, i18n and tests - so any source can be wired back in one edit once it proves itself. Reason: the owner judged even the self-dating Hat Yai feed not good enough to show residents yet; a flood app shows a camera only when the owner is confident it helps, and hides it otherwise (safety rules 8, 10). Supersedes the two 2026-11-24 CCTV entries above.

## 2026-11-24 - The new warning levels loaded, after the owner handed the choice back
The owner, asked to approve the lead-time table: "for that I let you chose the best." So the
choice was made here and the reasoning is recorded, gauge by gauge, for them to overrule.

**Seven level moves on four gauges are now in `jaga-dev`**: FOP041 (watch 6.50 -> 5.80, warning
6.70 -> 6.20), JNA001 (4.50 -> 4.15, 4.70 -> 4.45), X.73 (14.00 -> 13.10, 14.85 -> 14.30) and
X.119A's watch (8.20 -> 7.80; its warning stays at the 8.80 the owner accepted on 14 Oct). Every
one rescues a level that bought 0 to 6 h against the hours it stands for - two of them bought
exactly **0.0 h**, which is a record of the past, not a warning. Nothing else moved but 5 cm of
drift at PTTEP4 and X.173A from a month more archive.

**Reading the first table changed the answer, and three of its ten moves were dropped.** The floor
exists because a level buys too little time; it had never tested whether the level *already* bought
it. ONE039 (6.0 h against a 6 h target), X.274 (6.5 h) and X.283 (9.0 h) were already sound, and
the proposed moves were the expensive ones - ONE039 would have paid 0 -> 7 false alarms to turn a
good 6 h warning into a 117 h one, which describes a season, not a flood. Guard: **a level whose
measured lead already meets its target does not move.**

**PAT001 was dropped too, and it exposed a real hole.** A gauge a dam swings once a day keeps its
bank - the river still leaves the channel there - but a level *lowered* reaches down into the
turbine swing: 5 -> 17 false alarms for 3 h. Guard: **never lower a level on a gauge with a
once-a-day swing**; like the sea's nine, it needs levels measured on the daily minimum (decision
2026-11-22). This corrects the 24 Nov choice to drop `pulse_driven` from the exemption, which was
right about the bank and wrong about everything below it.

**A level that is wrong more often than right still moves, but the gauge is flagged** - JNA001 (11
false against 5 floods) and X.73's watch (18 against 14). A level buying no time at all is worse;
the hours are real and the judgement is a person's, so it is said in the report, not hidden.

**The seed now refuses a gauge the report refuses**, which it had not done: a month more archive
had let two gauges past the scoring test that the report says plainly must not be armed, and the
seed wrote them anyway. Left out, each named in the file with its reason: TBV029 (0.1 years),
X.73A (2.9 years), SAY001 (its three levels each exceeded 35.8 % of the record, which describes
the river rather than a flood) and X.181 (**highest reading 16.60 m, 11.60 m above its 5.00 m
bank, yet no flood found in 6.3 years** - the bank and the readings are not on the same datum, the
22 Nov X.67A fault again). Leaving a gauge out erases nothing: the upsert only touches rows it
names, so a gauge keeps what it has and one with no row stays ungraded - unjudgeable, never
"normal" (safety rule 10). Also fixed: a latent bug where the guards' reasons were overwritten by
the moves, and a `source` date hardcoded to 14 Oct in every row.

Loaded by dry run first (`.claude/local/seed-dryrun.mjs`, which prints every row a load would
change and rolls back): no admin-set level was touched, and every armed gauge's latest reading sat
far below its warning, so the load could not trip an alarm. The alarm grades at Warning and never
at Watch, so X.119A - the one armed and zoned gauge affected - is unchanged in what it would
publish; only the Kolok zone's advisory suggestion threshold moved. Reason: a warning level must
buy the time it promises, and where it already does, moving it only spends trust; where no honest
level exists, the gauge is flagged for a person instead of being given a number that looks like an
answer. 334 pipeline tests pass, 4 new.

Still open and still the owner's: arming gauges (42 of 48 remain off), tide-aware levels for the
nine gauges the sea reaches plus the five a dam swings, and a basin for the 18 unzoned stations.

## 2026-11-25 - Flood scene stage 2: the bell carries a bank-reached measurement that opens the scene
Stage 2 of the flood scene (decision 2026-11-24). When a river within `GAUGES_NEAR_KM` of one of the person's saved places is at or over its bank, the bell shows a new `bankReached` notice item - "a river near you has reached its bank", the gauge named - and a button that opens the full-screen scene (components/map/BankScene fetches the one full gauge on demand). It is a **measurement**, not an alert: it never rings as one, and a published alert still rings entirely on its own (safety rule 2) - so "alert like the evacuate alarm" is the real alert's existing alarm, and the scene is what's new on the bell. Detection reuses the light gauge summary the home screen already caches (`status === "above_evacuate"` is exactly "at or over bank"; `worthNaming` names such a station), filtered to the person's own points by `gaugesNear`, so the always-on bell adds no new server load and no weight from the monitoring graph (the geo helper moved to the leaf module `lib/near`). A visitor who has saved no place gets nothing. Commits f72e9f2 (stage 1) and dd3b3cd (stage 2). New Thai and Malay lines (notices.bankReached.*, map.gauge.scene.*) await the reviewers.

## 2026-11-26 - An admin can edit a registered team
Until now a team could say who it is only once, at registration, and only while pending. An admin could verify, reject or suspend it but not correct it, so a mistyped contact phone, a wrong unit name or an area ticked too wide meant a new registration. Owner asked for admins to edit the team's information. Owner chose, with the options in front of them: **all four parts** (unit and organization details, point of contact, capabilities, coverage) and, for a changed contact phone, **reset the confirmation and make the admin tick "I called this number"** (not "keep it confirmed", not "suspend until re-verified": a typo fix must not take a team off the board mid-flood).

Built as three admin-only database functions, one per form on `/admin/authorities/<unit>/edit`, so each saves on its own: `admin_update_unit_details`, `admin_update_unit_contact`, `admin_update_unit_scope` (migration `20261126100000_a10_admin_edit_team`). Any status can be edited; the status is never touched. Each writes `audit_log` under the admin's account with old and new values; a change that changes nothing writes nothing. **The contact's name and phone and the office phone are logged as "changed", never by value** (safety rule 5: personal data does not get a second home in the log), and the edit form shows the contact only after the same logged `reveal_poc_phone` as the verification queue, so fixing a unit name never logs a phone reveal. A new contact phone clears both confirmation flags; on a verified team the function refuses it without the call (23514), on any other status it is saved unconfirmed and verification still asks for the call. Coverage is replaced whole or not at all (an area that expands to nothing is refused and the old coverage stays), and what a team reads follows it at once. **Taking `dam` away unties the unit from its dams**: a dormant link would revive the day someone ticked `dam` again, without any admin having confirmed with the dam a second time. The organization is shared by every unit under its name, so renaming it renames it for all; the screen says how many. The registration form's area picker became a shared server component (`components/authority/CoverageFields`) so both forms use one picker; its markup is unchanged.

Not done, and the owner's call: telling the team when an admin changes its contact, capabilities or area (today it finds out when its board changes); a warning when a coverage change would leave a case the team has already accepted outside its area. New Thai and Malay lines (`admin.authorities.edit.*`) await the reviewers. The migration is committed but **not applied to jaga-dev**, and the new RLS test (`tests/rls/admin-edit-team.test.ts`) has not been run: jaga-dev is the live database, so both wait for the owner's yes.

## 2026-11-26 - Editing a team: the team may be told, and a rescue in progress is not cut off by accident
Follows "An admin can edit a registered team" (same day). The owner answered three questions: apply the migration to `jaga-dev` (done, verified live 20 checks); let the admin tick whether the team gets notices, **ticked by default**; and warn so that the wrong coverage is not selected.

**Notices.** Each of the three forms has a "tell the team" box, ticked when the page is drawn. It is written into the audit row (`notify`). A ticked edit reaches the account that registered the unit by the road a verification takes: the bell (`my_unit_edits()`, a new bell item of kind `unit` with the events `editedDetails`, `editedContact`, `editedScope`) and LINE (a new `unit_edited` branch in `admin_decisions_since()`, rebuilt from the live definition after checking it matched its 16 Oct migration). The message names the unit and the part, never the new values, because a contact's name and phone are personal data. An unticked edit is invisible to the team, and a form that never had the box tells nobody (the harmless direction). The notice goes to the registering account, as the other three unit decisions do, not to every member of the team.

**The warning, and what I found under it.** The owner's words were "the team should get the warning to not let them select wrong coverage". I read it as "stop the wrong coverage being chosen", so the warning is on the admin's edit form, not a message to the team. Under it is a real hazard: `sos_requests` is read through `covers_tambon(tambon)` and `my_unit_for_sos()`, so a team that has accepted a case and then loses its tambon (or is left with only planning and dam work, which is "an office, not a team in the field", A19) can no longer read that case. A page that says so is not enough, so the database holds the line: `admin_update_unit_scope` refuses with 23514 "Open cases would be cut off: N" when an accepted, unreleased case in an open status (`received`, `assigned`, `en_route`, `on_site`) that the team can read now would be unreadable after, unless the caller passes `p_confirm_open_cases`. The page names the tambons and counts live as the admin ticks and unticks, through a small client component, and the box it shows must be ticked to save. A confirmed change records `open_cases_cut_off` in the log. Cases already finished, claims already released, and cases with no tambon do not count.

Not done: the team's own members other than the registering account are not told; the cut-off is only warned about, not prevented, because an admin may have a reason (the team asked to hand the area over) and the owner asked for a warning. New Thai and Malay lines (`admin.authorities.edit.notify*`, `openCases*`, `error.open`, `notices.unit.edited*`) await the reviewers. Verified against a throwaway Postgres (35 checks, stand-in tables), 61 unit tests, and the migration live on `jaga-dev`; the RLS test file `tests/rls/admin-edit-team.test.ts` is written but not run, and awaits the owner's yes for the suite.

## 2026-11-27 - SOS asks for a number; every team's number and alarm are on by default; teams are told to install the app
Nine SOS in five days (24-27 Nov) all arrived with no phone number, and none was accepted by a team: seven were offered, nobody answered, and an admin assigned each by hand. Three decisions from the owner:
1. **The SOS screen asks for a phone number before sending**, with one way out: a tick "I can't give a number". Location stays required. Narrows safety rule 1 (which made the phone optional); the tick keeps the rule's point, that a request is never refused. The server is unchanged, so a LINE or SMS or queued request still goes in without one. Reason: a request nobody can call back is half a request.
2. **"Show the team's number to the person" is locked on for every team**, backfilled (2 of 12 verified units had it off) and forced by a trigger (migration `20261127120000_a15_team_number_always_shared`, applied to jaga-dev). The switch is a fact on the team's screen now. Reason: the owner wants the person to be able to call whoever accepts.
3. **The alarm sound is on by default, as far as a browser allows.** A browser plays nothing before a tap, so the console arms the sound on the first tap anywhere, and until then a banner at the top of the board asks for it. Nothing can arm it without a tap. **Teams are told to install Jaga:** a bell item for every verified team's people (owner and active members) on a device where Jaga is not installed, linking to `/authority/install`, the home screen's animated guide opened. Reason: an alarm that sounds only while the page is open, and on iPhone only an installed app can be notified at all.

What the check of the five days found (nothing was changed for it): 9 of 12 verified teams have **no device subscribed to push at all**, including the coordination team that was offered almost every case, so the offers were "sent" (written to `push_log`) to nobody; most of the tambons had no verified rescue-capable team, so the relay went to coordination only; the unclaimed push to the admins did fire each time.

## 27 Nov 2026: The Team coverage switch on the map is open to everyone (supersedes 25 Oct)
Decision (owner, 27 Nov, after not seeing the switch on a phone while signed out): the "Team coverage" switch on the main map is offered to every visitor, off by default. A visitor reads a new public cut, `public_team_coverage()` (migration `20261127131000_a15_public_team_coverage`, applied to jaga-dev; endpoint `/api/public/team-coverage`, kept five minutes at the edge): per tambon, covered or not, where "covered" means a verified team with the rescue capability is registered there. **No team count, no unit or organization, no coordinator or supplies cover, no "mine", no phone** (safety rules 5 and 6). A dam-only or planning unit never makes a tambon covered. Verified teams and admins keep the fuller `coverage_summary()` rows (their own tambons outlined); it stays closed to everyone else.
Wording (owner): an uncovered tambon is "no team registered yet", never "nobody will help", and the note under the switch always adds that SOS, 1784 and 1669 work in every tambon (safety rules 1 and 10). The 25 Oct line that the layer was "deliberately NOT added to How this map was made" no longer holds: it now has its own point there (`mapMethod.coverage`). Spec 14 access matrix row updated. The new Thai, Malay and English lines await the reviewers.
Reason: the owner wants anyone to see how many tambons registered teams cover so far, which also shows teams where more are needed. The public map shows only aggregated data (rule 6); a yes or no per tambon is that.

## 2026-11-27 - SOS cases nobody has heard from in 72 hours leave the war room's work list
The owner asked for old unanswered SOS cases to be cleared from the admins' war room, or for an admin to be able to remove them from the to-do list. Chosen: **an admin button with a reason, nothing automatic.**
- A case is "stale" when it is open, no unit holds it, and **the sender has not been heard from for 72 hours**. The clock is the sender's last word (a repeat send merged into the case, details, media or a new location), not the first send: a person still re-sending is not obsolete. The database decides (`admin_sos_stale_ids()`), and the clearing functions use the same test, so the screen and the rules cannot disagree.
- On the war room those cases move to their own fold ("Older than 72 hours"), out of the counters, the "Do this now" gate and the longest-wait clock. Nothing is hidden and nothing is closed by code.
- An admin clears one case, or the whole fold, with a written reason (`admin_sos_clear_stale`, `admin_sos_clear_stale_all`). The case closes as `dismissed`, a `cleared_obsolete` line goes into its history and `audit_log`, nothing is deleted, and `admin_sos_reopen` undoes it. A held case, a fresh case and a closed case are refused by the database.
- Safety rule 1 holds: a sender who is still waiting can send again, and a repeat SOS opens a new case once the old one is closed. Auto-closing at 72 h was offered and not chosen, because a real person may still be waiting.
- Migration `20261127130000_a6_clear_stale_sos` (not applied to `jaga-dev` yet); RLS tests `tests/rls/clear-stale-sos.test.ts`.

## 2026-11-27 - A page for the free plans, and a push to the super admin when one must be upgraded

Vercel (75% of its 4-hour Active CPU) and Supabase ("depleting its Disk IO budget") both emailed on 27 Nov. The owner will upgrade, but wants to watch both until the limit is close, to be told when it is critical, and to see an estimate of when each needs upgrading. Chosen: **`/admin/plans`, advice only, readings typed in by an admin plus what the database measures about itself.**

- Neither provider lets a project read its own plan usage from inside the database, and Jaga holds no token for their dashboards. Adding one (a new secret on the live site, for a number readable in ten seconds) was not worth it. So an admin types the figure from the provider's page (`3h 12m`, `192k`, `3.6 GB`), and the database writes down its own size and I/O totals each night (`plan_snapshot()`, cron `jaga-plan-snapshot`).
- One rule file, `lib/plan-limits.ts`, makes the verdict (now, soon, later, done) and a range of dates for the upgrade line and for the limit. The page and the push both use it, so they cannot disagree. The recent pace is multiplied by 1.5 in October to December (a flood day brings several times the usual visitors), shown on the page as an assumption (safety rule 8: ranges, never certainty).
- "Upgrade now" means a meter is at the runbook's upgrade line (Vercel Active CPU 75%, others 50%, database 60% of 500 MB), is on course to pass its limit before the cycle resets, or the provider has warned about its Disk IO budget in the last 14 days.
- **The push goes to the super admin only**, from the existing push tick (checked once an hour): one ordinary notice, no level and no ring, once per episode and then every two days until an admin marks the service "upgraded". It says what is at its line and that nothing has stopped. It cannot touch an alert (safety rule 2); `tests/rls/plan-meters.test.ts` checks it.
- Found on the way: nothing ever cleared `cron.job_run_details`, and the every-minute push tick writes to it all day (about 1,900 runs, 3,800 writes a day). It is a likely part of the Disk IO warning. The nightly snapshot now keeps seven days of it. The page lists the heaviest tables and every cron's runs so the cause can be seen rather than guessed.
- Upgrading Vercel to Pro removes the 4-hour cap (CPU is billed by use against the $20 credit); Supabase Pro's compute credit covers a Micro instance, whose base disk speed is about twice the free one's. Prices and speeds are the providers' as of Nov 2026: check their pages before paying.
- Migration `20261127140000_a10_plan_meters` (**not applied to `jaga-dev` yet**: the owner's call, it is the live database), seed `63_plan_readings.sql`; the page opens and says what it is waiting for until then.

## 2026-11-27 - An SOS no team has taken pulses on the public map, by tambon
The owner asked that an SOS no team has accepted shows as an animated signal on the Live map for everyone to see. Safety rule 6 keeps exact SOS points off the public map, so the owner chose **the public sees the area, not the place**: the tambon's own outline pulses red, with the words "SOS - no team yet" at its middle, and a line above the map names the tambon, the number of requests and how long the oldest has waited (to ten minutes), with the 1784 and 1669 buttons.
- Data is `public_unanswered_sos()` (migration `20261127150000_a6_public_unanswered_sos`, **not applied to `jaga-dev` yet; it needs `20261127130000_a6_clear_stale_sos` first**), served at `/api/public/sos-pulse`, kept 30 seconds at the edge, asked again every minute while the map is open. No point, accuracy, phone, sender, text or case id leaves the database.
- A case counts when it is open, a rescue (not a request for supplies), not merged into another, held by no unit, not a suspected nuisance send an admin has not cleared (a bot must not light the map), and heard from within 72 hours. It shows from the second it arrives.
- A copy older than five minutes is dropped, so a signal that was probably answered is never left lit, and a database that cannot be reached shows nothing rather than "nobody is waiting". Wording is "no team yet", never "nobody will come" (safety rules 1 and 10). The pulse is slower than twice a second and is held still for a reader who asked for less motion; the line above the map says it in words, so it is never colour or motion alone.
- Part two of the same request (teams outside the area see the case's exact point and phone after 30 minutes) is a separate decision below.

## 2026-11-28 - Free-plans migration applied to jaga-dev; the cron jobs failed to start for five hours on 27 Nov

The owner said "migrate now". `20261127140000_a10_plan_meters` and seed 63 are applied to `jaga-dev` (supersedes "not applied yet" above). Checked live: all seven functions exist, `jaga-plan-snapshot` is scheduled at 02:10 Bangkok, the table has no grant to `anon` or `authenticated`, `admin_plan_health()` answers as the super admin, and a snapshot written inside a rolled-back transaction gave 95.9 MB, 0.03 GB read and 5.08 GB written since the counters began on 25 Aug.

- **Found while checking, and not caused by this change:** between about 03:40 and 08:40 Bangkok on 27 Nov, 215 of the 1,436 `jaga-push-tick` runs, 48 of the 287 `jaga-auto-alarm` runs and 17 gauge-reading runs failed with "job startup timeout" (pg_cron could not start them in time). None have failed since. For those hours the automated alarm check and the push dispatch were mostly not running. The likely cause is the exhausted Disk IO budget Supabase warned about the same day, but that is an inference, not something measured. It is the strongest reason to upgrade Supabase now rather than later, and `admin_plan_health()` now shows failed runs per cron so a repeat is visible.
- The first nightly snapshot will delete about 71,000 old cron-log rows once (84,336 now, 13 MB); after that the log holds seven days.
- The table counters run since 25 Aug (the whole life of the project, including every `db:reset`), so the "heaviest tables" list is history, not the current rate. The real daily rate appears after two nightly snapshots.

## 2026-11-27 - A gauge's drawn banks are put on the bank line its words use

The owner, seeing X.90 and SLA002 in the same place on the map with different cross-sections:
"fix it so the drawn walls match the bank line."

The two are different gauges - RID 2589 บ้านบางศาลา and HII 860 คลองหอยโข่ง - that ThaiWater
itself places 1 m apart at one bridge, and their readings agree to 0.13 m, so they measure the
same water. The cards differed because a gauge's **bank line** (`stations.bankfull_m`, the
agency's own ตลิ่ง) comes from ThaiWater's water-level feed, while the **drawn walls** come from
its station record, and the two sources disagree. Both feeds put that bank at 9.34 m and each
feed's own arithmetic confirms it (2.62 + 6.72 and 2.49 + 6.85), SLA002's record agrees to the
centimetre, and X.90's record lists both walls at 11.25.

Measured over all 45 drawn channels on jaga-dev, 27 Nov 2026: every HII, EGAT and FOP station
matches its bank line exactly, and **ten RID stations list walls above it** - X.77 0.60 m,
X.173A 0.81, X.283 1.21, X.44 1.26, X.113 1.43, X.90 1.91, X.240 1.98, X.184 2.91, X.73 3.91,
X.274 4.28 (X.119A 0.48). Six of the seven gauges armed for the automatic alarm are among them.
On those cards the Evacuate line was drawn below ground on both sides: a river comfortably
inside its channel beside words saying it was at Evacuate (safety rule 10).

So `riverSection` now anchors the walls: the **lower** wall is put on the bank line and the
other moves with it. The step between the two banks is a real measurement and decides which side
the water spreads over, so it is kept; the absolute height the record gives for a wall above the
ตลิ่ง is not kept, because it is not the bank - it is whatever stands behind it, a deck or a
levee crest. **The bed is not moved**: it is already on the levels' datum (X.90 lists -0.88
where SLA002 lists -1.01), so moving it would break the one height that agrees, and the depth
the card prints stays bank-line-to-bed. Checked against all 45: eleven move, none puts a wall
below its own bed, and the resulting depths run 4.79 m to 12.38 m.

A record on a genuinely different datum is still thrown away whole rather than anchored - at
X.67A the bed is on that other datum too, so there is nothing worth keeping (decision
2026-11-22). Reason: the picture and the sentence under it must agree, and of the two it is the
sentence that is checked - so the drawing is made to follow the bank the words use.

## 2026-11-27 - An SOS nobody has taken for 30 minutes opens to every rescue team (amends safety rule 5)
The owner asked that teams outside an SOS's coverage can see the case's information, so they can contact the rescue outside the app, and chose to **escalate after a wait** (not at once, and not by an admin relaying details by hand). The wait is **30 minutes** (`system_settings.sos_escalate_minutes`).
- After 30 minutes with no unit holding it, every verified team with the `rescue` capability - the owner and active dispatchers - reads the case's exact point on a new list on its console ("Requests no team has taken, outside your area"), and the sender's number through `reveal_escalated_sos_phone()`, which writes the case and the caller's teams to `audit_log` on every look. Reading the list is logged once in ten minutes. Jaga assigns nothing: the team reaches the person and arranges help outside the app.
- **Who stays out:** field members (they read only what is assigned to them), dam-only and planning units (no `rescue` capability), pending or suspended units, ordinary users and visitors. **Which cases stay out:** a supplies request, a case merged into another, a suspected nuisance send an admin has not cleared, a case a unit holds, a closed case, and a case the sender has not been heard from on for 72 hours. It closes again the moment any of these becomes true. A team's own coverage is left off the list: those cases are already on its board.
- **What goes with it:** the point (5 decimals), tambon, people, vulnerable flags, depth, injuries, the sender's words and whether there is a number. No photo, no voice note, no name, no account, and no number in the list.
- **How it is built:** new functions only (`is_rescue_team_person`, `sos_is_escalated`, `authority_escalated_cases`, `reveal_escalated_sos_phone`; migration `20261127160000_a6_escalate_unanswered_sos`, **not applied to `jaga-dev` yet; it needs the 72-hour migration `20261127130000` first**). `authority_board()`, `reveal_sos_phone()` and the `sos_requests` policies are untouched, and a test asserts a team outside the tambon is still refused by them. The rule 5 line in CLAUDE.md and the access matrix in spec 14 say it.
- Not done, and open for the owner: no push or alarm tells a team a case has opened to it (the list is on the console, which polls). The case has no "I am going" button, so two teams could both go. Both are small to add if wanted.

## 2026-11-28 - The sender of an open request with no number is asked for one, by push and on the case page
The owner asked for a push notification to the owners of the open cases, asking them to give their phone number. On 27 Nov six requests were open and none had a number.
- **What:** five minutes after a rescue request is sent with no number, the phones that follow it are told once ("Add a phone number: your request has no number, so rescuers can't call you. Tap to add one") and the tap opens the sender's own case page. The page now has a card at the top of an open case with no number: one field, the existing wording "Without a number, rescuers can't call you", saved through the same `details` call (queued if the connection is down). It is optional and never in the way of the request (safety rule 1). Supplies requests, merged cases, suspected nuisance sends and closed cases are not asked. A case a team already holds is still asked, because the team needs the number too.
- **How:** its own function `push_need_number_due()` (migration `20261128100000_a7_push_need_number`) and its own tick, in the style of the plan and season pushes. `push_due()`, the function that carries the offers and the admins' call, is untouched. The case is marked as asked only when a phone follows it, so a phone that turns notifications on later still hears it once.
- **Limit found, and not fixable by code:** none of the six open cases had a phone following it (`push_sos_followers` was empty for all six), and five were anonymous. Jaga holds no address for those six people, so this push cannot reach them. It reaches senders from now on who tap "tell this phone when a team accepts". A single-use link, or the LINE account where there is one, would be the other ways.

## 2026-11-28 - Jaga asks to be installed, in a banner under the hero, and lets Chrome ask as well
The owner asked whether Jaga could be installed silently on a visitor's laptop or added to a phone's bookmarks without the person doing anything, and failing that, whether it could ask clearly. **It cannot be silent**: no browser lets a page install itself, add a desktop icon or write a bookmark - Chrome and Edge only hand the page a one-shot `beforeinstallprompt` event it may open *their* dialog from, and iOS, Safari and Firefox give nothing at all. So the answer is "one tap, clearly asked", and the owner chose all three of the ways offered.
- **A banner, not a pointer.** The one line that used to point down at the install card (19 Oct) is gone, with its copy (`install.line`, `install.lineShow`). In its place, in the same spot, `components/InstallBanner.tsx`: the title, one reason, a button and "Not now". It is drawn on phones **and** laptops - the old line was `lg:hidden`, so a laptop visitor was never pointed at anything. The button opens the browser's own dialog where there is one and otherwise opens the card's steps below, through a shared `openGuide()` signal, so there is never a second set of instructions.
- **Under the hero, never above it.** The level of the reader's own tambon stays the first thing on the home screen (safety rule 3), and like the card the banner is dropped entirely at Warning and Evacuate (HomeView's `tense`) and never appears in the SOS flow (safety rule 1). One "Not now" writes the existing `install.later` and silences banner and card together for three weeks.
- **It asks in their own terms** (`installReason()`, pure and tested): notifications already on -> the bell is surer from the icon; places of their own saved -> those keep working with no signal; been here before -> put it on once; otherwise the plain first-visit reason. Visits are counted once per browser session in `install.visits`, and only ever pick wording. The notification permission is read straight from `Notification.permission`, not through `usePush`, because only the wording hangs on it.
- **Chrome may now ask by itself too**, superseding 19 Oct's "the card's button is the one way in": `beforeinstallprompt` is still kept but no longer cancelled, so Chrome's own install bar is free to appear beside our banner. Cancelling it meant an Android visitor who never scrolled was asked by nobody. An e2e test asserts the event is not cancelled.
- Reason: everyone who opens Jaga should end up with it on their device, and the only lever a web app has is to ask well and to ask in the one place a person is already looking.

## 2026-11-28 - Jaga can be prompted from the owner's phone: cloud sessions stop at a pull request
The owner asked whether an idea that arrives away from the laptop can be given to Jaga there and then, and reach the live site without them coming back to `C:\dev\jaga`.
- **Yes, through a cloud session**, started from the Claude mobile app or claude.ai/code on `nafiscode/flood-warning-app`: Anthropic's machine clones the repo, works there and opens a pull request. Vercel already deploys `main` to jagaapp.org, so the whole path from an idea to the live site exists.
- **Such a session has no keys**, because everything useful here is gitignored: `.env.local`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, `.claude/local/`, `pipeline/data/`. So no database, no R2, no Earth Engine, no ThaiWater, and no RLS or e2e suite. Screens, wording, docs, tests and migration *files* are fair game; applying a migration, a pipeline run and the policy tests stay on the laptop.
- **The gate is the pull request, and the owner merges.** `CLAUDE.md` gained a "Sessions without a database" section forbidding a push to `main`, forbidding any claim that a migration is applied or a suite has passed, requiring the unique-prefix check (`ls supabase/migrations | cut -c1-14 | sort | uniq -d`), and warning against rebuilding a function from a body copied out of an older migration - the drift that has bitten this project twice. CI on a throwaway database and the Vercel preview are the real checks.
- **`docs/state.md` is new**: a short public, personal-free summary of where things stand, for a session that has only the repository. `CLAUDE.local.md` stays on the laptop and is not duplicated there.
- Walkthrough 11 on the progress tracker (part 135) has the eight steps, including the GitHub permission screen - one repository, code and pull requests only, never the private `jaga-data`.
- Reason: ideas should not have to wait for the owner to be at the laptop, and life-safety code should not reach the public from a session that cannot run its tests. A pull request satisfies both.
